Jeeves ERP Purchases 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0034-01.png)


<mark>combination. The matrix is used in the whole process flow, from creating purchase orders from requisitions to receiving deliveries.</mark> 

#### 5.2.3 Seasonal products 

<mark>You can set up seasonal items, orders and purchase orders, which can be used together with, or without, the product variant matrix. If used together with the matrix, fields for items that are out of season are inactivated.</mark> 

<mark>If you specify a season on a purchase order header, you can enter purchase order rows for both seasonal and non-seasonal items. When entering purchase order rows for seasonal items the following checks are made:</mark> 

- <mark>That the season complies with the season on the purchase order header</mark> 

- <mark>That the purchase order type is allowed for this season</mark> 

- <mark>That the delivery date complies with this season</mark> 

#### 5.2.4 Item status 

<mark>There are other alternatives for handling the status of items in sales and purchases, i.e. whether an item may be purchased or sold. In Jeeves purchases, discontinued products cannot be ordered.</mark> 

<mark>Besides these, you can use Jeeves ERP’s batch functionality to control the status of items. See the description of the batch functionality under the Inventory module.</mark> 

#### 5.2.5 Acquisition methods 

<mark>Purchased items have a default acquisition method – purchase to order point, to plan or to requirement – which also controls the work process. This can be changed on individual purchases. It is also possible to have different acquisition methods for different inventory locations (warehouses).</mark> 

### 5.3 Costing 

<mark>Please refer to the section on costing in the chapter on</mark> _<mark>Jeeves Inventory.</mark>_ 

### 5.4 Purchase prices and agreements 

<mark>Purchase prices can be entered manually on requisitions and purchase orders, in which case the manually specified price applies. Otherwise the purchase price is retrieved as follows:</mark> 

- <mark>One or more purchase agreements with one or more suppliers, including tiered prices and discounts if required (if there are several agreements the main agreement or the one with highest priority applies)</mark> 

- <mark>Purchase price lists per supplier, including tiered prices and discounts if required, as well as any campaign price lists</mark> 

- <mark>Fixed purchase prices for items</mark> 

<mark>Purchase prices can also be set up to include VAT/tax.</mark> 

<mark>3</mark> 4 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Purchases 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0035-01.png)


#### 5.4.1 Purchase agreements 

<mark>There are several ways to enter purchase agreements with prices agreed upon with the company's suppliers. The most common methods are as follows:</mark> 

- <mark>Manual entry of purchase agreements with an agreement ID and header in the Purchase agreement/RFQ, supplier program. In this case you enter an ID and general information on the header and associated rows in the embedded list window (the Alternative suppliers table).</mark> 

###### **Note** 

In Jeeves, purchase agreements and RFQs are stored in the same table, but with different statuses. 

- <mark>Manual entry of purchase prices without an agreement ID and header directly in the Alternative suppliers table, which is accessed via the menu or a node in the Items program.</mark> 

- <mark>Automatic entry/update of purchase prices without an agreement ID for new suppliers or prices, in the Alternative suppliers table. This is done automatically if you check in the Update agreement price checkbox for the supplier, purchase order or at invoice matching. If checked, new combinations of items and suppliers generate new records, and new prices for existing combinations of items and suppliers update existing records in the Alternative suppliers table.</mark> 

<mark>These methods can be combined.</mark> 

<mark>You can also create purchase agreements or RFQs automatically based on existing records in other tables:</mark> 

- <mark>Entry of purchase agreements/RFQs (with header) based on requisitions in the RFQ from requisition program. In this case you enter an ID and general information on a header and select rows from existing requisitions.</mark> 

- <mark>Automatic entry/update of purchase agreements/prices (with header) based on customer quotations/agreements in the Quotations/agreements program. In this case a header is created automatically with associated details, which is linked to the customer quotation/agreement.</mark> 

<mark>You can use the agreement status to control if a purchase order can be entered against the agreement. You can also set up purchase agreements with tiered prices.</mark> 

#### 5.4.2 New purchase prices on agreements 

<mark>There is a function for recalculating purchase prices in an agreement. You calculate new, preliminary purchase prices and analyze the results before updating the current purchase prices. The purchase price changes are logged.</mark> 

#### 5.4.3 Purchase price lists 

<mark>If you have not specified a purchase price on an agreement, Jeeves ERP looks for a purchase price on a price list.</mark> 

<mark>You can enter purchase price lists per supplier and product class or per supplier and item. You can also combine these alternatives, in which case price lists per item have higher priority.</mark> 

<mark>If a supplier advertises campaign prices, you can connect a campaign price list to the supplier’s normal price list.</mark> 

#### 5.4.4 Fixed purchase price on item 

<mark>Normally, the purchase price is retrieved from the item/supplier record or the purchase price list. If there are none it can be retrieved from the item, on condition that you enter a purchase price</mark> 

<mark>3</mark> 5 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Purchases 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0036-01.png)


<mark>currency for the item. Purchase orders in this currency will automatically retrieve the fixed purchase price on the item.</mark> 

#### 5.4.5 Early settlement discounts 

<mark>You can set up early settlement discounts from suppliers with one level or tiered levels.</mark> 

#### 5.4.6 Posting of accrued royalties 

<mark>You can define royalties per item, item group, or supplier. Accrued royalties are posted according to posting rules when you create the receiving journal and voucher.</mark> 

### 5.5 Requests for quotations (RFQs) 

<mark>Requests for quotations can</mark> 

- <mark>be entered manually for distribution to an individual supplier or</mark> 

- <mark>originate from a requisition for distribution to several suppliers via a distribution list</mark> 

<mark>Requests for quotations can be converted to purchase agreements simply by changing status and making any adjustments to validity, prices etc.</mark> 

### 5.6 Requisitions 

<mark>Requisitions are a means of getting an overview of your purchase or manufacturing requirements. The basis for the requirements can be created via</mark> 

- <mark>one of the programs for manual entry of requisitions</mark> 

- <mark>one of the reports for creating requisitions against order points or against planned inventory activities</mark> 

- <mark>one of the reports for MRP</mark> 

- <mark>a function button on customer orders</mark> 

- <mark>a function button on manufacturing orders</mark> 

- <mark>purchase forecasts</mark> 

<mark>In the requisitions routine the requirements are analyzed, a supplier chosen and quantities and time for deliveries adjusted. Based on the requisition you can then automatically create purchase orders by running a report in one of the programs for requisitions.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP checks against any minimum/maximum inventory balances, order points, order quantities etc.</mark> 

#### 5.6.1 Replication of requisitions 

<mark>Some companies that use Jeeves Master scheduling and forecasting need to replicate relevant data, such as item forecasts and requisitions, between sales units and manufacturing units. There is a report with which you approve selected requisition rows for replication to another company.</mark> 

#### 5.6.2 Fixed delivery days from supplier 

<mark>You can set up fixed delivery days from suppliers to automatically get suggestions for delivery dates on purchase requisitions and purchase orders. When entering and creating requisitions, transforming requisitions to purchase orders and when manually entering purchase orders, a delivery date is retrieved from the calendar connected to the supplier.</mark> 

#### 5.6.3 Forecast purchase orders 

<mark>You can create preliminary purchase orders, forecast purchase orders, to send to suppliers as advance information on what, according to forecasts, will be ordered in the future in a longer term than can be calculated from lead times, etc.</mark> 

<mark>3</mark> 6 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Purchases 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0037-01.png)


<mark>You can then convert the forecast purchase orders into definite purchase orders after making any necessary adjustments.</mark> 

#### 5.6.4 Alternative unit on requisitions 

<mark>Jeeves supports alternative units on requisitions. You can specify an alternative unit code and add the required quantity in the alternative unit. Note that you can only do this for manual entry of requisitions as a first step.</mark> 

### 5.7 Purchase orders 

<mark>Purchases can be created in many ways. You can either enter purchase orders manually or generate them automatically from</mark> 

- <mark>purchase agreements</mark> 

- <mark>requisitions</mark> 

- <mark>customer orders</mark> 

- <mark>service orders</mark> 

- <mark>net requirements</mark> 

- <mark>material reservations on manufacturing orders</mark> 

#### 5.7.1 Purchase order types 

<mark>The work process is controlled through the choice of purchase order type. Purchase order types are predefined and determine how the relevant type of purchase order is created, how it is numbered, if the goods should be received to inventory, how part deliveries should be processed, and so on.</mark> 

#### 5.7.2 Purchase value 

<mark>Purchase values on purchase orders are updated upon receipt of incoming deliveries. You can  view the actual value of the purchase order, irrespective of if any deliveries have been received in fields to retain the values:</mark> 

- <mark>Purchase order value in base currency for the whole purchase order</mark> 

- <mark>Purchase order value in currency for the whole purchase order</mark> 

#### 5.7.3 Inventory order 

<mark>Used when purchasing items to own inventory, i.e. the inventory balance etc., is updated. Also used to order goods that are not stocked, for example, items with the consumables inventory stock type.</mark> 

#### 5.7.4 Drop shipment 

<mark>Used when goods are sold but NOT from own inventory. The supplier sends the goods directly to the customer. Inventory balances are not affected. Purchase orders that are created automatically from customer orders with the Drop shipment order type are automatically assigned this type.</mark> 

#### 5.7.5 Transit 

<mark>A variant of drop shipment in which a purchase order is created from a customer order, but the goods are shipped via own inventory. Processing is more like normal orders except that picking never needs to be done. The received delivery automatically reports dispatch of the customer order.</mark> 

#### 5.7.6 Order specific inventory 

<mark>The goods are placed in an inventory linked to the customer/manufacturing order. The items are picked from this order specific inventory. Via a parameter you can control that any goods remaining</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

<mark>3</mark> 7 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Purchases 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0038-01.png)


<mark>in the order specific inventory when the order is released should be picked automatically. An order specific inventory is useful when the goods have a high value and remain in stock a while before being shipped to the customer. There are programs and lists that show the value of the order unique stock.</mark> 

#### 5.7.7 Subcontract 

<mark>Refers to purchasing of labor for an operation in a manufacturing order. Instead of performing the labor on the shop floor, the labor is purchased.</mark> 

#### 5.7.8 Advance purchase for manufacturing 

<mark>Used when you know in advance that certain material with a long delivery time must be used in a manufacturing order. Preparation/design may not be ready, but you know that certain critical material/components will be used (order specific). The purchase order row or rows must be flagged with manufacturing order number (suborder number and operation number should be blank, and are set to 0 when the purchase order row is saved). Later, when you create a manufacturing order with corresponding material reservations and press the button to create order specific purchase orders, the material is linked to the advance purchase. If no such purchase exists, purchase orders or purchase requisitions are created.</mark> 

#### 5.7.9 Transfer between inventory locations 

<mark>Used to create customer orders for transfer between inventory locations in the order routine, which means that you can create picking lists and delivery notes in the same way as for normal customer orders. The receiving inventory location sees the planned delivery in the planning list and reports the delivery when it arrives.</mark> 

#### 5.7.10 Payment terms 

<mark>You can store an unlimited number of payment terms, per supplier, per agreement or general. Credit periods and any early settlement discounts can be set up in numerous ways.</mark> 

<mark>There are several methods for calculating due dates:</mark> 

- <mark>Calculate period from arrival or invoice date or from beginning of following month(s)</mark> 

- <mark>Extend period to end of month</mark> 

#### 5.7.11 VAT 

<mark>In Jeeves Purchases/Accounts payable you set up suppliers and items for VAT. Your company can apply any number of VAT rates.</mark> 

<mark>See also separate sections describing VAT in the</mark> _<mark>Sales</mark>_ <mark>module</mark> _<mark>, VAT declarations</mark>_ <mark>, as well as</mark> _<mark>VAT reporting within the European Union.</mark>_ 

#### 5.7.12 Fixed delivery days from supplier 

<mark>You can set up fixed delivery days from suppliers to automatically get suggestions for delivery dates on purchase requisitions and purchase orders.</mark> 

#### 5.7.13 Lead times 

<mark>Suggested delivery dates, whether on fixed days or not, always take lead times into consideration. The purchase agreement shows to the total lead time, i.e.,:</mark> 

<mark>Administrative lead time</mark> 

- <mark>+ Lead time from supplier</mark> 

- <mark>+ Transport time</mark> 

- <mark>+ Possible extra lead time for, for example, checks, inspection, etc. (LedTidExtra1)</mark>

Jeeves ERP Purchases 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0039-01.png)


<mark>The lead time on the item per inventory location record is updated automatically from the purchase agreement that is flagged as the main agreement.</mark> 

<mark>See also the section on Jeeves’ lead time matrix in the chapter on the</mark> _<mark>Sales module.</mark>_ 

#### 5.7.14 Buffer days on order-specific purchases and requisitions 

<mark>If no buffer days are set up, the requirement date is based on the customer order's estimated dispatch date. The standard functionality for determining the number of buffer days between the order row's estimated dispatch date and the preparation date is determined via Dispatch methods, sales, the Internal lead time days field.</mark> 

<mark>Alternatively, you can use the Preparation date field on the customer order row to receive the requirement date for linked purchase and manufacturing orders or requisitions. This means the preparation date for the customer order is taken into consideration in the programs for creating order-specific purchase orders and manufacturing orders and in the MRP program. The preparation date can in turn be calculated based on lead times for the item/item group.</mark> 

#### 5.7.15 Alternative units 

<mark>Alternative units can be used for automatic or manual unit conversion in the Inventory, Sales and Purchasing modules. If the function is activated, items can be purchased, stocked, sold, and returned in different units. Costing is, however, based on your inventory stock unit.</mark> 

<mark>You can enter inventory transactions, order, requisition and purchase order rows, dispatches and received deliveries in your own inventory unit or in the alternative unit. You can set the function up to allow entry of quantities in both units, if the relationship between stock units and alternative units vary. This is useful in the food industry, for example, where a stock unit can vary in weight and you need to specify both the number of units and the weight. This setup also accumulates inventory balances in alternative units in Inventory balance records.</mark> 

<mark>If you set the function up for automatic conversion according to a fixed conversion rate, the unit you enter is automatically converted into the other unit based on the conversion rate. In other words, if you enter a quantity in an alternative unit, it is automatically converted to your inventory unit, and vice versa.</mark> 

<mark>Example: An item is stocked per piece. It can be purchased and sold per piece but can also be purchased and sold in the alternative units of 6 pack, 12 pack or 24 pack. If you use 6 pack as alternative unit when entering an item on an order or purchase order and specify a quantity of 3, the quantity is automatically converted to 18 in your stock unit.</mark> 

<mark>If you set the function up for manual conversion, you enter quantities in both units and Jeeves ERP checks that the relationship between them is reasonable according to the given conversion factor and tolerance percentages. When using manual conversion rates, Jeeves ERP suggests the quantity in the alternative unit but you can change it. This applies only when entering new order rows, not when changing existing rows.</mark> 

<mark>You can search for the price from a price list that is set for an alternative unit.</mark> 

<mark>You can also deactivate alternative units that are no longer to be used.</mark> 

#### 5.7.16 Own or supplier's item number 

<mark>You can enter purchase order rows using your company’s own item numbers or the supplier's item numbers, when using the supplier's number Jeeves ERP automatically retrieves your own numbers.</mark> 

#### 5.7.17 External texts 

<mark>General texts can be used to print standard texts on documents. For example, if you want to add a New Year's greeting or the like to all external documents, you can enter the greeting as a general text.</mark> 

<mark>You can decide on which documents the text is printed, for example, on purchase orders, delivery notes, invoices, or all external documents. On the other hand, you cannot control that the text</mark>

Jeeves ERP Purchases 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0040-01.png)


<mark>should only be printed on certain purchase orders, delivery notes or invoices. If you specify that the text is to be printed on a document, it will be printed on all printouts of that document.</mark> 

<mark>Texts can be controlled by date using the Start date and End date fields. If you have specified several texts within the same date interval on the same document, the most recently entered text will be used.</mark> 

<mark>In the same way, you can create supplier-specific standard texts and control which documents these can be printed on.</mark> 

#### 5.7.18 Surcharges 

<mark>Automatic item surcharges can be applied on both sales and purchases. On sales you get automatic entry and invoicing of deposits, environmental charges, etc., which by law must be reported and paid in connection with sales of certain types of products, for example, DVDs. On purchases you can control and reconcile charges on supplier invoices for such products.</mark> 

<mark>A surcharge is entered as a non-stocked item connected to a (normal) stocked item. It is priced according to the price given in the item table, and a customer/purchase order row is generated with the same quantity as the basic order row.</mark> 

#### 5.7.19 Joint purchases 

<mark>Joint purchases can be used to decrease the number of purchases when purchase orders are created automatically from customer orders, and there are several order rows for items to be delivered from the same supplier within a specific time.</mark> 

#### 5.7.20 Subcontract 

<mark>Subcontract purchase orders are for purchasing labor operations to be carried out at another workshop/factory. This can be necessary, for example, if the workload at your own workshop is too high or for a specific type of work.</mark> 

#### 5.7.21 Order confirmation from supplier 

<mark>There is a function for entering delivery confirmations from suppliers. With this function, purchase order rows can be assigned a status that shows they have been confirmed by the supplier. At the same time, the promised and confirmed delivery dates are updated on the row.</mark> 

<mark>Purchase order rows are automatically split if the supplier does not confirm the whole quantity, one row for each part delivery confirmation.</mark> 

### 5.8 Received deliveries 

<mark>The work process for receiving deliveries can be set up depending on the company’s routines and the products’ need for inspection or quality control etc. It can be anything between extremely simple and very thorough, and steps can be automated or manual. Different methods can be also be used within the same company.</mark> 

<mark>You can use purchase order reports as a basis for checking received deliveries. Some of these reports can be set up to include information about customer orders waiting to be dispatched, in order to save time by not having to unpack and put away the goods.</mark> 

#### 5.8.1 Goods arrivals and receipt 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP, you can choose from three alternative ways of handling goods arrivals and received deliveries against purchase orders. The alternative you choose depends on the elements included in the company’s goods arrivals routines; arrival entry, arrival check and received deliveries, and who does them. The choice can be made per supplier and/or item.</mark> 

<mark>Receive directly without checking is the simplest process and means that updating inventory balances is done at arrival entry.</mark> 

<mark>4</mark> 0 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Purchases 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0041-01.png)


<mark>Arrival check means that after arrival entry the goods are sent to a special table for goods that need to be checked. From this control table, you can print reports or find the items waiting to be checked. It is from the control table that you enter the approved quantity either for further processing by delivery personnel or for delivery directly into inventory.</mark> 

<mark>At arrival entry, you can enter remarks and also create return delivery notes directly if something or everything is to be sent back to the supplier.</mark> 

#### 5.8.2 Receive shipment 

<mark>There is a program for finding arrival entries per shipment ID in order to report received deliveries per shipment.</mark> 

#### 5.8.3 Part deliveries/backorders 

<mark>You can choose between manual or automatic numbering of part deliveries against purchase orders. You can enter the supplier's delivery note number on the order header, which will be automatically copied onto the deliveries you enter.</mark> 

#### 5.8.4 Automatic bin location 

<mark>Jeeves ERP can automatically assign a bin for received goods depending on bin type and quantity.</mark> 

#### 5.8.5 Blocked inventory 

<mark>You can block a quantity in stock from being used or dispatched directly during goods receipt or at a later stage. This can be necessary if special inspection is required, for example, or if you receive many complaints.</mark> 

#### 5.8.6 Returns 

<mark>Returns can be entered for any items that are to be returned to the supplier. A return delivery note can be created automatically. If there is an existing, unprinted delivery note for the supplier in question you can add to it.</mark> 

#### 5.8.7 Forward cover 

<mark>There is a simple function for managing forward cover of foreign currency. It calculates the value of the received delivery at the purchase order’s exchange rate.</mark> 

### 5.9 Supplier invoices 

#### 5.9.1 Matching supplier invoices against received deliveries 

<mark>Supplier invoices can be linked to purchase orders for checking before final approval and posting. You can control whether only received rows may be matched and whether goods arrivals and matching can be done simultaneously.</mark> 

<mark>There is also an option to automatically link and match supplier invoices and purchase orders as far as possible. If the data in the invoice agrees with the data in the received purchase order, and if the specified limits for differences have not been exceeded, then matching and final approval will occur completely automatically and the invoice will be marked as ready for payment.</mark> 

#### 5.9.2 Purchasing costs on supplier invoices 

<mark>There are several alternatives for handling purchasing costs on supplier invoices in connection with invoice matching. The work method you choose depends on whether you apply FIFO (First In First Out), if you work with average weighted costs, etc., as well as if the overheads should be added to or should replace any markup on the items, suppliers, etc.</mark> 

<mark>4</mark> 1 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Purchases 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0042-01.png)


### 5.10 Contract matching 

<mark>Jeeves ERP supports contract matching for supplier invoices. With contract matching, organizations can automatically match invoices that they receive at regular periods and for predictable payments to an underlying contract, without the need for any supporting documents; for example, purchase orders.</mark> 

<mark>You can enter preapproved contracts along with valid from-valid to dates, posting, and periodicity information. You can set reminders for when a contract time is about to run out, match incoming invoices automatically against existing contracts and set a matching tolerance on your supplier.</mark> 

<mark>You can define how difference amount will be handled during invoice matching, have different suppliers on invoice and contract, multiple supplier invoices for the same contract period and specify percentage instead of a fixed amount when defining the contract posting template.</mark> 

### 5.11 Supplier statistics and evaluations 

<mark>Thanks to Jeeves ERP’s superior search possibilities, and to the fact that you can customize forms and lists by adding fields, columns, totals etc., you can compile information on many dimensions in more or less any table. You can also build your own reports and business graphics based on own search criteria and layouts. Besides this, there are a number of standard forms for viewing, and reports for printing, statistics regarding purchases and supplier performance.</mark> 

#### 5.11.1 Statistics 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes forms and reports for received delivery and invoicing statistics per supplier. There are also connections between suppliers, purchase agreements, purchase orders etc. with received delivery statistics, for example, to check previous prices and matched invoices, delivery performance etc.</mark> 

#### 5.11.2 Evaluations 

<mark>You can award points to suppliers based on any concept, standard or own to perform the required evaluations. The standard evaluation includes different aspects of delivery precision, such as the number of deliveries between ordered and delivered quantity, the number of late and early deliveries, as well as the number of returns.</mark> 

### 5.12 Cost calculation templates/Detailed purchase calculation 

<mark>You can set up cost calculation templates to split costs and control their postings. This is currently based on the former Import module functionality, which means it requires that the product account is set up for Lot handling and that the relevant items are stocked in batches.</mark> 

<mark>A unique template ID is defined along with a template description when you create a new template for purchase/inventory cost templates. You can connect a Template ID to alternative suppliers, items, and suppliers. The purchase/inventory cost calculation template is applied when entering a purchase order for such an item, and the value is calculated on the average or FIFO value of the item.</mark> 

### 5.13 Posting and integration with general ledger 

<mark>You create inventory postings upon final approval after invoice matching. One final approval record is created and posted per invoice. Each final approval record generates a voucher in the general ledger.</mark> 

<mark>You post deliveries that have not yet been invoiced and matched at desired intervals, for example, during monthly or more regular reconciliations. One delivery journal is created and posted per delivery date. Each journal generates a voucher in the general ledger.</mark> 

<mark>Posting is done according to posting rules for inventory transactions that control to which account and account segments (cost center, cost unit, etc.) the delivery should be posted.</mark>

Jeeves ERP Purchases 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0043-01.png)


#### 5.13.1 Manual posting of purchases 

<mark>It is possible to manually specify account segment 1, 2, 3 and 4, when purchasing for own use (consumables), as an alternative to automatic posting through the product account.</mark> 

#### 5.13.2 Reconciliations 

<mark>During period reconciliations you can use Jeeves ERP’s function for reconciliation of delivered but non-invoice matched quantities and values.</mark> 

<mark>There are also functions for reconciliation and analysis of differences in quantities and/or amounts, as well as for analysis of postings, on customer orders with associated purchase orders, for example, Drop shipments and Transit orders.</mark> 

### 5.14 Claims 

<mark>You can enter and log purchase claims, i.e. remarks and complaints about items, purchase orders and supplier invoices, and record how they are to be settled as well as actions to be taken.</mark> 

<mark>You can define different work flows for different types of claims, specify base data for scheduling, and control how activities should be reported.</mark> 

<mark>You can also access any claims records from the purchase order rows.</mark> 

### 5.15 Industry-specifics 

<mark>Most functions in Jeeves Purchases are common to all industries. There are some, however, that are specific to certain industries, as described below.</mark> 

#### 5.15.1 Product variant matrix 

<mark>The product variant matrix mentioned earlier was developed with the fashion industry in mind, but can be used for any products with variants, i.e. combinations of models, colors, sizes etc., or the like.</mark> 

#### 5.15.2 Alloy surcharges 

<mark>You can set up different surcharges per kilo or percent, connected to purchase agreements for items. A surcharge can occur when purchasing metal plate as an addition to the base price. Note that alloy surcharges cannot be combined with purchase price lists.</mark> 

<mark>You can also set up future surcharges. If a future alloy surcharge has been set up and the estimated dispatch date on the purchase order row is the same or later than the date when the new surcharge is to take effect, the new alloy surcharge is applied instead.</mark> 

#### 5.15.3 Import module/Detailed purchase calculation 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes a special function for cost estimate and actual cost on purchase order rows and complete traceability through lot numbers that follow the purchased items throughout the system.</mark> 

<mark>You determine which items should be handled in this way using the inventory stock type and the product account (the Lot handling checkbox). This means that a project with an associated purchase cost record for the inventory location in question is automatically created when you order such items, which is assigned a project number from the purchase order row</mark> 

<mark>A batch is created in the purchasing routine when the items in question are received, which is automatically given the same project number (lot number).</mark> 

<mark>If you use allocations, during an allocation you can retrieve the project/lot number automatically from the batch number and the number is copied when picking. Otherwise, a batch ID is required when picking items that are stocked in batches, and you must enter it manually. In this way, you obtain a link and follow-up of a lot through the project/lot number from the batch.</mark>

Jeeves ERP Purchases 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0044-01.png)


### 5.16 Localizations/internationalization 

<mark>Most functions in Jeeves Purchases are common to all countries. There are some, however, that are specific to certain countries or trading areas, as described below.</mark> 

#### 5.16.1 Cost posting and/or inventory posting of received deliveries 

<mark>Some countries, for example, France and Belgium, apply cost posting at received delivery instead of inventory posting. The received delivery value is posted to an interim cost account which is then reposted to a final cost account at supplier invoice matching. It is sometimes necessary to combine the two and in Jeeves ERP it is possible to do so.</mark> 

<mark>The system does not continuously post inventory accounts, nor is Cost of sold goods posted when posting customer invoices. The cost is taken directly in connection with received deliveries of goods or in connection with arrival or approval of supplier invoices. Invoice matching against purchase orders in Jeeves ERP only functions if the amounts are posted to an interim account.</mark> 

#### 5.16.2 Intrastat reporting (European union) 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes functions for producing the required declarations of dispatches and arrivals between the company’s own country and other countries within the EU, including drill down to the invoices included in the dispatches and arrivals totals. This is to make it possible to reconcile the data in the report.</mark> 

<mark>If you choose to use the functions, you can either use the information generated as a basis to manually fill out the Intrastat form or you can compile the Intrastat report in an external report generator. You can also export the data to MS Excel using Jeeves ERP's Dynamic reports. You can then use Intrastat Data Entry Package (IDEP), which is free software that is available on the web, to automatically import your Intrastat reports into the tax authority’s web site.</mark> 

<mark>See the chapter on</mark> _<mark>Jeeves General ledger</mark>_ <mark>for further information.</mark>

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0045-01.png)


## 6 Jeeves ERP Sales 

<mark>Jeeves ERP’s sales module can help business enterprises, organizations and authorities improve and fine-tune their sales process, irrespective of industry or field.</mark> 

<mark>With Jeeves Sales you manage the entire sales process – from quotations and agreements, to invoicing customer orders and generating sales statistics – all within the same application. Communication with external applications, for example, with e-sales for webshops, is fully integrated and handled from within the Jeeves ERP application.</mark> 

<mark>The main sales process and common sub processes are packaged with pre-defined process charts, roles, role-specific menus and forms as well as user guides and training material. Besides these processes, you can activate other, wider functionality for specific company needs.</mark> 

### 6.1 Companies and customers 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP, you have a common Company table for company information regardless of the type of company – prospective customers and business opportunities, customers, carriers and suppliers. This eliminates the need for duplicate entries and maintenance of company information.</mark> 

<mark>Additional, customer-specific information is managed in related customer tables. Here you can handle all types of customers, domestic and international, customers with another payer, with factoring, with several points of delivery etc.</mark> 

<mark>You can click a button to automatically create an order (header) for the selected customer, based on default values for example from the customer. The order rows form is opened in Add mode so that you can directly start entering order rows for the customer.</mark> 

#### 6.1.1 Customer categorization 

<mark>You can handle any number of customer categories for posting control and statistics etc., any number of customer discount classes for discount management, also customer classes for own classification purposes.</mark> 

#### 6.1.2 Language management 

<mark>You can manage the language of item names, units, payment terms, delivery terms, delivery methods, currency clause texts, etc. and print external documents in the customer's language.</mark> 

### 6.2 Products and services 

<mark>Jeeves ERP also has a common Items table for information regardless of the type of products and items – finished products, semi-manufactured, components, raw materials, packaging, consumables, stocked or non-stocked.</mark> 

#### 6.2.1 Product/item classification 

<mark>There are many possibilities for categorizing and classifying items and products, for example:</mark> 

- <mark>product classes – can control pricing and be used for statistics</mark> 

- <mark>product accounts – control inventory management and valuation, and can control inventory postings</mark> 

- <mark>item groups – can control pricing and be used for statistics and budget allocation</mark> 

- <mark>item classes – can be used for purchase planning, statistics and budget allocation</mark> 

- <mark>item categories – can be used for statistics, for example, for collections of item groups</mark> 

- <mark>item types– can be used for statistics</mark> 

<mark>4</mark> 5 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0046-01.png)


#### 6.2.2 Product variants 

<mark>There is functionality for handling product variants, useful for companies in the fashion industry, for example, for defining applicable combinations of say, models, colors, sizes etc. of a product with the help of a matrix. The matrix has various layers that you can switch between for viewing current and planned inventory balances, item numbers, alternative item descriptions, delivery dates, prices etc. for each combination. Allocation and picking can be reported per item (master item) and season or assortment, if desired. The matrix is used in the whole process flow, from placing orders to printing invoices.</mark> 

#### 6.2.3 Seasonal products 

<mark>You can set up seasonal items, orders and purchase orders, which can be used together with, or without, the product variant matrix. If used together with the matrix, fields for items that are out of season are inactivated.</mark> 

<mark>If you specify a season on a customer order header, you can enter order rows for both seasonal and non-seasonal items. When entering order rows for seasonal items the following checks are made:</mark> 

- <mark>That the season complies with the season on the order header</mark> 

- <mark>That the order type is allowed for this season</mark> 

- <mark>That the delivery date complies with this season</mark> 

#### 6.2.4 Item status 

<mark>There are other alternatives for handling the status of items in sales and purchases, i.e. whether an item may be purchased or sold. Statuses in the sales module:</mark> 

- <mark>Item OK to use – whether the item is available for use at all (in sales, service and production)</mark> 

- <mark>Sellable item – whether the item is available for sales and service orders</mark> 

- <mark>Item OK to quote – possibility to override the above statuses for quotation only</mark> 

- <mark>Discontinued – the item is available for sale until the inventory balance reaches 0</mark> 

<mark>Besides these, you can use Jeeves ERP’s batch functionality to control the status of items. See the description of the batch functionality under the Inventory module.</mark> 

### 6.3 Sales prices and discounts 

<mark>You can set up sales prices in a number of ways. The most common is to work with price lists and one or more discount matrices, or customer and order value discounts. You can also combine the different pricing alternatives, for example, link a customer to a price list and a tiered order value discount.</mark> 

<mark>You can control which date should be used when retrieving prices to quotation and order rows, order/quotation date or today’s date.</mark> 

<mark>As standard, Jeeves ERP searches for the relevant price in the order given below. Your company can, however, apply a different pricing method and change priority or disregard one or more of the pricing alternatives.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP automatically sets the pricing method that has been used on each order row.</mark> 

#### 6.3.1 Quoted/agreed prices 

<mark>If there is a quotation or agreement, the quoted/agreed prices apply.</mark> 

#### 6.3.2 Customer-specific price list 

<mark>If you set up a customer-specific price list, i.e. with a company ID in the price header, and the function is activated in the system data, these prices apply for that customer in combination with any discount rates.</mark> 

<mark>4</mark> 6 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0047-01.png)


#### 6.3.3 Price lists 

<mark>If you set up a price list, connect customers to the price list in the Customers table, and if the function is activated in the system data these prices apply for these customers in combination with any discount rates.</mark> 

<mark>You can set up price lists per item or item group. Price lists can have set prices or be based on markups or reductions on cost/list prices. Validity can be controlled against order entry or dispatch date. You can combine price lists with discounts with or without quantity limits. You can link a customer to one or more price lists.</mark> 

#### 6.3.4 Campaign price lists 

<mark>You can set up date controlled campaign prices. When entering orders, Jeeves ERP compares these with the customer's normal price list and applies the lowest price.</mark> 

#### 6.3.5 Supplementary price lists 

<mark>Supplementary price lists can be used for items that are not included in a customer's normal price list. The idea is that you use supplementary price lists together with customer-specific price lists, to hold the prices that are common to several or all customers. They only work in one step.</mark> 

#### 6.3.6 Fixed prices for items 

<mark>You can enter a fixed sales price in the item’s list price field, which applies if agreement or price list exists.</mark> 

#### 6.3.7 Prices per product class 

<mark>You can use the item’s product class to either set up fixed or tiered prices, or to calculate sales prices based on cost prices per product class. This will override any calculated price per product class. Pricing method on order rows is automatically set to the method that has been used on each order row.</mark> 

#### 6.3.8 Net prices 

<mark>Prices can be set up as net prices on price lists and quotations/agreements.</mark> 

#### 6.3.9 Prices including value added tax 

<mark>Prices can also be set up to include VAT/tax when selling to consumers.</mark> 

#### 6.3.10 General customer discounts 

<mark>You can give general customer discounts, or code controlled customer discounts i.e. controlled by codes on the customer and item.</mark> 

#### 6.3.11 Discount matrix 

<mark>The discount matrix enables you to automatically determine sales discounts depending on which item the customer purchases and, if so desired, in what quantity. You can also link customers to price lists via the discount matrix.</mark> 

<mark>A discount is retrieved from the matrix to the order row when you enter the order row quantity, if a discount has been specified for the combination of customer discount class, item discount class and quantity.</mark> 

<mark>You can set the availability for a discount matrix based on date. The addition of the date fields allows you to preconfigure the discount matrix and simplifies the process for future changes.</mark> 

<mark>4</mark> 7 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0048-01.png)


#### 6.3.12 Order value discounts 

<mark>You can set up discounts to be given on customer orders based on the order's total discountable net/gross value. These can be general order value discounts, according to agreements with customers, according to a discount matrix or determined by codes on customers and items.</mark> 

<mark>You can combine the various pricing and discount alternatives, for example, a customer can be granted both a price list and a tiered order value discount.</mark> 

#### 6.3.13 Early Settlement Discount 

<mark>You can set up early settlement discounts on one level or on tiered levels. You can also calculate these discounts on the invoice total or on the value of goods. If calculating on the value of goods, you can also determine which goods are to be included in the calculation.</mark> 

#### 6.3.14 Manual discount on order 

<mark>Users can, of course, manually enter discounts on order rows, for all types of discounts except for order value discounts. A discount can be specified on the order header and copied to existing order rows.</mark> 

#### 6.3.15 Combined costing and pricing 

<mark>Most functions for costing are gathered in Jeeves Inventory, this applies to both purchased and manufactured items. There is, however, a function in Jeeves Sales in which you can calculate both cost prices and list prices from the same program. Via this function you can also import price lists from suppliers via Microsoft Excel. The import function is based on Jeeves Message Integrator (JMI).</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP provides a standard XML mapping for import of price lists, based on the columns that are possible to update in the costing and pricing programs. You can adjust the mapping, for example, if the columns names in the file from the supplier are different from Jeeves ERP's column names. You can also add any additional calculations to be done in conjunction with the import.</mark> 

### 6.4 Quotations and agreements 

<mark>You can either enter quotations</mark> 

- <mark>in the quotations/agreements program and, when the customer makes an order, convert the quotation into an order (the quotation is then stored in the quotation system's tables) or</mark> 

- <mark>in the order entry programs but with order status Quotation (the quotation is then stored in the order system's tables).</mark> 

#### 6.4.1 Quotation templates 

<mark>You can set up one or more standard templates for quotations and simply change the customer number when creating orders from the quotation templates. For this, the customer number specified in the template should be coded as an internal customer in the company table.</mark> 

#### 6.4.2 Quotations with tiered prices 

<mark>You can specify tiered prices on quotations to customers where the price is determined by either a calculated dispatch date or the order row quantity.</mark> 

#### 6.4.3 Quotations connected to mailing lists/campaigns 

<mark>When you connect a quotation to a mailing list or campaign, the customers in the campaign are copied to the Customers on quotation node. When you print the quotation, it is printed out for each customer and the print date is updated on these records.</mark>

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0049-01.png)


### 6.5 Sales orders – process/work flow 

<mark>Order processing is controlled by:</mark> 

- <mark>The order program you use, i.e. Order from quotation/agreement or Order processing, standard, complete or package.</mark> 

- <mark>The order type you choose on each order or order row. The order type controls which activities the order is to go through. By this we mean, for example, order acknowledgement, allocating against inventory or purchase/manufacturing orders, picking, release and invoicing, as well as printing documents corresponding to these activities. The order type also determines the order and invoice number series to be used.</mark> 

<mark>Customer orders and order changes can also be received and processed automatically through standardized APIs. For more information, see</mark> _<mark>Digital business documents</mark>_ <mark>later in this chapter.</mark> 

#### 6.5.1 Order types 

<mark>Any number of order types can be applied. A set of pre-defined order types are included but can be adjusted according to own requirements:</mark> 

- <mark>Normal - Used when items are sold to customers from own inventory. Order acknowledgements, picking lists, pack lists, freight bills, invoices, etc. can be printed. Picking can be done by button or via the picking program/node.</mark> 

- <mark>Internet - Used when you want a separate number series for Internet orders (necessary for replication). Works the same as Normal order in all other respects.</mark> 

- <mark>Direct issue - A variant of Normal order but where picking is automatically reported when an order row is entered. No picking lists are printed. Used when entering orders after dispatch or when selling over the counter.</mark> 

- <mark>Automatic release - A variant of Direct issue, but picking is automatically reported when the order is released. This means that order rows that are changed or canceled before release do not generate inventory transactions (since no picking is done). When you exit order row entry you are asked if you wish to release it.</mark> 

- <mark>Drop shipment (supplier->customer) - Here the items are not sold from own inventory. A purchase order is created from the sales order and is sent to the supplier for direct delivery to the customer. No picking lists, delivery notes or freight bills are printed. The order is invoiced when the supplier confirms shipment to the customer, or when the supplier invoice arrives. Inventory balances are not affected.</mark> 

- <mark>Transit (supplier->inventory->customer) - A variant of Drop shipment, but the goods pass through own inventory. The work flow is much like Normal order, except that you do not need to pick the items; they are automatically shipped upon receipt.</mark> 

- <mark>Order-specific inventory - A variant of Transit via own inventory but the goods are not automatically picked on receipt. They are placed in a separate inventory linked to the customer order (order-specific inventory). The items are then picked from this orderspecific inventory. Via a parameter you determine how any remaining, unpicked items are to be handled. Order-specific inventory is useful when the goods have a high value and remain in stock a while before being shipped to the customer. There are programs and lists that show the value of the order-specific stock.</mark> 

- <mark>Stock replenishment ICT - Used for internal stock replenishment from own, delivering business unit to other, selling business unit (internal company/inventory location) via Inter Company Transactions (ICT). A purchase order is created at the business unit where the stock is to be replenished, and a customer order is automatically created from the purchase order at the business unit to supply the goods for replenishment. Customer/purchase orders of this type require a company ID that is flagged as an internal company, when you enter the supplier ID on the replenishment purchase order at the own business unit, and also for the customer ID used on the customer order that is automatically created at the supplying business unit. For further information, please see the chapter on</mark> _<mark>Jeeves ICT</mark>_ <mark>.</mark> 

- <mark>Credit order - A Credit order is a negative Normal order. The quantity on the order row must be entered as a negative quantity for return to inventory. (Note: The order type does not control the return to inventory; the quantity field does this.) Jeeves ERP does not actually</mark> 

<mark>4</mark> 9 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0050-01.png)


<mark>use the order type to create a credit note/invoice; it is the invoice total that determines if the invoice is a debit or a credit invoice.</mark> 

- <mark>Price adjustment - Used to adjust incorrect prices on invoices issued. The cost price is set to zero on the invoice. No inventory balances are affected.</mark> 

- <mark>Sample - A variant of Normal order where the sales price on the invoice is set to zero. If you have also flagged the Temporary inventory transfer field on the order type, the items are automatically moved to the inventory location specified in the To inventory location field, i.e. the inventory location for loans/temporary deliveries. Order type flagged in this way are automatically blocked from invoicing.</mark> 

- <mark>Transfer between inventory locations - Used for transferring goods between inventory locations to allow order and inventory personnel to plan orders, generate picking lists and delivery notes as with normal orders. This order type does not generate an invoice; the inventory value is instead transferred between the inventory units via inventory postings.</mark> 

#### 6.5.2 Order entry templates 

<mark>You can create templates to make it quicker and easier to enter orders with the same/similar content. When entering an order based on a template, the order rows can be generated all at once or one at a time.</mark> 

#### 6.5.3 Parameter controlled work flows 

<mark>Standard order statuses control the roles that can access the order and the activities that can be performed, as well as giving information about how far it has come in the order process.</mark> 

<mark>There are also parameters to control the work flow according to own processes, for example, which status changes are automatic and which are manual, which status changes/entries are allowed in which programs, which documents can be printed for a certain status etc.</mark> 

#### 6.5.4 Available to promise 

<mark>Available to promise (ATP) functionality gives an accurate picture of the available quantities of a product on an estimated dispatch date. These calculations/queries can be run from customer orders and/or from the Assembly analysis program.</mark> 

### 6.6 Sales orders – terms and credit control 

#### 6.6.1 Payment terms 

<mark>You can store an unlimited number of payment terms, per customer or payer, per agreement or general. Credit periods and any early settlement discounts can be set up in numerous ways.</mark> 

<mark>There is an array of methods for calculating due dates:</mark> 

- <mark>Credit period in days or in months</mark> 

- <mark>Calculate period from delivery date or from beginning of following month(s)</mark> 

- <mark>Extend period to end of month</mark> 

- <mark>Set dates, for example 10th, 20th and last day of month</mark> 

- <mark>Cash on delivery</mark> 

- <mark>Advance payment before delivery</mark> 

#### 6.6.2 Orders for payment by installments 

<mark>You can handle customer orders whose invoices shall be paid by installments. For each payment installment you specify the credit period in days and the percentage of the total amount to be paid in the installment. The sum of the percentages must be 100.</mark> 

<mark>5</mark> 0 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0051-01.png)


<mark>For example, you could define:</mark> 

- <mark>two equal installments with 30 days between them; enter one row for 30 days and one for 60 days with 50 percent on each or</mark> 

- <mark>three installments; a first installment of 20 percent after 10 days, a second of 30 percent after 20 days and the remaining 50 percent after 30 days.</mark> 

<mark>On the payment terms you can also enter a minimum limit for splitting invoices into installments.</mark> 

<mark>You can adjust the installments, to create order specific installments, if desired but the sum of the percentages must be 100.</mark> 

<mark>When invoicing, Jeeves ERP creates one invoice with installment records. There is a special window in the invoice report template that you can make visible in order to print information about the installments.</mark> 

#### 6.6.3 Credit limit check 

<mark>Credit limit checks can be performed for customers and/or payers. Aggregated sales order and service order value will be included in the credit check. If the combined value exceeds the credit limit set for the customer, an appropriate message will be displayed.</mark> 

<mark>There are two ways of working with credit limits in the order routine:</mark> 

- <mark>Basic function with only a manual credit block</mark> 

<mark>In order for the automatic credit limit check on orders to take effect you must define a credit limit for the customer. If you set a limit, the customer's credit will always be automatically checked and the user will receive a message, when entering order headers or rows, if the specified limit is exceeded by the total of the current A/R balance and order balance.</mark> 

<mark>This type of credit limit check does not provide automatic credit blocking of orders during entry, but you can manually block an order by changing the credit block status field on the order header.</mark> 

- <mark>Extended function with manual or automatic credit block</mark> 

<mark>As an alternative to the basic function, you can specify which type of credit limit check is to be applied for the customer. This type of credit limit check allows you to manually or automatically block an order being entered if the customer's credit limit has already been exceeded or is exceeded during order entry. You can continue to add to and change a blocked order but the order cannot be dispatched.</mark> 

<mark>You can turn off Jeeves credit control for individual users, for example, for warehouse personnel.</mark> 

<mark>There is also a function in the accounts receivable for automatic credit check of customers based on the customers' due invoices.</mark> 

#### 6.6.4 Addresses 

<mark>You can store an unlimited number of invoicing and delivery addresses connected to customers and enter a one-time address manually on orders. You can also specify additional information such as building name, GPS coordinates or other references regarding a delivery address.</mark> 

<mark>5</mark> 1 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0052-01.png)


### 6.7 Sales orders – inventory control 

#### 6.7.1 Availability check 

<mark>You can set up automatic availability check at order row entry as required, for example:</mark> 

- <mark>simple availability check – only current inventory balance minus order rows – or</mark> 

- <mark>more complex availability check – current inventory balance minus planned receipts and dispatches</mark> 

- <mark>whether or not to include inventory allocations</mark> 

- <mark>whether or not to include purchases and manufacturing orders</mark> 

- <mark>only within the item’s lead time or up to the order row’s estimated dispatch time</mark> 

<mark>You can receive proposals for new dispatch dates. On part deliveries Jeeves ERP performs a new availability check and calculates the delivery date for the backorder.</mark> 

#### 6.7.2 Lead time matrix 

<mark>Jeeves ERP also offers more advanced functionality for describing different items' distribution via the company's warehouses and the lead times involved, in the form of a matrix. The advantage of the lead time matrix is that it can describe a distribution chain without the need for alternative supplier records and allows availability checks in multiple levels.</mark> 

<mark>You enter the various types of lead times, for example, lead time from supplier, transport time and administrative lead time, between each To-From company and connected inventory location. The information displayed by the availability check will be based on the balances at each warehouse and the lead times between them.</mark> 

<mark>When you enter an order row for an item recognized by the matrix, the matrix displays the available balance at each consecutive inventory location that is needed in order to fulfill the order quantity. It also shows the lead time and estimated delivery date for each step in the distribution. You are then asked if you wish to change the estimated delivery date according to this information.</mark> 

<mark>You can include net requirement planning for each step in the distribution chain, which is an efficient way of working with the lead time matrix, but it is not mandatory. External business units can also be part of the distribution setup, in which case you will need to read about the setup and use of Jeeves ICT.</mark> 

#### 6.7.3 Shortage check 

<mark>Jeeves ERP can check for shortages automatically each time you open a customer order and/or by running a report, with or without a printout, in a special order planning program. You determine which alternative you want to use with an application parameter. If you run the shortage check via report, order rows and the order header are updated with status codes which can then be used for searching.</mark> 

#### 6.7.4 Allocations routine 

<mark>Allocating customer orders ensures that the items are in stock, so that you do not generate picking lists unnecessarily. Jeeves ERP offers several ways of working with allocating items in inventory:</mark> 

- <mark>There is an allocation routine where you manually allocate orders by running a report (or pressing a button) according to rules for how your own company and your customers allow partial allocations and deliveries. You can also manually allocate individual order rows to set priorities or to make exceptions to the rules when necessary. There is also an option to delete and re-run allocations that have been made.</mark> 

- <mark>In addition, there is a direct allocating function that can be useful for certain types of commerce, for example, Web commerce, which means that if there are items in inventory when placing an order they are allocated directly. This function provides the only possibility for re-allocate if other items become available, for example, through cancelations or the like.</mark> 

> <mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

<mark>5</mark> 2 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0053-01.png)


<mark>You can also allocate items against purchase/manufacturing orders in the case of inventory shortages.</mark> 

#### 6.7.5 Requisition/purchase order/manufacturing order from order 

<mark>Jeeves ERP informs you if you need to order items to fulfill an order. This is the case for certain order types (for example, drop shipments and transit orders) and for items with certain acquisition methods (for example, purchase or manufacture to order).</mark> 

<mark>You create the purchase order/requisition/manufacturing order simply by pressing a function button on the order header.</mark> 

<mark>For orders that generate requisitions for material from net requirements, purchase or manufacturing requisitions, you can have an automatic pre-allocation when the requisition becomes an actual purchase or a manufacturing order.</mark> 

#### 6.7.6 Allocation and picking batches 

<mark>You can allocate and pick items per batch and control the order in which the batches are to be proposed for allocation:</mark> 

- <mark>Use/Best before date – against order or delivery date</mark> 

- <mark>Sell before date – against order or delivery date</mark> 

- <mark>Batch number order</mark> 

<mark>If allowed, you can allocate a customer order row against several batches.</mark> 

<mark>You can also set up any number of status controls, OK/not OK to allocate, to pick, include/exclude from available balance.</mark> 

#### 6.7.7 First in first out (FIFO) batches 

<mark>Jeeves ERP can be set up for automatic allocation and picking of the oldest batch.</mark> 

#### 6.7.8 Allocating and picking serial number items 

<mark>When allocating and picking you can enter an item’s serial number. The Use/Best before date for the individual serial number item can be controlled in the same manner as for batches. The sale and delivery of the item, as well as any other activity, are possible to trace. For example, if service or reparation is to be performed on a serial number item in the service module, the service planner automatically gets information about the customer to whom the item was sold, as well as connections to the sales order and inventory transaction.</mark> 

### 6.8 Sales orders – additional functions 

#### 6.8.1 Package sales 

<mark>You can set up fixed and configurable packages for sales order entry. You can run availability checks to see if a whole package can be delivered within the requested dispatch date. There are numerous ways of controlling how partial deliveries, price calculations and postings should be handled. You can also set up how package details should be presented on internal and external documents.</mark> 

##### 6.8.1.1 Price calculation types, package items 

<mark>You can define alternatives that can be used to control package price calculations. For example, the price can be calculated from either the package header down to details or totaled from details up to the package header.</mark> 

<mark>When you enter packages, this is retrieved first from the item and then from the system table for your own company.</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

<mark>5</mark> 3 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0054-01.png)


<mark>You can also specify if the list price on the package items shall be calculated by adding the cost price of the included package details.</mark> 

<mark>The following price calculation types are available for package items:</mark> 

- <mark>0, Price->details, post det (sales price): Price is calculated and set for the components based on current quantity and sales price for components and sales price for the package header. Posting of revenue and cost in connection with invoicing is performed based on the components’ respective product accounts.</mark> 

- <mark>1, Price->details, post pkg (sales price): Price is calculated in the same as for Price calc meth, pkg = 0, i.e. Price->details, post det (sales price). The difference is that posting of revenue and cost in connection with invoicing is done based on the package item’s product account.</mark> 

- <mark>2, Price->details, post det (cost price): Price is calculated and set for the components based on current quantity and cost price for components and sales price for the package header. Posting of revenue and cost in connection with invoicing is performed based on the components' respective product accounts.</mark> 

- <mark>3, Price->details, post pkg (cost price): Price calculation for details is the same as for Price calc meth, pkg = 2, i.e. Price->details, post det (cost price). The difference is that posting of revenue and cost in connection with invoicing is done based on the package item’s product account.</mark> 

- <mark>5, Price=details, post details: Sale price of the package item is calculated and set by totaling the sale prices of the included details. Posting of revenue and cost in connection with invoicing is performed based on the components’ respective product accounts.</mark> 

- <mark>6, Price=details, post package: Price calculation for package item is the same as for Price calc meth, pkg = 5, i.e. Price=details, post details. The difference is that posting of revenue and cost in connection with invoicing is done based on the package item’s product account.</mark> 

<mark>A number of application parameters are available for handling package items including an alternative price calculation method that can be activated for components.</mark> 

#### 6.8.2 Surcharges 

<mark>Automatic surcharges can be used on both sales and purchases. On the sales side this gives automatic entry and invoicing of deposits, environmental charges, etc. that by law must be reported and paid in connection with sales of certain types of products, for example, DVDs. On purchases, you can control and reconcile charges on supplier invoices for such products.</mark> 

#### 6.8.3 Options/accessories 

<mark>The function for options/accessories can be used during quotation and order entry to automatically propose available options/accessories for an item. For example, when selling a laser printer you could also offer an extra paper tray, color cartridges, etc. This can help generate additional sales. You can apply either fixed or automatic pricing based on the "main" item. You can have different options, a different validity period or a different price depending on the customer group.</mark> 

#### 6.8.4 Alternative/replacement items 

<mark>Alternative/replacement items is a function that allows you to specify a replacement item if the item in the customer order is not available in stock during order entry. A checkbox on the customer controls if you are allowed to sell alternative/replacement items to the customer.</mark> 

#### 6.8.5 Weight and volume 

<mark>If you specify of weight and volume on items, Jeeves ERP automatically calculates the total weight and volume on dispatch orders and invoices. There is also functionality for calculating the volume of stacked goods, where the volume is not always the same as the individual item's volume multiplied by the order row quantity. This is useful for furniture for example, and other goods that can be stacked.</mark> 

<mark>5</mark> 4 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0055-01.png)


#### 6.8.6 Units of measure 

<mark>You can define any number of units of measure, such as piece, dozen, meter, m³ etc. Each item is connected to a default sales unit, which can differ from stock and purchase units.</mark> 

#### 6.8.7 Alternative units 

<mark>Alternative units can be used for automatic or manual unit conversion in the Inventory, Sales and Purchasing modules. If the function is activated, items can be purchased, stocked and sold in different units. Costing is, however, based on your inventory stock unit.</mark> 

<mark>You can enter inventory transactions, order, requisition and purchase order rows, dispatches and received deliveries in your own inventory unit or in the alternative unit. You can set the function up to allow entry of quantities in both units, if the relationship between stock units and alternative units vary. This is useful in the food industry, for example, where a stock unit can vary in weight and you need to specify both the number of units and the weight. This setup also accumulates inventory balances in alternative units in Inventory balance records.</mark> 

<mark>If you set the function up for automatic conversion according to a fixed conversion rate, the unit you enter is automatically converted into the other unit based on the conversion rate. In other words, if you enter a quantity in an alternative unit, it is automatically converted to your inventory unit, and vice versa.</mark> 

<mark>Example: An item is stocked per piece. It can be purchased and sold per piece, but can also be purchased and sold in the alternative units of 6 pack, 12 pack or 24 pack. If you use 6 pack as alternative unit when entering an item on an order or purchase order and specify a quantity of 3, the quantity is automatically converted to 18 in your stock unit.</mark> 

<mark>If you set the function up for manual conversion, you enter quantities in both units and Jeeves ERP checks that the relationship between them is reasonable according to the given conversion factor and tolerance percentages.</mark> 

#### 6.8.8 Forward cover 

<mark>There is a simple function for managing forward cover of foreign currency, which implies invoicing of orders at the orders’ exchange rate.</mark> 

#### 6.8.9 External texts 

<mark>General texts can be used to print standard texts on documents. For example, if you want to add a New Year's greeting or the like to all external documents, you can enter the greeting as a general text.</mark> 

<mark>You can decide on which documents the text is printed, for example, on purchase orders, delivery notes, invoices, or all external documents. On the other hand, you cannot control that the text should only be printed on certain customer orders, delivery notes or invoices. If you specify that the text is to be printed on a document, it will be printed on all printouts of that document.</mark> 

<mark>Texts can be controlled by date using the Start date and End date fields. If you have specified several texts within the same date interval on the same document, the most recently entered text will be used.</mark> 

<mark>In the same way, you can create order-specific texts and/or customer-specific texts and control which documents these can be printed on.</mark> 

#### 6.8.10 Order linking 

<mark>With the order linking function you can automatically or manually link existing open orders or backorders for a customer to the order currently being entered for that customer. This can be used to gather backorders into a new order. It can also be used if a customer places many small orders each day and you wish to gather them to one order.</mark> 

<mark>5</mark> 5 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0056-01.png)


#### 6.8.11 Create credit order from invoice 

<mark>You can use this function to create a credit order linked to an original order and thus get exactly the same data, such as price data, on the credit note as on the original order. The function also allows you to create a return of the same items that were shipped against the original order. You can create single or multiple credit orders from collective invoice and specify the desired functionality.</mark> 

#### 6.8.12 Sales against advance payment 

<mark>You can handle advance payments from customers from different modules. In the sales module you can handle sales orders against advance payment in the following ways:</mark> 

- <mark>You can generate an advance payment from an order</mark> 

- <mark>You can link an order to one or more existing advance payment(s)</mark> 

- <mark>You can enter an advance invoice and payment and manually link the advance invoice to an order or invoice</mark> 

#### 6.8.13 Delivery confirmations 

<mark>You can enter delivery confirmations to customers. This implies that when a part delivery is confirmed the order row is automatically split, one row for each part delivery, and the order row is updated with a confirmed delivery date.</mark> 

#### 6.8.14 Order analyses and order entry statistics 

<mark>Order statistics and analyses can be produced at any time and for any period:</mark> 

- <mark>Order intake</mark> 

- <mark>Open order analyses</mark> 

- <mark>Order analyses, customer/item</mark> 

- <mark>Cross-analyses, customer/supplier</mark> 

#### 6.8.15 Order cancellation 

<mark>You can cancel orders using the button on the order header or by changing the status to Canceled. You can also cancel order rows using the button or Status field on the individual order rows.</mark> 

#### 6.8.16 Order row change log 

<mark>You can log certain changes on customer order rows in the Change log, order rows table. If activated, a record is created here when a change is made to a logged field directly on an order row, or indirectly from the order header. There are fields to show the current values and corresponding fields to show the previous values before the change.</mark> 

### 6.9 Jeeves Mobile order routine 

<mark>A standard mobile routine for handling sales orders is developed and maintained in Jeeves standard.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves Mobile order routine is used for the most basic needs of salespeople out in the field. It provides a simple and efficient way of entering and managing orders for customers, and to create and send order confirmations to them.</mark>
