Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0057-01.png)


### 6.10 Dispatches 

#### 6.10.1 Shipment planning 

<mark>There are two sets of programs for shipment planning; one set for use with Jeeves Dispatch planning and one set for use with Jeeves integration with Astro. You use the functionality to group and allocate dispatch orders to shipments, and to monitor that the whole shipment is picked, as opposed to the individual orders or dispatches to individual customers.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves shipment planning is based on:</mark> 

- <mark>fixed departure days and times</mark> 

- <mark>transportation resources and their capacity</mark> 

- <mark>lead times for processing customer orders</mark> 

<mark>The function also gives you the possibility to plan ahead to fill capacity.</mark> 

<mark>The setup is described in more detail under the section on Jeeves Sales-WMS integration.</mark> 

#### 6.10.2 Dispatch planning 

<mark>You can use Jeeves ERP's dispatch planning to coordinate a customer's orders to one or more dispatches, with collective invoicing or invoicing per order.</mark> 

#### 6.10.3 Transport automation 

<mark>Jeeves ERP can be set up to automatically propose a carrier as well as fixed dispatch dates depending on transport route, carrier and dispatch method.</mark> 

#### 6.10.4 Pick lists 

<mark>There is a choice of single or multiple pick list(s) per dispatch order, which gives you the possibility to have several pickers on large orders.</mark> 

#### 6.10.5 Picking 

<mark>You can also choose between different picking programs and functions depending on information needs, work method etc. For example, there is a function for automatic picking of all order rows that have not been picked on an order and its release for invoicing. There is also a function for scanning the barcode on a product package, automatically matching records in the left list with the scanned barcode, and moving these records to the right list. This function only works if Serial No., qty=1.0 field is set to N on the relevant inventory stock type.</mark> 

<mark>Once you scan the barcode and click OK, Jeeves ERP initiates the process of sending the information to the EMVS system for validation. However, if EMVS is not enabled for the inventory stock type, the item is moved for picking to the right list as normal. EMVS is described further on, under Industry-specifics.</mark> 

#### 6.10.6 Joint picking 

<mark>With Jeeves ERP's function for joint picking you can coordinate picking per item, inventory location, dispatch date and picker/packer. The objective is to increase efficiency for packers so they do not need to retrace their steps if an item can be picked for several orders at the same time.</mark> 

<mark>5</mark> 7 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0058-01.png)


#### 6.10.7 Split item packages and miscellaneous inventory 

<mark>You can set up a markup percentage or amount on split item packages. You can also store split item packages in a separate bin for miscellaneous inventory and automatically allocate inventory from this bin if the order row quantity is less than the item's package size.</mark> 

#### 6.10.8 Blocked balance/quantity 

<mark>You can block a whole inventory balance, or a certain quantity within the balance, from being used or dispatched directly during goods receipt or at a later stage. There is a possibility to open up for users to pick from blocked balance, if necessary.</mark> 

#### 6.10.9 Part deliveries and backorders 

<mark>Via parameters you can control if part delivery is allowed and if backorders should be created and shown on external documents.</mark> 

#### 6.10.10 Labels 

<mark>You can print labels with item numbers in the desired format, such as bar code format.</mark> 

#### 6.10.11 Automatic freight calculation 

<mark>You can set the order routine up to automatically calculate freight on customer orders. You can have any number of matrices to calculate freight by weight, volume or number of pallets, as well as by distance or area (County, District, Region or ZIP/Post code). Which matrix is used is controlled by dispatch method per inventory location. Jeeves ERP can handle fixed and variable costs and bulk goods.</mark> 

#### 6.10.12 Freight documents 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes a number of standard freight bills and address labels (SIS, Posten, STE etc.) for the Nordic countries. You can set up freight document and freight bill texts, as well as automatic sequence numbering, for example, as per dispatch method and freight document type. There is also integration with third party software for continuous updates of the standard freight documents in Nordic countries.</mark> 

#### 6.10.13 Packaging/packing routine 

<mark>Jeeves ERP can propose the type of packaging and the number of packages required for the order row or you can manually pack orders. One packing slip is printed for each package.</mark> 

<mark>You search for the order to be packed using one of the programs. If a packaging type has already been specified for the items, a packing record should have already been created and you can click on the Auto packing button to pack the order. The Create package function only works if there is a link between the packaging type and the item or items that you wish to pack. The function can be used to recreate a packaging proposal, for example, if you have accidentally deleted it.</mark> 

<mark>If there is no link between the item and a packaging type (or if you want to pack the shipment differently), you must enter package contents and pack manually.</mark> 

#### 6.10.14 Transport administration 

<mark>Jeeves ERP:s generic Transport administration (TA) API enables integration with multiple TA service providers' systems, for handling standardized transport documentation from Jeeves ERP:s dispatch programs.</mark> 

<mark>5</mark> 8 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0059-01.png)


<mark>This includes provisions for:</mark> 

- <mark>defining system data or loading the data from the external TA system, such as general/provider/user-specific settings to control which information is to be sent to TA, if and how delivery notifications are to be sent, if addresses are to be validated, etc.</mark> 

- <mark>defining or loading base data, such as the service providers, carriers, transport products, package types and add-on services used</mark> 

- <mark>setting additional, TA-specific data on customers, inventory locations, dispatch methods and dispatch terms</mark> 

- <mark>validating addresses via the external TA system</mark> 

- <mark>setting up notification methods</mark> 

- <mark>setting up items for dangerous goods</mark> 

- <mark>specifying shipment data and parameters on orders, dispatch orders and delivery notes at dispatch</mark> 

- <mark>viewing the shipments that are created and requesting price quotes for them</mark> 

- <mark>printing, releasing, tracking, recreating and deleting shipments</mark> 

- <mark>viewing the message log between Jeeves ERP and the external TA system</mark> 

<mark>The connector to the external TA system is set up by Jeeves Professional Services using Jeeves API platform.</mark> 

#### 6.10.15 Shipment planning for WMS (to be discontinued) 

<mark>If you use Jeeves ERP’s integration with Astro WMS, you use a dedicated function for shipment planning. See the section on the Sales-WMS integration.</mark> 

### 6.11 Sales invoices 

#### 6.11.1 Invoicing 

<mark>Invoicing can be done from a number of order or picking programs or from a separate invoicing program (depending on the setup of your company’s roles), and can be done at any time. It is possible to postpone invoicing of individual order rows if necessary.</mark> 

<mark>In the Sales module, you can invoice reported service orders from the Service module.</mark> 

#### 6.11.2 eInvoicing 

<mark>Jeeves ERP supports electronic invoicing through third-party services for distribution, sender and receiver notification, storage, and online tracking.</mark> 

<mark>Invoice information can also be made available through standardized APIs for transfer to external EDI and eInvoicing service providers, supporting automated electronic exchange.</mark> 

#### 6.11.3 Invoice number series 

<mark>You can set up different number series for different order types and divide them into sub-number series linked to users/groups or salespersons.</mark> 

#### 6.11.4 Invoicing freight, insurance, handling charge etc. 

<mark>When closing orders, besides entering the number of packages, weight, volume, etc., handling charges and other costs that the customer is to be invoiced can be specified. The charges can be entered manually or created automatically.</mark> 

<mark>5</mark> 9 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0060-01.png)


#### 6.11.5 VAT 

<mark>In Jeeves Sales you set up customers, customers' points of delivery and items for VAT. Your company can apply any number of VAT rates. There is an option to create VAT specifications if there are items with different VAT rates on an invoice. You can set up any deviations from and additions to default VAT rules, for example:</mark> 

- <mark>if the customer controls the VAT code (If any order row/item has a deviating VAT code it is ignored)</mark> 

- <mark>if tax amount and VAT basis amount shall be calculated including or excluding VAT</mark> 

- <mark>if the VAT code from sales rows is to be stored on voucher rows</mark> 

- <mark>if VAT accounting date, VAT basis amount, etc. are to be stored on voucher rows</mark> 

- <mark>if the VAT matrix is to be used to retrieve the VAT code</mark> 

<mark>If you use the VAT matrix the VAT code on order rows (and the equivalent business transactions) considers the following:</mark> 

- <mark>Ship to (country code and trading area on the company and order)</mark> 

- <mark>Ship from (country code and trading area from the delivering inventory location or from the supplier in case of Triangulation)</mark> 

- <mark>valid VAT number on the customer (if not a warning is displayed)</mark> 

- <mark>VAT code on item if different from default domestic VAT code</mark> 

- <mark>for 3rd party trade, Order type (a drop shipment indicates triangulation)</mark> 

- <mark>customer's VAT code, if the VAT code from customer field is checked</mark> 

<mark>See also separate sections describing Sales tax for the US and Canada and VAT/tax declarations.</mark> 

#### 6.11.6 Collective invoicing 

<mark>Jeeves ERP offers collective invoicing of several released orders per payer, per delivery note, or delivery customer. You can create normal invoices and collective invoices in the same program or you can create collective invoices in a separate program.</mark> 

#### 6.11.7 Automatic matching of credit notes against debit invoices 

<mark>If you create a credit note from an invoiced order, you are asked if you want to create a credit note to be automatically matched against the relevant invoice. Otherwise you can enter another invoice number to match against or leave the field blank (if no automatic matching is to take place). If you create a credit note from an order that has not yet been invoiced, you also get the possibility to enter an invoice number to match against, if desired.</mark> 

<mark>The credit note cannot be matched against more than one debit invoice. Jeeves ERP also checks that the company ID is correct. Both invoices are cross-referenced. You then release and invoice the credit note.</mark> 

<mark>The credit note is posted in the normal manner, during printout of the invoice journal, using templates connected to the dedicated payment method called CTD, Credit to debit. One payment journal is automatically created per invoice journal. If the debit invoice is already paid the credit note remains open. If the credit note only covers part of the invoice amount, the remaining amount remains open. If it covers more than the invoice amount, the debit invoice is fully paid and the remaining amount on the credit note remains open.</mark> 

#### 6.11.8 Currency clause 

<mark>Currency clauses can be applied as protection against large changes in exchange rates, both increased and decreased rates. The currency clause makes it possible to automatically adjust the sale price during invoicing if there is a large difference in the exchange rate between the time of ordering and the time of invoicing. On the currency clause, you specify how big the difference must be for a price adjustment to be made.</mark> 

<mark>6</mark> 0 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0061-01.png)


<mark>There are a number of cases when a currency clause can come into effect:</mark> 

<mark>1) If the item is purchased in foreign currency and the exchange rate for the foreign currency changes during the period between the time of ordering and invoicing.</mark> 

<mark>2) If the item is purchased in base currency and you invoice the customer in foreign currency and the exchange rate for the currency you invoice in changes between the time of ordering and invoicing.</mark> 

<mark>3) If the item is purchased in a foreign currency and the customer is invoiced in another foreign currency.</mark> 

#### 6.11.9 Internal sales 

<mark>This function makes it possible to account for revenues, costs and margins regarding internal sales, both for the department that purchases the product from supplier to inventory, and the department that purchases the product internally and sells to the customer. In "normal sales" you only account for the department that sells the product to the customer. Using this function you also account for the department that purchases the product from supplier to inventory in connection with sales.</mark> 

<mark>There are three ways of activating internal sales:</mark> 

- <mark>Through the combination of internal sales type, retrieved via the customer category, and item.</mark> 

- <mark>If the cost centers on the salesperson and the stock location are not the same.</mark> 

- <mark>If the cost centers on the salesperson and the product account are not the same.</mark> 

<mark>The activation of internal sales gives two results; 1) cost, sales price and margin for internal sales are calculated and presented on quotations and orders and 2) additional postings and eliminations are created on the customer invoice journal.</mark> 

<mark>Via a parameter you can select between the internal sales price and the cost price to be used while creating postings from the invoicing and journal voucher report.</mark> 

#### 6.11.10 Invoice history 

<mark>In many countries it is legally required that you store historical data regarding invoices so that an invoice duplicate can be reproduced if necessary.</mark> 

<mark>You can print duplicate invoices with historical data, i.e. even if company information for the customer or payer has been changed since the original invoice was printed, the change does not affect the reprint. There is also functionality to ensure that the same template is used as when printing the original invoice.</mark> 

### 6.12 Posting and integration with ledgers 

<mark>Invoices are normally automatically entered in the accounts receivable ledger upon creation.</mark> 

<mark>Customer invoices are normally posted automatically in a separate routine/process after one or more batch of invoices have been produced. Invoicing can be done several times a day and posting at the end of the day or first thing the next day. One invoice journal is created and posted per invoice date. Each journal generates a voucher in the general ledger.</mark> 

<mark>Posting is done based on posting rules for invoice totals and details that control all accounts and account segments (cost centers, cost units etc.) to which receivables, revenues, costs, VAT/tax etc. are posted.</mark> 

<mark>You can post delivered orders/dispatch orders that have not yet been invoiced and posted at any interval, for example, during period or more regular reconciliations. If an order is picked in one period but invoiced and an invoice journal created in another, the picking journal must be created before the invoicing journal. One picking journal is created and posted per dispatch date. Each journal generates a voucher in the general ledger. Posting is performed according to posting rules</mark> 

<mark>6</mark> 1 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0062-01.png)


<mark>for inventory transactions that control to which account and account segments (cost centers, cost units, etc.) the uninvoiced delivery is posted.</mark> 

#### 6.12.1 Exceptions to posting rules 

<mark>You can set up which cost centers, cost units, etc. can manually be entered on order rows as exceptions to the posting rules that have been defined.</mark> 

#### 6.12.2 Reconciliations 

<mark>There is a program for reconciliation of dispatched but not yet invoiced orders, which can be used in connection with period reconciliations.</mark> 

<mark>There are also functions for reconciliation and analysis of differences in quantities and/or amounts, as well as for analysis of postings, on customer orders with associated purchase orders, for example, Drop shipments and Transit orders.</mark> 

### 6.13 Sales statistics and budget 

<mark>Thanks to Jeeves ERP’s superior search possibilities, and to the fact that you can customize forms and lists by adding fields, columns, totals etc., you can compile information on many dimensions in more or less any table. You can also build your own reports and business graphics based on own search criteria and layouts. Besides this, there are a number of standard forms for viewing, and reports for printing sales statistics.</mark> 

#### 6.13.1 Invoicing statistics 

<mark>Statistics can be produced for any period, by customer (delivery customer or payer), customer category, item, item group, item class, salesperson and project.</mark> 

#### 6.13.2 Sales budget 

<mark>You can enter sales budget for comparisons against actual sales. There are programs for entering total budget, budget per customer, per item group and/or per item class. There is also a functionality for allocating sales budget per sales manager.</mark> 

<mark>You can either enter each period budget manually or distribute the annual budget automatically over the periods by specifying an allocation key and pressing the Allocate budget button.</mark> 

#### 6.13.3 Intrastat statistics and European sales lists 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes functions for producing the required declarations of dispatches and arrivals between the company’s own country and other countries within the EU, including drill down to the invoices included in the dispatches and arrivals totals. This is to make it possible to reconcile the data in the report.</mark> 

<mark>If you choose to use the functions, you can either use the information generated as a basis to manually fill out the Intrastat form or you can compile the Intrastat report in an external report generator.</mark> 

<mark>See the chapter on Jeeves</mark> _<mark>General ledger</mark>_ <mark>for further information. You can also export the data to MS Excel using Jeeves' Dynamic reports. You can then use Intrastat Data Entry Package (IDEP), which is free software that is available on the web, to automatically import your Intrastat reports into the tax authority’s web site.</mark> 

<mark>During Intrastat reporting, you can choose to group invoices only on TARIC code or on TARIC code and delivery term. Note that the Intrastat report in Sweden is grouped on TARIC code only, whereas in Poland, Belgium and a few other countries, it is grouped both on TARIC code and delivery term.</mark>

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0063-01.png)


### 6.14 Claims 

#### 6.14.1 Customer claims 

<mark>You can enter and log customer claims, i.e. remarks and complaints about items, orders and invoices, and record how they are to be settled as well as actions to be taken.</mark> 

<mark>You can define different work flows for different types of claims, specify base data for scheduling, and control how activities should be reported.</mark> 

### 6.15 Sales-WMS integration (to be discontinued) 

<mark>If you use Jeeves ERP’s integration with Astro WMS, you use a dedicated function for shipment planning as described under the section about dispatches. Here we give an overview of the integration.</mark> 

#### 6.15.1 Activation of integration 

<mark>The integration can be activated per order type, inventory location and/or item.</mark> 

#### 6.15.2 Fixed departures 

<mark>You set up fixed departures with lead times for when orders need to be entered, picked, packed and loaded in order to be included in a departure. These criteria can be set up per dispatch method or dispatch method and inventory location.</mark> 

#### 6.15.3 Transport resources 

<mark>You also set up the company’s transport resources, i.e. trucks, containers etc., with capacity information. The shipment planning function gives you the possibility to plan ahead to fill capacity.</mark> 

#### 6.15.4 Shipment plans 

<mark>There is a special function for dispatches if Astro WMS is used, for:</mark> 

- <mark>creation of shipment plans for fixed departures</mark> 

- <mark>allocating orders on shipment plans</mark> 

- <mark>information about transport resource capacity and usage, with the possibility to plan ahead to fill capacity</mark> 

- <mark>release of shipment plans for picking in WMS</mark> 

#### 6.15.5 Information from WMS 

<mark>Picking status is automatically updated from WMS back to the Sales module when the goods have been picked and dispatched. Balances are automatically updated on received deliveries and on returns from credited customer orders.</mark> 

### 6.16 Digital business documents 

<mark>Jeeves ERP supports automated, API-based exchange of digital business documents with customers and other trading partners. Standardized APIs can receive customer orders, order changes, and delivery schedules, provide order responses, and make invoice information available to external systems.</mark> 

<mark>Automated exchange reduces manual processing and the risk of data-entry errors. Incoming orders use prioritized delivery-address selection, while administration functions support resetting and reprocessing failed transactions.</mark>

### 6.17 e-Applications 

#### 6.17.1 Jeeves e-Sales 

<mark>There is an optional e-application for B2B trade via the web. The solution is built on Liferay open source portal and interacts with Jeeves ERP through Jeeves Service Gateway and Jeeves Service builder (JeevesAPI). e-Sales is based on Jeeves standard business logic and is a fully functional webshop application. For more information please refer to the chapter on</mark> _<mark>Jeeves e-Applications</mark>_ <mark>.</mark> 

<mark>The application can be summarized as follows:</mark> 

- <mark>Setup of the web interface, which is comprised of so-called classic or web-based product tree, shopping cart, currencies etc.</mark> 

- <mark>Order entry with search functions and selection from product tree</mark> 

- <mark>View deliveries, accounts receivable and order history</mark> 

- <mark>Entry/update of company data</mark> 

<mark>Please note that Jeeves e-Sales is to be discontinued and replaced by another offering. The chapters in Jeeves documentation remain for the information of any companies still using its functionality, but will be removed in a future version.</mark> 

#### 6.17.2 Integration with PEPPOL 

<mark>Jeeves ERP supports the exchange of electronic business documents according to Pan-European Public Procurement On-Line (PEPPOL). PEPPOL is an infrastructure for e-commerce and e- procurement within the EU.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves solution is a cloud-based integration solution to support companies that have to provide information in the PEPPOL 3.0, SEPA, and other electronically supported formats as per legal requirements.</mark> 

<mark>The eInvoicing functionality harmonizes with Jeeves ERP standard, so that if a payer ID exists on the order, the payer’s customer details such as VAT number, address and the like are displayed on the invoice. If the payer ID does not exist, the details are fetched from the customer field and displayed on the invoice.</mark> 

<mark>While additional setup is required to enable invoicing in the PEPPOL format using the eInvoice program, Jeeves ERP standard logic regarding order management and invoicing is applied in all other aspects.</mark> 

<mark>You can include detailed package information along with package items on the PEPPOL invoices.</mark> 

<mark>When an invoice or advance invoice is generated, the information is posted to a queue and then picked up by JSB for further processing. The data is validated and converted to PEPPOL format, and sent to a third party (like Pagero eInvoice  service) or via HTTP (Hypertext transfer protocol)/ FTP (File transfer protocol)/ SFTP (Secure file transfer protocol).</mark> 

<mark>The generated XML contains the invoice as an embedded PDF document in binary format, provided the Include PDF check box is selected and the PDM configuration is enabled.</mark> 

<mark>To send a credit invoice in the PEPPOL format, the order must be released for invoicing, and the status must be changed to Invoiced. This updates the queue with the data which is then sent for further processing. The data is then validated, converted to the PEPPOL format, and sent to Pagero.</mark> 

<mark>Once the order is invoiced and the invoice number is generated, the status can be checked in the Attribute queue reference program.</mark> 

<mark>You can also import a PEPPOL XML invoice with a PDF file embedded in it. Once the file is imported, it is available in the Review imported supplier invoices program against the corresponding supplier invoice number. Additional details such as the PDF file name and its location are also displayed.</mark>

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0065-01.png)


<mark>The information about the invoice agreement is available on the invoice header. If the EInvoice check box is selected, the report will be eInvoice d and transferred to the Rules for printout program when the eInvoice  agreement is activated.</mark> 

<mark>If the Include PDF checkbox is selected, you can provide information about whether or not to include a PDF for the invoices with the eInvoicing agreement.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves e-Invoicing also includes support for Business response message handling. PUF invoice responses can be exchanged between buyer/receiver and supplier/issuer to inform the issuer of an invoice about business decisions. The delivered invoice may be technically correct and delivered to the recipient/buyer but there might be the need for a buyer to inform the issuer if the invoice is accepted or if there is a reason for a rejection.</mark> 

#### 6.17.3 Integration documents 

<mark>The functionality to handle integration documents allows you to track progress, send and receive documents, validate documents, etc. with ease. You can browse the contents of the integration document and save it as a file in a chosen folder.</mark> 

### 6.18 Industry-specifics 

<mark>Most functions in Jeeves Sales are common to all industries. There are some, however, that are specific to certain industries, as described below.</mark> 

#### 6.18.1 Product variant matrix 

<mark>The product variant matrix mentioned earlier was developed with the fashion industry in mind, but can be used for any products with variants, i.e. combinations of models, colors, sizes etc., or the like.</mark> 

#### 6.18.2 Sales configurator (powered by Tacton) 

<mark>Jeeves ERP’s Sales Configurator supports salespersons in the creation of order unique bills of material, and if required routing, from customer specific demands, and from these create customer orders in Jeeves to generate customer specific manufacturing or purchase orders. The configurator not only helps the user avoid invalid or unsuitable configuration alternatives, it can also help find the optimal configuration.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP's configurator is constraint-based, which means you specify general attributes and rules for groups of items/parts, rather than for individual items. This keeps maintenance of configuration rules down to minimum. The rules are set up in Tacton studio.</mark> 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP, the integration is comprised of</mark> 

- <mark>Basic data for verifying and mapping models created in Tacton studio. Models are saved in Jeeves EDM for version management purposes.</mark> 

- <mark>A batch handler and batch queue that enables parallel processing of the result from Tacton in order to optimize performance.</mark> 

- <mark>A special order program. The configurator window is accessed from the order row and the result can either be displayed as a package with several BOMs and operations, or the BOMs and the operations just presented in an embedded window on the order row. The items are treated as any other items in Jeeves ERP.</mark> 

- <mark>Functionality for copying existing order configurations to new ones in Jeeves and Tacton, to create new order rows and/or new standard items.</mark> 

- <mark>A log for viewing imports, statuses and any error messages, also buttons for updating and deleting. The log is also accessed via a node in the order program.</mark> 

- <mark>Auxiliary properties, which make it possible to customize the Jeeves interface, for example, insert graphics.</mark>

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0066-01.png)


#### 6.18.3 Retail sales/Electronic point of sales (EPOS) 

<mark>There is a special program for entering EPOS orders. Elements that are specific to EPOS are</mark> 

- <mark>that search items and order entry are performed in a single screen where all the necessary forms and lists are available,</mark> 

- <mark>that much of the functionality is linked to shortcut keys so that most things can be done from the keyboard,</mark> 

- <mark>that the salesperson's authorization is checked via an acknowledgement code and</mark> 

- <mark>that for closed orders picking, release and receipt printout are performed automatically. At the same time a command is sent to open the POS.</mark> 

#### 6.18.4 Retail/pallet discounts 

<mark>There is a special solution for retail/pallet discounts that allows any number of discount types. The solution offers two alternatives:</mark> 

- <mark>a separate discount matrix based on the standard item and customer discount classes</mark> 

- <mark>pallet discounts per customer and item</mark> 

<mark>With both alternatives you can specify discounts in percent and/or as amounts in currency.</mark> 

#### 6.18.5 Trading 

<mark>There is a special order program for trading, with automatic creation of purchase orders for drop shipments. The cost price and sales price are automatically calculated based on a markup on the purchase price.</mark> 

#### 6.18.6 Order shuffler 

<mark>The order shuffler gives the possibility to create amendment orders from ordinary orders and to credit invoices and re-invoice them, with retained traceability throughout the whole process from the order to the general ledger. You can even shuffle order rows to several different customers, for example, to different business units within your company, simply by entering a different ID for order shuffle for each customer on the order rows.</mark> 

#### 6.18.7 Estimator 

<mark>The Estimator supports the estimate to quote process. Estimates can be prepared for products, services (services from both projects and service orders) as well as manufacturing operations and material. Users can create and compare different estimates and see a summarized view of different calculations, follow the process and analyze won-not won quotations and/or estimates.</mark> 

<mark>The Estimator program can be used for any of the following:</mark> 

- <mark>preparing price estimates for standard products</mark> 

- <mark>modifying standard products, package items and product levels</mark> 

- <mark>configuring products comprised of standard modules, package items and product levels</mark> 

- <mark>creating new order specific products, package items and product levels including costing</mark> 

- <mark>configuring services/maintenance, both during and after implementation of the products</mark> 

- <mark>configuring project services, for example, for construction/design</mark> 

<mark>The header includes a large number of fields for total costs and revenues summed up from the underlying details. With the Estimator you can produce several estimates/versions from which you, or your customer, can choose the most suitable solution. You can also transform order specific products into standard products and replace them on existing orders.</mark>

### 6.19 Localizations/internationalization 

<mark>Most functions in Jeeves Sales are common to all countries. There are some, however, that are specific to certain countries or trading areas, as described below.</mark> 

#### 6.19.1 Transport administration integration (Nordic countries only) 

<mark>There is an optional integration with a third party product for transport administration (LogTrade or KSD Edicom), i.e. for handling standardized transport documentation from Jeeves' dispatch programs. This complements the standard freight documents provided in Jeeves ERP.</mark> 

#### 6.19.2 VAT refunding 

###### _Originally developed for the UK, but available to all users_ 

<mark>In some countries, such as the UK, the VAT/tax value is based on the discounted amount even if the customer does not take advantage of the early settlement discount. In order to be able to reconcile the VAT at the end of the period, Jeeves ERP creates additional, preliminary postings on the customer invoice journal, which are subsequently, automatically reversed on the payment journal. You can adjust your invoice templates to show the required information, for example, the discount terms and the VAT amount based on the discounted amount.</mark> 

#### 6.19.3 Amendment invoicing 

###### _Originally developed for Poland, but available to all users_ 

<mark>Amendment invoicing is a function that lets you create a so-called amendment order from an existing (normal) invoice in the database, containing corrections of quantity, price, discounts, VAT etc. against one or more invoice rows on the original invoice. The difference with amendment orders is that Jeeves ERP automatically creates a credit record with the original invoice data that corresponds to each corrected row. These credit rows cannot be changed by the user. This is handled completely automatically from the amendment order, via picking and release to invoicing.</mark> 

<mark>Amendment orders are invoiced via a special invoicing program, which produces an invoice printout that contains both rows with invoice data from the original invoice and the new, corrected invoice rows from the amendment order. The invoice produced contains subtotals from both the original and the new, corrective invoice data, as well as totals for the whole invoice, which show the difference between the original and the corrective invoice data.</mark> 

<mark>During packaging and delivery of goods to customers, the dispatch can potentially contain several batches and/or serial numbers. Sometimes the dispatched goods may be returned to the supplier, either entirely or just encompassing specific sets of batches or serial numbers, or part of the quantity originally dispatched. You can specify which transactions can be picked to be returned if the original order included several transactions with different serial numbers and/or batch numbers in the Temp table, changes on invoice embedded list (right list) in the Create credit order from invoice program.</mark> 

<mark>For countries where it is required that the currency rate on the amendment invoice is the same as on the original invoice, Jeeves ERP includes functionality to fulfill this requirement.</mark> 

#### 6.19.4 Advance for full amount 

###### _Originally developed for Poland, but available to all users_ 

<mark>In certain countries, a special type of invoice document is required by law if an advance invoice is for the full amount. By this we mean that the advance payment implies full deduction of the final invoice amount as well as the VAT amount.</mark>

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0068-01.png)


<mark>The invoice type is handled in the same way as normal invoices apart from:</mark> 

- <mark>it uses a unique number series only applicable to invoice type 100, i.e. invoices from customer orders,</mark> 

- <mark>it displays a special header on the printout and</mark> 

- <mark>it not being transferred to the VAT register</mark> 

#### 6.19.5 Tax accounting on advance invoices 

###### _Originally developed for France, but available to all users_ 

<mark>The tax authorities in some countries require tax accounting on advance invoices even before payment. Jeeves ERP includes functionality to fulfill this requirement.</mark> 

#### 6.19.6 Reminder charge and interest at invoicing 

###### _Originally developed for Norway, but available to all users_ 

<mark>You can invoice reminder charges and interest in connection with normal invoicing of orders in the Sales module, instead of in a separate routine in the Accounts receivable. This is a common way of working in certain countries.</mark> 

#### 6.19.7 Sales taxes (USA and Canada) 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes programs for setup and collection of sales taxes according to legal requirements in the USA and Canada, comprised of the following:</mark> 

- <mark>Tax jurisdictions for all tax areas in up to five levels (State/PST, County/GST, City/HST etc.)</mark> 

- <mark>Tax codes (rates) connected to customers/points of delivery and items</mark> 

#### 6.19.8 Excise Tax (USA and Canada) 

<mark>Jeeves ERP also includes functionality to handle excise tax where applicable, for example on sales of certain medical devices. When you enter an order, the excise tax is calculated according to the specified percentage of either the net amount or the amount including sales tax. The resulting excise tax is displayed in the same embedded list as the sales tax. When you invoice the order, the same information is displayed in a corresponding list in the invoice form.</mark> 

#### 6.19.9 Invoice numbering 

_Originally developed for Hungary, but available to all users_ 

<mark>In certain countries, original invoices (i.e., when the invoice is printed the first time) and invoice reprints (when making a reprint of the original invoice) must be numbered in a specific way. Jeeves ERP includes functionality to fulfill this requirement.</mark> 

#### 6.19.10 European Medicines Verification System (EMVS) 

<mark>Following the introduction of the European Medicines Verification System (EMVS) for the implementation of a functioning, secure, interoperable and cost effective system across Europe that ensures only prescription medicines which bear safety features may be put into circulation, it makes it mandatory for distributors of medical packages and pharmacies to ensure that each medical product package is qualified for consumption before sending it forward in the supply chain.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP provides functionality that allows companies in the life science industry, including distributors, wholesales companies and pharmacies, to comply with the regulations set down to ensure the validity of each medical product package that is dispensed through each stage of the supply chain till it reaches the consumer. The product packages are validated against the data made available to the EMVS by the manufacturer or the parallel distributor to ensure that they can be dispensed to consumers for usage.</mark> 

<mark>6</mark> 8 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Sales 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0069-01.png)


<mark>Jeeves ERP functionality for validation of dispensed medical product packaging covers the following areas:</mark> 

- <mark>Verification of single or multiple packages of a product</mark> 

- <mark>Dispense package management</mark> 

- <mark>Decommission package management</mark> 

- <mark>Reactivation of packages that were previously decommissioned or dispensed</mark> 

- <mark>Product verification and dispense during order picking</mark> 

<mark>You can specify whether medical product verification should be enabled for the inventory stock type and select valid actions for each EMVS state to reduce external requests and attain better performance while triggering a state change for a product. You can also specify if a product package that is assigned to a particular state is allowed to be forwarded to the next stage of the supply chain or if it is valid for decommissioning. Further, you can also define if the EMVS state is applicable for pharmacies or wholesalers and if the EMVS functionality should be activated for a customer.</mark> 

##### 6.19.10.1 Initiate EMVS connections by selecting request type and method for validation 

<mark>All requests from Jeeves ERP to the EMVS system are initiated from the Serial number items program.</mark> 

<mark>A request type can be one of the following:</mark> 

<mark>1 - Authenticate: Send an authentication request to the EMVS system. The EMVS system responds by providing the bearer token which can be used to access any service in the EMVS verification process. The token has a lifespan of 24 hours.</mark> 

<mark>2 - Verify: Send a request for verification of a package.</mark> 

- <mark>3 - Supply: Send a request to confirm if a package can be supplied further into the supply chain.</mark> 

<mark>4 - Decommission: Send a request for removing a package from the supply chain.</mark> 

<mark>5 - Reactivate: Send a request for reactivating a previously decommissioned package. If you select an option other than authenticate or decommission, you can proceed by selecting an input method by which the product package information is captured and sent to the EMVS system for validation. If you select Decommission, you can proceed by selecting a Decommission state describing the reason for requesting the removal of a package. The EMVO has defined different decommission states for pharmacists and wholesalers and these may be subject to change, so we recommend you refer to the EMVS website for the latest information.</mark> 

##### 6.19.10.2 Verify package information during order picking/dispatch order picking 

<mark>During order picking/dispatch order picking, product package information is captured and sent to the EMVS system for verification if the EMVS functionality is enabled for the inventory stock type/customer. The EMVS system responds by sending a bearer token which can be used to access any service in the EMVS verification process. The token has a lifespan of 24 hours. Once you have received the token, you can proceed with the picking as usual.</mark> 

<mark>In case of returns, reactivation must be done manually in the Serial number items program.</mark> 

#### 6.19.11 OVT number for Finland 

<mark>The Finnish Party Identification number (OVT-tunnus) is a unique 12-17 digit number linked to a business ID, which is usually used in Finland as an e-ID. You can enter the OVT number in the System data, own company and Companies tables.</mark>

## 7 Jeeves ERP Inventory 

<mark>Jeeves ERP’s inventory module can help business enterprises, organizations and authorities improve their inventory performance, irrespective of industry or field.</mark> 

<mark>With Jeeves Inventory you manage inventory processes – such as costing, inventory control, managing balances, counting and inventory planning including net requirement calculations – within the same application.</mark> 

<mark>The main inventory process and common sub processes are packaged with pre-defined roles, rolespecific menus and forms as well as user guides and training material. Besides these processes, you can activate other, wider functionality for specific company needs.</mark> 

### 7.1 Products and services 

<mark>Jeeves ERP has a common Items table for information regardless of the type of products and items – finished products, semi-manufactured, components, raw materials, packaging, consumables, stocked or non-stocked.</mark> 

#### 7.1.1 Product/item classification 

<mark>There are many possibilities for categorizing and classifying items and products, for example:</mark> 

- <mark>product classes – can control pricing and be used for statistics</mark> 

- <mark>product accounts – control inventory management and valuation, and can control inventory postings</mark> 

- <mark>item groups – can control pricing and be used for statistics and budget allocation</mark> 

- <mark>item classes – can be used for purchase planning, statistics and budget allocation</mark> 

- <mark>item categories – can be used for statistics, for example for collections of item groups</mark> 

- <mark>item types– can be used for statistics</mark> 

#### 7.1.2 Product variants 

<mark>There is functionality for handling product variants, useful for companies in the fashion industry for example, for defining applicable combinations of say, models, colors, sizes etc. of a product with the help of a matrix. The matrix has various layers that you can switch between for viewing current and planned inventory balances, item numbers, alternative descriptions, delivery dates, prices etc. for each combination. The matrix is used in the whole process flow, from automatic creation of variants and pre-packed assortments to physical inventory counts.</mark> 

#### 7.1.3 Seasonal products 

<mark>You can set up seasonal items, orders and purchase orders, which can be used together with, or without, the product variant matrix. If used together with the matrix, fields for items that are out of season are inactivated.</mark> 

<mark>If you specify a season on a sales/purchase order header, you can enter order rows for both seasonal and non-seasonal items. When entering order rows for seasonal items the following checks are made:</mark> 

- <mark>That the season complies with the season on the sales/purchase order header</mark> 

- <mark>That the sales/purchase order type is allowed for this season</mark> 

- <mark>That the delivery date complies with this season</mark> 

### 7.2 Costing 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP, there are three ways of handling cost prices on purchased items:</mark> 

<mark>1) Standard cost price, which you calculate using buttons or reports designed for this purpose</mark> 

<mark>7</mark> 1 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Inventory 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0072-01.png)


<mark>2) Average weighted price, which is calculated automatically, i.e. the cost price is recalculated automatically when receiving deliveries or when approving supplier invoices</mark> 

<mark>3) FIFO, also calculated automatically when receiving deliveries</mark> 

<mark>You control this from a checkbox on the product account.</mark> 

<mark>The following markups can be made on purchase prices:</mark> 

- <mark>Freight cost</mark> 

- <mark>Delivery cost</mark> 

- <mark>Various markups in both percents and/or amounts</mark> 

- <mark>Material overhead in percent</mark> 

- <mark>Setup purchase price</mark> 

- <mark>Start cost per unit</mark> 

- <mark>Customs duties</mark> 

<mark>The markups are totaled and can be retrieved from the:</mark> 

- <mark>item</mark> 

- <mark>item per inventory location*</mark> 

- <mark>TARIC code for the item</mark> 

- <mark>markup template for the item</mark> 

- <mark>item data per supplier</mark> 

- <mark>item data per supplier and inventory location*</mark> 

- <mark>supplier</mark> 

- <mark>product class</mark> 

- <mark>product account</mark> 

- <mark>delivery method and/or</mark> 

- <mark>delivery terms</mark> 

_*) This implies that you can apply different cost markups for different suppliers and warehouses, if required. This applies regardless of whether you use standard or average cost price or FIFO._ 

<mark>Costs for order specific operations and material per order row can be entered manually and taken into consideration when running costing.</mark> 

<mark>You can add delivery costs as percentages or amounts, by entering the shipment cost on purchase order rows. This cost is posted with the value of goods when receiving the delivery.</mark> 

<mark>In the Inventory locations program, you can set up if extra costs (refinement) should be calculated and added to the receiving value during transfers to the inventory location.</mark> 

#### 7.2.1 Standard cost prices 

<mark>Cost prices are calculated based on the purchase price specified in the item data per supplier, or if there is none, the purchase price on the item. Overheads and markups, which can be entered on many different levels, are then added to the purchase price and the results are shown in the various cost price fields, either on the item or item per inventory location.</mark> 

<mark>If you have several suppliers the agreement with the main supplier is used, i.e. the agreement that is flagged as the main agreement. If there is no main agreement, the supplier with the highest priority is used, i.e. the agreement with the lowest priority code (= highest priority).</mark> 

<mark>Costs are also totaled upward in the structure of manufactured items and package items.</mark> 

<mark>Depending on which work method you choose, the calculated cost price can either update the current cost price directly or be stored in a field for new, preliminary cost price along with a start date from which the cost price is valid. In the latter case, you transfer the preliminary price to the current price field when you are ready.</mark> 

<mark>7</mark> 2 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Inventory 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0073-01.png)


#### 7.2.2 Average cost prices 

<mark>You use average weighted cost prices if you want the inventory to be valued according to the value of received deliveries. The function means that when new deliveries are received, the inventory is revalued with regard to the purchase price for the delivery. The delivery is "weighed in" according to the calculation. The calculation also takes into account any overheads such as delivery, freight, customs duties, startup costs, etc.</mark> 

<mark>You can control whether average cost prices should be recalculated when receiving deliveries or when matching supplier invoices.</mark> 

#### 7.2.3 Distinction between costing from items and from Estimator 

<mark>Companies who run costing from items, item revisions, orders and from the Estimator need to be able to distinguish between these costing records. Costing type is defined per calculation version, to determine whether calculations are of the type Item, Estimator or Item revision. You can only see versions for the relevant purpose in the different programs where you can run costing, for example, in the Estimator program you can only see calculation versions for the Estimator.</mark> 

#### 7.2.4 Cost price changes 

<mark>Every cost price change is logged. If there is an inventory balance for the item, transactions are also created for integration with the general ledger in order to update the inventory value.</mark> 

### 7.3 Revision control 

#### 7.3.1 Item master data 

<mark>Jeeves Revision control is a set of programs that allows you to modify item master related data without affecting the currently approved revision. You can compare current calculations to see what effect the changes will have on cost prices. There is also a traceability perspective when using revision control, and Jeeves Revision control always saves all revisions of basic data.</mark> 

<mark>The item master related basic data tables and programs include:</mark> 

- <mark>documents</mark> 

- <mark>routings</mark> 

- <mark>BOMs</mark> 

- <mark>attributes</mark> 

- <mark>supplier/item records</mark> 

- <mark>item balance records and</mark> 

- <mark>item record related fields</mark> 

<mark>You specify which item master related data, i.e. which tables and fields, are to be revision controlled. Changing one or more revision controlled field(s) activates color coding at record or field level:</mark> 

- <mark>Yellow means one or more fields are changed</mark> 

- <mark>Green means you have added a record.</mark> 

- <mark>Red means you have deleted a record.</mark> 

#### 7.3.2 Revision process 

<mark>Jeeves Revision control helps you visually track and compare current and new revisions. Your changes are also summarized, with color coding. When all changes have been made you can run a cost price estimate or check the assembly analysis tool, or start the approvals process. The final step is to manually update the revision status to Approved, ok to use in the item revision header, whereby the changes are incorporated in the item master. Any earlier version is automatically closed and automatically updated fields, such as Revision date, are color coded on the changed records.</mark>

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Inventory 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0074-01.png)


### 7.4 Quality control 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes extensive functionality for quality control. Companies within the Process industry will benefit from using these quality control tools, but they are a strong member of the ERP support for discrete manufacturers and other industries too.</mark> 

#### 7.4.1 Attribute management 

<mark>You define attributes or characteristics as standalone objects and then assign them to products at item, component or routing level. Often, a product's quality varies over time and these characteristics or attributes need to be part of the product's inventory definition.</mark> 

<mark>The attributes can describe:</mark> 

- <mark>an item's specific characteristics</mark> 

- <mark>how and what to measure or</mark> 

- <mark>what and how to visually inspect, describe appearance or</mark> 

- <mark>act as a checklist or</mark> 

- <mark>describe what to do at different stages in a product process and</mark> 

- <mark>make it possible to report on these objects, make decisions whether or not to approve steps/objects, trace and see the status and quantified target these objects had when reported on.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP also supports specific types of attributes that can represent the following:</mark> 

- <mark>An active component, for example, potency, stated as the power, concentration, affect, force, impact of a specific ingredient. Ingredients or materials that have an active component that is a percentage, content of the overall weight or volume of the material.</mark> 

- <mark>A specific gravity when we need to calculate how much water is required to dilute the solution to the recommended grade or concentration, described in its form of occurrence in the raw material.</mark> 

- <mark>Moiety can be used when the above two attributes cannot cover the required concentration demands. Moiety relates to parts of “something”.</mark> 

<mark>When defining a quality attribute you also link the object to the sales, manufacturing, and purchasing or customer claims process. This means you can define different quality attributes depending on the process.</mark> 

#### 7.4.2 Quality control processes 

<mark>If Jeeves Quality control (QC) is activated, QC records are automatically created when order or delivery rows and/or claims are entered for quality controlled items in the following processes:</mark> 

- <mark>sales orders</mark> 

- <mark>customer claims</mark> 

- <mark>purchase orders</mark> 

- <mark>goods arrivals, checking goods and received deliveries from purchases</mark> 

- <mark>purchase claims</mark> 

- <mark>manufacturing/work orders</mark> 

- <mark>received deliveries from manufacturing</mark> 

- <mark>internal claims</mark> 

<mark>There are alternative programs for QC reporting in the different processes. This is to allow you to choose the alternative the suits your company's processes.</mark> 

#### 7.4.3 Traceability and recall management 

<mark>Many companies, especially in the Process industry, face the risk of product recalls and the consequential requirements regarding traceability. Jeeves ERP's traceability functionality fulfills</mark> 

<mark>7</mark> 4 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Inventory 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0075-01.png)


<mark>the requirements of both forwards and backwards lot traceability, with information pertaining to customer, supplier, supplier batch/lot number, work order, work order batch/lot number, etc.</mark> 

<mark>Recall management often relates to product safety, regulatory compliance and demand for efficient support when communicating with the affected customers. Our recall functionality is built into the program for handling customer claims and uses our campaign and traceability functionality to find all customers who have received a specific product or a specific ingredient in the product.</mark> 

<mark>When you enter a recall claim for an item and batch/lot between certain dates, a list is displayed showing all customers who have received the item within the period. You can then send out e-mails using the built-in e-mail functionality, or produce various types of documents.</mark> 

<mark>You use Jeeves ERP's campaign functionality in this customer communication phase. You can handle information in different ways; enter text to be displayed in the message field of the e-mail and/or attach documents to e-mails in the recall campaign using the document management system.</mark> 

<mark>You can register and follow up on the recall campaign responses. Select a response alternative from a drop-down list of standard responses and/or type in a brief note. You mark responses when the planned activity is complete and no further action is required, also report time, if required.</mark> 

#### 7.4.4 Quality control manager 

<mark>As the QC manager, you can use a special program for replanning QC activities by moving records to other signatures. There is also a program for viewing the results from quality reporting in sales, purchase and manufacturing orders. Here you can select and compile QC data in order to prepare reports, certificates etc. according to your company's own work flow and quality requirements.</mark> 

### 7.5 Item analysis and planning 

#### 7.5.1 ABC analysis 

<mark>You can set up rules for and generate ABC classification of stocked items. This can be done for purchased and manufactured items, either jointly or separately. You can perform ABC analyses per</mark> 

- <mark>item/item group/supplier number interval or the like</mark> 

- <mark>inventory location or</mark> 

- <mark>item type</mark> 

<mark>The classification is then used in the planning of inventory levels to avoid shortages of the most sold items and also to avoid unnecessarily large inventory, thereby limiting inventory costs etc.</mark> 

#### 7.5.2 Safety stock 

<mark>You can set safety stock levels for any selection of items and in four different ways:</mark> 

<mark>1) Calculation based current consumption. You can use this method to calculate on actual shipped quantities and desired service levels by setting up inventory strategies to handle stock items based on ABC volume values and pick frequencies. The inventory strategy defines the service level (the probability of not being out of stock at the time of any demand), which in turn gives security factors. The safety stock is then calculated based on the security factor and the actual consumption (picked quantity per week and month).</mark> 

<mark>2) Manually, and block automatic calculation. You can use this method for items that are new or to be discontinued for example.</mark> 

<mark>3) Calculation according to safety stock in number of days to cover dispatches above the average usage. The safety stock level is calculated based on the given number of days, which is compared to annual forecast and usage.</mark> 

<mark>4) Calculation according to ABC rule. The safety stock level is, in principle, calculated as in 3) but takes into consideration number of days and safety stock lead time in percent from the combination of item type and ABC class.</mark> 

<mark>Items can be updated directly or via interim work tables.</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

<mark>7</mark> 5 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Inventory 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0076-01.png)


#### 7.5.3 Order point 

<mark>You can enter order points manually or calculate them automatically based on usage forecast and lead time for any selection of items. Items can be updated directly or via interim work tables. The order points are then used in purchasing and/or manufacturing to order point.</mark> 

#### 7.5.4 Inventory turnover 

<mark>The items’ rate of turnover is calculated automatically based on the usage statistics.</mark> 

#### 7.5.5 Economic order quantity 

<mark>You can enter the items’ economic order quantity manually or calculate it automatically according to Wilson’s formula for any selection of items. Items can be updated directly or via interim work tables. The economic order quantities can then be used to control the calculation of proposed quantities to purchase and/or manufacture.</mark> 

#### 7.5.6 Inventory control 

<mark>You can set up inventory controls in the different routines that generate inventory transactions according to own needs, for example against available balances, allocated balances, lead times, minimum/maximum balances and order points for stocked items, and against lead times for nonstocked items.</mark> 

#### 7.5.7 Available to promise (ATP) 

<mark>Jeeves ERP’s Available to promise (ATP) functionality provides a more accurate picture of the available quantities of a product on a requested or estimated dispatch date. ATP queries apply to package items (i.e. items with an inventory stock type set up for package items) and manufactured items (i.e. items with an acquisition method regarding manufacturing).</mark> 

<mark>The ATP functionality takes into consideration requirements from customer orders where the availability has been checked but the production has not yet been planned. When using the ATP functionality, Jeeves ERP takes many more parameters and factors into consideration, and the resulting underlying requirements from previous queries are used the next time someone queries the same item.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP offers alternatives for running ATP queries from sales and from assembly analysis. Via parameters, you can control if ATP calculations should be done based on planned inventory transactions, instead of the base tables, in order to speed up the calculations.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ATP enables you to do the following:</mark> 

- <mark>Enter a horizon for future requirements in ATP queries.</mark> 

- <mark>Create temporary manufacturing orders, i.e. "soft" reservations from ATP queries.</mark> 

- <mark>Delete all or individual temporary manufacturing orders that have been created for items by the ATP functionality.</mark> 

- <mark>View the results of ATP queries on items with temporary manufacturing orders or temporary material reservations.</mark> 

#### 7.5.8 Inventory valuation 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP, the following alternatives apply for stock valuation:</mark> 

- <mark>Stock valuation at current inventory value, i.e. current inventory balances * current cost prices</mark> 

- <mark>Stock valuation per specified date, i.e. inventory balance as of the specified date * the relevant cost price as of the specified date</mark> 

- <mark>Stock valuation per period, i.e. inventory balance per specified period end * the relevant cost price per specified period end</mark> 

> <mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

<mark>7</mark> 6 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Inventory 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0077-01.png)


The latter two reports calculate values according to standard and average cost price, as well as FIFO where applicable. 

<mark>You can do an automatic search of slow moving items in an inventory. The search is based on input about the number of months backwards in time from today’s date that you want to search.</mark> 

<mark>You can also change the current cost price of multiple selected items in an inventory location and update item data and inventory location records with the revalued cost price. This includes the current cost price of selected FIFO batch(es) in an inventory location.</mark> 

#### 7.5.9 Inventory revaluation 

<mark>Jeeves Inventory revaluation lets you change the current cost price of selected item(s) or FIFO batches in an inventory location and update item data and inventory location records with the revalued cost price.</mark> 

<mark>Standard Jeeves ERP logic for cost price changes automatically handles the update of revalued cost prices from the Inventory revaluation program, which means that the following types of transactions are automatically created:</mark> 

- <mark>Cost price change log, Items</mark> 

- <mark>Cost price change, inventory value</mark> 

- <mark>Inventory history transactions</mark> 

##### 7.5.9.1 Integration source and inventory transaction types 

<mark>You can specify if the functionality for cost price per inventory location should be active or not in the system and set the integration source and inventory transaction type for inventory revaluation transactions. You add details to your posting templates using the dedicated inventory transaction types.</mark> 

##### 7.5.9.2 Inventory revaluation reason 

<mark>You can define the applicable reasons for inventory revaluation by selecting the required option. By default, the Invtry rev reason field has the value obsolescence when you open a new Inventory revaluation journal record. You can change this value by selecting the appropriate option from the drop-down list.</mark> 

##### 7.5.9.3 Revise cost price of item and update item data/inventory location 

<mark>You can revise the current cost price of selected item(s) or FIFO batches in an inventory location and update the item data/inventory location with the revalued price(s). If you select an inventory location in the journal header but have the same cost price for the items on several inventory locations, Jeeves ERP will create transactions for all these inventory locations.</mark> 

<mark>When you have created a journal header with an inventory location and revaluation percentage, you can search for the items to be included for revaluation in a specific inventory location. Alternatively, you can do an automatic search of slow moving items in an inventory. The search is based on input about the number of months backwards in time from today’s date that you want to search.</mark> 

<mark>The items are presented in the left list window. Select the relevant items and transfer them to the right list window. The current value and the revised value of the contents of the right list window are summarized and shown in a total window below the Inventory revaluation, rows on the form. Double-click a record in the right list window to view the revised current cost price, average cost price, and calculated cost price of the selected item in the inventory. Note that if you want to reduce the current cost price, enter a negative (-) percentage value.</mark> 

<mark>After the item values are revised, you can update the inventory revaluation values in the inventory revaluation journal, create a voucher from the inventory revaluation, and integrate with the general ledger. Note that the voucher is created only if the inventory cost price has been updated.</mark>
