Jeeves ERP Inventory 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0078-01.png)


<mark>You can also indicate if the current transaction for the selected item(s) in the inventory location is an inventory deposition, i.e. impacts only the voucher and not the inventory or cost price. If not, the inventory values are updated as per the revised cost price along with the update to the voucher.</mark> 

### 7.6 Master scheduling and forecasting 

#### 7.6.1 Master scheduling 

<mark>Jeeves Master scheduling makes it possible for wholesalers and manufacturing companies to use the forecasts produced by the marketing department to efficiently plan future material and resource requirements.</mark> 

<mark>The function consists of programs for entry and revision of forecasts for different market segments and product families or individual items, which are then compiled into a master schedule. The master schedule is then transferred to the regular tables for item forecasts used as a basis for net requirement planning. This also includes functions to retrieve actual figures from invoicing statistics for comparison against the forecast.</mark> 

<mark>There is also a function to see, on an aggregate level, how the capacities of different manufacturing resources are impacted by the master schedule.</mark> 

<mark>Companies that use both master scheduling and package items can set up forecasts to be created on the underlying, stocked items in their packages.</mark> 

<mark>Some companies that use Jeeves Master scheduling and forecasting need to replicate relevant data, such as item forecasts and requisitions, between sales units and manufacturing units. There is a report with which you approve selected requisition rows for replication to another company.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves Master scheduling can also be used with Product variant management. Since different models can have different sizes (or other attributes) on different markets, you can connect the same forecast family to several forecast markets. Since forecasts usually vary between SKUs for master items, you can create forecast families per master item and allocate forecast shares to their variants items.</mark> 

#### 7.6.2 Item forecasts 

<mark>If your company does not work with advanced forecasts or master scheduling, you can create forecasts at item level for use as a basis for MRP.</mark> 

<mark>You can enter a forecast quantity manually in the highest forecast period and then automatically distribute the forecast over the underlying forecast periods. You can define any time units as well as any number of forecast versions for these forecasts.</mark> 

<mark>You can also calculate forecasts for items based on item statistics using weighting rules. When creating forecasts from history, Jeeves ERP informs if there are item statistics for the selected year and period that have not yet been updated. If this is the case you are given the possibility to update the statistics first, before creating the forecast.</mark> 

#### 7.6.3 Advanced forecasting 

<mark>Jeeves Advanced forecasting consists of two tools, Stock wizard for target management (what ifanalysis) and Amase for managing forecasts and alarms. In Stock wizard you classify your products and set your objectives. In Amase you manage new and changed forecasts as well as alarms. The alarms suggest corrections in order to stay on target with your objectives.</mark> 

<mark>In general, the advanced forecasting tool helps you maintain the following objectives:</mark> 

- <mark>satisfyingly high inventory turnover rate</mark> 

- <mark>small standard deviations in service level per item</mark> 

<mark>7</mark> 8 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Inventory 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0079-01.png)


<mark>and in production planning maintain:</mark> 

- <mark>more accurate forecasts</mark> 

- <mark>short/accurate delivery times</mark> 

<mark>With this tool you can enter forecasts on four different levels; Item, Item group, Item class and Total. The tool supports both amount and quantity forecasts.</mark> 

<mark>Amase has support for the following forecast models</mark> 

- <mark>Mean</mark> 

- <mark>Trend</mark> 

- <mark>Season</mark> 

- <mark>Crostonts</mark> 

- <mark>Brown’s method</mark> 

- <mark>Own forecasts</mark> 

- <mark>Campaigns</mark> 

<mark>The forecasting models also monitor the products' lifecycle and support the following three phases:</mark> 

- <mark>Growth</mark> 

- <mark>Stationary</mark> 

- <mark>Negative trend or phase out.</mark> 

<mark>It helps you decide what to have in stock and enables you to keep inventory levels in line with forecasts.</mark> 

<mark>Amase suggests a forecast based on the items’ order history and it is updated monthly. Amase lets you know for which items the forecast deviates from the requirements. It also calculates the safety stock, order point and the economic order quantity (EOQ). Each items forecast is presented in a graph.</mark> 

### 7.7 Inventory management 

#### 7.7.1 Inventory locations 

<mark>Jeeves ERP can handle an unlimited number of inventory locations and related, inventory location specific item data.</mark> 

#### 7.7.2 Inventory stock types 

<mark>You can define any number of inventory stock types for detailed control of different types of items’ inventory management, printing to internal and external documents etc., for example:</mark> 

- <mark>stocked items</mark> 

- <mark>non-stocked items</mark> 

- <mark>batch items</mark> 

- <mark>package items</mark> 

- <mark>serial number items</mark> 

- <mark>consumables</mark> 

- <mark>phantom items</mark> 

<mark>and other stock types.</mark> 

<mark>Through a number of parameters on the inventory stock type you control exactly how each type is to be handled.</mark> 

<mark>7</mark> 9 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Inventory 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0080-01.png)


#### 7.7.3 Blocked balance/quantity 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP you can block a whole inventory balance, or a certain quantity within the balance, from being used or dispatched. You can do so directly during goods receipt or at a later stage. This can be necessary if special inspection is required, for example, or if you receive many complaints.</mark> 

<mark>There is also a possibility to open up for users to pick from blocked balance, if necessary.</mark> 

#### 7.7.4 Blocked inventory location 

<mark>There is also a checkbox to disable an inventory location from receiving deliveries against manufacturing or purchase orders. The value defined for this checkbox in the Item balances program overrides the value set for the checkbox in the Inventory locations program.</mark> 

#### 7.7.5 Miscellaneous item 

<mark>You can define a collective item number for handling miscellaneous non-stocked items that do not require individual item numbers. If this item number is used during order entry, the order row is flagged for order-specific cost price.</mark> 

#### 7.7.6 Batch management and FIFO 

<mark>Batch management means that every received delivery of goods is allocated a unique identification number. The function is used for certain types of goods that, for example, require a use by, best before or sell by date (for example: perishable goods and medicines, etc.) or the manufacturing batch (for example: wallpaper).</mark> 

###### <mark>When receiving deliveries:</mark> 

<mark>When receiving deliveries to inventory the batch ID is either set automatically based on the purchase order number, row number and date, or is specified manually (for example according to the supplier's manufacturing order, delivery note or purchase order number). The information is used to follow up received deliveries, for example, in order to follow up the use/best before date, or to trace lots with defects, at allocation and picking and in FIFO valuation.</mark> 

###### <mark>When allocating and picking:</mark> 

<mark>You can allocate and pick items per batch and control the order in which the batches are to be proposed for allocation:</mark> 

- <mark>Use/Best before date – against order or delivery date</mark> 

- <mark>Sell before date – against order or delivery date</mark> 

- <mark>Batch number order</mark> 

<mark>You can also set up any number of status controls, OK/not OK to allocate, to pick, include/exclude from available balance. You can also specify a default number of days for auto allocation for any batches that are missing a Use/best before date. Batches with a Use/best before date should normally be considered first, which implies that the default number of days should be set to a high value, if specified.</mark> 

<mark>Some companies order items with a fixed batch ID from a catalogue, and that same batch ID needs to be used also when the delivery is received. If a batch ID is specified on a purchase order row it will be inherited to the received delivery row.</mark> 

#### 7.7.7 Serial number management 

<mark>Serial numbers items are item individuals identified by a combination of item number and serial number. The function allows you to trace item individuals and fixed assets in all inventory movements wherever required, such as received deliveries, production, quotations, dispatches, allocating, picking, service etc. The purpose is to quickly identify where an individual is, both physically and in the relevant workflow, to which customer it has been sold, whether it is still under guarantee, etc. Serial numbers are usually entered when receiving deliveries against</mark> 

<mark>8</mark> 0 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Inventory 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0081-01.png)


<mark>purchase orders, but can be entered in a separate program for manual entry. You can also apply serial numbers only at dispatch without affecting the inventory.</mark> 

#### 7.7.8 Bin management 

<mark>You use Jeeves bin management if you need to track the location of items within an inventory or inventory location. This can be, for example, to separate different types or sizes of an item or for customer specific bins. Bin management can apply to physical inventory counts, received deliveries, allocating and picking as well as in manufacturing.</mark> 

<mark>You can also associate bins with packaging types, to indicate the physical properties of the bin. This does not restrict the bin to the given packaging type; it only serves as a help to the user/planner. In addition, when creating load carriers, you can link a packaging type with an item, item balances, or inventory locations. This is then used as the default packaging type during load carrier creation.</mark> 

<mark>You can add checksums (alphanumeric characters) to each bin to enable Jeeves WMS to validate that the user is at the correct physical bin when completing WMS assignments.</mark> 

<mark>The purpose of load carriers/master load carriers is to hold a collection of items to be able to receive, move or pick them all at the same time, making inventory management quicker and more efficient. Considering this - and using the example of a pallet (master load carrier) containing boxes (load carriers) - it is only logical that the collection of items should always be in the same bin. The contents cannot be spread out over several bins, as that would inhibit you from receiving, moving or picking them all in the same transaction.</mark> 

<mark>Several controls exist in the transactions for arrivals, received deliveries, returns, etc. including order-specific inventory:</mark> 

- <mark>If bin is not to be entered, it cannot be entered for child load carriers or for the master load carrier to which they belong.</mark> 

- <mark>A child load carrier cannot be in a different bin than the master load carrier to which it belongs. The existing bin is defaulted on all transactions and updates the Bin field in the Load carrier program.</mark> 

- <mark>If a master load carrier does not have any bin, and you put a load carrier into it and enter a bin, the master load carrier inherits the child load carrier's bin.</mark> 

- <mark>If you want to change the existing bin for a load carrier, you first need to unmap from the master load carrier and assign a new bin (requires that the balance is 0).</mark> 

<mark>When receiving deliveries:</mark> 

<mark>You determine whether a bin must be entered when receiving deliveries on the inventory location. If so, you set up whether the bin should be entered manually or whether Jeeves ERP should attempt to place the item automatically. You can also have Jeeves ERP check the quantity, and the maximum weight and volume that can be received and stored in the bin.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP also checks if several items, i.e. with different item numbers, can be received to the same bin.</mark> 

<mark>If you apply automatic bin allocation, and no suitable bin is found, i.e. with the right bin type and where received delivery quantity does not exceed the specified maximum quantity, a message displays that there is no suitable bin for the received delivery.</mark> 

<mark>In the order routine:</mark> 

<mark>When allocating items for customer orders, a suitable bin is checked and suggested according to a number of parameter settings but you can change the default information (bins, quantity) manually for each order row. Example of the parameter settings are customer specific bins, if it is allowed to allocate order rows against several batches etc.</mark> 

<mark>There are also settings to control the bin information to be printed on picking lists and the desired sort order.</mark>

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Inventory 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0082-01.png)


<mark>In manufacturing:</mark> 

<mark>Manufacturing companies can apply bin management for material reservations in much the same way as in the order routine described above.</mark> 

#### 7.7.9 Load carrier management 

<mark>A load carrier is typically a container or pallet (with or without collars) where you load goods to store in the inventory or that may be received from the supplier. You can use the Load carrier functionality if you need more specific tracking of items within an inventory location, for example, to separate different types or sizes of an item or for a specific customer. You can activate/inactivate the Load carrier functionality per company in the database. If the load carrier functionality is activated, the priority for setting whether load carrier is required between the various levels is:</mark> 

- <mark>bins per item – highest</mark> 

- <mark>items per inventory location</mark> 

- <mark>inventory locations</mark> 

- <mark>items – lowest</mark> 

<mark>Any exceptions to this order of priority can be handled by creating separate bins per item- You can set the control on specific bins and inventory locations. You can also set it as default per inventory stock type, which is inherited on items with the stock type.</mark> 

<mark>You can also specify a load carrier to store work in progress (WIP) items, or to move them between work order operations. When you place your WIP in a load carrier, you cannot have inventory balance in the same load carrier on several bins in the specified inventory location at the same time i.e. the load carrier can only exist in one bin in an inventory location at a time. One work order operation may have several bins, but only one load carrier can be specified on one bin in an inventory location. The option to specify load carrier is available in the programs for labor reporting, where you manually report work order operations. Once you transfer the partially/fully completed labor operation from the left-hand embedded window to the right, you can include the load carrier details in the specific fields i.e. Inventory location, Load carrier ID, Batch No., Item serial No. and save the record.</mark> 

<mark>Load carriers/master load carriers are supported in inventory allocations, both for customer orders and for material reservations on manufacturing orders, and in both manual and automatic allocations.</mark> 

<mark>A load carrier can be moved around in the inventory to different areas/bins and the goods or items stored in the load carrier should reflect this in their inventory balance. Items can also be moved between load carriers and a load carrier can be delivered to the customer complete or in parts, i.e. one or more specific items in the load carrier can also be extracted and delivered to a customer.</mark> 

<mark>You can use the Load carrier program if you need to track the location of items within an inventory location. This can be, for example, to separate different types or sizes of an item or for a specific customer.</mark> 

<mark>You can also do inventory transfers of load carriers/master load carriers, or content within them, including order specific inventory. You also have the possibility to automate transfers when the whole content of the load carrier/master load carrier is to be transferred to a different warehouse or bin.</mark> 

<mark>You can apply automatic sequence numbering to load carriers by defining one or more sequence number series connected to the number series type 8, Load carrier and/or packaging type.</mark> 

Several controls and rules can be applied on different levels to refine how load carriers are used in the inventory, and what details load carriers should carry and update in the process. 

<mark>Load carriers are primarily intended for use in sales/inventory/purchases/manufacturing. Item balances stored on specific load carriers or master load carriers can be allocated to customer orders both manually and automatically. Automatic allocation of inventory stored on load carriers is supported for both order fulfillment and manufacturing processes, streamlining picking and material reservation workflows.</mark>

![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0083-01.png)


##### 7.7.9.1 Master load carrier 

<mark>You can use the Master load carrier functionality to make inventory management even more efficient. Any updates to a master load carrier affect all the load carriers within the master. If you use it, you can search for master load carriers using the Master load carrier checkbox.</mark> 

<mark>A master load carrier can contain any number of load carriers, for example a pallet (master load carrier) containing boxes (load carriers), and can also contain other master load carriers.</mark> 

<mark>If a master load carrier is moved/picked, all the load carriers within the master are also moved/picked. The same applies if you change the status on a master load carrier; the status is also changed on the load carriers within it.</mark> 

<mark>You can select a load carrier to be a master load carrier and map details between master load carriers and the load carriers that they contain. In case another load carrier is connected to the load carrier, it is automatically marked as a Master load carrier.</mark> 

<mark>Placing load carriers and items into master load carriers is usually done through the relevant inventory transactions. However, manual mapping can only be done if there are no balances in the Load carrier details table by selecting the load carrier to which you want to connect another load carrier and creating new mapping rows. There, enter the ID of the load carrier to be connected and adjust its status if required. Save and return to the main form to view the mapping in the embedded list.</mark> 

<mark>If the load carrier in the program header was not already marked as a master load carrier, it is automatically marked as soon as there is a mapping in the embedded list. Also, the Master load carrier ID and description are automatically filled in from the Load carrier ID and description fields.</mark> 

<mark>You can calculate/recalculate the gross weight, net weight and volume of master load carriers and the connected child load carriers.</mark> 

##### 7.7.9.2 Create/change load carrier ID 

<mark>The change load carrier button allows you to create a load carrier ID when entering arrivals and receiving deliveries into an inventory location.</mark> 

- <mark>From program header when entering arrival and receiving delivery to inventory location:</mark> You can create a new load carrier ID based on selected package type or manually entered load carrier ID and inventory location specified in the arrival entry/receive deliveries header for the selected purchase order number. 

   - When entering the arrival if you have selected more than one package type or manually entered more than one load carrier ID for a purchase order, multiple load carrier IDs are created. 

- <mark>From rows in right list when entering arrival and receiving delivery to inventory location:</mark> You can also specify or change the load carrier ID after opening a row in the right list in the arrival entry and/or received deliveries programs. 

   - If you have linked a package type with an item, item balances, or inventory locations, it is considered the default package type when assigning a load carrier at the row level. The priority is as follows: 

   - a. <mark>Item/balance record</mark> 

   - b. <mark>Item record</mark> 

   - c. <mark>Inventory record</mark> 

   - d. <mark>Packaging types</mark> 

Similarly, if you have linked a load carrier rule with an item, item balances, or packaging type, it is considered the default rule when assigning a load carrier at the row level. The priority is as follows: 

- a. <mark>Item/balance record</mark> 

- b. <mark>Item record</mark> 

- c. <mark>Packaging types</mark>

Jeeves ERP Inventory 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0084-01.png)


- <mark>In manufacturing:</mark> 

You can create a load carrier ID while manually receiving deliveries from manufacturing orders/suborders based on selected package type or manually entered load carrier ID, inventory location, and load carrier rule specified for the selected MO rows. 

If you have linked a package type with an item, item balances, or inventory locations or a load carrier rule with an item, item balances, or packaging type, the priority remains the same as when entering arrival/receiving delivery as mentioned above. 

- <mark>In the physical inventory programs:</mark> 

You can update the physical inventory bin for an item with the existing load carrier ID in the Physical inventory journal program as follows: After you have created a journal header with a journal number and inventory location, you can search for items to be included in the physical inventory bin by choosing the inventory location and searching for the specific item with the relevant load carrier ID. These items are presented in the left list of the journal. When you select the relevant items and transfer them to the right list window, the existing load carrier ID is copied to the right list. 

You can also update the physical inventory results record with the existing or a new load carrier ID by selecting the physical inventory bins within the journal and transfer them to the right list window. If a load carrier ID is associated with a physical inventory bin, it is copied to the right list. Alternatively, you can add a new row to the existing journal directly in the right list with the same inventory location as in the journal header and an existing or new load carrier ID. 

##### 7.7.9.3 Make load carrier mandatory 

<mark>You can indicate if load carrier is mandatory when changes are made to inventory when</mark> 

- <mark>reporting arrivals in the Arrival entry</mark> 

- <mark>receiving deliveries to inventory</mark> 

- <mark>receiving deliveries from manufacturing orders</mark> 

- <mark>updating the physical inventory balance in the inventory bin for an</mark> 

- <mark>transferring load carriers between inventory</mark> 

- <mark>manually entering inventory transactions</mark> 

- <mark>receiving order-specific deliveries or adjusting order-specific balances</mark> 

- <mark>picking customer orders in the picking programs.</mark> 

<mark>That load carrier is mandatory can be set on different levels and the priority between the levels is set as follows:</mark> 

- <mark>bins per item – highest</mark> 

- <mark>items per inventory location</mark> 

- <mark>inventory locations</mark> 

- <mark>items – lowest</mark> 

<mark>Any exceptions to this order of priority can be handled by creating separate bins per item.</mark>

Jeeves ERP Inventory 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0085-01.png)


##### 7.7.9.4 Load carrier ID on returned goods 

- <mark>Load carrier ID on reversed arrivals: The load carrier ID and description assigned to an item in Arrivals is automatically transferred to the reverse quantity transaction that has been created automatically in the right window when you click the Reverse arrival button. Load carrier ID on returns at arrival check: In the case of returns, when you enter the Returned qty and save the record, the return transaction is saved with the load carrier ID inherited from Arrivals.</mark> 

- <mark>Load carrier ID when returning goods received directly:</mark> 

   - From arrival entry: The load carrier ID associated with an item in the Arrivals node is assigned to the return transaction record in the Returns program when you enter the return quantity for the item and save the record. 

   - From received deliveries: When you enter the return quantity on goods in the Returns node in the Received deliveries program and save the record, the load carrier ID inherited from Received deliveries and associated with the record is transferred to the new received delivery row that has been created automatically in the right window with a negative quantity according to the entered return. 

##### 7.7.9.5 Calculate load carrier weight/volume if changed in Items program 

<mark>You can (re)calculate the load carrier weight and/or volume based on the selected LC calculation method if the gross/net weight or volume of any item associated with the load carrier ID is modified in the Items program.</mark> 

#### 7.7.10 Manual inventory transactions 

<mark>There are several ways of reporting inventory movements that are not handled via integrated routines:</mark> 

##### 7.7.10.1 Direct entry 

<mark>There is a simple transaction entry form in which you choose a transaction type and inventory location, enter an item number and, where appropriate, a transaction specific price. If required, you fill in a bin, batch number and/or serial number. This form can also be accessed from master items and the Item balances form.</mark> 

##### 7.7.10.2 Entry journals 

<mark>There is also an optional, overlying form for transaction entry via a journal. This is a legal requirement in certain countries. Entry journals give greater control over which users are allowed to enter the various types of inventory transactions, and in which inventory locations. The entry journal must be authorized and its status set to Ready before a posting journal can be created and integrated with the general ledger.</mark> 

<mark>Manual inventory transactions is also the normal way of entering inventory balances at implementation, that is to say, if the balances are not transferred automatically.</mark> 

#### 7.7.11 Alternative units 

<mark>Alternative units can be used for automatic or manual unit conversion in the Inventory, Sales and Purchasing modules. If the function is activated, items can be purchased, stocked and sold in different units. Costing is, however, based on your inventory stock unit.</mark> 

<mark>You can enter inventory transactions, including physical inventory counts, order, requisition and purchase order rows, dispatches and received deliveries in your own inventory unit or in the alternative unit. You can set the function up to allow entry of quantities in both units, if the relationship between stock units and alternative units vary. This is useful in the food industry, for example, in which you need to specify both the number of units and the weight. This setup also accumulates inventory balances in alternative units in Inventory balance records.</mark> 

<mark>8</mark> 5 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Inventory 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0086-01.png)


<mark>If you set the function up for automatic conversion according to a fixed conversion rate, the unit you enter is automatically converted into the other unit based on the conversion rate. In other words, if you enter a quantity in an alternative unit, it is automatically converted to your inventory unit, and vice versa.</mark> 

<mark>Example: An item is stocked per piece. It can be purchased and sold per piece, but can also be purchased and sold in the alternative units of 6 pack, 12 pack or 24 pack. If you use 6 pack as alternative unit when entering an item on an order or purchase order and specify a quantity of 3, the quantity is automatically converted to 18 in your stock unit.</mark> 

<mark>If you set the function up for manual conversion, you enter quantities in both units and Jeeves checks that the relationship between them is reasonable according to the given conversion factor and tolerance percentages.</mark> 

<mark>You can now plan a manufacturing order with alternative units in the Manufacturing suborders program and report manufactured quantity for alternative units in the Show recd delivs from MO node in the Material issues, allocation (suborders) and Mtrl issues, labor and rec deliveries programs. For more details, see the chapter on</mark> _<mark>Jeeves production</mark>_ <mark>.</mark> 

#### 7.7.12 Assembly 

<mark>There is a function for quick and simple reporting of received deliveries and picked components for packages and assembled items, without manufacturing orders. Because no manufacturing order is created, the program can be used for simple items that are manufactured as required or when there is free capacity.</mark> 

#### 7.7.13 Transfers between/within inventory locations 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP there are three different ways to transfer items between inventory locations, either via inventory transactions or via a customer or purchase order:</mark> 

- <mark>Using a special program for transferring items in the Inventory module. When you perform inventory transfers in this program you enter the item number and the FROM and TO inventory location in the same transaction. Delivery notes, picking lists and the like cannot be created in this program. The program can also be used for transfers within inventory locations, i.e. to another bin or batch within the same inventory location.</mark> 

- <mark>Using an order type for transfer between inventory locations in the order routine, which means that you can create picking lists and delivery notes in the same way as for normal customer orders. The delivery is received when the customer order is released.</mark> 

- <mark>Using a purchase order type that creates customer orders for transfer between inventory locations in the order routine, which means that you can create picking lists and delivery notes in the same way as for normal customer orders. The receiving inventory location sees the planned delivery in the planning list and reports the delivery when it arrives.</mark> 

<mark>For all three methods you can set up how allocated inventory, statistics etc. should be updated.</mark> 

#### 7.7.14 Vendor managed/Call-off stock 

<mark>To reduce the delivery time, suppliers often transfer goods to a warehouse of a customer in another EU member state. The goods remain the property of the supplier until the customer takes the goods out of the stock. This is called vendor managed or call-off stock. As per the VAT rules, effective from 1 January 2020, when the customer takes the goods out of the stock, the supplier performs a direct intra-community supply of goods to the customer. The supplier does not have to register for VAT purposes in the EU member state of arrival of the goods.</mark> 

<mark>However, if the goods are not sold within 12 months to the customer or in case of theft/loss of the goods, the aforementioned measures are not applicable.</mark>

### 7.8 Physical inventory counts 

#### 7.8.1 Normal physical inventory 

<mark>Physical inventory counts can be performed continuously during the year. When you make a physical inventory, you select the items to be counted, usually based on search criteria such as when they were last counted, physical inventory code, inventory balance and the signature of the person responsible.</mark> 

<mark>The inventory balance of the selected items is copied to a physical inventory journal, which you print out. The physical inventory date and accounting data are set to the date indicated in the journal header. The inventory is counted, the result is entered and the balance is updated. The inventory balance is adjusted (increased/decreased) with any difference between actual and registered quantity.</mark> 

<mark>Since the stock is adjusted with the difference, normal inventory transactions can be made as soon as the stock has been counted, i.e. even before the results have been entered and the balances updated.</mark> 

<mark>You can view physical inventory differences in both quantity and value, in the stock unit and any alternative unit. The value is updated when the physical inventory result is transferred to inventory (updates the inventory balance).</mark> 

#### 7.8.2 Quick counts 

<mark>Jeeves ERP also has a function for quick physical counts. This means that you can enter the actual inventory balance directly in a quick and easy way, without the need to create an inventory journal etc.</mark> 

<mark>You do so by entering a manual inventory transaction of the type Quick count. When you specify the physical count quantity, any difference between the current inventory balance and the physical count quantity is calculated, which is then entered in the field for inventory transaction quantity. The inventory balance is updated immediately, which is why the transaction type should be used with caution.</mark> 

<mark>The inventory difference is posted and integrated with the general ledger when printing the inventory journal.</mark> 

#### 7.8.3 Jeeves Warehouse mobile routine 

<mark>A standard mobile routine has been developed for Warehouse operations. Jeeves standard mobile routines are developed and maintained in Jeeves standard.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves Mobile warehouse routine is used by warehouse staff to report their work directly from where the work is performed. They can report inventory transfers, any inventory that is scrapped and also report quick physical counts.</mark> 

#### 7.8.4 Posting and integration with general ledger 

<mark>The posting of inventory transactions is controlled by inventory posting templates. The templates are normally defined per product account and inventory location, though there can also be general templates that apply to several/all product accounts and inventory locations. The templates consist of account numbers, and other accounts segments such as cost centers where applicable, connected to the relevant inventory transaction type, such as received delivery or dispatch, and split into integration details such as Inventory account, Contra account, Price difference account etc.</mark> 

<mark>Inventory transactions and associated postings are normally created in other modules. For sales from stock, inventory issues are posted according to posting templates when printing invoice journals and integrating with the general ledger. The same applies to purchases to stock; postings are created when printing received delivery journals and integrating with the general ledger. For</mark>

Jeeves ERP Inventory 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0088-01.png)


<mark>manual inventory transactions that are entered directly in the inventory module there are separate programs you can use to create journals and integrate with the general ledger.</mark> 

#### 7.8.5 Reconciliations 

<mark>During period reconciliations you can use Jeeves ERP’s functions for inventory valuation and reconciliation of inventory against the general ledger. The latter compares postings on the relevant inventory transactions with the corresponding postings in the general ledger, and shows any differences. If there are differences, you repeat the procedure, each time with an earlier reconciliation date, until the accounts reconcile. In this way you can find out the date on which the difference arose and correct it.</mark> 

### 7.9 Project manufacturing 

<mark>Project manufacturing supports the business transactions of companies that supply customerspecific products or solutions that are meant for use in a specific project. It covers the processes of a manufacturing process from quotation to payment using a project ID. This section provides an overview of the components that are specific to inventory. Other components are described in the chapter on Jeeves Time, projects, assignments.</mark> 

#### 7.9.1 Inventory locations setup 

<mark>You can define inventory locations to be used in Project manufacturing. You can also create a fictitious project to represent the normal inventory location when transferring stock to and from Project manufacturing inventory locations.</mark> 

#### 7.9.2 Requests for inventory transfers 

##### 7.9.2.1 From material reservations 

<mark>If the required material is not available in the project inventory, the shop floor personnel can create a request for that material to the inventory personnel. They can do this from the material reservations in a new node in the manufacturing order programs.</mark> 

##### 7.9.2.2 From inventory request 

<mark>You can search for requests created by shop floor personnel requesting inventory transfers. These requests are created in a node in the manufacturing order programs when the required material is not available in the project inventory.</mark> 

<mark>It is also possible to print the requests for inventory transfers corresponding to a picking list. The report shows where the material is available to pick, and includes the items, quantities, inventory location, and the suggested bin to which the item is to be transferred for a particular requisition.</mark> 

<mark>You can also manually enter requisitions for inventory transfers.</mark> 

#### 7.9.3 Movement of items between project inventories 

<mark>You can move items between inventory locations with or without project ID, between bins within an inventory location, and back to an inventory location.</mark> 

<mark>The inventory transfer creates manual inventory transactions in the normal manner, with the addition of the project ID.</mark> 

#### 7.9.4 Allocation of items from project bin 

<mark>It is possible to allocate items for sales orders or material reservations to a prioritized bin. For instance, when an allocation is made for a sales order or for a material reservation associated with an inventory location that is linked to a project ID, the allocation logic first searches the item balances for the bin associated with the project ID and the allocation for the order will be made against the specified bin.</mark>

#### 7.9.5 Material requirements planning 

<mark>You can run material requirements planning (MRP) for a selected project and one or all inventory locations from the Projects programs. This helps you plan for the project inventory and gain an overview and control over the records created to supply the project with goods.</mark> 

<mark>You can run MRP, either according to a pre-defined template or manually specified parameters. You can select an inventory location to run the MRP for the combination of project ID and inventory location, or leave the field blank to run it for the selected project ID and all inventory locations.</mark> 

### 7.10 EU sustainability regulations 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes functionality to support companies in the EU to comply with sustainability regulations:</mark> 

#### 7.10.1 Carbon Border Adjustment Mechanism 

<mark>Importers bringing Carbon Border Adjustment Mechanism (CBAM) goods into the EU from other countries must pay a fair price on the carbon emitted during the production of those CBAM goods. CBAM generates a cost on certain items (TARIC codes) imported from specific countries where the carbon credit requirements differ from those imposed on European producers. When determining the purchase cost of an item, CBAM related costs are included in the calculation and taken into consideration when receiving deliveries.</mark> 

#### 7.10.2 Corporate Sustainability Reporting Directive 

<mark>Corporate Sustainability Reporting Directive (CSRD) is a regulatory framework that helps companies to determine the accurate carbon dioxide equivalent (Co2e) footprint of their products and overall operations, by following the Greenhouse Gas (GHG) Protocol. This is essential not only for regulatory compliance but also for identifying opportunities for emissions reduction, enhancing sustainability, and improving the company’s competitive advantage in increasingly environmentally-conscious markets.</mark> 

#### 7.10.3 SCIP management 

<mark>The Substances of Concern in Products (SCIP) Management capabilities supports you with your obligation to report where there are substances of concern. It supports how you manage related products efficiently in Jeeves ERP, both in terms of structuring the relationships between substances and items as well as extracting information that needs to be reported to the ECHA system.</mark> 

#### 7.10.4 <mark>EU Deforestation Regulation (EUDR)</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes functionality to support compliance with the EU Deforestation Regulation (EUDR), which requires that products sold in or exported from the EU market do not contribute to deforestation or forest degradation. The regulation applies to products that contain, are made from, or are produced using cattle, cocoa, coffee, oil palm, rubber, soy, or wood.</mark> 

<mark>A dedicated program called EUDR DDS Information stores metadata and content related to Due Diligence Statements (DDS), which are legal declarations required for placing regulated commodities on the EU market. A EUDR checkbox is available on TARIC codes and Trading areas records to indicate that a Due Diligence Statement is required for</mark>

<mark>Support for managing and distributing Due Diligence Statement information across the supply chain strengthens audit readiness and traceability for organizations subject to the regulation.</mark> 

### 7.11 Industry-specifics 

<mark>Most functions in Jeeves Inventory are common to all industries. There is one, however, that is specific to certain industries, as described above in the section on products and services.</mark>

## 8 EazyStock adapter 

<mark>Jeeves offers integration with EazyStock (ES) inventory optimization software (powered by Syncron). Jeeves ERP's EazyStock adapter is intended for exporting the relevant data from Jeeves ERP and for importing inventory optimization data from ES according to standardized file formats defined for ES.</mark> 

<mark>Once you set up an item to be handled by EazyStock, you need to define rules for the import of data from EazyStock to Jeeves ERP. Here you define the items whose data is to be imported and the actual data that is to be updated. If you define more than one rule you should also specify their relative order of priority.</mark> 

<mark>JIS uses Jeeves Service builder (JSB) in the integration between Jeeves ERP and EazyStock. JSB is an information gateway which provides a single point of access to information resources. Information can be retrieved, created, modified or deleted depending on what is supported by a socalled resource handler. It is not an integration server but a dynamic endpoint that can be used by integration servers to connect to other systems.</mark> 

<mark>As part of the integration between Jeeves ERP and EazyStock (ES), you can enter a combination of own company ID and inventory location that should be used when exporting to and importing from EazyStock.</mark> 

<mark>When exporting using the following procedures, the values from these two Jeeves fields are kept together as one value as required in EazyStock.</mark> 

- <mark>JEEVES_EAZYSTOCK_ReadTransactionalDemands_SelectionID</mark> 

- <mark>JEEVES_EAZYSTOCK_ReadPurchaseOrders_SelectionID</mark> 

- <mark>JEEVES_EAZYSTOCK_ReadItems_SelectionID</mark> 

- <mark>JEEVES_EAZYSTOCK_SrprForecast_SelectionID</mark> 

- <mark>JEEVES_EAZYSTOCK_PneForecast_SelectionID</mark> 

This ensures that the exported records can be correctly identified and processed in EazyStock. 

<mark>When importing using the following procedures, they are separated as required in Jeeves:</mark> 

- <mark>JEEVES_Eazystock_Insert_Tmp</mark> 

- <mark>Jeeves_Init_Insert_EsInvtryParameters</mark> 

<mark>This ensures that the record is imported to the correct company (foretagkod) and set to the correct inventory location (LagStalle).</mark> 

### 8.1 Export considerations 

#### 8.1.1 Export of relevant item data 

<mark>Jeeves ERP automatically exports the relevant item related data, for example item information and stock balances and sends them to ES. Much of this data depends on whether the item is a purchased item or a manufactured item.</mark> 

#### 8.1.2 Export of transaction demand data 

<mark>Jeeves ERP also exports so-called transactional demand, i.e. requirements from manufacturing orders and customer orders, and sends the data to EazyStock.</mark> 

#### 8.1.3 Export of open purchase and manufacturing orders 

<mark>In addition, Jeeves exports certain data from open purchase orders, i.e. purchases with a remaining quantity to be delivered, as well as open manufacturing orders, i.e. manufacturing orders with a remaining quantity to be manufactured, for items set up for export to EazyStock.</mark> 

<mark>9</mark> 1 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

EazyStock adapter 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0092-01.png)


#### 8.1.4 Export of key forecast data 

<mark>Jeeves Service Builder (JSB) checks for items that are flagged for export to EazyStock and Jeeves exports the key forecast data for these from Jeeves Forecast/net requirements, Forecast, broken down per BOM, and related tables.</mark> 

### 8.2 Inventory optimization data 

<mark>You can view inventory optimization data from EazyStock if you open an item balance record either directly or from the Items program and open the ES inventory parameters node. You can see the result of the latest import from EazyStock and any updates made for the item and inventory location in question. The data is cleared and updated every time a new import is done to Jeeves ERP. The import is run automatically by Jeeves Service Builder (JSB). Jeeves ERP then updates the relevant item balance records according to the rules you have defined.</mark> 

### 8.3 Import of purchase and requisition proposals 

<mark>Data imported from ES files can be used to create requisitions or purchase orders as follows:</mark> 

<mark>1) JSB reads each row in the ES file with proposals for requisitions or purchase orders. The rows are flagged in ES for one or the other. Corresponding rows are created in the temporary table with information about item number, inventory location, supplier ID, purchase quantity, requested delivery date and any internal text.</mark> 

<mark>2) JSB initiates the creation of purchase orders from the rows in the temporary table that are flagged as purchases. The purchases are grouped by supplier, i.e. one purchase order is created for each supplier. When this is complete, the corresponding rows are flagged as processed. The next time the temporary table is loaded these are cleared from the table.</mark> 

<mark>3) JSB initiates the creation of requisitions from the rows in the temporary table that are flagged as requisitions. Requisitions are not grouped. When this is complete, the corresponding rows are flagged as processed. The next time the temporary table is loaded these are cleared from the table.</mark> 

### 8.4 Export of BOM 

<mark>When an item consists of a BOM, EazyStock can more accurately calculate and optimize the inventory balances for all items with BOMs if these are exported as well in a structured manner.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP exports bill of materials to EazyStock based on certain conditions set up in the Items program as listed below:</mark> 

- <mark>A BOM must be connected to the item in the BOMs/Package items table</mark> 

- <mark>The top item must be marked for export to EasyStock</mark> 

<mark>In addition, if there is a BOM revision, the BOM rev. field should specify which BOM records under an item are to be used.</mark>

## 9 Jeeves ERP's Astro integration 

<mark>Jeeves ERP can be run integrated with Astro warehouse management. This means that Jeeves ERP offers different levels of inventory management:</mark> 

- <mark>Jeeves' own basic inventory module/processes, for companies with low volumes and a low level of complexity</mark> 

- <mark>Jeeves Astro integration, for companies with high volumes and a high level of complexity</mark> 

<mark>There are also other, customized solutions such as EasyWMS.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP's Astro integration is comprised of the following components:</mark> 

- <mark>An external integration component called Transaction handler together with a Jeeves program for the configuration of the Transaction handler.</mark> 

- <mark>A number of static system tables for the definition of statuses, number series and underlying configuration data.</mark> 

- <mark>A mapping tool comprised of a number of programs in Jeeves ERP for mapping outgoing and incoming XML messages.</mark> 

- <mark>A few Astro specific basic data programs and fields.</mark> 

- <mark>A number of Jeeves programs for monitoring XML messages, both confirmations and error messages.</mark> 

### 9.1 Supported processes 

<mark>Jeeves' Astro integration, with Jeeves' standard XML mapping, supports the processes listed below.</mark> 

#### 9.1.1 Maintenance of basic data 

- <mark>Entry and maintenance of item data</mark> 

- <mark>Entry and maintenance of shipment routes</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP "owns" all item and route data, which means new entries and maintenance can only be performed from within Jeeves ERP. Astro stores item data on the item and inventory location level.</mark> 

#### 9.1.2 The customer sales order process 

- <mark>Order entry, shipment planning, dispatch and closing</mark> 

- <mark>Returns</mark> 

- <mark>Reverse picking</mark> 

<mark>The integration between the sales process and Astro is comprised of the Shipment planning routine, which is described in the chapter on the</mark> _<mark>Sales</mark>_ <mark>module.</mark> 

#### 9.1.3 The purchase order process 

- <mark>Purchase order entry, received delivery against purchase order</mark> 

- <mark>Update purchase order</mark> 

- <mark>Cancel purchase order</mark> 

- <mark>Returns</mark> 

- <mark>Backorders</mark> 

<mark>Purchase orders are entered as usual in Jeeves purchasing module but with a stock location prepared for Astro. When the delivery is received in Astro, an XML message is sent to Jeeves ERP that creates received delivery rows. When all rows have been fully delivered the purchase order status is automatically set to Delivered. Any returns to suppliers must be handled within Astro before the received delivery is reported back to Jeeves ERP.</mark> 

<mark>9</mark> 3 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP's Astro integration 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0094-01.png)


#### 9.1.4 Basic inventory management processes 

- <mark>Manual inventory transactions, including scrapped items</mark> 

- <mark>Physical inventory counts</mark> 

<mark>Astro "owns" all inventory balances, which means these inventory management processes are handled from within Astro. These trigger XML messages to be sent to Jeeves ERP, which create inventory balance records.</mark> 

### 9.2 Transaction handler 

<mark>The Transaction handler is an external integration component between Jeeves ERP and Astro WMS.</mark> 

<mark>The Transaction handler must be configured to transform incoming messages to a format that Jeeves ERP understands, and vice versa. Messages from Astro WMS are in XML format and they are transformed into a format that Jeeves can interpret and generate SQL code from.</mark> 

<mark>There is a program for setting up a standard configuration of the Transaction handler. The configuration program is distributed as part of the Transaction handler installation. All configuration is done in Jeeves ERP in order to make it as dynamic as possible for each individual installation.</mark> 

### 9.3 Mapping XML files between Jeeves and Astro 

<mark>Jeeves' Astro integrator is delivered with Jeeves' base Astro mappings. Jeeves Information Systems also distributes any new or changed templates. Partners can import these templates simply by pressing an import button and entering the name of the message in question. The template can then be copied to a mapping in the XML mapping to Jeeves program. This implies that the main task is to make adjustments to these, for example specify the name of your Astro installation, create any additional mappings or change the provided base mappings. To be able to do so you will need training in the mapping technique.</mark> 

<mark>There is a base integration transaction for each process/sub process. Example: the BalanceChange transaction for updating item balances after making manual inventory transactions and/or physical counts in Astro. Balance changes are sent from Astro to Jeeves ERP and affect the inventory balance records in Jeeves ERP. The relevant columns in the inventory balance table are defined in the mapping, and they are grouped, sorted and assigned XML tags, with or without conditions etc. When a balance update is sent via XML from Astro, the XML message is interpreted according to the mapping, and results in a transaction affecting the inventory balance.</mark> 

### 9.4 Monitoring and tracing transactions 

<mark>There is a number of programs for day-to-day monitoring of transactions to and from Astro WMS. There are programs for monitoring the incoming and outbound transactions queues. Here you can monitor and search on the status of transactions. When a transaction has been executed and the import/export has been completed it is removed from the queue, after which the transaction or message can be traced via the Log - search node or the incoming/outgoing transactions log program.</mark> 

#### 9.4.1 Error handling and data synchronization 

<mark>The majority of errors occur during the implementation phase and are caused by incorrect base data or balances converted from legacy systems, or by mapping problems. When these initial problems have been resolved, the only errors that will occur will be incorrect transactions, i.e. transactions containing data that does not comply with business rules or database constraints.</mark> 

<mark>Errors in Jeeves’ Astro integration can occur both in Jeeves and in the Transaction handler and the errors are logged in Jeeves. The Transaction handler also logs errors to its own log file which can be useful if it cannot log to Jeeves. The Transaction handler can be configured to send e-mails when an error occurs. When you receive error information you can, for example, use the transaction ID</mark>

## 10 Jeeves ERP ICT 

<mark>ICT, Inter Company Transactions, is a module that can be used to manage logistics and associated financial transactions in more complex company structures, i.e. between companies or within a company with multiple sales units, distribution units/central warehouses, and perhaps manufacturing units.</mark> 

<mark>The module comprises functions for:</mark> 

- <mark>automatic generation of delivery customer orders at the delivering unit from purchase orders generated from customer orders in the selling unit</mark> 

- <mark>item planning list for delivering unit accessible from the selling unit</mark> 

- <mark>automatic creation of received deliveries against purchase orders, if required, as well as automatic picking of customer orders in selling unit, when picking delivery customer order in delivering unit</mark> 

- <mark>generation and arrival posting of supplier invoices in selling unit when invoicing delivery customer orders in the delivering unit</mark> 

- <mark>manual final approval or automatic matching and final approval posting of supplier invoice in the selling unit</mark> 

###### _Explanation of terminology used in this chapter:_ 

- <mark>The ‘selling unit’ is the company or unit that makes the sale and takes the order from the customer.</mark> 

- <mark>The ‘delivering unit’ is the company/warehouse/manufacturing unit that fulfills the order. Delivery to the customer can either be made by drop shipment or by transit via the selling unit.</mark> 

### 10.1 General features 

#### 10.1.1 Intercompany relationships and connection types 

<mark>Jeeves ICT can handle transactions between several warehouses in the same company, or between several companies in the same database or in different databases, even if using different servers.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP makes defining internal and external business units and the relationships between them quick and simple. You set up the ICT process for each relationship, for example,</mark> 

- <mark>when customer orders should automatically generate purchase orders at the delivering unit</mark> 

- • <mark>if received quantities should be automatically updated based on what has been delivered between the units</mark> 

- <mark>when the status on purchase orders should be automatically updated based on events in the delivering unit</mark> 

- <mark>when customer orders in the selling unit should be released</mark> 

- <mark>how supplier invoices should be generated when the delivering unit invoices the selling unit and</mark> 

- <mark>whether users are allowed to change the order quantity and sales price on customer order rows in the delivering business unit</mark> 

- <mark>whether customer orders can be canceled in the delivering unit, or only in the selling unit</mark> 

#### 10.1.2 Activation of ICT functionality 

<mark>The ICT functionality can easily be activated for all or for individual users.</mark> 

#### 10.1.3 Translation of item numbers between units 

<mark>If the different units use different item numbers, you can specify the delivering unit's item numbers and via a parameter control that these numbers are used on the orders that are created in the delivering unit.</mark> 

<mark>9</mark> 6 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP ICT 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0097-01.png)


#### 10.1.4 Pricing between units 

<mark>At the delivering unit you enter a price list for managing the prices between business units, for example a price list with a certain markup on the cost price. A price list can be connected to one or more business units.</mark> 

<mark>You can retrieve the price based on the priority by mapping the pricing type priority parameters. You can also set the price adjustment factor for each priority type as desired in the respective Adjust factor % field. Following are the priority types that are used to retrieve the price based on the priority:</mark> 

- <mark>0 - Sales price from alternative supplier/price list + adjustment factor in percent for this pricing priority. Only used if the net price acc. to higher pricing priorities is zero.</mark> 

- <mark>1 - Sales price from delivering sales order + adjustment factor in percent for this pricing priority. Only used if the net price acc. to higher pricing priorities is zero.</mark> 

- <mark>2 - Cost price from delivering sales order + adjustment factor in percent for this pricing priority. Only used if the net price acc. to higher pricing priorities is zero.</mark> 

- <mark>3 - Net price from delivering sales order + adjustment factor in percent for this pricing priority. Only used if the net price acc. to higher pricing priorities is zero.</mark> 

<mark>The price is always retrieved based on the hierarchy in the pricing priority (in descending order with prio 1 having the highest priority). A pricing priority that is lower is only considered if the net price in a higher priority is zero.</mark> 

<mark>If no mapping is done, the current price is determined through a relationship between the Alternative supplier record for Items in the selling business unit and a specific price list designated for the ICT-flow in the delivering business unit.</mark> 

<mark>In the underlying exchange rate tables for currencies, you can maintain agreed and fixed exchange rates per currency for trading between different business units in the ICT-flow.</mark> 

### 10.2 Sales and purchase order entry 

<mark>The customer order is entered in the normal way in any order program. A sales order at the selling unit automatically creates a purchase order, which in turn automatically creates a sales order at the delivering unit. Via parameters you can decide whether values must be retrieved from the selling unit customer order to the delivering unit customer order.</mark> 

<mark>You can set up the desired functionality for the alternative supplier record to be optional regarding entry of Items for the Sales customer order (SCO) in the ICT-flow. If you set up ICT to make the Alternative supplier (AL) record mandatory for the ICT-flow, you need to provide the following information.</mark> 

<mark>In the selling unit, you must enter the delivering unit as supplier of the items in the Alternative supplier program or node. This record must also point to the fictitious inventory location for the delivering unit.</mark> 

<mark>Note that, if you use the ICT replenishment functionality you need to have two alternative supplier records:</mark> 

- <mark>one for the ICT supplier, connected to the inventory location with Stock replenishment ICT =</mark> N 

- <mark>one for the ICT supplier, connected to the replenishment inventory location with Stock replenishment ICT = Y</mark> 

<mark>If the different units use different item numbers, you can specify the delivering unit's item number. You can also specify if the delivering unit's item number should be used on the orders that are created in the delivering unit.</mark> 

<mark>If you set up ICT so the Alternative supplier (AL) record is not mandatory for the ICT-flow, the values regarding lead times will be retrieved from the internal company defined as ICT supplier in the current ICT connection.</mark> 

<mark>9</mark> 7 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP ICT 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0098-01.png)


#### 10.2.1 Automatically create manufacturing and/or purchase order 

<mark>You can automatically create a manufacturing and/or purchase order if required for items in the delivering business unit set up with acquisition method Purchase to requirement in the selling unit as well as in the delivering unit. In the case of automatic manufacturing orders, the acquisition method must be set to Purchase to order point in the selling unit and Manufacture to requirement in the delivering unit.</mark> 

#### 10.2.2 Dispatch date management 

<mark>You can take both the selling and the delivering unit's fixed delivery days and various lead times into consideration. This is useful for transit and order specific order types where the goods are delivered to the selling unit for further delivery to the customer.</mark> 

#### 10.2.3 Traceability Sales order->Purchase order->Delivery sales order 

<mark>On the purchase order at the selling unit you can see the ID of the delivering unit and the number and status of the sales order at the delivering unit. You have the corresponding traceability on the resulting inventory transactions.</mark> 

<mark>On the sales order at the delivering unit you can see the ID of the selling unit and the number of both the sales and the purchase order at the selling unit. You have the corresponding traceability on resulting inventory transactions.</mark> 

#### 10.2.4 Availability and status information at selling unit 

<mark>There is an ICT specific planning list that makes it possible for the selling unit to see the current inventory balance and planned stock movements at the delivering unit.</mark> 

<mark>In many steps in the work process, when the reservation status of the order rows in the customer order at the delivering unit is set, the corresponding status is automatically set on the customer order at the selling unit. The information is shown on customer order and purchase order rows at the selling unit.</mark> 

### 10.3 Delivery reporting 

<mark>You can choose between manual pick reporting at the selling unit and automatic reporting when the goods are picked, or when the order is released for invoicing, at the delivering unit. You can also automate the release of the order in the selling unit, if desired.</mark> 

### 10.4 Stock replenishments 

<mark>You can initiate internal stock replenishments, i.e. requirements that are not specifically connected to customer orders. The functionality can also be used in combination with requirement calculations. This means that a purchase order is entered or created at the business unit where the stock is to be replenished, and a customer order is automatically created from the purchase order at the business unit to supply the goods for replenishment. Jeeves has functionality to specify whether or not you can change the supplier after the stock replenishment order has been created.</mark> 

### 10.5 View inventory movements and balances 

<mark>You can view item balances at different inventory locations across companies. You can view inventory movements and the resulting balances in ICT inventory locations</mark> 

<mark>An ICT-specific planning list allows you to analyze the inventory activities planned for sales, purchasing, production, and service. You can also view information about batches and item balances. In addition, you can view current inventory balance and shortage check statuses in the selling business unit for sales order rows in either the delivering or the selling business units. If the inventory location is an ICT delivering unit, the planned activities in the list and the shortage statuses are retrieved from the relevant inventory location in the delivering unit.</mark> 

<mark>9</mark> 8 ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP ICT 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0099-01.png)


### 10.6 Change log 

<mark>You can enable/disable the log for ICT order row changes. If enabled, specific changes regarding ICT order rows are logged, including changes to order row status, inventory location, estimated dispatch date, row quantity, price currency, volume discount %, and discount%. The logs can be used to trace and view specific changes that are performed regarding Customer order rows</mark> 

<mark>Note that normal changes of order row status are not logged, except when the status of the order row changes to Canceled. This is because the log of all status changes results in a heavy load to this log function. The status of customer order rows is changed during many phases of processing a customer order row.</mark> 

### 10.7 Product configurator setup in an ICT environment 

<mark>Any configurable item can be used as a configuration model in the product configurator. In an ICT environment, the configuration model is defined in both the delivering and selling units while the configuration is done in the selling unit.</mark> 

<mark>You can create a stocked item with a new Configuration unique ID in the delivering unit in an ICT environment. The configuration unique ID is connected to the model in the Configuration user friendly names program. The BOM, package items and operations records will be created for the new stocked item in the ICT/non-ICT environment as required.</mark> 

### 10.8 Invoicing between units 

<mark>You can choose between manual entry of supplier invoices at the selling unit or automatic creation when creating customer invoices at the delivering unit. If you choose to automatically create supplier invoices, Jeeves ERP can create arrival postings only, or automatically match against purchase orders and create final postings.</mark> 

<mark>You can specify the invoicing type for customer invoices (in the delivering unit) on ICT transactions in the Auto invoicing types ICT program and control whether drop shipment invoices and transit/order specific invoices should be created automatically from the ICT delivery unit without printing the invoice in the Order program.</mark> 

<mark>If you select No auto invoicing in the Link between business units ICT program, invoices need to be created and printed in the delivering unit, else if you select Auto invoicing delivering dispatch order, invoices are generated automatically (without creating and printing the invoice in the Order program).</mark>
