## 11 Jeeves ERP Production 

<mark>Jeeves Production includes functions for production preparation, costing, material planning, production control, shop floor reporting and attendance, as well as for configuration and net requirements planning. There are also reports and programs for following up actual costs and other forms of financial follow-up.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP also offers an integration with a fully functional graphical planning tool called EQ Plan that is developed and maintained by a chosen solutions provider.</mark> 

### 11.1 Production preparation - items/material 

#### 11.1.1 Acquisition methods 

<mark>Items have a default acquisition method – purchase or manufacture to order point, to plan or to requirement.</mark> 

#### 11.1.2 Direct status control 

<mark>There is a parameter to control whether a check should be performed directly when a manufactured item is specified that the component material is OK to be used. You can check at manufacturing order entry, and if required at customer order entry when the item BOM is manufactured to order.</mark> 

#### 11.1.3 Replacement items 

<mark>Production planning may have specified an item that Production replaces with another item, which is used in other places in the BOM or which is easier to acquire. If this field is filled in on an item in a BOM, the item in question is automatically replaced in the list of material on manufacturing orders.</mark> 

#### 11.1.4 Bills of materials 

<mark>Jeeves ERP supports:</mark> 

- <mark>any number of BOM levels</mark> 

- <mark>order unique BOMs</mark> 

- <mark>BOM revisions</mark> 

- <mark>explosion and implosion analyses</mark> 

- <mark>fixed quantity in BOM</mark> 

#### 11.1.5 Change item in BOM 

<mark>There is a program in which you can quickly and easily replace items in bills of materials. In the main form you enter the item to replace one or more items in bills of material. In the left embedded window you search all BOMs in which the old item is included. From these you select the BOM rows in which the component is to be replaced, transfer them to the right window and save. The relevant BOM rows are changed. The right window also shows any previous changes to the item specified in the main form.</mark> 

#### 11.1.6 Copy BOM 

<mark>You can copy a BOM to another item in the Items program. There are two alternatives:</mark> 

<mark>Simple copy:</mark> 

<mark>Select the item whose BOM you want to copy. Press the Copy item button. In the dialog that is opened, enter a new or existing item number and check the Copy BOM box. There is a corresponding box for copying operations.</mark> 

<mark>100</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Production 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0101-01.png)


<mark>Copy with simultaneous adjustment:</mark> 

<mark>Select the item you wish to alter and select the Copy BOM node. In left window, enter the item whose BOM rows you want to copy. Transfer the desired BOM rows to the right window. This can also be used to compare BOMs of different items.</mark> 

#### 11.1.7 Item variants (earlier, simple solution) 

<mark>There is a simple solution for handling different variants in connection with order-specific manufacturing, such as sizes and colors of an item in a BOM, with retrieval of components for manufacturing orders via a variant code. See also the later solution described in the section on product variants.</mark> 

#### 11.1.8 Workshop design 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes functionality for definition of:</mark> 

- <mark>manufacturing operations</mark> 

- <mark>manufacturing groups</mark> 

- <mark>flow groups</mark> 

- <mark>tools and tool lists</mark> 

- <mark>specification of costs</mark> 

- <mark>scrap reasons and</mark> 

- <mark>instructions and linked documents on both item and operation level</mark> 

<mark>…also for:</mark> 

- <mark>handling design changes</mark> 

- <mark>calculation of design changes and</mark> 

- <mark>calculation of lead times for manufactured items based on operation times</mark> 

#### 11.1.9 Alternative routings 

<mark>There is a possibility to define alternative routings for items, which can be controlled per plant/line (via the inventory location) to handle varying costs.</mark> 

<mark>You can manually change to another alternative routing on a manufacturing order when manufacturing is overloaded, when manufacturing smaller quantities, etc., and then replan the order.</mark> 

#### 11.1.10 Alternative routing per inventory location 

<mark>You use the Inventory location field to connect alternative routings to inventory locations where necessary. An item can have different routings within the same inventory location or depending on inventory location.</mark> 

<mark>Routing can be pre-defined or can be chosen after the manufacturing order has been created. This is determined by how you set up combinations of Inventory location and Main alternative routing. If the routing is pre-defined, when manufacturing orders are created from net requirements, they are automatically assigned the correct routing and the relevant operations are automatically created.</mark> 

#### 11.1.11 Manufacturing orders with alternative units 

<mark>You can now plan a manufacturing order with alternative units in the Manufacturing suborders program and report manufactured quantity for alternative units in the Show recd delivs from MO node in the Material issues, allocation (suborders) and Mtrl issues, labor and rec deliveries programs.</mark> 

<mark>If the Fixed conversion factor checkbox is checked, the manufacturing order quantity will be updated as a multiple of the conversion factor of that alternative unit from the Alternative units per item program when the alternative unit is selected and the quantity of the alternative unit is</mark> 

<mark>101</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Production 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0102-01.png)


<mark>defined. If it is not available in the Alternative units per item program, the conversion factor will be retrieved from the Alternative units program.</mark> 

<mark>If you enter a conversion factor, leave the Fixed conversion factor checkbox unchecked, specify tolerance percentages, and enter quantities in both the stock unit and an alternative unit. Jeeves ERP then uses the conversion factor and the tolerance percentages to check that the relationship between the quantities specified is reasonable.</mark> 

#### 11.1.12 Overlapping operations 

<mark>With this function you can plan the start of a subsequent labor operation before the preceding operation is completely finished. Overlapping can be either on quantity or on time. By doing so the manufacturing order's throughput time is reduced.</mark> 

#### 11.1.13 Subcontract 

<mark>There is a possibility to define alternative data on operations used only when operations are performed by a subcontractor.</mark> 

#### 11.1.14 Product variants 

<mark>There is functionality for handling product variants, useful for companies in the fashion industry for example, for defining applicable combinations of say, models, colors, sizes etc. of a product with the help of a matrix. The matrix has various layers that you can switch between for viewing current and planned inventory balances, item numbers, alternative item descriptions, delivery dates, minimum purchase quantities - or if there are none, economic order quantities, prices etc. for each combination. The matrix is used in production preparation, entry of manufacturing orders, material issues and ordering subcontract labor.</mark> 

#### 11.1.15 Revision control 

<mark>See the section on</mark> _<mark>Jeeves Revision control</mark>_ <mark>in the chapter on</mark> _<mark>Jeeves Inventory</mark>_ <mark>.</mark> 

### 11.2 Costing 

<mark>See also the section on costing in the chapter about</mark> _<mark>Jeeves Inventory</mark>_ <mark>.</mark> 

<mark>You can set up manufacturing costs and overheads in the following programs:</mark> 

- <mark>Flow groups</mark> 

- <mark>Manufacturing groups</mark> 

- <mark>Monetary factors</mark> 

- <mark>Tools</mark> 

- <mark>Operations</mark> 

<mark>On operations you define the time to perform the operation, for example setup time, run time, and on the manufacturing groups and flow groups you define the costs of performing the operations for example fixed and variable labor, material etc. You can have different monetary factors for different manufacturing groups. Tools can also add to the costs on operations.</mark> 

#### 11.2.1 Subcontract in currency and discounts 

<mark>In the Supplier/item node you can connect an alternative supplier record (al) to labor operations. This means that you can enter unit price, currency, start and setup costs here and use the standard functionality for price calculation in the Alternative supplier program. If you do not enter an alternative supplier record, the information is retrieved from the operation in the usual manner. If you need to enter several records for the same item and subcontractor, you use the Priority field to differentiate between them. There is, however, no logic for retrieving the records in this case.</mark> 

<mark>102</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Production 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0103-01.png)


#### 11.2.2 Costing per inventory location 

<mark>As mentioned above, alternative routings for items can be controlled per plant/line (via the inventory location) to handle varying costs. If costing is to be done per inventory location you should ensure that inventory locations are defined for each plant/production line or equivalent, i.e. each manufacturing unit that has varying manufacturing and material costs. For the relevant items and routings, you define purchase agreements with their costs per inventory location (plant/line).</mark> 

#### 11.2.3 Costing with or without calculation version 

<mark>Jeeves ERP offers several methods of costing, and storing the results:</mark> 

- <mark>Without calculation version means that the results are stored totaled on the item.</mark> 

- <mark>With calculation version means that the results are stored on a calculation version. These results can be stored for any period of time and calculation versions can later be used to update the field for new cost price on items. This is a way to calculate future costs and, if required, transfer the result to the new cost price field, which is in turn transferred to the current cost price at a certain date.</mark> 

#### 11.2.4 Simple or detailed costing 

<mark>The simpler costing method saves less information in the database.</mark> 

<mark>The detailed method saves the entire calculation per selected item to see the result of all components down to the lowest level and all corresponding operations. This allows you to estimate costs for individual items and add on a simulation percentage for future costs.</mark> 

<mark>The detailed method always works against a calculation version.</mark> 

<mark>In detailed costing, logs are also created at a detail level as to how costs have arisen and been totaled upwards in BOMs. The log is created per calculated item.</mark> 

<mark>The results of cost price calculations can be analyzed in a special program before you update the item with the new cost price.</mark> 

### 11.3 Master scheduling/forecasting 

<mark>Please refer to the section on Master scheduling/forecasting in the chapter on</mark> _<mark>Jeeves Inventory</mark>_ <mark>.</mark> 

### 11.4 Material planning 

#### 11.4.1 Net requirements planning 

<mark>We support a number of ways to control how net requirements should work, i.e. if to include orders already started, safety stock, allocations, finite capacity etc.</mark> 

<mark>You can define templates for MRP with one or more sets of rules for how MRP is to work at your company. You can set up shortage tolerances, replanning horizons, excess inventory tolerances etc. You can also define rules for items to be included in MRP. Items can, for example, be filtered per item group or planning manager.</mark> 

<mark>You can choose between:</mark> 

- <mark>Material requirements planning from inventory location. This processes all items with balance records at the inventory location in question.</mark> 

- <mark>Material requirements planning from item balances. You can select the items that are to receive requisitions.</mark> 

<mark>103</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Production 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0104-01.png)


<mark>The result of the requirements calculation can be viewed in the following programs:</mark> 

- <mark>Requisition, details – in a program node you can create purchase orders from the requisition details</mark> 

- <mark>Manufacturing orders from net requirements</mark> 

- <mark>Labor operations from net requirements</mark> 

- <mark>Material reservations from net requirements</mark> 

<mark>You transfer the requisitions to manufacturing orders in the following programs:</mark> 

- <mark>Multiple MOs from requisition. Each requisition creates a manufacturing order header with a suborder.</mark> 

- <mark>Single MO from requisition. One manufacturing order header is created under which all transferred requisitions are entered as suborders.</mark> 

- <mark>Planning mgr, requisitions->MO</mark> 

<mark>Via parameters, you can monitor the location of retrieval of planned inventory transactions during net requirements calculations</mark> 

#### 11.4.2 Shots management 

<mark>Shots management is a functionality used in the molding industry, specifically in injection molding companies, to manage and monitor the number of shots required to produce an item.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP provides support to allow you to configure the tool used for manufacturing orders (MO) to provide additional information to support shots management and to configure operations for shots management. It also allows you to calculate the time consumed (unit time) for manufacturing when entering manufacturing orders for shots management and update the values in case of any changes.</mark> 

<mark>You can enter shots information in the shop floor program. Also, when reporting the number of shots in the Shop floor program, the tool is updated with the accumulated number of shots. This information is helpful to keep a track of the maintenance/overhaul of the tool.</mark> 

#### 11.4.3 Lead time control and buffer days 

<mark>You can choose between manual entry of start dates for manufacturing or automatic calculation based on manufacturing lead times and buffer days.</mark> 

<mark>You can define whether the calculated start date and ready date are to be based on the manufacturing order’s calendar connection. This makes it possible to take shifts that do not work the same hours every day into consideration. Jeeves ERP can be set up to check the feasibility of the calculated start date.</mark> 

#### 11.4.4 Lead time control for order specific manufacturing 

<mark>If no buffer days are set up, the requirement date is based on the customer order's estimated dispatch date. The standard functionality for determining the number of buffer days between the order row's estimated dispatch date and the preparation date is determined via Dispatch methods, sales, the Internal lead time days field.</mark> 

<mark>Alternatively, you can use the Preparation date field on the customer order row to receive the requirement date for linked purchase and manufacturing orders or requisitions. This means the preparation date for the customer order is taken into consideration in the programs for creating order-specific purchase orders and manufacturing orders and in the MRP program. This is determined by a parameter. The preparation date can in turn be calculated based on lead times for the item/item group. This is also determined by a parameter. Note, however, that you cannot use this together with the standard functionality described above.</mark> 

<mark>104</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Production 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0105-01.png)


#### 11.4.5 Net requirement pegging 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes a program for analysis of net requirements and order fulfillment. The program shows the relationship between demand and supply, order dependencies and potential shortage dates.</mark> 

<mark>You can analyze the relationship between orders on different BOM levels. For example you can see which sales orders depend on a specific semi-manufactured order, a number of levels below, being completed in time.</mark> 

#### 11.4.6 Replanning proposals 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP you can receive proposals for replanning of existing orders. Replanning proposals are received when the system sees that a requirement can be covered by an already planned order, but it has the wrong quantity or date (shortage situation) and therefore needs to be replanned. Replanning proposals can also be received when the system sees that there is a planned order without a corresponding requirement (excess inventory situation).</mark> 

<mark>You can bring forward or postpone production, increase or reduce quantities, delete or suggest new orders, divide orders when maximum quantity is exceeded.</mark> 

<mark>There is also functionality to automatically execute the proposed re-planning.</mark> 

#### 11.4.7 Manufacturing orders 

<mark>Manufacturing orders can be entered manually or generated automatically from customer orders, or semi automatically via purchase/manufacturing requisitions or via MRP. You can control whether a manufacturing order header should be created per flow group that will manufacture the items. When you create a customer order-specific manufacturing order you can control whether a manufacturing order header should be created per delivery date, or per sales order row.</mark> 

#### 11.4.8 Suborders 

<mark>In Jeeves, there are two levels in a manufacturing order, a manufacturing order header and a suborder. A manufacturing order header always consists of one or more suborders.</mark> 

- <mark>The manufacturing order header contains only one manufacturing order number and can be considered a collection of manufacturing orders under the same number.</mark> 

- <mark>The suborder contains information about the item to be manufactured, quantity, planned date, and the current costs. The suborder is also linked to a material list and an operation list.</mark> 

<mark>You can plan/replan from the manufacturing order header, with sequential or parallel planning of included suborders.</mark> 

#### 11.4.9 Availability check 

<mark>Once you have created a manufacturing order, you can do an availability check of materials. This check is done by running one of the availability check reports (several orders can be selected and processed in one batch) or using the buttons on the window for manufacturing orders (can only process one order at a time).</mark> 

<mark>There are two types of availability checks:</mark> 

<mark>1) Against planned balance (current inventory balance plus or minus planned deliveries and dispatches up to the picking date of the manufacturing order).</mark> 

<mark>2) Against available balance (current inventory balance minus planned dispatches).</mark> 

<mark>The result is marked on each material reservation (OK or Not OK to pick) and the connected operation, manufacturing order and MO header (all material OK or shortage in material). In this way you can see whether there is a shortage of material for any operation. If all operations are OK, the manufacturing order is also marked as OK. If all manufacturing orders are OK, the manufacturing order header is also marked as OK.</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

<mark>105</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Production 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0106-01.png)


#### 11.4.10 Allocation of material 

<mark>You can allocate material in stock to manufacturing orders. The function is used to ensure that there are items available to pick for manufacturing orders.</mark> 

<mark>If a manufacturing order is fully allocated, it means that all material is available in stock and has been allocated to the manufacturing order in question. If, on the other hand, it is only partially allocated, it means that some of the material is not available. If partial allocation is not allowed and material is not available, the manufacturing order will be assigned the status: Could not allocate acc to rule.</mark> 

<mark>Some types of items that are used as raw material require storage in special environments and the supplier will only guarantee the sustainability/best before date for these items under acceptable conditions. However, when the items are delivered these conditions might not be fulfilled and the best before date will deteriorate. In such scenarios, it is important to use the raw material that was delivered first rather than by the best before date.</mark> 

<mark>You can prioritize the usage of items by received delivery date when making allocation to material reservations for items that are delivered in batches. Note that the best before date is also checked since the batch might be unusable.</mark> 

<mark>Allocations are done as follows:</mark> 

- <mark>Received delivery date</mark> 

- <mark>Best before date</mark> 

- <mark>Last Entry date</mark> 

#### 11.4.11 Subcontract 

<mark>You can use subcontract purchase orders to purchase labor operations to be carried out at another workshop/factory. This can be necessary, for example, if the workload at your own workshop is too high or for a specific type of work. There is a special purchase order type with a separate number series for this purpose.</mark> 

<mark>You can produce a delivery note, via subcontract operations, with any material which belongs to the work and which will be sent to the supplier. You can do this using a function that automatically starts the operation.</mark> 

#### 11.4.12 Forecast orders 

<mark>Besides the normal manufacturing order type, there are two order types for forecast orders:</mark> 

- <mark>Forecast including material can be used when a material reservation is not to be made exactly according to the BOM or if material reservations should always be shown in the planning list.</mark> 

- <mark>Forecast excluding material makes it easier and quicker to enter, change and delete the order, but on the other hand you will need to create material reservations each time you run MRP.</mark> 

<mark>Forecast orders cannot be reported.</mark> 

#### 11.4.13 Vacation planning 

<mark>Jeeves’ function for stock build-up is based on using the safety stock internally in MRP, in a special way before the vacation period.</mark> 

<mark>The quantity to deliver during the vacation period is calculated for manufactured items from existing customer and manufacturing order rows. The safety stock is increased linearly from the build-up horizon's start date to the vacation period's start date. The result is that the requirement during the vacation period is produced in the form of a safety stock. The manufacturing requisition includes information about what is directly connected to orders and what is connected to the safety stock build-up.</mark>

Jeeves ERP Production 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0107-01.png)


### **11.5** Alternative units for manufacturing and material handling 

<mark>You can specify if the alternative unit is valid to be used on a material when creating a bill of material and in the material reservation that is added manually on manufacturing orders. Similarly, you can also specify if the alternative unit is used as the standard unit in the Bill of materials and Material reservations programs.</mark> 

<mark>In addition, you can specify alternative unit quantity at parent level when creating manufacturing orders.</mark> 

<mark>Note that only one alternative unit can be defined as standard and valid to use per material unit and per item number.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves also has functionality for sales, purchasing, and manufacturing to handle alternative units to your own stock unit and to handle alternative units when making material reservations. You can use the alternative units in two ways:</mark> 

- <mark>Fixed relationship between own stock unit and alternative unit: When entering order, purchase order and manufacturing order quantities in alternative units, these are automatically converted to your own stock unit.</mark> 

- <mark>Varying relationship between own stock unit and alternative unit: There is no automatic conversion to your own inventory stock unit. You enter order, purchase order, manufacturing order, and physical count quantities in both units. Jeeves checks that specified quantities are reasonable in relation to each other.</mark> 

<mark>You can also view alternative unit used in item explosion and implosion in the Multilevel explosion embedded list.</mark> 

### 11.6 Production control 

#### 11.6.1 Workload control 

<mark>In Production control you analyze and, where applicable, replan the workload generated from manufacturing orders. Jeeves has the following levels for workload analysis:</mark> 

- <mark>Total</mark> 

- <mark>Product area</mark> 

- <mark>Foreman area</mark> 

- <mark>Work group</mark> 

- <mark>Manufacturing group</mark> 

- <mark>Flow group</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves includes functionality for workload control against finite capacity, i.e. a workload ceiling limited by available capacity regarding machine time (manufacturing group) or man hours (work group). Note, however, that finite capacity is not supported in order-specific manufacturing.</mark> 

<mark>There are functions for definition of resources in manufacturing with links to current work schedule and any exceptions. This can be for machines, workshop personnel, or other resources. There are also functions for the display of workload details and links between manufacturing orders (labor operations) and workload details.</mark> 

<mark>When a manufacturing order is created, Jeeves attempts to find available time in the time pockets for the relevant manufacturing resources to cover the capacity requirement for each operation. For manufacturing orders planned by Ready date, if there is not sufficient time available up to and including today's date, you will receive a message informing that the completion date has been moved forward. If the completion date for the manufacturing order has been locked, the work overload will instead be “dumped” on today's date.</mark>

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Production 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0108-01.png)


#### 11.6.2 Jeeves Graphical planning 

<mark>You can work with workload control in Jeeves and in Jeeves Graphical planning.</mark> 

#### 11.6.3 Graphical planning system integration 

<mark>Jeeves offers an integration with a fully functional graphical planning tool called EQ Plan that is developed and maintained by a chosen solutions provider. EQ Plan facilitates the management of manufacturing orders in Jeeves ERP and helps customers achieve transparency and flexibility by providing a way to interact with their manufacturing related data graphically. Note that this integration is currently only implemented with the production module.</mark> 

<mark>As part of the integration, Jeeves allows you to define the different planning levels that can be set for a resource group. Based on the selected planning level, the system will load the relevant manufacturing related information from Jeeves ERP to EQ Plan (via the integration endpoint). It also lets you define resource groups and their properties for the graphical planning system. Once you have defined a resource group, you can link it to one or more manufacturing groups from the GPS resource group row embedded list. In addition, you can define settings (for e.g. prefixes, colors, fonts) and skills (for e.g. sewing) and link them to a manufacturing group. Skills can be used to assign operations to manufacturing groups. These are read by EQ Plan when loading data from Jeeves ERP. Further, admin users can trigger a locking mechanism to make sure that other users running EQ plan do not interfere with the ongoing planning.</mark> 

### 11.7 Reporting 

#### 11.7.1 Reporting completed labor 

<mark>There is a choice of reporting methods such as one single or separate programs for labor, material and received deliveries. There is also a choice between manual entry via forms or checklists, or by button, as well as automatic reporting (backflushing).</mark> 

<mark>You can also set up reporting so that subsequent operations (including the material reservations linked to them) and the manufacturing order itself shall be re-planned in accordance with the reported approved quantity of the current operation.</mark> 

#### 11.7.2 Reporting material issues 

<mark>You can manually report material issues:</mark> 

<mark>per suborder or</mark> 

<mark>per operation in the operation queue</mark> 

<mark>Alternatively, material can be issued automatically (backflushing) at:</mark> 

- <mark>Operation start. Material is issued automatically on startup of an operation. All material reservations belonging to the operation are picked from inventory.</mark> 

- <mark>Operation reporting. Material is issued automatically according to reported labor based on the manufactured quantity of the operation. The material is picked from inventory based on planned manufacturing and on the BOM of the manufacturing order (i.e. not based on the BOM register).</mark> 

- <mark>Closing of manufacturing orders</mark> 

#### 11.7.3 Scrap management 

<mark>You can report scrap when receiving deliveries. The scrapped quantity and reason can be entered when reporting received deliveries of finished products. There are two, parameter-controlled alternatives; the remaining quantity is calculated either as only corrected quantity or both corrected and scrapped quantity.</mark> 

<mark>108</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Production 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0109-01.png)


#### 11.7.4 Delivery of manufactured items to inventory 

<mark>You can manually report received deliveries from own production from the programs for reporting picked material. Alternatively, received deliveries can be reported automatically based on the approved manufactured quantity on the operation. You can set up tolerance percentages for deliveries to be considered fully delivered.</mark> 

#### 11.7.5 Integration with general ledger 

<mark>Posting of material issues, labor and received deliveries from production is normally done automatically via a separate batch, for example, every day or week. One material journal, one labor reporting journal and one received delivery journal are created per reporting date. Each journal generates a voucher in the general ledger.</mark> 

<mark>Posting is done according to posting rules per product account and/or flow group. The posting rules control the accounts and account segments (cost center, cost unit, etc.) on which inventory transactions, WIP, labor and production costs, etc. are to be posted.</mark> 

#### 11.7.6 Closing manufacturing orders 

<mark>There is a function button for flagging manufacturing orders as completed/closed. When you do so, all material reservations and planned labor operations are checked to ensure they have been reported. The system also checks that the planned delivery has been received. It also checks against rules for cost differences.</mark> 

<mark>This function changes the status of the selected manufacturing order and underlying suborders to Completed. When this has been done the manufacturing order is closed, which implies that final, actual costing can be done. The manufacturing order also disappears from the WIP list.</mark> 

<mark>As long as the manufacturing order is not finally posted (Manufacturing order, profit/loss), you can open it again by going into a suborder and changing the status to Partially delivered.</mark> 

#### 11.7.7 Quick-reporting 

<mark>There is a program that can be used for quick-reporting of completed manufacturing orders, in which each flow group can deal with their own orders. The function processes the manufacturing orders of the current flow group in the work table for orders to be closed.</mark> 

<mark>For the order to be closed the following must have been reported:</mark> 

- <mark>material issues</mark> 

- <mark>labor</mark> 

- <mark>received delivery from production</mark> 

<mark>The following is also checked:</mark> 

- <mark>there is no order specific inventory</mark> 

- <mark>any manufacturing cost difference lies within the limits specified on the flow group</mark> 

### 11.8 Attendance and Shopfloor control 

<mark>Jeeves Attendance and Shop floor control are optional sub modules to the Production module that give extended, but at the same time, quick and simple-to-use functionality regarding attendance reporting and shop floor control.</mark> 

<mark>The unique ID of job cards can be generated based on Jeeves Automatic sequence numbering functionality.</mark> 

<mark>There is functionality to filter inactive flow groups, manufacturing groups and employees, as well as to see who is currently clocked in to work and, if applicable, what jobs they are currently working on.</mark> 

<mark>109</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Production 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0110-01.png)


<mark>You also have the possibility to restrict the display of inactive manufacturing groups in Shopfloor/Mfg operation queue programs through the Active setting in the flow groups and/or Manufacturing groups programs</mark> 

<mark>Reporting is made easy by lists that are updated in real-time with logical color-coding, and buttons for quick-reporting.</mark> 

#### 11.8.1 Attendance 

<mark>The attendance module is a clock-in - clock-out system that results in calculated time per salary type. The system allows you to see who is currently clocked in to work and, if applicable, what jobs they are currently working on. The calculation can be transferred to payroll systems.</mark> 

<mark>Reporting can be done by entering the employee ID number manually, or by means of barcodes or magnetic cards. The latter approach provides improved security. The employees can view their most recent clock-ins and clock-outs directly on the screen by striking a function key. This makes it easy to check that the actual hours worked are correct.</mark> 

<mark>Reason codes are managed automatically by means of clock-in and clock-out rules on the schedule. If the employee clocks in and out within scheduled time, only reasons for absence are displayed, while outside the schedule only reasons for overtime are displayed.</mark> 

<mark>The system allows the employees to switch schedules without having to involve the payroll administrator. This can be useful, for instance in cases where it is necessary to change schedule on days when the payroll administrator is out of the office.</mark> 

#### 11.8.2 Flexi-time and work metrics 

<mark>The system has support for flexi-time, work metrics and work time reduction, including the possibility to handle work time reductions even on non-working days.</mark> 

#### 11.8.3 Integration with payroll systems 

<mark>Time is calculated for each employee per selected period; this information can be transferred to payroll systems (in Sweden, for example: ADGA, Kontek, Hogia, Visma and SPCS).</mark> 

#### 11.8.4 Shop floor control 

<mark>Jeeves Shop floor control is designed for manufacturing companies with intensive production reporting. The sub module consists of a real-time operation queue, where the operator can see what is to be done, in which order, and any shortages in materials. It provides an overview and a basis for decision-making for the manufacturing groups and flow groups.</mark> 

<mark>Additional added value is derived from improved item balance reliability, costing and follow-up of scrapping.</mark> 

<mark>The Shop floor control sub module is integrated with the Attendance sub module, which means that the production reports consider the work schedule for the employee and the employees' clocking in and out.</mark> 

#### 11.8.5 Operation card 

<mark>The operation card contains detailed information about the operation, such as planned, reported and remaining quantity. You can see the previous and the next operation, and if there are drawings, the drawings can be displayed from the operation card.</mark> 

<mark>The operation card also contains information about different tools that you can use. Reporting (start, sub-reporting, completion, interruption, etc.) is done from the operation card. There are shortcut commands for all buttons to finish setup, start production etc. The buttons can be enabled depending on the operation’s status.</mark>

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Production 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0111-01.png)


#### 11.8.6 Time dividing (machine, employee) 

<mark>Machine time: If there is more than one job on a manufacturing group at the same time you can choose to divide time for every job.</mark> 

- <mark>Do not divide machine time</mark> 

- <mark>Divide time evenly</mark> 

- <mark>Divide time according to planned time</mark> 

<mark>Man time: If there is more than one job on the employee at the same time you can choose to divide time for every job.</mark> 

- <mark>Do not divide labor time</mark> 

- <mark>Divide time evenly</mark> 

- <mark>Divide time based on planned personnel/operation on operation</mark> 

- <mark>Divide time according to planned time</mark> 

#### 11.8.7 Team reporting 

<mark>This is used to reduce the number of reports prepared in the situation when a work team collectively loads a job.</mark> 

<mark>It is only possible to be a member of one team at time.</mark> 

#### 11.8.8 Indirect job 

<mark>Indirect job is used in situations where the time cannot be loaded on specific operations, example cleaning, union work and the like.</mark> 

#### 11.8.9 Assembly list 

<mark>It is possible to group your operations and start them simultaneously. The reported time will be divided among the different operations.</mark> 

#### 11.8.10 Jeeves Mobile shopfloor routine 

<mark>A standard mobile routine for individuals in manufacturing to report operations.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves Mobile shopfloor routine provides a productive and efficient way of reporting time and deliveries along with the statuses of operations in the manufacturing process.</mark> 

#### 11.8.11 Filter inactive employees 

###### <mark>Shopfloor program</mark> 

<mark>The 'or choose from list' drop-down field in the Start labor dialog is shown when you open a record in the Operation queue and click Start Prod . It is validated on whether the Cancel and Not active checkboxes are checked as well as on valid Active from and Active to dates in the Personnel and Shop floor personnel programs. Following the validation, only active personnel are displayed in the drop-down list.</mark> 

<mark>Attendance report program:</mark> 

<mark>Validation is done to check if the Cancel and Not active checkboxes are checked as well as on valid Active from and Active to dates in the Personnel and Shop floor personnel programs when;</mark> 

- <mark>displaying the Employee is not active message when searching for employees in the Attendance program</mark> 

- <mark>displaying the Employee is not active error message,</mark>

#### 11.8.12 Validation rules 

<mark>Jeeves provides for maintenance of the rules in Shop floor control.</mark> 

<mark>You can add records on different levels; company, flow group, manufacturing group and employee. Observe that not all keys are applicable on all levels. Company is the highest level, and there must be a record for each company. When a key value is requested, Jeeves first searches at employee, then manufacturing group, flow group and finally at company level.</mark> 

<mark>If no value has been entered  the key is not applicable and the system goes up one level to retrieve the key.</mark> 

<mark>You can specify starting an operation only if the immediately previous operation or any previous operations are NOT finished, if there is a rule that does not allow you to start an operation before completion of the previous operation. In addition, you can also choose to divide labor time if a manufacturing group has more than one active job at the same time.</mark> 

<mark>To improve the understanding of the value option of each setting when setting up the shop floor controls, fields used to control many settings are displayed as drop-down lists with the description of each value.</mark> 

### 11.9 Internal claims 

<mark>Jeeves contains a function for entering and logging internal claims (manufacturing claims), i.e. remarks and complaints about items and manufacturing orders, and for recording how they are to be settled as well as actions to be taken.</mark> 

<mark>You can define different work flows for different types of claims, specify base data for scheduling, and control how activities should be reported.</mark> 

### 11.10 Follow up and queries 

#### 11.10.1 Production information 

<mark>There are programs for displaying/printout of completed material issues, reported labor and received deliveries and related transactions and postings. These display accumulated time, setup time, as well as manufactured and scrapped quantity per flow and production group.</mark> 

###### <mark>Financial follow-up</mark> 

<mark>All reporting on manufacturing orders has financial consequences. The costs are of different types:</mark> 

- <mark>Material costs (all material included)</mark> 

- <mark>Labor costs:</mark> 

   - <mark>Wage costs (including social contributions)</mark> 

   - <mark>Machine costs</mark> 

   - <mark>Tool costs</mark> 

- <mark>Cost for any subcontracting</mark> 

<mark>As soon as anything is reported on the manufacturing order, it becomes part of Work in Progress (WIP). Any partially received deliveries of finished goods reduce the value for the order in the WIP list. When you close a manufacturing order with the Flag order completed button, the order disappears from the WIP list. You print WIP lists using, for example, the report programs:</mark> 

- <mark>WIP list, manufacturing order</mark> 

- <mark>WIP list, suborder details</mark> 

<mark>You can print actual costs using, for example, the report programs:</mark> 

   - <mark>Actual cost, summary</mark> 

   - <mark>Actual cost, transactions</mark> 

- <mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark>

Jeeves ERP Production 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0113-01.png)


<mark>The report shows the value of all transactions on a manufacturing order. The actual cost is listed beside the estimated costs for comparison. You can print the report to as a preliminary cost at any time.</mark> 

<mark>You create the profit/loss journal for completed manufacturing orders, as a part of the final costing and financial follow-up. Postings are created for all selected, completed manufacturing orders (suborders) that have not previously been posted on any journal. One journal is created per order completion date.</mark> 

### 11.11 Reconciliations 

<mark>During period reconciliations you can use Jeeves’ function for reconciliation of work in progress.</mark> 

### 11.12 Industry-specifics 

<mark>Most functions in Jeeves Production are common to all industries. There is one, however, that is specific to certain industries, as described below.</mark> 

#### 11.12.1 Product variant matrix 

<mark>The product variant matrix mentioned earlier was developed with the fashion industry in mind, but can be used for any products with variants, i.e. combinations of models, colors, sizes etc., or the like.</mark>

## 12 Jeeves ERP Product configurator 

<mark>Jeeves Product configurator is a proprietary module in Jeeves ERP, which means that configuration rules for products are established when products are defined in Jeeves ERP and not managed as a separate system or integrated application.</mark> 

<mark>The product configurator is used to create an order specific configuration of an item and is meant for companies that want to configure individual products/machines. A product configurator model is created to represent a generic product structure. A model can have a set of standard material and additional material that is model specific. Once the model is set up, items with unique bills of material and unique routings can be created from a master bill of material.</mark> 

<mark>Instead of entering material and operations manually for the configured item, the model sets up the item for you based on the answers (regarding features, options, etc.) that you have entered for the configuration. For example, when configuring lighting, a certain socket would be picked based on the type of light bulb. A base item could be a set of screws that is always part of the product, or an assembly instruction, while the rest of the configuration is selected.</mark> 

<mark>The definitions created from your selections are used in order entry and quotations and make the process of responding to a customer request for a quotation and order confirmation easier and faster.</mark> 

<mark>When configuring, you can work with Jeeves Product configurator in an interactive question and answer dialog. All input is validated against predefined rules for each product ensuring that the resulting configuration is valid. Cost prices are calculated dynamically and lead times are displayed. By classifying questions, work flow can be applied to have multiple departments respond or validate customers’ specifications and enter comments to each question.</mark> 

<mark>With Jeeves Product configurator, you can manage configuration scenarios in any of the following sourcing strategies:</mark> 

- <mark>Make-to-stock</mark> 

- <mark>Assemble-to-order</mark> 

- <mark>Engineer-to-order</mark> 

- <mark>Pick-to-order</mark> 

- <mark>Buy-to-order</mark> 

<mark>The product configurator can be used with most applications and routines, for example the following:</mark> 

- <mark>Sales order</mark> 

- <mark>Quotation</mark> 

- <mark>Quotation Estimator</mark> 

- <mark>Production order</mark> 

- <mark>Consist of (explosion)</mark> 

- <mark>ICT flow</mark> 

- <mark>Price simulation</mark> 

- <mark>Cost price simulation</mark> 

- <mark>Create item numbers with standard configuration</mark> 

<mark>114</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Product configurator 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0115-01.png)


### 12.1 Configuration feature types 

<mark>The following feature types are available for product configuration:</mark> 

- <mark>Option: A dynamic configuration feature; it can be a question about measurements or color, a question to which the answer is Yes or No or it could be other types of options.</mark> 

- <mark>Material: Configuration feature connected to an item in the Items table. This can be any type of item, such as a package item, a phantom item, or an individual component of material.</mark> 

- <mark>Numeric: Configuration feature for answering with any numeric value, with the only limitations being the number of characters and decimals allowed in the field, for example 110 cm.</mark> 

- <mark>Discrete numeric: Configuration feature for generating pre-defined numeric values, where you enter a range of values and a factor, for example from 100 cm to 300 cm and a factor of 10. This would generate possible values 100 cm, 110 cm, 120 cm etc. up to 300 cm.</mark> 

- <mark>Text: Configuration feature for entering or generating text, for example to enter a customer or purchase order specific item description or any other text to be included in the model. For example, you can set up a condition where if the user does not answer a question about an item's description, its standard description is retrieved from the Items table. However, if an answer is given, the user is prompted to enter an order specific item description.</mark> 

### 12.2 Configuration answer types 

<mark>User inputs is used to define the answer type. Currently, only three values are available</mark> 

- <mark>User Input: The feature is generated according to the answer from the user</mark> 

- <mark>Formula: The feature is generated according to a pre-defined formula</mark> 

- <mark>Default: The feature is generated according to a pre-defined default value that can be overridden by the user if required.</mark> 

<mark>The specified values are displayed as drop-down list options when you have answered a question to identify if a feature has been set up using a formula, user input, or a default value.</mark> 

### 12.3 Configuration formula types 

<mark>You can use formulas to define a feature by returning values that determine how a feature should be answered. A formula must be defined as one of the following types: formula returning numeric, alphanumeric (string), or an item number or formula calling a procedure.</mark> 

<mark>Assigning these different formula types to formulas will help you when using and referring to formulas.</mark> 

### 12.4 Configuration status 

<mark>The applicable statuses of configurations in the product configurator are as follows:</mark> 

- <mark>10, Under entry - Configuration status when you open the configuration in the product configurator for the very first time.</mark> 

- <mark>20, Entered/changed - Configuration status when reconfiguration is done for even a single feature.</mark> 

- <mark>30, Invalid configuration - Configuration status when you press Done but the configuration is not valid or an error occurs when creating BOM and Labor.</mark> 

- <mark>40, Activated - Configuration status when you press Done and the configuration is valid.</mark> 

- <mark>90, Canceled - Configuration is canceled; this status sets manually.</mark> 

<mark>115</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Product configurator 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0116-01.png)


### 12.5 Configuration model items 

<mark>You must set up an item as a model item. Unless this is done, key configuration functionality will not be available for the item as various programs and functions in the product configurator validate that the model item is set up as a configurable item.</mark> 

### 12.6 Configuration feature groups 

<mark>You can logically group features by feature groups. This is useful when you want to group related configuration questions; for example, when configuring the features for manufacturing a vehicle you might group questions relating to exterior, interior, motor, accessories, etc. Or when configuring the features of a bicycle, you may choose to group questions by main components, optional components, alternative components, and accessories. Each feature group is displayed as a tab in the Configurator, with its features on the rows.</mark> 

<mark>In the product configurator, some feature groups may be invisible to the user depending on the condition set in the formula. The feature group is set to be visible or not only when the condition is met; i.e. if the value returned is True, the feature group is hidden, else if the value returned is False, the feature group is visible.</mark> 

<mark>An example of when a feature group may be hidden could be when two people are responsible for the configuration of the product and the first step is performed by person 1 and additional steps by person 2. Then the second person who opens the same configuration can view additional information that person 1 does not have permission to view.</mark> 

### 12.7 Configuration features 

<mark>You can define all features that the order entry personnel must specify to transform requirements into an order specific product/resulting bill of material. Features characterize a product and each feature defines a question that is prompted during configuration or closed as a formula or matrix result.</mark> 

<mark>You can enter a unique user-friendly name (alphanumeric) for referring to the feature. This will make it simpler to refer to the feature when you create a formula or condition involving the feature. If quick reference is not defined or found, the feature number is used as normal.</mark> 

<mark>You can create features and link them to different feature types, user input options, and feature groups. Features can be linked to options or materials or used for user input of text, values, etc. Features can also be linked to several models (re-used), which means that a change in a feature will affect all the models to which it is linked.</mark> 

<mark>You can define various combinations of configuration features, for example:</mark> 

- <mark>Feature type Text and Answer type User input linked to Feature group</mark> 

- <mark>Feature type Text and Answer type Formula linked to Feature group</mark> 

- <mark>Feature type Option and Answer type User input linked to Feature group</mark> 

- <mark>Feature type Option and Answer type Formula linked to Feature group</mark> 

- <mark>Feature type Numeric and Answer type Formula linked to Feature group</mark> 

- <mark>Feature type Material and Answer type Default linked to Feature group</mark> 

#### 12.7.1 Value limits on numeric features 

<mark>When setting up a numeric feature, you can assign minimum, maximum, and factor values to the feature depending on the selections made for other features.</mark> 

<mark>You can do this by specifying values, or by creating formulas to define these values for a configuration feature or configuration model feature.</mark> 

<mark>These values are automatically updated when you use the tab key to exit an input cell while configuring an item in the Product configurator. If the value is not within the defined range, an appropriate error message is displayed.</mark> 

<mark>116</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Product configurator 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0117-01.png)


### 12.8 Configuration model features 

<mark>You can define Configuration model features with a set of standard options/materials in order to reuse questions and additional options/material that are model specific.</mark> 

<mark>You can select a feature to add to the list of model features linked to the model and specify if item significance key (ISK) values (explained in detail later in this chapter) are defined for the configuration model features linked to the model and if this model is the main model or a sub model. You can also add options/materials specific to a model feature. When configuring a model, a configuration option/material linked to a configuration model feature has a higher priority than one that is not linked to the configuration model feature.</mark> 

#### 12.8.1 Configuration model features matrix 

<mark>You can define applicable options and/or material for model features using the model features matrix. All options and material connected to the feature (as rows) and all models connected to the feature (as columns) are displayed in the form of a matrix.</mark> 

<mark>This is particularly useful when all options, for example colors, might not be applicable to all models. Likewise, all material might not be applicable to a certain model.</mark> 

<mark>Once you mark the valid options and material and save your choices, the results are saved in separate tables for options and for material.</mark> 

<mark>When configuring an item, only the valid options and material are presented if data exists in these tables for the model and feature.</mark> 

#### 12.8.2 Configuration model feature groups 

<mark>You can manage the Configuration feature group that a feature belongs to from the specified model. If it does not exist in the Configuration feature groups table, it is added.</mark> 

### 12.9 Configuration options 

<mark>Options for a product or feature must be defined separately and entered for each feature or product/feature or customer/product/feature combination. Valid options can be alphanumeric or discrete numeric.</mark> 

### 12.10 Configuration material 

<mark>You can select the quantity and/or material for a line in a BOM or routing using selection matrices. A selection matrix consists of a multidimensional selection area that handles up to nine dimensions. This can contain values for features and item numbers. A selection line can also be date controlled to manage selections that change over time.</mark> 

<mark>When doing the configuration, after you have answered all the questions, the model generates a unique bill of material and unique routing.</mark> 

- <mark>The bill of material consists of the items in the BOM structure and the quantities.</mark> 

- <mark>For the routing, the model generates the operations that should be included.</mark> 

<mark>For the unique BOM and routing that have been generated, it is possible to calculate the cost of the configured item.</mark> 

### 12.11 Configuration formulas 

<mark>A formula can be any SQL select statement or function to select or process the information in the database. For example, you can concatenate a string of item numbers, or retrieve values such as costs from a selection of items and total them, or control settings of application parameters. It is used to create a query that returns a value that can be used to answer a feature when configuring a model. A formula can be a standard formula that has been created manually or can be generated from a formula matrix.</mark> 

<mark>117</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Product configurator 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0118-01.png)


<mark>Formulas use standard T-SQL but with a simplified user input interface. Once you have entered a formula, you can validate the SQL syntax of the code.</mark> 

<mark>The following formula factor types are available in the product configurator:</mark> 

- <mark>Internal result, calculated in the formula and reused</mark> 

- <mark>Constant</mark> 

- <mark>Option from a feature or value from a feature</mark> 

- <mark>Retrieved value/basic data from the BOM, routing, or item master</mark> 

- <mark>Stored procedure</mark> 

<mark>Information about the resulting feature, answer and formula is stored for analysis purposes.</mark> 

#### 12.11.1 Formula matrix 

The formula matrix in the Configuration formulas program guides and assists you in creating and maintaining formulas. You can use the formula matrix to define formulas, even if you have no knowledge of how to write code. You can also configure advanced formula matrices to generate code for setting up advanced conditions. 

<mark>The configuration formula matrix provides valid combinations of features/dimensions that can be activated by setting the value of a cell to an arbitrary value or by specifying that the cell value should be parsed to another formula for further validation. The output is determined by a formula type that specifies if the output is numeric or alphanumeric.</mark> 

<mark>The following function types can be associated with a specific cell in the formula matrix:</mark> 

- <mark>Formula: Associates a value returned by a formula with the specified cell.</mark> 

- <mark>Feature: Associates the value from a feature with the specified cell.</mark> 

- <mark>Item: Associates an item with the specified cell.</mark> 

- <mark>Constant: Associates a constant value with the specified cell. If the formula is of type Numeric, the value in this cell should also be numeric</mark> 

<mark>The formula matrix guides the user to create formulas and formula output value for any given combination of feature answers and/or conditions or even with only conditions defined on columns and rows. It provides a visual overview of the results based on formulas. The output can be a constant, another formula, feature, configurator parameter, or even another formula matrix. This output can be used to update BOMs, operations, item rows, as input to another formula to answer a feature or change feature values depending on where you connect the formula. When defining a formula, you can also assign a default value that should be used when no other value is returned from the formula.</mark> 

<mark>This functionality opens huge possibilities to create and administer formulas. You can use it to assign different formulas that can return answers or embed logic in the formulas to consider different variables like customer category, region, etc. in combination with other variables like trading area or materials.</mark> 

### 12.12 Conditions 

<mark>You can set conditions on a feature or model feature by entering a valid SQL statement. A condition set on a model feature overrides a condition set on a feature when configuring an order specific item.</mark> 

<mark>You can also set a condition on an option or on material, if you want to choose a specific option or specific material although no condition is set on the feature, which means that the feature is always enabled.</mark> 

<mark>118</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Product configurator 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0119-01.png)


### 12.13 Comment code in formulas, conditions, etc. 

<mark>You can comment code in fields that contain formulas, conditions, drop-down list, and code that can be parsed as follows:</mark> 

- <mark>Comment row</mark> 

   - <mark>The -- mark indicates the start position for the comment on the row.</mark> 

- <mark>Comment text block</mark> 

   - <mark>The /* mark indicates the start position of the comment.</mark> 

   - <mark>The */ mark indicates the end position of the comment.</mark> 

<mark>Note that the commented rows/text blocks will not be considered when validating formulas.</mark> 

### 12.14 Configuration models 

<mark>You can view the configuration model features and feature groups associated with a model (item) and mark the model as valid. Jeeves ERP does a number of checks before marking the model as valid.</mark> 

<mark>You can also copy features and associated feature groups linked to a configuration model to another configuration model.</mark> 

<mark>A model can also be marked for quick configuration which means that an order specific item can be automatically configured based on the significance key values.</mark> 

<mark>You can create a hierarchy of components within a model item (model in model) by linking sub model items to a main model item. The hierarchy creates a link between the main model item, such as a car and its accessories such as radio, speaker, and GPS. In this case, a model item becomes a sub model item when you add it to a top model item in the product configurator. Similarly, you can also link sub model items with other sub model items such as a speaker within a radio.</mark> 

### 12.15 Configuration feature default 

<mark>You can define configuration feature default values and specify if default values should be retrieved and applied in the Product configurator/Product designer (in e-Sales) as follows:</mark> 

- <mark>0 = at closing when the configuration is being validated</mark> 

- <mark>1 = at opening when starting a new configuration.</mark> 

<mark>If no value is entered (NULL), the default value 0 will be used.</mark> 

### 12.16 Significance key values 

<mark>You can use the Configuration item significance key to identify features whose answers can be reused in multiple configurations.</mark> 

<mark>Example: When configuring, you may want to search among earlier configurations based on a set of base features, select one configuration and copy its answers to these base features. Then you can continue configuring the remaining features. For example, search among earlier configurations of children’s bikes and copy one with the relevant frame and wheel size.</mark> 

<mark>For a stocked (ISK_SKU) item that is defined with an item significance key (ISK), you can define that the latest information must be updated in the BOM/labor records when creating a manufacturing order. If you enable this function, the BOM, package items (sr) and operations (op) will be updated/recreated by using the CnfgrModelISK information and connected configuration, when the MO is created.</mark> 

<mark>As an item significance key (ISK) comprises a set of answers to the features linked to a model, it can be a relatively long alphanumeric value that may be difficult to remember. To make it easier to remember, you can create a user-friendly name for the ISK. A user-friendly name is a short alphanumeric value that can be used instead of the corresponding significance key when placing an order.</mark>

Jeeves ERP Product configurator 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0120-01.png)


### 12.17 SQL code syntax 

<mark>A validation button is provided in several programs to allow you to check the validity of the SQL code syntax entered by you.</mark> 

### 12.18 Configuration model standard answer 

<mark>You can set standard answers for a feature that are specific to an item (model) and customer. This will ensure that the model is configured as per the customer's requirements or as per the agreement with the customer.</mark> 

<mark>You can define answers per customer, model, and feature. If you then choose to use the standard answers during product configuration, the answers provided are displayed as the default answers for the feature(s) when configuring an order specific item for a specific customer.</mark> 

### 12.19 Product configuration, test 

<mark>You can view product configuration records for testing and trouble-shooting purposes.</mark> 

<mark>When setting the configuration of a product, the starting point should be to answer questions, either by:</mark> 

- <mark>Picking an item from a list: If an item is picked as an answer to a question, it will be included in the BOM. The same could also apply to operations.</mark> 

- <mark>Answering Yes or No: If a question is of the type Yes or No, the different alternatives will determine different things in the model. For example, the answer could determine whether a specific item or operation should be included in the model.</mark> 

- <mark>Answering by picking an option in a list. If the answer is an option, it could determine different things in the same way as for the Yes/No question. An option could, for example, determine a specific optional item in the BOM or optional operations in the routing.</mark> 

### 12.20 Product configurator screen 

<mark>You can create/update an order specific item based on the selected configuration model item in the Product configurator screen.</mark> 

<mark>The features connected to the model are displayed in an embedded tab grid, with one or more tabs and a grid with several rows. Each tab represents a feature group and the rows contain features linked to the model and grouped within the feature groups.</mark> 

<mark>Data is read from XML and loaded and presented in a tabular format within columns and cells.</mark> 

<mark>When setting the configuration of a configuration model, the starting point should be to answer questions, either by:</mark> 

- <mark>Picking an item of material from a list: If an item is picked as an answer to a question, it will be included in the BOM. The same could also apply to operations. Note that if a feature is answered by a formula, the answer is automatically updated on the order row. You can also automatically view an image of the item when the tab key is used to exit an input cell. To enable this functionality, associate the image with the configuration option or item and then specify the display parameters, path to the image file, and allow automatic display of the image during configuration.</mark> 

- <mark>Answering Yes or No: If a question is of the type Yes or No, the different alternatives will determine different things in the model. For example, the answer could determine whether a specific item or operation should be included in the model.</mark> 

- <mark>Answering by picking an option in a list. If the answer is an option, it could determine different things in the same way as for the Yes/No question. An option could, for example, determine a specific optional item in the BOM or optional operations in the routing. Alternatively, it could determine the color, quality or execution of an item of material. It could specify a numeric value from a list of predefined values.</mark>

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Product configurator 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0121-01.png)


#### 12.20.1 Copy matching configuration to current configuration 

<mark>You can search for configurations that match the current configuration and update the answers for the current configuration from an existing configuration.</mark> 

<mark>Search configuration - lists the configurations in the current company that have been made for a model that match the answers provided in case of a non-ISK model or match the ISK text value in case of an ISK model.</mark> 

<mark>Use configuration - copies the selected record to the current configuration. Answers are retrieved as defined in the item significance fields for an ISK model or from the answers provided for a nonISK model.</mark> 

#### 12.20.2 Create stocked item via Product configurator 

<mark>You can create a stocked item with a new Configuration unique ID in the delivering unit in an ICT environment.</mark> 

<mark>The new configuration unique ID is connected to the model in the Configuration user friendly name program. The BOM, package items and operations will be created for the new stocked item by using the information in the Configuration user friendly name program and connected configuration. This function works in an ICT/non-ICT environment.</mark> 

#### 12.20.3 Auto close 

<mark>If you have checked the Auto close option for any feature linked to the model, based on the answer selected for the feature which has this option checked, answers to the other features linked to the same model are displayed when you exit from the cell. Note that when only one value is valid, that value is used as the answer on the feature. The answers are displayed according to the following priority:</mark> 

- <mark>Default/AutoClose</mark> 

- <mark>Formula</mark> 

- <mark>User input</mark> 

#### 12.20.4 Validate configuration 

<mark>Once the configuration is ready for validation, you can validate if the configuration is complete, for example that it does not contain missing or conflicting answers.</mark> 

<mark>If a complete and valid model with connections to related features exists, configuration unique data is created in the Configuration unique BOM and Configuration unique labor work tables. In addition, BOM and routing as defined by the model are created.</mark> 

#### 12.20.5 Work tables 

<mark>You can view results in work tables before creating BOM and operation lists. These tables are updated with unique data for the complete product configuration structure/operations.</mark> 

<mark>After the configuration unique data for the complete BOM/operations are created, the selected values and columns are linked to the relevant tables for BOM per item, operations, and order rows to create order unique data and implement the BOM.</mark> 

<mark>You can view and change information relating to selected values/columns in the product configuration bill of material and to select values/columns in the configuration unique operations. You can also view and change information relating to models.</mark>

Jeeves ERP Product configurator

### 12.21 Configure package details 

<mark>You can configure package details as well as header information using the Jeeves ERP Product configurator. This provides more flexibility when configuring a product and can perform the following:</mark> 

- <mark>supports configurable package headers</mark> 

- <mark>supports configurable package details</mark> 

- <mark>does not activate the main model until the sub models are configured</mark> 

- <mark>when configuring a package where the main model and sub model contain the same features, the sub model inherits he feature value from the main model</mark> 

- <mark>changing an upper level feature value is reflected in the lower level value but changing the value at the lower level value does not affect the value at the upper level</mark> 

- <mark>updating a value at the lower level changes the status on the upper level</mark> 

- <mark>if a formula is used for a sub model and the feature referred to is not available, the value is retrieved from the upper level configuration</mark> 

- <mark>several levels are allowed below the main model</mark> 

### 12.22 Replace item on customer order row 

<mark>You can replace a configurable item on an order row with another configurable item or a nonconfigurable item by connecting a feature or a formula to the Item No. column in the Configuration order rows, items node that is accessed from the Configuration models program.</mark> 

<mark>For an item that is marked as configurable, from the order row you can click on the Configurator button and configure an item based on the selected product configuration model by answering questions that define your requirements for the finished product.</mark> 

<mark>After configuration, when you click the Done button,</mark> 

- <mark>If the feature linked to the Item No. column is answered by selecting a configurable item, this item replaces the model item in the configurator and the item on the order row.</mark> 

- <mark>If the feature linked to the Item No. column is answered by selecting a non-configurable item, this item replaces the item in the order row. However the model item in the configurator is retained as in the source item.</mark> 

- <mark>If the feature linked to the Item No. column is answered by selecting the same item as in the configuration header, a loop error is displayed as an existing item cannot be replaced with itself.</mark> 

- <mark>If the configurable item is replaced with another configurable item, the system will create order specific information using the replacement item.</mark> 

- <mark>If the configurable item is replaced with a standard item and the formula called in the Use model definition field returns a value of “1”, the system will create order specific information using the original configurable item (i.e. the model BOM and labor definition will be used).</mark> 

- <mark>Additionally, in an ICT environment, a configuration record with the same configuration unique ID as in the selling unit is created in the delivering unit.</mark> 

### 12.23 ICT business unit 

<mark>Any configurable item can be used as a configuration model in the product configurator. In an ICT environment, the configuration model is defined in both the delivering and selling units while the configuration is done in the selling unit. You can create a stocked item with a new Configuration unique ID in the delivering unit in an ICT environment. The configuration unique ID is connected to the model in the Configuration user friendly name program. The BOM, package items and operations records will be created for the new stocked item in the ICT/non-ICT environment as required.</mark> 

<mark>In an ICT environment, you can specify if order details such as calculated cost price or manufacturing lead time should be retrieved from the delivering unit when you create a customer</mark>
