Jeeves ERP Product configurator 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0123-01.png)


<mark>sales order in the selling unit and configure the item. The Use factory data function allows you to retrieve factory data regarding the product that is currently under configuration to update the order details on the order rows. So, when you configure the order specific item, the value from the specified field in the Delivering unit will be updated in the corresponding field on the order row in the Selling unit. You can view this information and make a decision on whether or not to proceed with creating the purchase/manufacturing order for the item.</mark> 

### 12.24 Product designer in e-Sales 

<mark>Support for designing items to order using the features and functions of product configuration is available in e-Sales. For more information on the Product designer, please refer to the chapter on</mark> _<mark>Jeeves e-Applications.</mark>_ 

#### 12.24.1 Interaction artefacts 

##### 12.24.1.1 Configuration feature web display type 

<mark>You can define how configuration feature selections should be displayed in the Product designer in e-Sales. The following display types are supported:</mark> 

- <mark>Slider: For discrete numerical features, you can choose to represent feature value selections with a slider. The slider can be adjusted based on the minimum and maximum values, factor, and the number of decimal places the feature may have.</mark> 

- <mark>Radio buttons: For options and materials, in addition to the drop-down list, you can choose to have each option or material represented in a radio button list from which the user can select their preference. If none of the options or materials are valid, the user can select None to indicate no selection. Radio buttons for options/materials that are not available or invalid are grayed out.</mark> 

- <mark>Card grid: Alternatively, you can choose to have each option or material presented as cards within a grid. Each card will have an image and description of the option or material. When a card is selected, the card frame will be highlighted and a tick mark will appear on the top right of the card. You can have from 1 to a maximum of 12 columns in the configuration feature card grid. Options or materials that cannot be selected with the current configuration will be hidden from the grid.</mark> 

<mark>You can also specify different ways features are presented in e-Sales. Apart from the web display type, you can also specify the preferred grid size and the web image URL as required.</mark> 

##### 12.24.1.2 Configuration information type 

<mark>You can define different information types to indicate the status of the current configuration in e- Sales. Currently, information type is used to group information for presentation in the Product designer in e-Sales. This includes displaying actions that are required to be taken or other information relating to the configuration, such as if the configuration has an error or conflict.</mark> 

<mark>The status of a feature is indicated by an icon displayed to the right of the answer. If the icon is absent, it means that the feature is not mandatory or that it contains a default value.</mark> 

### 12.25 Tab grid control and macro objects 

<mark>The tab grid control, which can be described as an embedded list with tabs, is available for the product configurator It is used to present data in the form of a tab grid when configuring an order specific item. Macro objects and related methods allow you to build up the tab grid control.</mark>

### 12.26 Pricing models 

<mark>Jeeves ERP has a rule based function to calculate the price of an existing or a non-existing item. A non-existing item can for example be a configurable item that is defined when placing a customer order or a quotation.</mark> 

<mark>You can create any number of pricing models, built up of rule types and rules, to calculate or simulate prices when configuring items on customer orders. You must connect the pricing model to a configuration model before configuring the item and validating the price definition and simulating the price list calculation.</mark> 

<mark>Calculation is based on a sequence of calculations with parameters and values that are defined and exist at the time when the price needs to be accessed.</mark> 

<mark>For non-configurable items and when a pricing model is connected to calculation of item list price in the Items program, the rule type Feature is not available since it is not related and connected to a configuration. Also, formulas referring to Feature are not valid.</mark> 

<mark>However, all rules types are considered when you open the Pricing model (prmh) program directly from the menu and configure the model.</mark> 

<mark>Note that the first version of the pricing model functionality has only been tested together with the configurator functionality.</mark> 

#### 12.26.1 Pricing model rule types 

<mark>You can use any of the following for building pricing models:</mark> 

- <mark>10, Base price - Retrieve calculated cost price from item or model</mark> 

- <mark>20, Markup - If the Related sequence exists, the calculation of the markup value will be performed by using the value on the referenced row, Operand, and Value. If the Related sequence does not exist, the value returned from the Configuration formula or Configuration feature will be used instead.</mark> 

- <mark>30, Feature - Retrieve value from the feature (FeatureID).</mark> 

- <mark>40, Formula - Retrieve the value from the Configuration formula by executing the formula.</mark> 

- <mark>50, Subtotal - Summarize all values up to the nearest subtotal rule type row for pricing models where the Sequence No. is less than the current subtotal row. Summarize all values from all rule type rows between the previous subtotal rule type row and the current row, excluding any Accumulated rule type rows.</mark> 

- <mark>60, Accumulated - Summarize the values from all of the previous rule type rows except the subtotal rule type rows.</mark> 

- <mark>70, Discount - If a reference to the Related sequence exists, the calculation of the discount value will be performed by using the value on the referenced row, Operand, and Value. If a reference to the Related sequence does not exists, the value returned from the Configuration formula or Configuration feature will be used instead.</mark> 

- <mark>80, All material - Summarize the price for all material at the top level in Order specific material/order row. Use the normal price calculation functionality.</mark> 

- <mark>90, Total price - Summarize the value from all previous rule type rows to the current row excluding the values in the Subtotal and Accumulated rows. Note that records after this record will not be considered as Active and hence, will not be calculated.</mark> 

#### 12.26.2 Quick costing 

<mark>The product configurator offers a quick way to calculate the cost for a configured, order specific manufacturing order when running the configurator, including when using ICT. No order specific order rows/purchase order rows, manufacturing order rows/operations are created; this is just simulated in order to quickly calculate the cost. The cost is given when you conclude the configuration by pressing Done, if you have connected a formula for the quick costing to the model.</mark>

#### 12.26.3 Simulate price list calculation 

<mark>You can validate the pricing definition and calculate the price of the item based on the currently defined pricing model.</mark> 

<mark>To simulate or test a price calculation for a Configuration item, an Activated configuration must exist and be connected to the current pricing model. By using the configuration and the defined pricing model, you can perform a simulation. You can also view the results of the pricing simulation.</mark>

## 13 Jeeves ERP Service 

<mark>Jeeves Service includes routines for entering service agreements, entering and maintaining items (material) and service items, service order processing, interruption management and reporting with subsequent invoicing. Invoicing is performed either via the Time, projects, assignments module or via the Sales module’s order routine.</mark> 

<mark>There are several different methods for entering a service order and its associated rows. This is either done manually row by row or you use the service flows, for example, to automatically generate predefined rows.</mark> 

<mark>Prices can be managed both from the order routine and the assignment routine with price lists, sector types and time types, and via the service routine where prices and discounts can be set for different service items and types.</mark> 

<mark>You can plan and report service activities. You can also handle items, which in Jeeves can be normal items, serial number items, where required, or service items. These alternatives allow different service models with different characteristics.</mark> 

<mark>If for any reason the service work has to be interrupted, interruption management can be used to keep track of where the object is during the interruption and the interruption's status.</mark> 

### 13.1 Items 

#### 13.1.1 Serial number items 

<mark>A serial number item is identified by a combination of item number and serial number. These combinations are stored in a dedicated table.</mark> 

<mark>You use the function if the company carries out service activities on stocked serial number items as part of the service routine. The purpose is to quickly identify where an individual item is, both physically and in the service flow, to which customer it has been sold, whether it is still under guarantee, etc. In this case, serial number items are usually entered in the Serial number items table when receiving deliveries against purchase orders.</mark> 

<mark>If the company carries out service on non-stocked items, you define them in the Service items program instead, and specify serial numbers directly on service orders.</mark> 

#### 13.1.2 Service items 

<mark>Service items are primarily intended to be used when the company carries out service activities on non-stocked items such as, for example, reparation of mobile phones, cars and the like. The service item is linked to the item table, which means that it must be entered in the item table first.</mark> 

### 13.2 Grouping/classification 

#### 13.2.1 Service orders 

<mark>You can have any number of management areas (service manager/office/department or equivalent), service report types, service codes (in 3 layers) and priority codes for own classification and searching.</mark> 

#### 13.2.2 Error, interruption and action codes 

<mark>You can have any number of own error, interruption and action classifications and search criteria. You can also use coding according to IRIS.</mark> 

<mark>126</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Service 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0127-01.png)


### 13.3 Price and discount management 

<mark>Prices can be managed both from the order routine and the assignment routine with price lists, sector types and time types, and via the service routine where prices and discounts can be set for different service items and reparation types.</mark> 

#### 13.3.1 Price lists 

<mark>You can set up price lists for material that has been entered in the item table or services (labor) per personnel category, time type and, if so desired, service technician. You can also set a default price list on customers to be proposed when creating new assignments.</mark> 

#### 13.3.2 Price per service item 

<mark>The pricing of service labor can be controlled by service items:</mark> 

- <mark>You can link different types of reparation to each service item and specify a price for every reparation type. At service order row entry, you enter a reparation type and the price is then retrieved from the service item.</mark> 

- <mark>You can also link several service items to a service agreement and there specify a discount rate per reparation type.</mark> 

#### 13.3.3 Discount agreements 

<mark>If you have entered service items, you can also enter a discount agreement for one or more service items and link one or more customers to the agreement. Discount agreements are built up per service item and reparation type. The discount rate is retrieved to service orders for customers who are linked to the agreement depending on the specified repair type.</mark> 

#### 13.3.4 How the price is retrieved on the service order row 

<mark>How the price is retrieved to the service order row depends on the type of row being entered, as described below. If no price is found, or if the suggested price is not applicable, you can enter a price manually. The price retrieved depends on what you specify in the following fields:</mark> 

<mark>If the row refers to an item that is not a service item:</mark> 

- <mark>Price list</mark> 

- <mark>Quotation/Agreement, if a price list has been specified on the service agreement</mark> 

- <mark>Company number, if a price list has been specified for the customer</mark> 

<mark>You must enter a quantity.</mark> 

<mark>If no price is found as above, the price is retrieved from the item's list price.</mark> 

<mark>If the row refers to a service item:</mark> 

- <mark>Reparation type, if a price has been entered per service item and reparation type</mark> 

- <mark>Quotation/Agreement, if the service item is included in a discount agreement under this agreement</mark> 

- <mark>Company number, if the customer is linked to the agreement (i.e. is included in the righthand embedded window in the agreement)</mark> 

<mark>You must enter a quantity.</mark> 

<mark>If no price is found according to the above, the price is retrieved from the service item's list price in the item table.</mark> 

<mark>127</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Service 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0128-01.png)


<mark>If the row refers to a service and invoicing is done via the assignment routine:</mark> 

- <mark>Technician reporting the work, if pricing on the assignment is done per person</mark> 

- <mark>Sector, if pricing on the assignment is done per sector</mark> 

- <mark>Personnel category, if pricing on the assignment is done per category</mark> 

- <mark>Time type (price lists for services are entered for combinations of time types and persons or sectors)</mark> 

<mark>You must enter the number of hours.</mark> 

### 13.4 Service order processing 

#### 13.4.1 Parameter-controlled work flow 

<mark>The work flow for service order processing is parameter-controlled, for example:</mark> 

- <mark>Service orders can be automatically closed when reporting the last activity</mark> 

- <mark>Partial invoicing can be allowed or when closed</mark> 

#### 13.4.2 Priority for retrieval of inventory location on service order rows 

<mark>The following priority has been set to retrieve the inventory location on the service order programs and display it on the service order rows:</mark> 

- <mark>1) Inventory location from the Service technicians.</mark> 

- <mark>2) Inventory location from the Service Managers connected to the service technician.</mark> 

- <mark>3) Inventory location from the Service Managers at service order.</mark> 

- <mark>4) Inventory location from System data, own company.</mark> 

#### 13.4.3 Service order entry 

<mark>There are several ways in which you can enter service orders; with or without pre-defined templates, so-called service flows, with or without a bill of material as a master, or manually or automatically from serial number items.</mark> 

<mark>You can have any number of Service order types for defining number series. You can also have different order types but with the same number series, in case you need to change the service order type after entry.</mark> 

#### 13.4.4 Master service order 

<mark>You can create related service orders from a master service order in order to handle them as a batch. For example, you can use the Master service order number to close a whole batch of related service orders.</mark> 

<mark>When a service order is connected to a master service order, the value in the Master order No. field is transferred to the corresponding field in the service order records header and row. Also, when you enter a service order in the embedded list, select fields inherit values from the service order header.</mark> 

#### 13.4.5 Service order copy 

<mark>You can create a service order from a selected service order or you can create a corresponding credit note if you want to reverse quantity, hours, or time accrued on a service order.</mark> 

<mark>When a new service order is created, it has the status Entered in the Service order rows. The Order number, Invoice number, and Preliminary invoice number are not copied into the new record. If you create the service order from the Estimator, the status is set to Quotation. You can also manually set the status to Quotation while entering the service order.</mark> 

<mark>128</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Service 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0129-01.png)


<mark>Once you confirm the creation of a service order/credit service order, Jeeves ERP runs a credit control check of the aggregated sales order and service order value. If the combined value exceeds the credit limit set for the customer/payer, appropriate messages will be displayed showing the amount by which the credit limit is exceeded in the current order and in total.</mark> 

<mark>Note that Service order rows that have a status of Canceled are not copied to the new record. If all rows are canceled, only the Service order header is copied. However, any Service order rows with a different status are copied when the Service order has a status of canceled on the header.</mark> 

#### 13.4.6 Generate planned activities from template on service order 

<mark>You can automatically generate planned activities on projects from service orders (incident reports, support requests, development suggestions etc.). This requires that you enter a project ID on the service orders in question. The activities are generated using pre-defined activity templates. The Activity templates node is available from the service order program. The automatically generated planned activities are assigned the connected service order number.</mark> 

<mark>You can accumulate planned and reported time on service activities and display the information on service order headers.</mark> 

#### 13.4.7 Create manufacturing orders from service orders 

<mark>You can create requisition(s), purchase order(s), or manufacturing orders from the current service order.</mark> 

<mark>The rows on the service order where the Order checkbox is checked will result in a requisition or a purchase order. Alternatively, a manufacturing order is created for items that have acquisition method Manufacture to requirement.</mark> 

<mark>The service order row that has created a purchase order/manufacturing order is flagged with the purchase order/manufacturing order number, so you can jump directly to the purchase order/manufacturing order by right-clicking the field that contains the purchase order/manufacturing order number.</mark> 

<mark>You can also create requisitions for manufacturing orders from service orders.</mark> 

<mark>If the service order contains material that needs to be ordered from a supplier, you can create a purchase order from the service order by pressing a button. The requirements for this to work are 1) that the service order has status: Planned or Ongoing and 2) that the rows have the field: Ordered checked and a supplier specified.</mark> 

#### 13.4.8 Planning service order rows via iterations 

<mark>You can define iterations to make it quicker and easier to schedule activities. Only active iterations are displayed in the list on planned activities and service order rows. If you select an iteration, the start and end date on the activity or service order row will be updated based on the start/end date of the iteration, but can be changed. The changed dates must, however, lie within the iteration’s date interval, otherwise you will have to remove the selected iteration.</mark> 

#### 13.4.9 Serial number items 

<mark>When entering service orders you can specify an item’s serial number. The sale and delivery of the item, as well as any other activity, are possible to trace. For example, if service or reparation is to be performed on a serial number item in the service module, the service planner automatically gets information about the customer to whom the item was sold, as well as connections to the sales order and inventory transaction.</mark> 

<mark>If only one serial number item is to be handled on the service order you enter the serial number on the service order header. If it is to handle multiple serial number items there is a special table, accessed from the service order rows. Here you can also enter a service order specific item description, if desired. There is a window in the reports that you can make visible in order to print information about the multiple serial number items.</mark> 

<mark>129</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Service 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0130-01.png)


#### 13.4.10 Credit limit check 

<mark>Credit limit checks can be performed for customers and/or payers. Aggregated sales order and service order value will be included in the credit check. If the combined value exceeds the credit limit set for the customer, an appropriate message will be displayed.</mark> 

<mark>In addition, you can recreate the total service order value for the customer. This function is useful if you intend to include service orders retroactively in the customer credit limit check. When recreating the service order values, the system will exclude service order rows with the billing code set to No invoicing and service order rows with status Cancelled.</mark> 

<mark>For information of working with credit limits in the order routine, see the Credit limit check section in the Jeeves Sales chapter.</mark> 

#### 13.4.11 Addresses 

<mark>The delivery address of the serial number item is entered from the customer order when delivered. If the serial number item is installed at another address, information can manually be entered in the installation address fields for the serial number item. In this case, the installation address is transferred to the delivery address fields in the service order header when entering a service order with serial number item. Otherwise, the delivery address is transferred.</mark> 

<mark>You can also specify additional information regarding a delivery address, for example a building, a project site, GPS coordinates, or other references.</mark> 

#### 13.4.12 Guarantee 

<mark>There are two fields on the service order header referring to a guarantee end date:</mark> 

- <mark>The Service guarantee field only refers to service items and shows a calculated guarantee end date based on the different guarantee times that can be specified for the service item. If the end date has not been passed, the Internal guarantee field is checked to indicate that the work should be done under guarantee.</mark> 

- <mark>The Individual guarantee field only refers to serial No. items. When a serial No. item is entered on a service order, a guarantee date according to information in the Serial No. items table is displayed on the service order header.</mark> 

<mark>This is to indicate that you must decide in each case whether it should be assigned a debit code for guarantee jobs that does not initiate invoicing.</mark> 

#### 13.4.13 Service options 

<mark>You can manage various options or executions/models of a service item that may affect the reparation price and/or discount.</mark> 

<mark>The function is based on the concept of selling and/or offering service on items that have various options. The items are entered as stocked package items and the options are entered as nonstocked package details/components. The items (package items) must also be entered as service items with data about price changes per option.</mark> 

<mark>When dispatching (suitably in Order processing, packages), the serial number is entered on the package item and the relevant option(s) are chosen. The dispatch creates a serial No. item for the service item (package item) as usual, but in this case, options are also created in the underlying program node called Service options, service items.</mark> 

<mark>There is also the possibility of processing options for items that you do not sell. In manual entry of serial No. items, there is a function button to create options according to the package item’s structure and thereafter manually delete non-relevant options.</mark> 

<mark>When entering service orders on service items with options, the price is calculated based on the price of the service item row and the price changes of the option(s) (+/-).</mark> 

<mark>130</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Service 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0131-01.png)


#### 13.4.14 Service labels 

<mark>There is a program for printing service labels with a bar code. The program prints the number of labels specified on the service order header. This function is used, for example, when an item is received in several parts.</mark> 

#### 13.4.15 Aggregated values 

<mark>You can view the following aggregated values for service orders in the list window:</mark> 

- <mark>Actual accrued time for the event(s).</mark> 

- <mark>Total time on a service report.</mark> 

- <mark>Total order value in base currency at the time of entry. Order rows and equivalent are subtracted from this total order value when the row is invoiced. This information forms the basis for order value discount. On customer orders, it helps in automatic calculation of the handling charges.</mark> 

- <mark>Total order value in currency at the time of entry. Order rows and equivalent are subtracted from this total order value when the row is invoiced. The order value discount is based on this information. On customer orders, this value forms the basis for automatic calculation of handling charges.</mark> 

- <mark>Total calculated cost value of the customer or service order, or row, expressed in base currency.</mark> 

- <mark>Total calculated cost value of the customer or service order, or row, expressed in the chosen currency.</mark> 

<mark>You can also view aggregated service order values incl VAT/tax in base currency and currency in the service order header and row.</mark> 

#### 13.4.16 Checklists 

<mark>Checklists are available for service technicians to maintain complex service objects and to ease the performance and reporting of service activities. Checklists consist of multiple rows called checkpoints linked to service order rows and are populated from a template. Each service order row can have a linked checklist with many checkpoints.</mark> 

### 13.5 Jeeves Graphical planning 

<mark>You can create logic for the Calendar field if you use Jeeves Graphical planning connected to Service. There must then be a calendar for every service technician.</mark> 

### 13.6 Reporting 

<mark>You can plan and report service activities. You can also process service objects which in Jeeves can be normal items, serial number items, where required, or service items. These alternatives allow different service models with different characteristics.</mark> 

<mark>On a service order, you can enter and report time without a link to any product. You can enter and report service for non-stocked products, which is normally done using service items. You can enter material from your own inventory/stores, or purchased from a supplier, that is used during service.</mark> 

#### 13.6.1 Reporting service labor and material 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP you choose between the following alternatives when reporting service:</mark> 

- <mark>"free" reporting</mark> 

- <mark>against a default service flow</mark> 

- <mark>against planned activities</mark> 

<mark>Your work method determines which program you use.</mark> 

<mark>131</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Service 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0132-01.png)


<mark>You can accumulate planned and reported time on service activities and display the information on service order headers.</mark> 

#### 13.6.2 Jeeves Mobile field service routine 

<mark>A standard mobile routine for individuals in manufacturing to report operations.</mark> 

<mark>In the Field service routine, own service orders are searched and the service technician’s list of service orders are displayed. The user can of course customize and add to the search conditions.</mark> 

#### 13.6.3 Inventory transactions 

<mark>There is a possibility to select, per debit code, which inventory transaction type is to be used for material issues.</mark> 

#### 13.6.4 Account segments on service order rows 

<mark>You can control account segments on inventory transactions from service order rows. If no account number is entered on the service order row the account is retrieved from the inventory posting template, on condition that an account segment has also been entered (i.e. cost center, cost unit, segment 4-7). If an account segment is entered on the service order row this overrides any account segment entered on the template.</mark> 

#### 13.6.5 Spare parts management 

<mark>There is an underlying program for spare parts entry, which is by default largely identical to the service order row form. The purpose of the node is to provide the option to create a separate form for entry of materials/spare parts that contain only the relevant fields.</mark> 

#### 13.6.6 Interruption management 

<mark>If for any reason the service work has to be interrupted, interruption management can be used to keep track of where the object is during the interruption and the interruption's status.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves service module contains functions for reporting interruptions in service work with information about the type of interruption, interruption status, the possibility to link item numbers and also error descriptions. An interruption report can be printed and sent to the customer.</mark> 

<mark>If the above information is not necessary and you only want to be able to signal that an interruption has occurred, you can instead enter a service order status called Interruption and simply change the status for the service order. The same applies when a cost proposal is first produced; if you do not want to use interruption management, you can simply check the Cost proposal box on the service order.</mark> 

### 13.7 Invoicing 

<mark>Service work that has been carried out and material that has been used can be invoiced via the order routine in the Sales module or via the assignment routine in the Time, Projects, Assignments module.</mark> 

#### 13.7.1 Invoice via order routine 

<mark>When you close and save a service order row which has an item number and quantity specified, and the Create order box is checked, a customer order with a new order number is created automatically. You invoice the order according to the normal order-invoicing routine.</mark> 

<mark>132</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Service 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0133-01.png)


#### 13.7.2 Invoice via assignments routine 

<mark>An assignment must be entered on the closed service order row from which the invoicing is done. If the row refers to time, a service technician, a sector and a time type must be specified. If the row refers to material, an item number must be specified. You create a preliminary assignment invoice or a preliminary service invoice and invoice according to the normal assignments routine.</mark> 

#### 13.7.3 Collective invoicing 

<mark>There is a parameter to determine if collective invoices should be created per service order number in the Invoicing, collective invoice program. If the parameter is set there will be one collective invoice per service order. If the parameter is not set different service orders can be included in the same collective invoice.</mark> 

#### 13.7.4 Payer on service orders 

<mark>You can specify a different company as payer on service orders in the same way as, for example, in the Sales module. At present this can only be used if you invoice service orders via the order routine.</mark> 

#### 13.7.5 Meter readings 

<mark>There is a function that can be used to automatically calculate and charge the difference between the previous and newly entered meter reading. The meter reading and date of the last reading for the serial No. item are updated automatically when you close a service order row with a meter reading specified. The difference is entered in the field for the quantity on the service row. But note that currently debit code 5 must be used on the service order row for updating to be performed.</mark> 

#### 13.7.6 Integration with ledger and general ledger 

<mark>The invoices are automatically entered in the accounts receivable ledger and posted according to the normal order or assignments routine. For further information about this, and about follow-up reports and statistics, please refer to the overviews of these modules.</mark> 

### 13.8 Other functions 

#### 13.8.1 Logging service orders 

<mark>Every status change of a service order can be logged in a log file in the service routine. The status code determines which changes are logged.</mark> 

### 13.9 Localizations/internationalization 

<mark>Most functions in Jeeves Service are common to all countries. There is, however, one function specific to Sweden. It is the possibility to integrate with the Kontek payroll system. This is described in the chapter on</mark> _<mark>Jeeves Time, projects, assignments</mark>_ <mark>.</mark>

## 14 Jeeves ERP Time, projects, assignments 

<mark>Jeeves Time, projects and assignments module includes routines for planning and managing customer assignments and projects, including planning and assigning resources, reporting and invoicing time and expenses, closing activities and follow-up. There are also functions for managing price lists regarding professional services and expenses, as well as costs for internal debiting.</mark> 

<mark>You can choose between fixed price and open account invoicing. You can invoice open account projects per assignment or per project at any time. Alternatively, you can set up open account invoicing per assignment to be done monthly. Invoices are automatically created in the accounts receivable when you invoice. The invoices are automatically posted and integrated with the general ledger when invoice journals and vouchers are created. There are also functions for on account invoicing and for manually entering advance invoices.</mark> 

<mark>All project documentation can be stored connected to the specific project through Jeeves EDM.</mark> 

### 14.1 Project management 

<mark>Jeeves ERP has a built-in project hierarchy that facilitates project management. On the top level you have an assignment with one or more master projects and/or projects connected to it. You can use the master project functionality to create a project hierarchy in any number of levels. Each project can have any number of planned activities connected to it. This structure allows you to gather all business agreements on assignment level and all planning and execution on project level. The project must be connected to an assignment in order to be able to invoice time and expenses related to the project.</mark> 

#### 14.1.1 Assignments 

<mark>The assignment contains information about the business agreement with the customer. It is on the agreement you specify the customer, price lists for service and expenses, pricing method, invoicing type, currency, payment plan if applicable, VAT/tax code etc.</mark> 

<mark>You must also set an agreement type for the assignment; there are four different types:</mark> 

- <mark>Open account – used for projects on which reported time and expenses are invoiced recurrently</mark> 

- <mark>Periodic – used for projects that you want to invoice for example every month, quarter or year.</mark> 

- <mark>Fixed price – used for invoicing fixed amounts on specific dates.</mark> 

- <mark>No invoicing – used for projects that are not to be invoiced, for example internal projects.</mark> 

<mark>The agreement type is inherited from the assignment to the connected projects, but can be changed. This implies that, even if an assignment is to be invoiced, you can set No invoicing on an individual project, and vice versa.</mark> 

<mark>The assignment status controls whether time, expenses and material can be reported and invoiced; only assignments with status Active can be invoiced.</mark> 

<mark>You use the invoicing type to control the level of detail on invoice rows as well as postings, for example per person, per category, per category and person, per activity, total time and expenses etc.</mark> 

<mark>There are numerous other parameters and defaults you can set on the assignment that control reporting, pricing and invoicing.</mark> 

#### 14.1.2 Projects 

<mark>All planning, reporting and follow up is done on the project. The project also contains information about how to integrate the project with the general ledger. The project must be connected to an assignment in order to be able to invoice time and expenses.</mark> 

<mark>You can connect project members to your projects, if required, by entering user signatures in the Project</mark> <u><mark>personnel node.</mark></u> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

<mark>134</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Time, projects, assignments 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0135-01.png)


#### 14.1.3 Master projects 

<mark>You can use the master project functionality to create a project hierarchy in any number of levels. A project hierarchy can have several master project and sub project levels.</mark> 

<mark>You can see all projects connected to the master project in the Consists of projects node in the Projects program. You can also make a setting in the list of this node in order to graphically display your project hierarchy in a tree structure.</mark> 

<mark>The following data is aggregated to the master project from underlying master projects and sub projects:</mark> 

- <mark>total estimated time</mark> 

- <mark>total accrued time</mark> 

- <mark>total remaining time</mark> 

- <mark>total accrued project cost</mark> 

- <mark>total theoretically remaining time</mark> 

<mark>The master project shows these values from all connected sub projects, plus planned and reported time on the master project itself.</mark> 

<mark>There is also an embedded list where you can view all planned activities on all connected projects. Note, however, that this only shows activities from the master project and one underlying level, but you can drill down to view activities on up to four levels.</mark> 

#### 14.1.4 Project customers/clients 

<mark>You can specify a project customer on projects, if desired. The drop-down list only displays users in the Accounting and time report users table with the Valid to use box checked.</mark> 

#### 14.1.5 Resources 

<mark>You can set up applicable resource types for example project manager, developer, tester etc. A default resource type can be entered on users, which is suggested as default when entering planned activities for the user, but can be changed.</mark> 

### 14.2 Project planning 

#### 14.2.1 Planned activities 

<mark>You can have an unlimited number of planned activities on each project. Each activity can have planned start and end dates. If the project is planned the activity’s planned start and end dates will be checked that they lie within the project's corresponding planned start and end dates. You can view planned, reported and remaining time on activities. On projects there are embedded lists to view all planned activities regardless of the activity status, or only current activities.</mark> 

<mark>The project manager always has an up-to-date view of all active activities directly in the project form. The project member always has control over his/her activities through a separate to-do list. Once an activity is finished, you check the Activity finished box. The activity is then removed from the to-do list.</mark> 

<mark>There are different ways you can enter planned activities on projects. You can manually enter planned activities either via the embedded list window or the node for planned activities. You can also automatically create planned activities based on the resource personnel category.</mark> 

#### 14.2.2 Activity templates 

<mark>Alternatively, you can generate planned activities from a template. You define your templates in the Activity templates program. Then you connect the relevant templates to your projects and automatically generate planned activities from them.</mark> 

<mark>135</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Time, projects, assignments 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0136-01.png)


#### 14.2.3 Main activities and sub activities 

<mark>You can use the main activity functionality to create activity hierarchies in any number of levels, for example a Test activity might consist of Test planning, Test execution and Test reporting. Note that you cannot plan or report time on, or assign resources, to main activities. Main activities are automatically assigned to the default user defined in the Accounting and time reporting users program. Time is automatically accumulated from planned and reported time on sub activities. Main activities show both planned, allocated time and remaining allocated time, i.e. allocated time not yet planned on resources.</mark> 

#### 14.2.4 Assign resources to planned activities 

<mark>Alternatively, you can use the function for assigning resources to planned activities, which automatically generates planned activities on the project for the resources. You will find the node for assigning resources via an underlying program node to Planned activities. If you assign several resources to the same activity, these will be connected to the first as sub activities in order to collect all time planned and reported on the same activity. The overlying activity will automatically be flagged as a main activity. New resources will automatically be added to the project personnel. You can then add information regarding dates and times to the automatically created project activities.</mark> 

#### 14.2.5 Iterations 

<mark>You can define and maintain iterations, if required. Iteration date intervals can be used to automatically set start and end dates on planned activities. Iterations are defined manually according to own requirements.</mark> 

#### 14.2.6 Milestones 

<mark>You can set planned activities as milestones. Only one planned activity with the same milestone ID can have this box checked. If you, for any reason, change the milestone ID of an activity defined as a milestone, Jeeves ERP warns you and gives you the possibility to change the milestone ID on all linked planned activities. If you use activity hierarchies the milestone ID is inherited from the main activity to the sub activities when resources are allocated, but can be changed.</mark> 

<mark>When you close a planned activity defined as a milestone, Jeeves ERP warns if there are any unclosed, planned activities linked to the milestone and asks if you want to close them.</mark> 

#### 14.2.7 Requirement IDs 

<mark>Requirement ID can be set on planned and reported activities, in order to be able to keep track of their related requirements. The requirement ID is copied to the reported activity when reporting time against the planned activity. Otherwise the field has no logic and the value does not need to be unique; several planned activities can have the same requirement ID. You can change a requirement ID on a planned activity, but note that any existing reported activities will not be updated with the changed ID.</mark> 

#### 14.2.8 Graphical planning 

<mark>There is a possibility to integrate with Jeeves Graphical planning where you can plan resources and activities graphically. Jeeves Graphical planning offers an entirely different dimension of resource planning on projects.</mark> 

#### 14.2.9 Document management 

<mark>All project documentation can be stored connected to the specific project through Jeeves EDM.</mark> 

<mark>136</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Time, projects, assignments 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0137-01.png)


#### 14.2.10 Budget 

<mark>You budget projects in the same way and in the same program as budgeting for other account segments in the general ledger. For further information, please refer to the chapter about</mark> _<mark>General ledger</mark>_ <mark>.</mark> 

### 14.3 Prices and discounts 

#### 14.3.1 Pricing of services and expenses 

<mark>You can set up price lists for both services and expenses.</mark> 

<mark>The services price list is used when reporting activities. Each services price list consists of three sections, price per</mark> 

- <mark>Person</mark> 

- <mark>Service</mark> 

- <mark>Personnel category</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP automatically retrieves the correct price depending on the pricing method declared on the assignment. In each section you can set up different prices for different time types (such as normal/overtime etc.). Exceptions from the price list can be specified in an assignment specific price list.</mark> 

<mark>The expenses price list is used when reporting expenses. This price list is the same as for sales orders.</mark> 

<mark>Both services price lists and expenses price lists are specified on the assignment, together with the pricing method.</mark> 

<mark>You can also apply general customer discounts on expenses.</mark> 

### 14.4 Project execution 

<mark>You can report time on planned activities but also directly on a project or an assignment with or without a planned activity. All reported time is aggregated on the project. You can use status reporting to report progress of an activity at different stages of the project. The statuses are userdefined.</mark> 

<mark>In the project header you have an overview of the project's progress with access to:</mark> 

- <mark>planned time</mark> 

- <mark>reported time</mark> 

- <mark>remaining time</mark> 

- <mark>accrued project costs</mark> 

- <mark>theoretic remaining time (planned time in hours minus reported time in hours)</mark> 

<mark>On master project level you have corresponding total fields for the entire project hierarchy.</mark> 

<mark>There is also a possibility for personnel to report time per week number. All reported time, irrespective of where it is reported, ends up in the weekly time report.</mark> 

<mark>You can report expenses directly on a project or an assignment. The expense report is connected to a specific signature and you can set up general expenses as non-stocked items in the item table. You can also issue material from inventory against an inventory location, and a bin if used. This includes availability information and update of inventory balances.</mark> 

<mark>Items that are purchased from suppliers can be entered as expenses on the project using a function called re-invoicing of supplier invoices. When re-invoicing you can add a markup either in percent or a fixed amount. The purchased item appears automatically as a project expense when posting the supplier invoice according to pre-defined rules. The expense is then included in the accrued project cost and you can invoice the project customer.</mark> 

<mark>137</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Time, projects, assignments 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0138-01.png)


<mark>The project status can be used to control if time and expenses can be reported and invoiced on the project.</mark> 

### 14.5 Invoicing 

<mark>You can create assignment invoices in different ways, based on reported time rows and expenses, fixed payment plans, periodic invoice templates, etc. You can also enter invoices directly in the system without using any preparatory program.</mark> 

<mark>You can mark all invoiceable expenses and reported activities as ready for invoicing, either for a project or for a project manager. Additionally, you can also mark newly entered expenses or time rows under a project as ready for invoicing.</mark> 

#### 14.5.1 Manual or automatic invoicing 

<mark>You can both manually enter invoices and automatically generate invoices based on reported time rows and expenses.</mark> 

#### 14.5.2 Preliminary invoicing 

<mark>You can choose between preliminary invoicing and direct release of invoices for all types of assignment and project invoicing.</mark> 

<mark>You can turn off automatic creation of preliminary invoice rows for manual entries on assignments. This is for companies who want to be able to invoice manually entered activities or item rows on assignments, without invoicing all other uninvoiced time and expenses on the assignment.</mark> 

#### 14.5.3 Invoicing open account projects 

<mark>You can invoice open account projects per assignment or per project at any time. Alternatively, you can set up open account invoicing per assignment to be done monthly.</mark> 

#### 14.5.4 Periodic invoicing 

<mark>Jeeves ERP’s assignment routine has a function for periodic invoicing of, for example, service agreements, rent, etc. You define templates containing the periods when different types of periodic invoicing of assignments is to be done. Then you define a template for the invoice. You can enter an item number if, for example, you have defined an item for rent in the item table. Alternatively, you can just enter a price, salesperson and posting information. You can set up the system for periodization of revenues and costs; see the section about integration with the general ledger.</mark> 

#### 14.5.5 On-account invoicing 

<mark>Invoicing can be based on a payment plan for an assignment (so-called on-account). On-account amounts entered on assignments can be set up to be automatically deducted when invoicing time and expenses.</mark> 

#### 14.5.6 Collective invoicing 

<mark>Jeeves ERP offers collective invoicing of assignments with the same payer or delivery customer, terms and currency. You can choose if the assignment should be summarized or specified on the collective invoice. There is the possibility combine collective invoicing for assignment invoices with invoices from the order routine.</mark> 

<mark>138</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Time, projects, assignments 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0139-01.png)


#### 14.5.7 Credit invoices and matching credit to debit 

<mark>You can manually enter credit invoices or automatically generate credit invoice based on debit invoices. Credit invoices can be automatically matched against debit invoices via parameter setup.</mark> 

<mark>If you create a credit invoice from an invoice, you are asked if you want this credit invoice to be automatically matched against the relevant invoice. Otherwise you can enter another invoice number to match against or leave the field blank (if no automatic matching is to take place).</mark> 

<mark>The credit invoice is posted in the normal manner, during printout of the invoice journal, using templates connected to the dedicated payment method called CTD, Credit to debit. . If the debit invoice is already paid the credit invoice remains open. If the credit invoice only covers part of the invoice amount, the remaining amount remains open. If it covers more than the invoice amount, the debit invoice is fully paid and the remaining amount on the credit invoice remains open.</mark> 

#### 14.5.8 Advance invoicing 

<mark>Advance invoices can be entered in the assignment routine. You simply set the invoicing type Advance invoice on the assignment and enter the invoice manually.</mark> 

#### 14.5.9 e-Invoicing 

<mark>You can create electronic invoices and use a third party service for electronic distribution, notification of sender and receiver, storage and online tracking. The advantages of electronic invoicing are many; it is quicker, less expensive, better for the environment, minimizes administration etc.</mark> 

#### 14.5.10 Forward cover 

<mark>There is a simple function for managing forward cover of foreign currency, which implies invoicing at the preliminary invoices’ exchange rate.</mark> 

#### 14.5.11 Extra invoice texts 

<mark>Via parameters you can retrieve extra texts describing reported time and expenses on invoice rows.</mark> 

#### 14.5.12 Invoice costs 

<mark>You can setup Jeeves ERP to automatically handle charges on assignment invoices.</mark> 

### 14.6 Integration with accounts receivable ledger and general ledger 

<mark>Invoices are normally automatically entered in the accounts receivable ledger upon creation.</mark> 

<mark>Customer invoices are normally posted automatically in a separate routine/process after one or more batch of invoices have been produced. Invoicing can be done several times a day and posting at the end of the day or first thing the next day. One invoice journal is created and posted per invoice date. Each journal generates a voucher in the general ledger.</mark> 

<mark>When manually added stocked items are invoiced, a record for the manual inventory transaction is created and a matching record in the Inventory transaction history is created when integrated with the general ledger.</mark> 

<mark>Posting is done according to templates or according to invoicing types that control all accounts and account segments (cost centers, cost units etc.) to which receivables, revenues, costs, VAT/tax etc. are posted.</mark> 

<mark>You can set up and run automatic posting of internal costs for assignments/projects. You set up a cost per user and cost price factors per time type.</mark> 

<mark>139</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Time, projects, assignments 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0140-01.png)


#### 14.6.1 Internal debiting 

<mark>You can set up and run automatic posting of internal revenues and costs when lending employees to other projects. The project and staff must have different cost centers for it to be possible to internally debit another department. You enter an internal price on the relevant price list.</mark> 

#### 14.6.2 Periodization of revenues and costs 

<mark>When invoicing periodic assignments, revenues and costs can be allocated to periods. This is controlled by specifying allocation templates for revenues and costs on the assignment’s template for periodic invoicing.</mark> 

#### 14.6.3 Project coding 

<mark>There is a function for coding projects in order to automatically change postings (cost units) when using certain combinations of account and project numbers on vouchers. This can be used, for example, to add a prefix that represents the department/business unit that the cost unit represents.</mark> 

#### 14.6.4 Balancing project costs 

<mark>On projects you can specify a template for translation of income accounts to balance accounts when balancing a project. In the template you enter the intervals of income accounts and to which balance accounts these shall be translated.</mark> 

<mark>If project costs are balanced, amounts are posted in the GL on the specified balance account every time vouchers are created on the accounts specified in the interval. Later, when you want to cost account the project, you simply delete the translation template from the project.</mark> 

### 14.7 Project follow-up 

#### 14.7.1 Follow-up of revenues and costs 

<mark>In a table accessed via a node in the Projects program you can see posted debit and credit amounts and accumulated revenues and costs in base currency from posted vouchers per master project ID, project ID, fiscal year, period and integration source. Only cost accounted projects are included, i.e. not balanced projects. If you have created a project hierarchy, the amounts are summarized on the master project. The window is updated on-the-fly and can take a while depending on the size of the general ledger and the project structure (level of hierarchies). The window also has an underlying node for viewing the related general ledger voucher details.</mark> 

<mark>If you include expenses in project costs in base currency the project cost will be broken down into time and expenses.</mark> 

<mark>There are nodes for viewing the individual customer and supplier invoices connected to the project and their invoice rows. Only customer invoices on posted invoice journals and posted supplier invoices are included.</mark> 

<mark>You can calculate internal costs based on an estimated hourly cost per user and time type. You can also perform internal debiting between different cost units or cost centers based on the user’s estimated hourly cost. For more information see the section about integration with the general ledger.</mark>

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Time, projects, assignments 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0141-01.png)


#### 14.7.2 Follow-up of accrued time 

<mark>You have several types of reports to follow up reported time on projects, for example reported time per project or per person.</mark> 

#### 14.7.3 Follow-up of time and expenses not yet invoiced 

<mark>From assignments you can follow up time and expenses that have not yet been invoiced by printing an overview comprised of the following parts:</mark> 

- <mark>summary of included projects with totaling of times and costs</mark> 

- <mark>list of any assignment-specific prices</mark> 

- <mark>list of reported but uninvoiced times</mark> 

- <mark>list of uninvoiced expenses</mark> 

- <mark>summary of preliminary invoices and associated invoices</mark> 

- <mark>payment plan, where applicable</mark> 

#### 14.7.4 Closing projects 

<mark>There is logic connected to project status 8, Closed that allows you to automatically close all open planned activities on a project when closing the project.</mark> 

### 14.8 Reconciliations 

<mark>There is a program for on-screen reconciliation of balanced projects, per specified date, against the account(s) for balancing of projects in the general ledger.</mark> 

### 14.9 Industry-specifics 

<mark>Most functions in Jeeves Time, projects, assignments are common to all industries. There are some, however, that are specific to certain industries, as described below.</mark> 

#### 14.9.1 Assignments with counter parties 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes a simple solution for client agencies for managing assignments in, for example, legal proceedings. The solution is comprised of a program in which you can directly see the counter entries on an assignment. You can also enter supervisors for various tasks in the proceedings.</mark> 

#### 14.9.2 Percentage of completion accounting 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes functionality for percentage of completion accounting, mainly used by companies in the construction industry.</mark> 

<mark>Percentage of completion accounting is a method for successively posting the results of long-term fixed cost projects. Postings are based on a revenue forecast and a percentage of completion that is calculated from cost forecasts and posted costs, i.e. the calculation does not take into consideration any posted revenues. By keeping track of the amount posted on the project between each period, you can create new postings for accumulated revenue, accumulated cost and accumulated result for the period.</mark> 

<mark>If the project is forecasted at a loss you make an appropriation for the loss risk based on the whole forecasted loss.</mark>

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Time, projects, assignments 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0142-01.png)


#### 14.9.3 Project manufacturing 

<mark>Project manufacturing supports the business transactions and processes of a manufacturing process from quotation to payment using a project ID. It covers the following features:</mark> 

- <mark>Quotations and sales orders from quotations</mark> 

- <mark>Project MRP</mark> 

- <mark>Project manufacturing</mark> 

- <mark>Project inventory</mark> 

- <mark>Invoicing and payment plans</mark> 

- <mark>Delivery</mark> 

- <mark>Project analysis</mark> 

<mark>This section provides an overview of the components that are specific to Time, projects, and assignments. Other components are described in the chapter on Inventory.</mark> 

##### 14.9.3.1 Inventory locations setup 

<mark>You can define inventory locations to be used in Project manufacturing. You can also create a fictitious project to represent the normal inventory location when transferring stock to and from Project manufacturing inventory locations.</mark> 

##### 14.9.3.2 Agreement types for project manufacturing 

<mark>You can associate an agreement type with project manufacturing. Projects connected to such quotations/agreements are invoiced according to a payment plan. They also require that quotation rows are defined as either main rows or optional rows.</mark> 

##### 14.9.3.3 Assign activity type to planned/reported activities 

<mark>If you intend to use the project activity type in project analysis and follow up, you can connect the relevant activity types to the planned and reported activities:</mark> 

- <mark>Project management</mark> 

- <mark>Administration 1</mark> 

- <mark>Administration 2</mark> 

- <mark>Administration 3</mark> 

- <mark>Administration 4</mark> 

##### 14.9.3.4 Cost types linked to cost account 

<mark>You can select a cost type to connect it to the cost account. The following cost types are available as standard:</mark> 

- <mark>Transportation</mark> 

- <mark>Travel</mark> 

- <mark>Sub contractor</mark> 

- <mark>Miscellaneous</mark> 

##### 14.9.3.5 Quotation and payment plan 

<mark>You can enter a quotation and specify whether an item is mandatory/main or optional.</mark> 

<mark>You can specify different payment plan templates from which to create payment plans and then link the templates to quotations. An example of a payment plan could be a first part payment when initiating the manufacturing, a second for procuring certain specialized and/or expensive parts with long lead times, one or more payments at different milestones in the project and a final payment upon full delivery.</mark> 

<mark>You can create or modify a payment plan from a quotation and then create or modify a project and associate it with a payment plan.</mark>

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Time, projects, assignments 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0143-01.png)


##### 14.9.3.6 On account records 

<mark>You can update on account records with quotation type set to Project manufacturing from projects and color code all account records connected to the quotation/assignment associated with the project for easy identification.</mark> 

<mark>After the payment plan is created, you can create milestone activities to control when the assignment is invoiced. When a planned activity associated with a project manufacturing milestone is closed, the on account amount to invoice record with the same project manufacturing ID as the planned activity is automatically updated to make it possible to invoice the record.</mark> 

##### 14.9.3.7 Project analysis: Estimations, forecasts, and follow up 

<mark>As a project progresses, differences occur between the original cost and time estimates and the actual course of the project.</mark> 

<mark>During and after the completion of a project, you can take stock of its progress through periodic analysis. Jeeves ERP allows you to do the following through a node in the projects programs:</mark> 

- <mark>View estimations based on quotations provided by sales representatives</mark> 

- <mark>View forecasts and calculations made by project manager/management team during various stages of the project</mark> 

- <mark>Create/update forecasts</mark> 

- <mark>Save/view old versions of forecasts</mark> 

- <mark>View actual, accrued project costs and time usage</mark> 

- <mark>Compare current forecast with actual numbers/calculations to know the updated cost of the project.</mark> 

##### 14.9.3.8 Sales order from quotation 

<mark>When using Project manufacturing, you create sales orders from quotations in the normal manner. There is, however, additional logic for Project manufacturing.</mark> 

<mark>You can specify an order type for the project manufacturing agreement type, in which case the order type is retrieved from the agreement type on the quotation when creating a sales order.</mark> 

##### 14.9.3.9 Split reports per shipment 

<mark>The shipment ID field primarily supports the Project manufacturing functionality, but can be used by all. In large manufacturing projects, it can be used to identify and specify the contents of each transport vehicle, when large constructions need to be dissembled for transport.</mark> 

### 14.10 Localizations/internationalization 

<mark>Most functions in Jeeves Time, projects, assignments are common to all countries. There are some, however, that are specific to certain countries, as described below.</mark> 

<mark>Integration with Kontek Payroll system (Sweden only)</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP offers Swedish users an integration with the Kontek payroll system. The integration is based on parts of the existing functionality for Attendance reporting in the Production module. The main components in the integration are:</mark> 

- <mark>employee agreements connected to applicable time types and salary types,</mark> 

- <mark>connection of employees to the relevant employee agreements,</mark> 

- <mark>day schedules with applicable attendance reporting time types,</mark> 

- <mark>work schedule templates based on the day schedules,</mark> 

- <mark>connection of employees to the relevant work schedule templates,</mark> 

- <mark>entry of any deviations and automatic creation of the current year’s work schedules,</mark> 

- <mark>connection of reported activities to relevant time types (via time codes) and</mark> 

- <mark>automatic creation of payroll files.</mark>

#### 14.10.1 VAT refunding 

###### _Originally developed for the UK, but available to all users_ 

<mark>In some countries, such as the UK, the VAT/tax value is based on the discounted amount even if the customer does not take advantage of the early settlement discount. In order to be able to reconcile the VAT at the end of the period, Jeeves ERP creates additional, preliminary postings on the customer invoice journal, which are subsequently, automatically reversed on the payment journal. You can adjust your invoice templates to show the required information, for example the discount terms and the VAT amount based on the discounted amount.</mark>

## 15 Jeeves ERP Accounts receivable 

<mark>Jeeves Accounts receivable helps you optimize the process from managing invoices to receiving payments. Invoices that originate from Jeeves Sales or Jeeves Time, project, assignments modules are automatically integrated. You can also enter invoices from other sources manually.</mark> 

<mark>Payments, including advance payments, can be entered manually or by importing payment files from financial institutions. For overdue invoices there is flexible functionality for managing reminders, interest invoices, debt collections, and payment injunctions.</mark> 

<mark>Integration with the general ledger is automatically made via a journal, which generates a voucher in the general ledger. There are also programs and reports for reconciliation and functionality for revaluation.</mark> 

### 15.1 Companies and customers 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP you have a common Company table for company information regardless of the type of company – prospective customers and business opportunities, customers, carriers and suppliers. This eliminates the need for duplicate entries and maintenance of company information.</mark> 

<mark>Additional, customer-specific information is managed in related customer tables. Here you can handle all types of customers, domestic and international, customers with another payer, with factoring, with several points of delivery etc.</mark> 

#### 15.1.1 Customer categorization 

<mark>You can handle any number of customer categories for posting control and statistics etc., any number of customer discount classes for discount management, also customer classes for own classification purposes.</mark> 

#### 15.1.2 Language management 

<mark>You can manage the language of payment terms, delivery terms, delivery methods, currency clause texts, reminder texts etc. and print external documents in the customer's language.</mark> 

### 15.2 Invoices 

#### 15.2.1 Manual invoices 

<mark>You can manually enter invoices and credit invoices without detail rows, for example invoices from external systems, in the Customer invoices program. Invoice totals can be entered but not invoice rows and invoices cannot be printed. You use this function for invoices that are not handled in the integrated modules, i.e. the Sales or the Time, projects, assignments modules.</mark> 

#### 15.2.2 Invoice notes/claims 

<mark>There is a basic routine for recording and searching claims regarding customer invoices.</mark> 

#### 15.2.3 Credit check 

<mark>There is a function in the accounts receivable for automatic credit check of customers based on the customers' due invoices. This is an additional check to the normal credit check during order entry in the sales module.</mark> 

<mark>You specify a minimum number of due days on invoices to be checked and a maximum total due amount for the customer/payer. You also choose between credit block and warning. To perform the credit check you first make a selection of customers or invoices and then run a report program.</mark> 

<mark>You can view the debt account for invoiced, unpaid customer advances in the Accounts receivable reconciliation program.</mark> 

<mark>145</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Accounts receivable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0146-01.png)


#### 15.2.4 Payment terms 

<mark>You can store an unlimited number of payment terms, per customer or payer, per agreement or general. Credit periods and any early settlement discounts can be set up in numerous ways.</mark> 

<mark>There is an array of methods for calculating due dates:</mark> 

- <mark>Credit period in days or in months</mark> 

- <mark>Calculate period from delivery date or from beginning of following month(s)</mark> 

- <mark>Extend period to end of month</mark> 

- <mark>Set dates, for example 10th, 20th and last day of month</mark> 

#### 15.2.5 Invoice history 

<mark>In many countries it is legally required that you store historical data regarding invoices so that an invoice duplicate can be reproduced if necessary.</mark> 

<mark>You can print duplicate invoices with historical data, i.e. even if company information for the customer or payer has been changed since the original invoice was printed, the change does not affect the reprint. There is also functionality to ensure that the same template is used as when printing the original invoice.</mark> 

### 15.3 Posting and integration with general ledger 

<mark>Customer invoices are normally posted automatically in a separate routine/process after one or more batch of invoices have been produced. Invoicing can be done several times a day and posting at the end of the day or first thing the next day. One invoice journal is created and posted per invoice date. Each journal generates a voucher in the general ledger.</mark> 

<mark>For manual invoices the income is allocated in its entirety on a single account according to a posting template for invoice totals, comprised of an account for customer receivables, for VAT/tax, and for income. If you want to split it into several accounts or account segments, you do this manually or via allocation.</mark> 

### 15.4 Integration with Intrum 

<mark>You can manage receivables and/or payments against invoices using integration with Intrum, a leading credit management services company in Europe. You can choose customers whose invoice information you want to send to Intrum. The information is sent in XML format either on the invoice creation date (outsourcing of entire Accounts receivable) or on the due date (outsourcing of demands and collections only). Also, you can set a grace period before sending the invoice information to Intrum, considering extra days for processing of payments done on the due date.</mark> 

<mark>For every invoice sent for collection, an update on the status is sent back from Intrum. It contains, amongst other things, information on the number of reminders sent to the customer and the latest date of reminder. The information is received in XML format which is then updated in the Customer invoices program.</mark> 

### 15.5 Incoming payments 

<mark>Incoming payments can be entered manually or imported from a bank or postal giro file.</mark> 

<mark>When entering payments manually, you can choose between a number of methods:</mark> 

- <mark>enter all today's incoming payments per payment method</mark> 

- <mark>enter payments per customer</mark> 

- <mark>if you know the invoice number, enter payment of an individual invoice</mark> 

<mark>You can set up import of payments to automatically create payment journal rows. The import can be stopped when an incorrect payment is identified so that the incorrect payment can be adjusted and the import resumed. Alternatively, you can control that incorrect payments should not create</mark> <u><mark>payment journal rows and have to be handled manually.</mark></u>
