<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Accounts receivable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0147-01.png)


<mark>You can receive payments to multiple (own) companies in the database. By default, Jeeves ERP determines the company by checking the bank account number in the payment file. However, companies using the BG Max and Total In payment services should turn off this functionality and enter the receiving account number for each company in the database. Jeeves ERP checks the bank account number in the payment file to decide which company the payments are connected to.</mark> 

<mark>You can view the payment information in a text field by clicking the Internal text button in the Incoming payment file detail node in the Incoming payment receipts program. In the Edit blob that opens, you can manually enter any own internal comments or information.</mark> 

#### 15.5.1 Partial payments, discounts, deductions, posting individual invoices 

<mark>You can manually enter a partial payment, an early settlement discount or any other deduction, or post the incoming payment in a way other than according to the posting templates for incoming payments, simply by double-clicking on the planned payment and changing the fields in question.</mark> 

<mark>You can set a parameter to automatically make proportional deductions of VAT when early settlement discounts take effect and other deductions are made on customer invoices.</mark> 

<mark>When importing from file, there is a function to capture incomplete transactions and add information to achieve a higher rate of matching.</mark> 

#### 15.5.2 Early settlement discounts 

<mark>Automatic calculation of early settlement discount based on discount date can be applied to invoice amount or value of applicable goods, on one level or as tiered discounts.</mark> 

##### 15.5.2.1 Early settlement discount on advance payments 

<mark>You can enter early settlement discount amount in currency at the time of incoming/outgoing payment. The discount is calculated automatically based on the terms specified for the payment terms in question, either in the Early settlement discount days and Early settlement discount % fields, or the Early settlement discount terms field. It can also be specified manually, in which case the paid amount must be manually adjusted correspondingly.</mark> 

#### 15.5.3 Payments via check 

<mark>There is functionality for preparation of payments made by check, integration with the general ledger, remittance and payment. There is also functionality for cancelation of checks.</mark> 

<mark>You can set the check number automatically on all paid invoices in the journal. If you want the same check numbers to be automatically added to a set of invoices when you enter received payments by check, you must enter the check number on the payment journal header.</mark> 

<mark>You can also specify that empty spaces in any field in the printout can be filled with a defined character, e.g. asterisk (*), using the Fill character field in the Template editor. In addition, you can reprint (print copy) of checks with same check number from the payment programs.</mark> 

<mark>There are various options for printing checks with different layouts.</mark> 

#### 15.5.4 Lump sum payments 

<mark>When entering incoming payments for individual customers you can handle lump sum payments, i.e. when a customer pays an amount that covers several invoices without specifying which invoices they intend to pay.</mark> 

#### 15.5.5 Miscellaneous payments 

<mark>A statement from the bank may contain miscellaneous types of payments. The Miscellaneous payments functionality makes it quicker and easier to handle incoming payments that are not</mark> 

<mark>147</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Accounts receivable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0148-01.png)


<mark>connected to customer invoices and to facilitate the reconciliation of the bank account. This can be, for example, a payment for something other than an invoice, or a payment to/from the bank.</mark> 

<mark>You enter the amounts in the currency of the payment journal. The exchange rate in the journal header is used when integrating with the General ledger. You include the amount of the miscellaneous payments in the Paid amount in the payment journal header. You only enter the contra posting to the bank posting. The bank posting is created automatically.</mark> 

#### 15.5.6 Advance payments 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP’s accounts receivable you can manage advance payments from customers. When you enter an advance payment from a customer, an on-account invoice is created automatically. This can later be used against the normal customer invoice or incoming payment.</mark> 

#### 15.5.7 Payments in other currency 

<mark>You can enter incoming payments in a currency other than the invoice's currency, if the function is activated. The work method is largely the same as for entering incoming payments in the invoice's currency. The difference in this program is that you can process invoices whose original currency differs from the currency in the journal header. If you open the form for an individual incoming payment (detail), you access program specific fields for entering received amounts, or deductions, etc., in the currency you have entered in the journal header.</mark> 

#### 15.5.8 Payment by installments 

<mark>You can handle customer invoices to be paid by installments. For each payment installment you specify the credit period in days and the percentage of the total amount to be paid in the installment. The sum of the percentages must be 100.</mark> 

<mark>For example, you could define:</mark> 

- <mark>two equal installments with 30 days between them; enter one row for 30 days and one for 60 days with 50 percent on each or</mark> 

- <mark>three installments; a first installment of 20 percent after 10 days, a second of 30 percent after 20 days and the remaining 50 percent after 30 days.</mark> 

<mark>On the payment terms you can also enter a minimum limit for splitting invoices into installments.</mark> 

<mark>You can adjust the installments, to create invoice specific installments, if desired but the sum of the percentages must be 100.</mark> 

<mark>When invoicing, Jeeves ERP creates one invoice with installment records. There is a special window in the invoice report template that you can make visible in order to print information about the installments.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP takes the payment installments into consideration when creating payment reminders. This implies that reminders only cover the amount that is currently due. There is a special window in the reminder report template that you can make visible in order to print information about the installments.</mark> 

<mark>The following applies if your company uses the Payment by installments functionality:</mark> 

- <mark>If you use the “traditional method” of interest calculation, i.e. if Balance method is not checked, Jeeves ERP takes the payment installments into consideration when creating interest invoices. This implies that the interest invoices only cover installments that are paid after their due dates.</mark> 

<mark>If you use the balance method you can create interest invoices before installments are paid, in which case this cannot be applied. Forms, lists and reports that show amounts due, and/or amounts becoming due, take the installments' due dates and amounts into consideration.</mark> 

<mark>148</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Accounts receivable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0149-01.png)


#### 15.5.9 Reverse journal or individual payment(s) 

<mark>There is a function for reversing a paid and posted payment journal. You use the function to reverse all the payments in the journal if you, for any reason, need to re-open one or more paid customer invoice(s).</mark> 

<mark>Alternatively, you can reverse individual invoices by creating a new incoming payment journal and entering a negative incoming payment record in the right window. You enter the customer number, invoice number and a negative amount. When you post the journal the status of the invoice is reset to Invoiced.</mark> 

#### 15.5.10 Offsetting customer receivables against accounts payable 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP you can transfer a customer invoice to the accounts payable ledger in order to settle one or more supplier invoices. This can be used to pay credit notes. The function can also be used for debit invoices if you wish to annul a payment that is already in the accounts payable ledger.</mark> 

#### 15.5.11 Direct debits 

<mark>For certain types of sales, for example sales via mail order, or when organizations charge student or member fees, it is common to have a special procedure to gain the customer's permission to transfer the amount from their bank account.</mark> 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP you can handle incoming direct debit payments within Sweden, Germany and the Single Euro Payments Area, SEPA. The function can also be localized by partners in other countries by replacing Jeeves ERP's standard procedure for creating payment files with an own procedure.</mark> 

<mark>There is one work flow based on a dedicated Direct debit journal program. This assumes that you first mark the individuals/customers who have given permission. Then you run a report to create a so-called permission file. At regular intervals, for example when invoicing, you create a direct debit file that is sent to the Bank Giro. There is also an alternative work flow for creating a direct debit file directly in the normal incoming payment journal program. You choose the latter, for example, if you need access to Jeeves ERP's standard functionality for early settlement discounts and adjustments of invoice amounts.</mark> 

### 15.6 Posting and integration with general ledger 

<mark>Incoming payments are posted automatically according to posting templates, and integrated with the general ledger, by running a report program. When posting incoming payments, the amount on the accounts receivable account is counter-posted and the bank account increased. Any bank costs, exchange rate differences, early settlement discounts and deductions are also posted.</mark> 

#### 15.6.1 Manual posting of incoming payments 

<mark>It is possible to manually specify account segment 1, 2, 3 and 4 for individual incoming payments.</mark> 

### 15.7 Payment reminders, collection and interest invoicing 

<mark>Jeeves ERP offers support for several different ways to urge customers to pay overdue invoices. The process supports the following routines, mainly based on how payment reminders are handled in the Scandinavian countries:</mark> 

- <mark>Payment reminders</mark> 

- <mark>Interest invoicing</mark> 

- <mark>Collection</mark> 

- <mark>Payment injunctions</mark> 

<mark>All parts can be set up dynamically. You can set a minimum amount for demands and even handle reminder charges. Reminder letters can be printed in different languages.</mark> 

<mark>149</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Accounts receivable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0150-01.png)


<mark>An alternative solution is also available to invoicing payment reminder charges as part of interest invoices. Instead of including reminder charges in payment reminders or future customer orders, you can include them directly when you run interest invoicing. The reason for this alternative solution is that a customer who pays late might be credit blocked, and/or they might never place another order with the company charging them interest and reminder charges. When the interest invoice amount is less than the payment reminder charge limit, extra invoice rows will be created. Since the charges are included as rows in the interest invoice, they are processed as claims and also update the general ledger.</mark> 

<mark>It is also possible to remove specific customers or invoices from a reminder batch.</mark> 

#### 15.7.1 Payment reminders 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP you can create up to four payment reminders regarding due and unpaid customer invoices and, if so desired, a collection journal. You set up the number of days between each reminder and before collection and via a parameter you control whether or not payment reminders should be printed. You can also set a parameter if you want to include invoices that are not yet due on reminders.</mark> 

<mark>During the reminder batch, accrued interest is calculated using the formula: (invoice amount*interest days/365)*penalty interest (or 30 days/month is used if that is stipulated in the interest agreement). Accrued interest is calculated for each invoice that has been paid after the due date and has not already been included in an interest invoice, as well as invoices that are due but not paid. The date you specify when printing the reminder is then used as the 'simulated payment date' of these customer invoices. Thus, interest is not only calculated on the invoices printed on the reminder.</mark> 

<mark>A reminder journal is created, which contains one reminder per customer and currency. This means that the reminder can consist of one or more invoices. If the reminder covers several invoices for which a different number of reminders have been sent, reminder text from the highest reminder number is used.</mark> 

<mark>You can create and print reminders in one or three steps. The latter is recommended because this enables you to change the reminder text and delete individual invoices from the reminders before printing. You can even cancel the entire reminder for a certain customer.</mark> 

<mark>Other features include:</mark> 

- <mark>any number of reminder rules, customers are linked to a rule</mark> 

- <mark>minimum amount for reminders</mark> 

- <mark>payment reminders in several languages</mark> 

- <mark>reminder charges</mark> 

#### 15.7.2 Interest invoicing 

<mark>Interest can be invoiced in two different ways in Jeeves ERP. Either according to the "traditional" method which calculates interest when the invoice is paid, or according to the balance method which calculates interest day by day on the balance of due invoices and then also takes credit invoices into consideration. If you use the balance method, there is a parameter with which you can determine whether interest should be paid on the total balance or on every invoice separately.</mark> 

<mark>There are a number of parameters that determine how interest invoices are calculated. These mainly lie in interest rules to which you link customers.</mark> 

<mark>You can create and print interest invoices in one or several steps. The latter is recommended because this enables you to check which customers and invoices will be invoiced interest, and where necessary to block individual customers or invoices before printing.</mark> 

<mark>Other features include:</mark> 

- <mark>any number of interest rules, customers are linked to a rule</mark> 

- <mark>minimum amount for interest invoices</mark> 

> <mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark>

Jeeves ERP Accounts receivable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0151-01.png)


#### 15.7.3 e- Invoicing interest 

<mark>You can also use Jeeves ERP’s eInvoicing functionality to create electronic interest invoices. This includes:</mark> 

- <mark>electronic distribution</mark> 

- <mark>electronic storage and online tracking</mark> 

- <mark>notification of sender and receiver (services via Pagero)</mark> 

#### 15.7.4 Collections 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes a function for handling invoices that need to be sent for debt collection. The routine consists of creating a collection journal according to rules connected to each customer, i.e. number of reminders before collection etc. From the journal a collection file is created which can either be directly sent by e-mail or printed and sent by mail to the collection company.</mark> 

#### 15.7.5 Payment orders/injunctions 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes a simple function for creating and logging payment orders/injunctions, i.e. handling of invoices sent to the authorities for enforcement of payment.</mark> 

#### 15.7.6 Credit control, interest and reminders 

<mark>There is a special program for making requests/searches regarding credit control, interest and reminders. The program only includes reports related to credit control.</mark> 

### 15.8 Doubtful and bad debts 

<mark>Doubtful debts are those debts that a business or individual is unlikely to be able to collect. The reasons for potential non-payment can include disputes over deliveries, the condition of received items or financial stress within a customer's operations.</mark> 

<mark>Bad debts is the continuation of doubtful debts management, when you know for certain that the debt is not possible to collect, for example because the customer has gone out of business.</mark> 

<mark>You can mark unpaid invoices as doubtful debts and post these values as doubtful receivables and as potential losses on the accounts receivable account.</mark> 

<mark>Integration source 105, Doubtful debt is used for the voucher source.</mark> 

<mark>You can set up posting rules with the following integration details for use in the vouchers created from the doubtful debt journal:</mark> 

- <mark>12, Doubtful debt (credit)</mark> 

- <mark>13, Counter posting of doubtful debt (debit)</mark> 

<mark>These two integration details are also used by integration source 6, Incoming payments along with 90, Bad debt, for reversing doubtful receivables and posting bad debts.</mark> 

<mark>You can also set up posting rules for the reversal of the doubtful debt when it becomes an actual loss, with the addition of integration detail 90, Bad debt.</mark> 

<mark>You can search for invoices marked as doubtful or bad debts and to view the doubt or bad debt amount in the Customer invoices program.</mark> 

<mark>151</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Accounts receivable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0152-01.png)


#### 15.8.1 Create doubtful or bad debt journal 

<mark>You can mark unpaid invoices as doubtful debts and post these values as doubtful receivables and as potential losses on the accounts receivables in the Doubtful debt journal program. You use the same program if it becomes necessary to transform these into bad debts and post the values as actual losses.</mark> 

<mark>The program comprises of three parts:</mark> 

- <mark>The journal header – here the Doubtful debt journal number is allocated and you enter general information such as journal date, currency code, etc.</mark> 

- <mark>The left embedded list window – here you can open customer invoices with due dates earlier than or equal to the Doubtful debt journal date are displayed.</mark> 

- <mark>The right embedded list window – here is an entry list to which you transfer the selected doubtful debt invoices. You can view invoice details such as outstanding amount, invoice total excluding VAT/tax, the number of days that have lapsed since the amount was due. You can also view or modify the doubtful debt amounts or change the exchange rate if required.</mark> 

#### 15.8.2 Post and integrate with general ledger 

<mark>You can create postings and general ledger vouchers for a selected doubtful or bad debt journal.</mark> 

<mark>If the Bad debt journal checkbox is unchecked (false) on the journal header, this is a doubtful debt journal. If the Bad debt journal checkbox is checked (true) on the journal header, this is a bad debt journal.</mark> 

<mark>Note that the journal must be closed before printing the report.</mark> 

#### 15.8.3 Reverse doubtful invoice 

<mark>You can reverse a doubtful invoice, for instance, if there is a mistake and the invoices are incorrectly selected as doubtful, but the journal has posted them to the General ledger. You can rectify this by creating a new reverse journal.</mark> 

#### 15.8.4 Recovery of doubtful or bad debt 

<mark>If a customer should pay off a doubtful or bad debt, you can create a normal incoming payment journal for that invoice. The voucher created for the incoming payment journal will reverse the postings for the doubtful or bad debt. The doubtful receivables will be debited and the loss on the accounts receivable will be credited.</mark> 

### 15.9 Ledger information 

#### 15.9.1 Customer solvency 

<mark>There are functions for analysis of customers' solvency, i.e. average payment times, payment habits, etc. The Customers program and the program for accounts receivable inquiries display information about payments and payment times per customer. If you select several customers, totals are shown at the bottom of the list. If you want to see totals for the entire accounts receivables, select all customers.</mark> 

<mark>The data is generated using the Generate customer solvency button and is calculated based on the specified starting invoice date. Note that only authorized users can run this calculation.</mark>

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Accounts receivable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0153-01.png)


#### 15.9.2 Invoicing statistics 

<mark>Statistics can be produced for any period, by customer (delivery customer or payer), customer category and project.</mark> 

<mark>There is also a function for creating records for European sales lists, i.e. reports on arrivals and dispatches within the EU, including drill down to the invoices included in the dispatches and arrivals totals. This is to make it possible to reconcile the data in the report. This function can be used by all companies within the EU that purchase from and/or sell to other countries within the EU. A report can be printed from Jeeves ERP as a basis for manually filling in the official form.</mark> 

#### 15.9.3 Reconciliation of accounts receivable ledger 

<mark>The accounts receivable ledger must regularly be reconciled against the general ledger, for example once a month. During reconciliation, the accounts receivable ledger is checked against the accounts receivable ledger account in the general ledger. You can also reconcile the accounts receivable per date by entering the from and to dates in the relevant program. The transactions for the specified general ledger account and the accounts receivable ledger for each of the dates in the date interval are generated. This makes it easier to identify which date has caused any difference immediately.</mark> 

<mark>The accounts receivable, i.e. debt due, can be reconciled in report form or on screen, after which a report is printed. If required, you can print the entire accounts receivable ledger as of the reconciliation period.</mark> 

### 15.10 Other functions 

#### 15.10.1 Customer number change 

<mark>There is a function for transferring individual or all outstanding invoices to a new customer (payer) by changing the customer number. The function is useful when a customer changes their line of business or when liability to pay is assumed by a factoring company or bankruptcy estate. A customer number change log, which you can print from the</mark> **<mark>Report</mark>** <mark>menu, shows the requested change, which invoices were processed and the new invoice numbers. This log can also be used to view the original invoice numbers.</mark> 

#### 15.10.2 Revaluation of accounts receivable 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP you can revalue the accounts receivable ledger at the current exchange rates. Exchange rate differences are posted automatically according to posting templates, and integrated with general ledger.</mark> 

<mark>There are three alternative ways of handling the revaluation:</mark> 

- <mark>as realized gains and losses in the accounts receivable</mark> 

- <mark>as unrealized gains and losses in the accounts receivable which are reversed when the final exchange rate difference is determined, i.e. upon payment</mark> 

- <mark>create a voucher in the general ledger to show unrealized gains and losses, which is immediately and automatically reversed, without affecting the accounts receivable</mark> 

### 15.11 Localizations/internationalization 

<mark>Most functions in Jeeves Accounts receivable are common to all countries. There are some, however, that are specific to certain countries, as described below.</mark>

Jeeves ERP Accounts receivable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0154-01.png)


#### 15.11.1 Bank details available in currency codes program and printed on invoices (Sweden only) 

<mark>The SWIFT code and IBAN/SWIFT account fields are available in the Currency codes program.</mark> 

<mark>If the report templates for customer invoices and preliminary invoices are configured to retrieve the bank account information, the information is retrieved and printed in the invoice footer as follows:</mark> 

- <mark>Swedish customer invoiced in base currency: Customer invoice PG and Customer invoice BG retrieved from System data, own company.</mark> 

- <mark>Foreign customer invoiced in other currency or Swedish Krona (SEK): IBAN/SWIFT account and Swift code retrieved first from the Currency codes table and if unavailable, from System data, own company.</mark> 

- <mark>Swedish customer invoiced in other currency: IBAN/SWIFT account and Swift code retrieved first from the Currency codes table and if unavailable, from System data, own company.</mark> 

#### 15.11.2 Invoice reference numbers and bank account validations 

Jeeves ERP supports localized reference numbers on customer invoices such as OCR in Sweden, FIK code in Denmark, KID reference in Norway, bank reference in Finland and Internal subscriber number in Switzerland. Bank account numbers are validated according to local standards. There is also the possibility to add own formats and validations. 

#### 15.11.3 Localized customer payments 

<mark>Jeeves ERP supports import of payment files for Sweden, Denmark, Norway and Finland. There is also the possibility to add other localized formats.</mark> 

#### 15.11.4 Import payments in Credit advice ISO 20022 XML, Camt054 format 

<mark>You can import supplier invoice payment files from banks in the Camt.054 format. The files are imported in the normal manner, using the Definition and import external data->work table program, and the result will be displayed in the Outgoing payment receipts program.</mark> 

<mark>Note that not only the setup is identical for both supplier and customer payments, the same payment file can also contain payments from both suppliers and customers. When importing, Jeeves ERP identifies the type of payment and stores them in the relevant program.</mark> 

#### 15.11.5 Import payments from Norwegian banks 

<mark>The currency code for the incoming payment receipts when importing a payment file from a Norwegian bank in Norwegian Crowns (NOK) is retrieved from the payment. This ensures that there is no currency mismatch when Jeeves ERP matches the payment file with the existing invoices in the accounts receivable ledger. The Receipt detail status shows the value as Transferred, the payment journal is created, and the invoice is paid as expected.</mark> 

#### 15.11.6 Interest invoicing in connection with normal invoicing 

<mark>In some countries, such as Norway, payment reminders and interest invoices are printed in connection with normal invoicing of orders in the Sales module, i.e. not in a separate routine in the Accounts receivable. Jeeves ERP includes functionality for interest invoicing at normal invoicing, which is described in the chapter on the</mark> _<mark>Sales</mark>_ <mark>module.</mark> 

#### 15.11.7 Advance invoicing 

<mark>In some countries, such as Slovakia, it is legally required that postings of net amounts are suppressed on advance invoices. There is an application parameter with which you can suppress the postings.</mark> 

<mark>154</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Accounts receivable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0155-01.png)


#### 15.11.8 Import and automatic matching of bank statements 

<mark>There is functionality for importing bank statements including customer and supplier payments and other bank transactions, matching the payments against open invoices and integrating the bank statement with the general ledger. The function is used in countries such as Germany and the Netherlands. Please refer to the chapter on the</mark> _<mark>General ledger</mark>_ <mark>module for a description of the function.</mark> 

#### 15.11.9 Tax clearing on sales 

<mark>With Jeeves ERP you can handle sales of items with interim posting of VAT/tax, i.e. where the outgoing VAT/tax is posted on an interim account until payment is made, at which time it is counter-posted to the normal VAT/tax account. This is used, for example, in France.</mark> 

#### 15.11.10 Split payments 

<mark>VAT regulations in Poland require customers to pay their suppliers via a split payment mechanism. This applies only to domestic transactions between companies with the product and service groups specified in the Accounting Act. The split payments mechanism is obligatory only for transactions over 15,000 PLN. Payments with lower amounts can be made through traditional bank transfer or cash. It applies on both full and partial payments.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP provides functionality to determine if customer payments should be made via a split payment mechanism, and to print information about split payments on customer invoices.</mark> 

<mark>For partial payments, the paid amount and the VAT amount needs to be changed manually in Jeeves.</mark> 

#### 15.11.11 BOE ledger, bills received from customers 

<mark>The bills of exchange ledger is a ledger for both received bills of exchange for payment from customers and issued bills of exchange for payments to suppliers. The BOE ledger in the accounts receivable includes functionality for preparation, acceptance and printout of bills of exchange received from customers. This is used, for example, in France. The process includes:</mark> 

- <mark>acceptance</mark> 

- <mark>collection</mark> 

- <mark>payment</mark> 

- <mark>integration with the general ledger</mark> 

#### 15.11.12 KSeF eInvoicing — outgoing customer invoices (Poland) 

<mark>Jeeves ERP supports electronic sales invoicing through Poland’s National e-Invoice System (KSeF) using the FA(3) format. Validation supports accurate processing and compliance with Polish eInvoicing requirements.</mark> 

<mark>After a customer invoice has been cleared through KSeF, the cleared legal invoice, including its KSeF ID and QR code, can be retrieved automatically and attached to the invoice header in Jeeves ERP.</mark> 

<mark>KSeF identifiers can be used in payment processing and reconciliation. They can also be used when processing incoming customer-payment notifications in ISO 20022 camt.054 format, supporting reconciliation and improving traceability between invoices and payments.</mark>

## 16 Jeeves ERP Accounts payable 

<mark>Jeeves Accounts payable reduces your administration and optimizes your payments by streamlining the management of supplier invoices, from purchase order matching at invoice arrival, via the approval process, to final payment. Much of the functionality is based on seamless integration with the following Jeeves modules/applications:</mark> 

- <mark>Jeeves General ledger – for automatic posting as well as reports and programs for reconciliation</mark> 

- <mark>Jeeves Purchases – for automatic matching of invoices against linked purchase orders</mark> 

- <mark>Jeeves Fixed assets – for automatically creating fixed assets at supplier invoice entry</mark> 

- <mark>Jeeves e-Approval web application – to let a workflow electronically route invoices for approval on a web interface</mark> 

- <mark>Jeeves e-Approval mobile routine – makes the management of supplier invoices even more accessible</mark> 

<mark>Payments are commonly made via payment files. There is support for many different localized formats. The invoices to be paid can be entered manually or selected automatically, from a cash optimizing point of view.</mark> 

### 16.1 Companies and suppliers 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP you have a common Company table for company information regardless of the type of company – prospective customers and business opportunities, customers, carriers and suppliers. This eliminates the need for duplicate entries and maintenance of company information.</mark> 

<mark>Additional, supplier-specific information is managed in related supplier tables. Here you can handle all types of suppliers, domestic and international, suppliers with another payee, with factoring etc.</mark> 

#### 16.1.1 Supplier categorization 

<mark>You can handle any number of supplier categories to control approval and posting templates as well as statistics etc. You can have any number of supplier types for supplier evaluations.</mark> 

#### 16.1.2 Suppliers’ bank accounts 

<mark>If you enter an account number for the supplier, a record is automatically saved in the Supplier's bank accounts. The number is validated according to the Bank acct check type field for the account, unless the validation is inactivated. The account check type can be, for example, PG or BG and numbers are checked to ensure that they are for a valid account, or valid plus giro or bank giro numbers. You can define additional bank account types and modification procedures are provided to allow other validations for the different standards that apply in different countries. You can also retrieve different bank accounts depending on the currency specified on the supplier invoice.</mark> 

#### 16.1.3 Supplier identification 

<mark>There is a possibility to set an alternative supplier ID to identify a supplier when importing payment files. This can be used as an optional identity for the supplier in the absence of normal identity data such as Bank giro number, Plus giro number, Vat number etc.</mark> 

### 16.2 Supplier invoices 

<mark>Supplier invoices can either be entered directly or imported from a file to a temporary table, to be reviewed before converting to a supplier invoice record.</mark> 

<mark>156</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Accounts payable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0157-01.png)


#### 16.2.1 Arrival handling of supplier invoices 

<mark>You can choose between different work methods for handling supplier invoices:</mark> 

- <mark>arrival entry with preliminary posting, then final approval with final posting</mark> 

- <mark>arrival entry with final posting, then final approval without posting</mark> 

- <mark>direct final approval and final posting</mark> 

<mark>These postings are based on pre-defined templates. You can create a new posting template with similar posting controls as an existing one using a function button in the Posting templates program.</mark> 

#### 16.2.2 Approval rights 

<mark>You can restrict approval rights to certain cost centers or group of cost centers.</mark> 

#### 16.2.3 Project ID linked to imported supplier invoice 

<mark>When you enter a purchase order number on an imported supplier invoice and save the record, the project ID associated with the PO is automatically retrieved from the purchase order header table and can be viewed in the Review imported supplier invoices program. The project ID can also be automatically moved when you transfer the invoice to the supplier invoices (lr) table if required.</mark> 

<mark>The project ID is copied to the preliminary posting, which can be viewed in the Preliminary posting node. This requires, according to Jeeves ERP’s standard logic, that the Project accounting segment should be combined with the account.</mark> 

#### 16.2.4 Update imported supplier invoices with error in data 

<mark>It is recommended that you regularly check imported supplier invoices for errors in data and keep the information updated using the Preliminary import/scanned suppl invoices program. From this program, you can view the PDF files with the preliminary supplier invoices. You can also check the invoices for errors in data (for example, incorrect Invoice date or invoice already exists in the Accounts payable). In case an error is found, you can correct the data and then transfer the invoice for review or you can delete it.</mark> 

#### 16.2.5 Manually transfer corrected supplier invoices 

<mark>After you make corrections to imported supplier invoices in the Preliminary import/scanned suppl invoice, you can manually transfer the corrected supplier invoices for review via a push button.</mark> 

<mark>When you click the Transfer to review button, a row-by-row transfer is made to the Review imported supplier invoices program and controls are performed as usual. If a row can be loaded, it is deleted from the preliminary table; otherwise the row remains. When all corrected records are successfully loaded, a message is displayed showing the number of records that have been transferred.</mark> 

<mark>When you click the Transfer to AP - PDM button, the invoice reviewal documents are automatically checked, one by one. All valid documents are converted to supplier invoices and transferred to the supplier invoices table and the accounts payable ledger.</mark> 

- <mark>If all documents are processed correctly, a message informing that all reviewed invoices are now transferred is shown.</mark> 

- <mark>If an error is found in any document, it is not converted into an invoice and a message referencing the sequence number of the documents that are not transferred is shown.</mark> 

- <mark>If no documents are found for processing, a message informing that there are no documents for processing is shown.</mark> 

<mark>The transfer status is also updated in the Review of imported supp.invoice table/program. In case of errors, the related error message is also displayed. For all records that have errors, you must correct the error and repeat the transfer.</mark> 

<mark>157</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Accounts payable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0158-01.png)


<mark>During transfer, a document record is created in the EDM table, that in turn creates an approval chain in Jeeves Workflow.</mark> 

#### 16.2.6 Approve or reject/reply supplier invoices 

<mark>You can approve a supplier invoice and send it to the next person/level in the approval chain or reject the invoice and send it back to the invoice clerk from the Jeeves client interface. This function facilitates invoice approval from within the Jeeves client particularly when using Jeeves Mobile client.</mark> 

You can display the list of approvers in the selected approval chain. When you click this button, a dialog opens displaying the level, name, and email ID (if available) of the approvers for the selected approval chain. 

#### 16.2.7 Payment terms 

<mark>You can store an unlimited number of payment terms, per supplier, per agreement or general. Credit periods and any early settlement discounts can be set up in numerous ways.</mark> 

<mark>There are several methods for calculating due dates:</mark> 

- <mark>Calculate period from arrival or invoice date or from beginning of following month(s)</mark> 

- <mark>Extend period to end of month</mark> 

#### 16.2.8 VAT 

<mark>In Jeeves Purchases/Accounts payable you set up suppliers and items for VAT. Your company can apply any number of VAT rates.</mark> 

<mark>See also separate sections describing VAT in the Sales module, VAT declarations, as well as VAT reporting within the European Union. There is also a number of country specific VAT declarations described among the localizations for the General ledger.</mark> 

#### 16.2.9 Advance invoice credit (on account) 

<mark>You can enter an invoice with this special type and pay it in advance. The invoice is automatically converted into a credit invoice and can later be used to credit the real invoice when it arrives.</mark> 

#### 16.2.10 Multiple tax amounts on supplier invoices 

<mark>It is possible to handle up to five VAT/tax amounts on the same supplier invoice.</mark> 

#### 16.2.11 Link to fixed assets ledger 

<mark>Fixed assets can automatically be entered when entering supplier invoices or general ledger vouchers by using an account with a dedicated account type. When you use an account of this type, you must specify whether the cost pertains to a new or an existing fixed asset.</mark> 

- <mark>If it pertains to an existing fixed asset and component, a transaction of the type Addition costs from supplier invoice is created. The acquisition amount and remainder to be depreciated are updated automatically on the fixed asset.</mark> 

- <mark>If it pertains to a new fixed asset or component, a transaction of the type Entry of new fixed asset is created. The component's fixed asset description is assigned the voucher number and, the supplier number is copied to the Supplier number field where applicable.</mark> 

#### 16.2.12 Canceling supplier invoices 

<mark>An invoice that has been entered incorrectly, for example with an incorrect amount or supplier ID, can be canceled if there is only one approval or if the balance on vouchers associated with each posting has been updated. The original invoice is allocated a new number by adding _1. This means that the invoice can be entered again with its original number and the correct information.</mark>

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Accounts payable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0159-01.png)


<mark>If you use e-approval of supplier invoices:</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP checks if there is a connected workflow instance in Jeeves Workflow that has not already been canceled. If there is a workflow instance connected, it will also be canceled. This eliminates the need for manual cancelation in Jeeves Workflow.</mark> 

###### **Cancelation of invoice in closed period** 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP, you can cancel a supplier invoice that has been arrival entered, or finally approved directly, even in closed periods.</mark> 

<mark>The invoice is paid with payment method MAK and arrival entry is reversed. The canceled invoice is renumbered so it can be re-entered. The VAT/tax accounting for the period in which the invoice was posted is not affected; the adjustment is made to the period in which the cancelation is posted.</mark> 

### 16.3 e-Application – e-Invoices 

#### 16.3.1 e-Invoices 

<mark>You can receive electronic invoices including invoice images via a third party service. There are standard functions in Jeeves ERP for interpretation, review and transfer to the accounts payable ledger. Approval can either be done in the accounts payable ledger or via Jeeves ERP's e-Approval module. The advantages of electronic invoicing are many; it is quicker, less expensive, better for the environment, minimizes administration etc.</mark> 

<mark>You can display electronic supplier invoices in a PDF browser. There are buttons for viewing invoices in an embedded browser and a button for viewing invoices in an external browser. This function requires that Adobe Acrobat Reader is installed on the client computer.</mark> 

<mark>API calls can also be used to import Supplier invoices and credit notes from integration partners (currently only Pagero) in the Peppol BIS Billing 3.0 format. The invoices are received via Pagero integration services, transformed into the JSON format, and sent to the Finance API for further processing. Companies can have access to multiple accounts in Pagero Online, in which case a Pagero company ID can be passed with the Peppol invoices during submission of eInvoice s (in and out), so Pagero can identify to which company the invoice belongs.</mark> 

<mark>The functionality can be set up so that only invoice headers are imported or also invoice rows. Additionally, business responses can be sent to the integration service, indicating whether the invoice is technically correct, accepted, or providing a reason if the invoice is rejected.</mark> 

### 16.4 Posting and integration with general ledger 

<mark>Supplier invoices are automatically posted and integrated with the general ledger according to general or supplier specific posting templates, and depending on the chosen method of arrival handling.</mark> 

### 16.5 Invoice matching against one or more purchase orders 

<mark>You can link a supplier invoice to one or more purchase order(s) in order to check it before final approval and posting. This can be set up to work in different ways, for example, you can control:</mark> 

- <mark>whether only received purchase order rows may be matched</mark> 

- <mark>whether received delivery reporting is allowed during invoice matching</mark> 

- <mark>if the company uses average cost prices, that cost prices are calculated upon final approval after invoice matching against received deliveries, instead of during entry of received deliveries</mark> 

- <mark>tolerances, i.e. maximum allowed differences between purchase order and invoice amounts</mark> 

<mark>You can handle any additional costs on the invoice, such as freight, any deviations in prices, partial deliveries, as well as any difference in quantity that is to be credited, or that is approved.</mark> 

<mark>159</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Accounts payable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0160-01.png)


#### 16.5.1 Automatic matching 

<mark>There is also a function for automatic matching of supplier invoices against purchase orders. Automatic matching means that if the data in the invoice agrees with the data in the received purchase order, and if the specified limits for differences have not been exceeded, matching and final approval will occur completely automatically and the invoice will be marked as ready for payment. Automatic matching forms a part of Jeeves e-Approval application, but a part of this functionality can be applied even if the e-application is not used.</mark> 

<mark>There are many requirements for automatic matching to function, including, among other things, a purchase order number must be specified on the supplier invoice, the invoice and purchase order must have the same supplier number, and the order must have been received or partially received. You can also specify if you want the delivery note number to be used in automatic matching of supplier invoices against purchase orders in order to improve the accuracy in the matching.</mark> 

<mark>For manual arrival entry of supplier invoices, you specify a purchase order number and arrival post the invoice on an interim account. In addition, you can control whether or not automatic review will be allowed when loading supplier invoices. If any of the imported invoices is incorrect, the incorrect invoices are skipped and all other invoices are set as OK.</mark> 

<mark>If automatic matching has been activated by a parameter, matching is carried out and a final approval record created when you save the arrival approval record. Otherwise, you activate it using the Auto-match invoice-PO button. You can also open imported supplier invoices and activate automatic matching with the Auto-match invoice-PO button if it has not been set up to occur automatically.</mark> 

<mark>You can also schedule automatic matching of several invoices at the same time and creation of matching records for the supplier invoices using an SQL procedure once the integration of received deliveries is completed. If received deliveries integration is not completed for any supplier invoice linked to a purchase order, that invoice is not matched and the process will continue to the next invoice that meets this requirement.</mark> 

<mark>Note that the entry date is automatically retrieved from the arrival voucher or set to today's date.</mark> 

### 16.6 e-Application – e-Approval 

#### 16.6.1 e-Approval 

<mark>Jeeves e-Approval is a function for the electronic distribution and approval of supplier invoices. This is built on the combination of a standard work flow in Jeeves Workflow and an e-application. The e-Approval work flow consists primarily of the following steps:</mark> 

<mark>1) Supplier invoices are scanned and stored in the form of Tif files and then interpreted.</mark> 

<mark>2) The files are imported, or, alternatively, the invoices can be opened and invoice data entered manually.</mark> 

<mark>If Pagero AB's service for receiving eInvoice s is used, Pagero creates an eInvoice  file in Jeeves ERP's standard format and a corresponding invoice file in Tif format, and notifies the user that there are files to be downloaded.</mark> 

<mark>3) Jeeves ERP’s programs for review of supplier invoices allow the accounts payable administration to open an image of the scanned invoice, enter approval data, and then transfer the invoice to accounts payable. Invoice receives status Pending approval.</mark> 

<mark>4) This creates one instance of the approval chain built according to the specified approval chain in Jeeves Workflow. At the same time, an e-mail is sent to the approver stating that an invoice has arrived for approval.</mark> 

<mark>5) The approver reviews the invoice in a special web interface for e-approval, and once the invoice is approved, a final approval record and a general ledger voucher that updates the balance are usually created. Alternatively, via a parameter, you can choose to let the accounts payable administration perform final review, approval, and balance update.</mark>

Jeeves ERP Accounts payable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0161-01.png)


### 16.7 Supplier payments 

<mark>Payments can be performed in two ways:</mark> 

- <mark>Manually (cash payments or manual payments over the internet) immediately marking the invoice as paid and creating a payment voucher in the general ledger</mark> 

- <mark>By payment file, with payments for several suppliers and payment dates, which is then sent to a financial institution. Before the file is sent, a payment proposal is created based on your payment rules and prioritizations. After matching against an imported payment file the invoices are finally flagged as paid and then posted.</mark> 

#### 16.7.1 Manual entry of individual payments 

<mark>You can enter individual payments of supplier invoices manually. For individual payments, you enter the payment and create the general ledger voucher directly. The invoices are considered as paid in the accounts payable ledger and general ledger.</mark> 

#### 16.7.2 Partial payments, discounts, deductions 

<mark>You can manually enter a partial payment, an early settlement discount or any other deduction, simply by double-clicking on the planned payment and changing the fields in question.</mark> 

<mark>You can set a parameter to automatically make proportional deductions of VAT when early settlement discounts take effect and other deductions are made on supplier invoices.</mark> 

#### 16.7.3 Bank and postal giro payments to suppliers 

<mark>You can handle bank and postal giro payments to suppliers in two ways:</mark> 

- <mark>Via an invoice payment service, which means that you send invoices to a bank and the bank monitors them and pays them on the due date.</mark> 

- <mark>Internal monitoring and payment. This approach means that you usually monitor your invoices yourself, select the ones you want to pay, create a bank or postal giro file and transfer it for immediate payment. This also creates a general ledger voucher. This in turn means that the invoices are considered to be paid in the accounts payable ledger and general ledger.</mark> 

#### 16.7.4 Individual supplier in invoice payment service 

<mark>While selecting approved invoices to be included in the invoice payment service journal, there is a possibility to select the invoices for an individual supplier by entering the company ID on the Invoice payment service program header.</mark> 

#### 16.7.5 Preliminary outgoing payments 

<mark>There is an alternative work method for the invoice payment service, which implies preliminary posting of outgoing payments.</mark> 

<mark>Preliminary outgoing payment means that you create an outgoing payment journal for the date on which you plan to pay a number of invoices, and then link the relevant invoices to the outgoing payment journal. It is then the planned date of payment in the outgoing payment journal that controls the actual payment date and not the due date on the supplier invoices.</mark> 

<mark>The outgoing payment journal can then be sent to the bank as an outgoing payment file. A voucher for 'Payment on the way' can be created when the payment file is sent from the outgoing payment journal. If such a voucher is created, it is counter-posted when the actual payment is acknowledged. When the acknowledgement is made by the bank, the outgoing payment journal normally only needs to be flagged as ready.</mark> 

<mark>161</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Accounts payable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0162-01.png)


#### 16.7.6 Priority 

<mark>You can set a priority directly on the supplier invoice, where 1 is the highest priority and 99 is the lowest priority. The priority is taken into account when a payment proposal is created.</mark> 

#### 16.7.7 Block supplier/invoice from outgoing payment 

<mark>You have the possibility to block all a supplier's open and new invoices, or individual invoices, from payment.</mark> 

#### 16.7.8 Payment before final approval 

<mark>Usually an invoice must be approved before it can be paid. In Jeeves ERP you can, however, specify that outgoing payments are allowed before final approval for individual suppliers or invoices.</mark> 

#### 16.7.9 Due date calculations 

<mark>Due dates can be calculated from arrival or invoice dates. (For details see the section on payment terms on supplier invoices.) Payment proposals can take early settlement discount days into consideration. There is also a possibility to automatically update the invoice if the date on a payment plan is changed.</mark> 

#### 16.7.10 Multiple accounts and currency accounts for outgoing payments 

<mark>If your company has multiple bank or plus giro numbers you enter them in the Own bank accounts program and link each to a payment method. When making outgoing payments you specify the relevant bank/plus giro number in the Account at bank field in the payment journal header.</mark> 

<mark>You can use different currency accounts for international payments. If the bank account is linked to a specific currency, the outgoing payment journal can only contain invoices in that currency.</mark> 

#### 16.7.11 Cancel erroneous outgoing payment 

<mark>It is possible to cancel an erroneous outgoing payment and reset the supplier invoice to unpaid. This is done by creating a new outgoing payment journal and entering the payment again but with a negative value. When you post this payment the status of the invoice is reset to Unpaid.</mark> 

#### 16.7.12 Reverse outgoing payment journal 

<mark>You can reverse a paid and posted payment journal. You can reverse all the payments in the journal if you, for any reason, need to re-open one or more paid supplier invoice(s).</mark> 

<mark>The journal can be reversed by pressing a button and you can determine how it should be done with an application parameter. The date of reversal with respect to the corresponding invoice is displayed in the Supplier invoices program. It is updated even for partial payments that are reversed and cannot be changed manually.</mark> 

<mark>When the journal is reversed, a new reversal journal is created with voucher updated in the General ledger (status = 8. Balance update complete). The reversal voucher counter-posts the original voucher. The supplier invoice is reset with previous status and outstanding amount. Alternatively, when the journal is reversed, a new reversal journal is created with status=1 Entry complete. The supplier invoice is reset with previous status and outstanding amount.</mark> 

<mark>You can also reverse individual invoices by creating a new outgoing payment journal and entering a negative outgoing payment record in the right window. You enter the supplier number, invoice number, and a negative amount. When you post the journal, the supplier invoice is reset with the previous status and outstanding amount.</mark> 

<mark>162</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Accounts payable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0163-01.png)


#### 16.7.13 Matching of completed payments 

<mark>Completed payments can be matched manually, or automatically by importing a payment file. There is also a function that can be used, if required, to capture incomplete transactions and add information to achieve a higher rate of matching.</mark> 

#### 16.7.14 Automatic matching 

<mark>Payments can be matched automatically by importing completed supplier payments in the form of a Bank giro file.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP creates one payment receipt journal per payment method for all invoices imported from file. The payment method is retrieved from each invoice. During import, Jeeves ERP checks that the supplier and invoice exist, that the amount is correct and that the invoice has not already been paid. Each invoice is flagged with its current status. Any incorrect invoices must be corrected manually.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP checks each invoice at import to see if it is included in an outgoing payment journal with status Entered, sent, and the status on journals is changed to Ready, paid when all invoices on the journal are matched at import and paid. You must then integrate the journal with the general ledger.</mark> 

#### 16.7.15 Manual matching 

<mark>There is a program for matching outgoing payments made through the invoice payment service. Only invoices that have been sent to the invoice payment service can be matched here.</mark> 

<mark>You match payments by creating an outgoing payment journal header, in which you specify how the outgoing payments were made (outgoing payment method), on which date and any bank cost, currency and rate.</mark> 

<mark>When you save the header, the invoice form opens in order for you to search for invoices, if necessary. You transfer the invoices back to the left list window in the journal form. Match and transfer the paid invoices to the right list window.</mark> 

#### 16.7.16 Capture of incomplete transactions 

<mark>For outgoing payment transactions that are missing information, and therefore cannot be matched with an invoice, you can manually add the integration source (for example, Manual entry), an account and any other account segments on the outgoing payment details in the embedded list in this program.</mark> 

<mark>You must also set the status to OK with other integration source, to indicate that the transaction has been completed manually and is ready for further processing.</mark> 

<mark>When creating an outgoing payment journal by pressing the Create journal button in the Outgoing payment receipts program, vouchers for these manually completed payment transactions are created and stored separately as interim vouchers.</mark> 

<mark>The interim vouchers can then be transferred to the general ledger in the normal manner, i.e. by flagging them ready and running the transfer report.</mark> 

#### 16.7.17 Miscellaneous payments 

<mark>The Miscellaneous payments functionality makes it quicker and easier to handle payments that are not connected to supplier invoices. This can be, for example, a payment for something other than an invoice, or a payment to/from the bank. You enter the amounts in the currency of the payment journal. The exchange rate in the journal header is used when integrating with the General ledger. You only enter the contra posting to the bank posting. The bank posting is created automatically.</mark>

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Accounts payable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0164-01.png)


#### 16.7.18 Advance payments 

<mark>To be able to handle advance payments when you have not received any invoice from the supplier but still want to be able to make a payment, there is an invoice type and payment method for advance credit. (This invoice type is mentioned above under Supplier invoices.) This type of invoice can also be created automatically. When a payment is entered with just a company ID and paid amount, i.e. without an invoice number, this automatically creates a supplier invoice of the type Advance invoice credit with final approval. You deduct the advance from the normal invoice when it arrives.</mark> 

#### 16.7.19 Recurring tax payments 

<mark>Many companies use the supplier payment routine for payments to the company's tax account against the tax authority's bank giro account. The tax authority identifies a company's tax payments through a 13 character long reference number.</mark> 

<mark>In order to be able to make recurring tax payments to the same supplier (the tax authority) and invoice number (tax reference number) you must enter a supplier invoice number more than 20 characters long and enter the tax reference number in the Invoice no OCR field. When you enter the numbers according to this method, the reference number is written to the bank giro payment file in place of the invoice number.</mark> 

### 16.8 Posting and integration with general ledger 

<mark>Outgoing payments are posted automatically according to posting templates, and integrated with the general ledger, by running a report program. When posting outgoing payments, the amount on the accounts payable account is counter-posted and the bank account decreased. Any bank overhead costs, exchange rate differences, price differences, early settlement discounts and deductions are also posted.</mark> 

#### 16.8.1 Manual posting of outgoing payments 

<mark>It is possible to manually specify account segment 1, 2, 3 and 4 for individual outgoing payments.</mark> 

#### 16.8.2 Reconciliations 

<mark>There is a program for on-screen reconciliation of the interim clearing account's balance in the ledger and the supplier debts in the general ledger.</mark> 

<mark>All supplier invoices with balances on interim account(s) in the accounts payable ledger for the processing date specified are displayed in the list. At the bottom, a total of the amount on the interim account is shown for the specified reconciliation date.</mark> 

### 16.9 Ledger information 

#### 16.9.1 Supplier statistics 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes forms and reports for received delivery and invoicing statistics per supplier. Invoicing statistics can be produced for any period.</mark> 

<mark>There is also a function for creating records for European sales lists, i.e. reports on arrivals and dispatches within the EU, including drill down to the invoices included in the dispatches and arrivals totals. This is to make it possible to reconcile the data in the report. This function can be used by all companies within the EU that purchase from and/or sell to other countries within the EU. A report can be printed from Jeeves ERP as a basis for manually filling in the official form.</mark> 

#### 16.9.2 Reconciliation of accounts payable ledger 

<mark>The accounts payable ledger must be reconciled against the general ledger regularly, for example, once a month. During reconciliation, the accounts payable ledger is checked against the accounts</mark> 

<mark>164</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Accounts payable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0165-01.png)


<mark>payable ledger account and the accounts payable interim account in the general ledger. The interim account can only be reconciled if invoices are arrival posted on an interim account before final posting in connection with final approval.</mark> 

<mark>The accounts payable ledger, or the outstanding debt, can be reconciled by printing a report or directly on the screen, after which a report is printed. The interim account can only be reconciled in report form. If required, you can print the entire accounts payable ledger as of the reconciliation period.</mark> 

<mark>You can also reconcile the accounts payable per date by entering the from and to dates in the relevant program. Transactions for the specified general ledger account and the accounts payable ledger for each of the dates in the date interval are generated.</mark> 

### 16.10 Other functions 

#### 16.10.1 Revaluation of accounts payable 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP you can revalue the accounts payable ledger at the current exchange rates. Exchange rate differences are posted automatically according to posting templates, and integrated with general ledger.</mark> 

<mark>There are three alternative ways of handling the revaluation:</mark> 

- <mark>as realized gains and losses in the accounts payable</mark> 

- <mark>as unrealized gains and losses in the accounts payable, which are reversed when the final exchange rate difference is determined, i.e. upon payment</mark> 

- <mark>create a voucher in the general ledger to show unrealized gains and losses, which is immediately and automatically reversed, without affecting the accounts payable</mark> 

### 16.11 Localizations/internationalization 

<mark>Most functions in Jeeves Accounts payable are common to all countries. There are some, however, that are specific to certain countries, as described below.</mark> 

#### 16.11.1 Invoice reference numbers and bank account validations 

<mark>Jeeves ERP supports localized reference numbers on supplier invoices such as OCR in Sweden, FIK code in Denmark, KID reference in Norway, bank reference in Finland and Internal subscriber number in Switzerland. Bank account numbers are validated according to local standards. There is also the possibility to add own formats and validations.</mark> 

#### 16.11.2 Manually enter Intrastat records on supplier invoices 

<mark>There is a possibility to manually enter information about purchases for reporting to Intrastat. You can manually enter invoice information without having to create a purchase order for the purchases or for the returns to suppliers.</mark> 

#### 16.11.3 Localized payments and international payments 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP, outgoing payment routes are used for communicating domestic and international payments, via banks, outside Sweden. These control the format of the payment file that you create with the Create payment file report. There are different file formats for localized payments, depending on the bank that makes the outgoing payment. A number of outgoing payment routes and/or stored procedures are supplied as standard.</mark> 

<mark>165</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Accounts payable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0166-01.png)


<mark>Some examples are:</mark> 

- <mark>Telepay – domestic and international payments via Norwegian banks</mark> 

- <mark>Credit advice (ISO 20022 XML) – domestic payments and cross border euro payments within the Single Euro Payments Area (SEPA)</mark> 

- <mark>DTAUS and DTAZV – domestic and international payments via German banks</mark> 

- <mark>BACS – domestic payments via British banks and building societies, as well as others, such as the Danske Bank</mark> 

- <mark>DTA – domestic and international payments via Swiss banks</mark> 

- <mark>ACH – domestic payments via United States banks</mark> 

- <mark>CFP – domestic and international payments via Nordea Corporate file payments</mark> 

<mark>There is also a procedure template, which can be adapted to create a payment file for other banks.</mark> 

#### 16.11.4 Payment routines for SEPA 

<mark>You can set the priority of international payments through payment services using SEPA format and set different conditions from specific banks and/or countries.</mark> 

<mark>The priority of international payments via payment service using SEPA format is as follows:</mark> 

- <mark>Normal = SEPA (Default)</mark> 

- <mark>Urgent = URGP</mark> 

- <mark>Same day value = SDVA</mark> 

<mark>A change bearer for SEPA can also be set. It can have the following values: 'DEBT', 'SHAR, CRED or SLEV. For SEPA, the default is SLEV.</mark> 

<mark>SEPA specific options can be used to set different conditions from specific banks and/or countries as follows:</mark> 

- <mark>Pos 1-1 - If the bank allows sub totals or if the currency should be retrieved from own accounts.</mark> 

- <mark>Pos 2-2 - Charge bearer for SEPA domestic payments.</mark> 

- <mark>Pos 3-3 - If currency code is to be picked from own accounts (syk) when creating payment journal.</mark> 

- <mark>Pos 4-4 - Charge bearer for SEPA international payments not IBAN.</mark> 

- <mark>Pos 5-5 – Instruction priority</mark> 

- <mark>Pos 6-6 - BANK or CUST in tag, depending on the bank.</mark> 

- <mark>Pos 7-7 - Payment per invoice on domestic invoices when summary on supplier is chosen.</mark> 

#### 16.11.5 Summarize payment for SEPA 

<mark>You can define if invoice information should be summarized in the SEPA payment file. If you select to summarize payments, the invoice information is grouped in the payment file for SEPA. This means that all payments on the same date and currency to each supplier are summarized into a total payment amount and information from all the invoices is specified on one row (when the file is unstructured) or each component of information from the invoices is shown in a separate row (when the file is structured). This needs to be done if you pay credit invoices because it is not allowed to send negative payment amounts in the SEPA payment file.</mark> 

<mark>Else, the information from the invoices is ungrouped in the payment file for SEPA. This means that each invoice is specified separately in the SEPA payment file.</mark> 

#### 16.11.6 Calculation of non-deductible VAT based on VAT amount 

<mark>Non-deductible VAT based on the VAT amount can be calculated and the results can be viewed whenever the VAT amount does not match with the VAT rate and invoice amount.</mark> 

<mark>In some countries, only a part of the calculated VAT amount is deductible. The deductible part of the VAT is posted to the VAT account, the non-deductible part is posted as a cost. You can view the</mark> 

> <mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

<mark>166</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Accounts payable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0167-01.png)


<mark>difference between the computed VAT and the actual entered VAT whenever the VAT/tax, curr amount field is changed and the VAT rate does not match.</mark> 

#### 16.11.7 Import and automatic matching of bank statements 

<mark>There is functionality for importing bank statements including customer and supplier payments and other bank transactions, matching the payments against open invoices and integrating the bank statement with the general ledger. The function is used in countries such as Germany and the Netherlands. Please refer to the chapter on</mark> _<mark>General ledger</mark>_ <mark>for a description of the function.</mark> 

#### 16.11.8 Payment to guarantee account 

<mark>In some countries the tax authorities require by law that main contractors pay a certain percentage to a special guarantee account when using the services of subcontractors. This is in order to guarantee that taxes and social security etc. can be paid even if a contract is not fully completed. When paying a supplier invoice the paid amount is divided between the supplier's normal bank account and this guarantee bank account according to the percentage specified in the Guarantee % field.</mark> 

#### 16.11.9 Import payments in Credit advice ISO 20022 XML, Camt054 format 

<mark>You can import supplier invoice payment files from banks in the Camt.054 format. The files are imported in the normal manner, using the Definition and import external data->work table program, and the result will be displayed in the Outgoing payment receipts program.</mark> 

<mark>Note that not only the setup is identical for both supplier and customer payments, the same payment file can also contain payments from both suppliers and customers. When importing, Jeeves ERP identifies the type of payment and stores them in the relevant program.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves uses End-to-end ID, a unique number that is a combination of payment date and time, to identify each payment in the payment file. It is updated during the creation of the SEPA file and is used to unambiguously identify the transaction when importing the CAMT053 and CAMT054 bank files. This unique identification key is passed on unchanged throughout the entire end-to-end supplier invoice payment chain and can be viewed in the Bank statement details embedded.</mark> 

#### 16.11.10 Overpayments 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP there is a possibility to allow overpayments, which can be applicable in some countries. If allowed, overpayments can be handled in two different ways:</mark> 

- <mark>If you simply enter an overpayment in the Plan/paid amount field, the supplier invoice will automatically be updated with a negative amount in the Remaining to pay field. The paid amount will be posted in the normal manner. The invoice can later be used as a credit invoice.</mark> 

- <mark>If you enter the overpaid amount as a negative amount in the Deducted amount field, the overpayment is handled and posted as a deduction.</mark> 

#### 16.11.11 Check payments 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP, you can pay supplier invoices by check. Check payments in Jeeves ERP’s accounts payable works the same way as other outgoing payments except it is possible to reconcile checks, rewrite them, etc. You can also reprint (print a copy) of checks with the same check number from the payment programs if required.</mark>

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Accounts payable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0168-01.png)


#### 16.11.12 Print checks for miscellaneous payments 

<mark>You can create/select miscellaneous check journals and print checks to employees or other persons not connected to the accounts payable and post the vouchers to the general ledger. This feature fulfills a requirement in the USA for printing miscellaneous checks for employees or others belonging to the employee cadre who are not regular suppliers. This is meant only to print checks and post them to the general ledger.</mark> 

<mark>Select the relevant payment method and account in the Print miscellaneous checks program. Enter the amount and company ID. This implies that you will need to define one company for employees or the like, in the company table. You can enter the check recipient’s name and address in the form before saving.</mark> 

#### 16.11.13 Cash supplier invoices (for EU stock replenishments) 

<mark>There is functionality for handling cash supplier invoices that are paid directly and posted according to own posting rules. This is primarily for use during intra community stock replenishments within the EU. You enter the supplier invoice in the normal manner but specify the cash payment terms and relevant posting template with the cash account. This results in the following, automatic steps, without any actual payment transaction:</mark> 

<mark>The paid amount is set according to the invoice amount including VAT and the outstanding amount is automatically set to zero.</mark> 

<mark>If the invoice is posted in two steps, after arrival entry and posting the supplier invoice is automatically assigned the status Paid, not posted. After final approval and posting the status is changed to Paid.</mark> 

<mark>If the invoice is posted in one step, after final approval and posting the supplier invoice is automatically assigned the status Paid. This requires that payment before final approval is allowed.</mark> 

#### 16.11.14 Interim posting of VAT/tax on supplier invoices 

<mark>You can handle supplier invoices with interim posting of VAT/tax, i.e. where the input VAT/tax cannot be credited until outgoing payment. The VAT/tax is posted to an interim account at supplier invoice entry. The deducted VAT/tax is then counter-posted against a definitive VAT/tax account on outgoing payment. In order for this to work, a posting template must be set up to direct the deducted VAT/tax to the intended account.</mark> 

#### 16.11.15 Poland — regulatory compliance 

##### 16.11.15.1 Split payments 

<mark>Polish VAT regulations require split payments for certain domestic business transactions involving specified goods and services when the transaction amount exceeds the applicable statutory threshold.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP supports the Polish split-payment mechanism for supplier payments and helps determine whether a payment should be made using split payment. The required split-payment information can be included in ISO 20022 pain.001 payment files.</mark> 

##### 16.11.15.2 KSeF information in payment processing 

KSeF identifiers can be included in payment references for outgoing supplier payments in ISO 20022 pain.001 format, supporting payment processing and traceability between supplier invoices and payments.
