Jeeves ERP Accounts payable 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0169-01.png)


#### 16.11.16 BOE ledger, bills issued to suppliers 

<mark>The bills of exchange ledger is a ledger for both received bills of exchange for payment from customers and issued bills of exchange for payments to suppliers. The BOE ledger in the accounts payable includes the following functionality:</mark> 

- <mark>Printing of bills</mark> 

- <mark>Posting of outgoing payments in two steps; 1) to the bill of exchange debt account and 2) bills of exchange paid</mark> 

- <mark>Preliminary payment and payment</mark> 

- <mark>Possibility to cancel a paid bill of exchange</mark> 

- <mark>Possibility to issue multiple bills of exchange against one invoice (part payments) or multiple invoices against one bill of exchange and one supplier</mark> 

<mark>Payment via bills of exchange is common in France, for example.</mark> 

#### 16.11.17 Global Cash Pool transfers 

<mark>If debit and credit account is not part of Nordea's Global Cash Pool service, then payment will be processed as an ordinary Intercompany payment. To enable processing of the payment as a Global Cash Pool transfer that does not attract an additional bank fee, you can now enter the code SDVA in the SEPA category purpose field in the AP payment routes program. In this case, SDVA is considered as the service level while processing the payment file, and the bank fee is not levied. Else, the existing value (NURG or SEPA) is considered as the service level while processing the payment file.</mark>

## 17 Jeeves ERP General ledger 

<mark>Jeeves General ledger is the central application in Jeeves ERP’s Financials suite, offering tools for planning, compiling business operations and analyzing the outcome in financial terms. It is completely integrated with all parts of Jeeves ERP that gives rise to financial transactions providing an overview of the entire business.</mark> 

### 17.1 Chart of accounts 

<mark>Jeeves ERP has eight accounting dimensions, called account segments. By default, segment 1 is called Account, 2 Cost center and 3 Cost unit, but this can be changed. Segments 4-7 can be used and named according to own requirements and the eighth segment is Project. Account segment IDs can be up to 8 alphanumeric characters and project IDs can be up to 10 alphanumeric characters.</mark> 

#### 17.1.1 Own account classifications 

<mark>You can define your own account classifications that can be used when searching for accounts or defining reports.</mark> 

#### 17.1.2 Currency accounts 

<mark>There is a possibility to limit postings to specific accounts to a certain currency.</mark> 

#### 17.1.3 Only debit or credit postings 

<mark>You can define that all postings to a certain account, or integration source, should be debit or credit postings, which is achieved by displaying a negative amount where necessary.</mark> 

#### 17.1.4 Detailed or aggregated postings 

<mark>You can also define whether all postings to a certain account should be detailed or aggregated. Detailed posting is used, for example, when posting and reconciling paid advance invoices from suppliers, also for bank accounts where you want to reconcile each individual invoice against a bank account statement.</mark> 

#### 17.1.5 Unique chart of accounts per fiscal year 

<mark>You can define a unique chart of accounts per fiscal year. The company's chart of accounts is copied between fiscal years and any variances between the fiscal years are maintained manually.</mark> 

<mark>It is possible to add and translate own accounts to the standard chart of accounts, as well as enter and translate own, say industry or country specific charts of accounts.</mark> 

#### 17.1.6 Change chart of accounts 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes unique functionality for supporting change-over to a new chart of accounts, with the possibility to automatically change the accounts used in the various posting templates with a minimum of manual activities.</mark> 

### 17.2 Daily routines 

<mark>The daily routines mainly consist of entering manual vouchers and posting integration journals from the sub systems, handling incorrect vouchers and if desired, performing daily reconciliations on account, segment or total level.</mark> 

#### 17.2.1 General ledger vouchers 

<mark>The General Ledger is the destination where all manually entered and automatically generated postings from the sub systems finally end up. Through automatic allocation rules, new postings can</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

<mark>170</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

Jeeves ERP General ledger 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0171-01.png)


<mark>be created or further details can be added to the financial transactions. Before a balance is updated several controls are made.</mark> 

#### 17.2.2 Integrated vouchers 

<mark>The general ledger is integrated with the logistics, production and project modules as well as the accounts receivable, accounts payable and fixed assets ledgers. This implies that vouchers are created automatically from these modules. All account segments can be used in the integrated modules, according to the general requirements from IFRS.</mark> 

<mark>The integration implies a high level of transparency and traceability. From a voucher you can directly identify and drill down to the source transaction in the relevant sub system. From a source transaction you can, in the same way, directly identify and drill down to the linked vouchers.</mark> 

#### 17.2.3 Manual voucher entry 

<mark>General ledger vouchers can be entered manually, with or without posting templates. You can also use shortcut codes for default combinations of account segments. The balance can either be updated directly or by running a journal or voucher list.</mark> 

<mark>You can type in a short text on vouchers and on individual rows. There are shortcut fields in several programs to simplify the entry of voucher rows, specially when the posting contains many account segments.</mark> 

<mark>In the manual voucher entry program, you can copy an existing voucher with its account, account segments and amounts. The Manual entry checkbox and the Copy voucher allowed checkbox must be checked on the integration source. This is helpful in case of recurring vouchers where most of the account structure is the same and only minor adjustments are to be made.</mark> 

#### 17.2.4 Manual voucher entry with quantity 

<mark>You can also specify a quantity on voucher rows, i.e. the number of pieces, packages, hours, or the like, that the invoice row or the voucher amount concerns.</mark> 

#### 17.2.5 Voucher number series 

<mark>You can choose between automatic and manual numbering of vouchers, which is controlled per user. You set up different number series for different subsystems or modules. You can also divide them into sub-number series and link users and/or groups to the sub-number series.</mark> 

#### 17.2.6 Voucher controls 

<mark>Only authorized users can enter data in any of the financials modules.</mark> 

<mark>You can hide any account segments and other fields that are not used in your company's accounts, and design tabs with only relevant fields. If your installation is based on the Jeeves or Selected package, you will already have pre-designed tabs and frames.</mark> 

<mark>You determine if and when individual accounts and other account segments can be used. A validity date interval can be specified for accounts, cost centers, cost units (account segment 1-3), segment 7 and Projects.</mark> 

<mark>For each account you can specify which other account segments are allowed to be used in combination with the account. You can also specify whether an account segment is mandatory for the account.</mark> 

<mark>You can set up a difference to be automatically balanced. If a difference arises between debit and credit during voucher entry, the amount is balanced if the difference falls below the amount you enter in this field.</mark> 

<mark>A voucher can encompass several periods, but to be able to update the balance, the voucher must balance within each individual period.</mark> 

<mark>171</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP General ledger 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0172-01.png)


#### 17.2.7 Voucher corrections 

<mark>You can analyze incorrect vouchers from several different programs, both on-screen and through running a report. Incorrect vouchers can be corrected in underlying program nodes or by rightclicking to the voucher entry program.</mark> 

<mark>There is a function to automatically reverse an existing voucher, for example if a voucher contains many errors. This creates a new voucher in which debit and credit have switched places. Also, there is a possibility to reverse the voucher in the same period as the original posting.</mark> 

<mark>There is also a function for re-opening a closed voucher for changes. This can only be done if a number of conditions have been met. It is not allowed to unlock a voucher that contains an auto posting template. Updates to the general ledger are reversed and any flags indicating the voucher has been printed are removed. Furthermore, the voucher header is flagged to indicate that the voucher has been re-opened. Any changes to updated vouchers are logged. You can complete and change a re-opened voucher in the same way as at new entry.</mark> 

#### 17.2.8 Deleting vouchers 

<mark>You can delete vouchers that are more than ten years old. The general ledger posting date is used to check if the voucher is old enough to be deleted.</mark> 

#### 17.2.9 Account reconciliation 

<mark>There is a program to query on screen and/or print account balances per a specific date. In an underlying program node you can display information on associated voucher details for every balance row.</mark> 

#### 17.2.10 Flagging voucher rows 

<mark>There is a help function for marking a selection of voucher rows with a text, for example for marking a selection of voucher rows that have been reconciled. The text is displayed in various programs for viewing vouchers and voucher rows.</mark> 

#### 17.2.11 Automatic posting/allocation on vouchers 

<mark>You can define rules to automatically supplement or allocate postings, at the same moment as a certain posting is created. This can be used, for example, to distribute rent or the like to subsidiaries, to supplement postings with additional account segments or even with additional posting rows, etc. When an amount is posted to the account and any account segments specified for automatic allocation, postings are automatically created, either by creating new rows in the same voucher or by amending the original voucher row. The normal posting is debit if the source account basis is debit, and vice versa.</mark> 

<mark>By using retrieval codes you can decide which normal and contra account segments are to be used. If the automatic postings are to be posted to the same account segment as the source row, you enter an asterisk (*). If you enter an equal to character (=), the value is instead retrieved according to what is specified in the auto posting rule.</mark> 

<mark>You control the amount to be automatically posted using a percentage or by retrieving a constant.</mark> 

#### 17.2.12 Period balance queries and general ledger information 

<mark>There are programs specially designed to make it quick and simple to make queries regarding period balances per account and account segment, in any currency, with the possibility to drilldown to underlying vouchers. You can use these queries for reconciliations per currency. You can also view any budget details for the selected account and account segment. Furthermore, you can print reports based on your queries.</mark> 

<mark>There are also programs for requesting printouts of the general ledger, for specific accounts or account segments or in its entirety, either detailed printouts or only totals, as well as programs for printing income statements and balance sheets.</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark>

Jeeves ERP General ledger 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0173-01.png)


### 17.3 Periodic routines 

<mark>The periodic routines mainly consist of creating periodic vouchers, reconciling the integrated modules with the general ledger and creating external statutory reports. Typical periodic vouchers are automatic postings based on account balances, allocation of account balances to new accounts or segments, posting of fixed vouchers and finally generation of period closing vouchers.</mark> 

#### 17.3.1 Automatic posting of balances 

<mark>Auto posting can be used to calculate, post and allocate business transactions related to a given account, based on the balance on the account and/or cost centers, cost units, etc. This can be used, for example, to calculate payroll tax or vacation pay based on the balance on the salary account. In most cases the source account's balance should not be affected.</mark> 

<mark>You can select the account segments whose balances are to be included in the calculation and the accounts and any other account segments which the auto posting is to be posted to.</mark> 

<mark>By using retrieval codes you can decide which normal and contra account segments are to be used.</mark> 

- <mark>If the account segment(s) are to be identical to the source row, you only specify an asterisk (*) in relevant field(s) for the retrieval code.</mark> 

- <mark>If you only want one specific account segment to be used as normal and/or contra account segment, you enter an equal sign (=) in relevant fields for retrieval code and the account segments in question in the next field.</mark> 

- <mark>You can also enter account segments via a separate translation table. In this case you enter a greater than sign (>) in the relevant fields, which will retrieve the translations via the original account segment.</mark> 

<mark>You determine the required calculation, by either using a constant or a percentage.</mark> 

#### 17.3.2 Automatic allocation of balances 

<mark>Auto allocation can be used to allocate an account balance, or the total of a number balance rows, to a number of other account segments. This could be used for accumulated costs that are first posted to accounts without specifying any cost centers, which need to be periodically allocated to profit centers. It could also be used to add together a number of balance rows regarding costs, and allocate them to cost centers according to certain proportions.</mark> 

<mark>You enter the accounts and any other account segments, to which the auto allocation is to be posted on.</mark> 

<mark>By using retrieval codes you can decide which normal and contra account segments are to be used.</mark> 

- <mark>If the account segment(s) will be identical to the source row, you only specify an asterisk (*) in relevant field(s) for the retrieval code.</mark> 

- <mark>If you only want one specific account segment to be used as normal and/or contra account segment, you enter an equal sign (=) in the relevant fields for retrieval code and the account segments in question in the next field.</mark> 

- <mark>You can also enter account segments via a separate translation table. In this case you enter a greater than sign (>) in the relevant fields, which will retrieve the translations via the original account segment.</mark> 

#### 17.3.3 Fixed periodic vouchers 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP, you can enter vouchers in advance, interim vouchers, which are posted periodically according to a template. You can use the function to handle recurrent transactions with fixed amounts, for example insurance and other fees.</mark> 

<mark>173</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP General ledger 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0174-01.png)


#### 17.3.4 Allocations over periods 

<mark>Jeeves ERP has two functions for allocating transactions of an interim nature over several periods. You can use the functions to allocate prepaid costs, such as rent, insurance premiums, fees or telephone costs, which are invoiced per quarter or per year and need to be distributed over several periods in the accounts.</mark> 

<mark>One of the functions is based on pre-defined allocation templates and the other, direct allocation, is based on a user dialog that poses a set of questions. The user's answers are used to create a temporary allocation template. Both alternatives can be used in parallel.</mark> 

<mark>There are two alternatives for setting up pre-defined allocation templates; one does not exclude the other.</mark> 

<mark>Alt 1: The whole cost is posted directly to an interim account. The allocation template contains the cost account to which the periodic allocation is to be made, and the periods in question. One template must be defined per cost and revenue type.</mark> 

<mark>Alt 2: The whole cost is posted directly to the relevant cost account. The allocation template contains the relevant interim account and to which periods allocation is to be done. There is also a row for transferring the entire cost to the interim account. The same template can be used for different types of costs and revenues.</mark> 

<mark>If you specify an allocation template for a voucher row, or at approval of supplier invoices, interim vouchers are automatically created based on the posting on the row when the voucher updates the general ledger balance. The basis can also be entered and created manually in a dedicated program.</mark> 

<mark>If you use the button for direct allocation, when you click OK, the basis for the periodic vouchers is created. These can be viewed and changed in a node. The amounts for each period can be changed, but the total of the amounts cannot differ from the sum on the account.</mark> 

#### 17.3.5 Trial balance 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes functions for preparing trial balances for the accounts receivable and accounts payable for any given period, also for specific accounts, customers or suppliers if desired. There are reports for printing the trial balance for the accounts receivable and the accounts payable.</mark> 

#### 17.3.6 VAT/tax reporting 

<mark>VAT reporting is set up using Jeeves VAT matrix, which is designed solely to meet VAT requirements from various countries. The aim is to make the grid solution as general as possible to allow all countries to build their country specific VAT/tax declarations based on the solution. This allows compilation of preliminary and final VAT/tax declarations and the possibility to split financial periods on a VAT declaration, for example if supplier invoices are received after the declaration has been submitted.</mark> 

<mark>The grid is built up by defining all relevant combinations of:</mark> 

- <mark>markets (represented by customer/supplier categories),</mark> 

- <mark>VAT/tax codes,</mark> 

- <mark>VAT/tax types (goods, services etc.),</mark> 

- <mark>transaction types (country specific definitions) or standard integration sources and</mark> 

- <mark>accounts</mark> 

<mark>each connected to a VAT/tax box in the declaration form. You define either an account or one or more intervals of accounts to which a box in the grid applies. The grid and tax codes can be set up in various ways using VAT/tax types and VAT/tax transaction types, and can be set up for special cases, such as reverse charge on purchases.</mark> 

<mark>If your company has several VAT/tax registration numbers you define them in the VAT reporting units program. These can be in the same country and/or in several different countries. The VAT reporting unit is retrieved to the business transactions and resulting voucher rows, and can be</mark> 

<mark>174</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP General ledger 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0175-01.png)


<mark>selected when creating VAT reports via the VAT grid, EC Sales lists, Intrastat reports or other reports used for internal and external reporting.</mark> 

<mark>On each reporting unit you specify the VAT/tax registration number, the tax office to which the VAT should be reported and in which currency. In an underlying node you can add/view the (ship from) inventory location(s) that are connected to the reporting unit. All relevant inventory locations must be connected to a reporting unit. In another node you define invoice No. series per reporting unit, which is mandatory for companies registered for VAT in several countries. You can also define an item's VAT code per country, if an item is sold from two different reporting units/countries where the VAT/tax rate is differentiated. The amount in the VAT report can be viewed in the reporting unit’s currency.</mark> 

<mark>You can automatically set the VAT code on the order and choose the text to be printed on invoices when you are selling goods exempt of VAT. This setting also recognizes if there are areas within a country that are exempt from VAT.</mark> 

<mark>When producing the VAT/tax declaration you can select VAT/tax periods from the accounting periods defined in Fiscal year and periods. Alternatively you can define the periods in your VAT/tax statements, if your VAT/tax periods differ from your accounting periods. You can create VAT/tax declarations for more than one period (for example, a quarter) before you print the report and you can use the function as many times as you want, for example if you have made corrections for a period, as long as you have not set the period to Ready.</mark> 

<mark>You can have different VAT/tax periods for different reporting units. For example, you can have VAT/tax periods 1-12 for one unit and just 4 periods for another unit.</mark> 

<mark>There is also a number of country specific VAT declarations, as mentioned in the section on localizations for the General ledger.</mark> 

<mark>If a user makes mistakes it is sometimes necessary to have the option to re-open the VAT report. There is a function for setting the status of a VAT report back to Preliminary, which gives the possibility to re-create the VAT basis, set the report status to Final and re-print it. This can only be done in the last period for which the VAT report has been finalized.</mark> 

<mark>You can reopen the VAT reports that are balanced and closed. This can be done by counter-posting the VAT balance journal.</mark> 

#### 17.3.7 One Stop Shop (OSS) 

<mark>One Stop Shop (OSS) affects EU based businesses that sell to private customers (B2C) in other EU countries. OSS is a way to report tax for sales over €10,000 per year, but it can be done even for smaller amounts.</mark> 

<mark>OSS implies that B2C sellers dispatching their goods from a single country are not required to register for foreign VAT and complete multiple VAT filings in countries where they are selling. Instead, they may opt to simply complete and file a new OSS filing alongside their regular domestic VAT return that will list all their pan-EU sales.</mark> 

#### 17.3.8 Standard audit file for VAT/tax (SAF-T) 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes functions to prepare VAT codes for export of financial data in order to prepare Standard audit files for VAT/tax in accordance with the legal requirements in OECD member countries.</mark> 

<mark>There is also a general program for exporting financial data and creating standard audit files. The purpose is to provide a program that can be used for creating audit files in all countries.</mark> 

<mark>You can enter valid from and to dates for the export definition and can control whether the export should be based on the date interval. You control this on the individual export procedures, depending on the country for which the files are being prepared, for e.g., Norway, Denmark, or Poland.</mark>

Jeeves ERP General ledger 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0176-01.png)


#### 17.3.9 Intrastat reporting 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes functions for producing the required declarations of dispatches (including twodigit codes) and arrivals between the company’s own country and other countries within the EU, including drill down to the invoices included in the dispatches and arrivals totals. This is to make it possible to reconcile the data in the report.</mark> 

<mark>If you choose to use the functions, you can either use the information generated as a basis to manually fill out the Intrastat form or you can compile the Intrastat report in an external report generator. You can also export the data to MS Excel using Jeeves ERP's Dynamic reports. You can then use Intrastat Data Entry Package (IDEP), which is free software that is available on the web, to automatically import your Intrastat reports into the tax authority’s web site.</mark> 

<mark>One record is generated per TARIC code, country, dispatch terms, transaction type, Intrastat transport method, and per country of origin for arrivals, based on customer invoice rows and invoice-matched purchase order row. The statistics include only sales and purchases from other countries (in other words where the country code <> your own country's country code) within the EU trading area.</mark> 

<mark>The header includes definition of the report period, data about reporting manager as well as buttons for generation/deletion of dispatch and arrival records for the period. Generated totals are displayed in the header.</mark> 

<mark>You can search in the left or right embedded list for any erroneous records, i.e. records with missing values, and correct them. You can search on "Taric code missing" in the Taric code field, on "Intrastat data missing" in the EU report message field. You can analyze the transactions in more detail by selecting the desired record in the embedded list window and then opening the log node. There are also dedicated programs to make it easier to search among details in Intrastat statistics.</mark> 

<mark>You can view the summarized values from Intrastat report period and generation and European sales list programs in the VAT/tax reporting periods programs.</mark> 

<mark>You can group invoice amounts by TARIC code by checking the relevant checkbox in the VAT/tax reporting units program.</mark> 

<mark>Returns and replacement deliveries are reported in the Intrastat report according to the direction of the delivery, i.e. received goods as purchases and sent goods as sales, and depending on the company's duty to report purchases and/or sales.</mark> 

<mark>From January 2022 when reporting intrastat export sales to SCB, you must send the reports with 2- digit codes and grouping must be done on EU VAT/tax registration number and Country of origin code.</mark> 

#### 17.3.10 Goods in transit 

<mark>Jeeves ERP's includes functionality for posting goods in transit, i.e. interim accounting of items that have been shipped by the supplier, but have not yet been received by the customer. One of the two companies must add the cost of the goods in transit to the cost of the inventory that it owns. It is the delivery terms that determines which company is to report the goods in transit as its inventory.</mark> 

<mark>You create the postings manually each month before closing the period.</mark> 

#### 17.3.11 Automatic clearance of VAT accounts 

<mark>You can automatically clear the company's VAT accounts at the end of each period, after you have sent the VAT report to the tax authorities. The balance on the clearance account shows the VAT amount to receive or to pay to the tax authorities.</mark> 

<mark>You compile the balances for all VAT accounts from voucher rows in a journal. You review and approve the result before setting the status to Ready and creating the voucher. When you create the voucher, the balances on the VAT accounts are cleared and the VAT balance journal's status is set to Closed. Finally, you can print the voucher from the VAT balance journal if desired.</mark> 

<mark>176</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP General ledger 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0177-01.png)


#### 17.3.12 Period closing vouchers 

<mark>There is a function that simplifies the entry of period closing vouchers through reversal, even over the year end.</mark> 

<mark>You create period closing vouchers by specifying in the voucher header that you want to reverse the voucher on a date in another period. Only future periods can be entered manually in the Reversal date field. When you enter the reversal date, the integration source is replaced with Period closing voucher and the voucher number from this series is retrieved on the source voucher as well as the reversal voucher. When you approve the voucher, a reversed voucher is automatically created on the specified reversal date.</mark> 

#### 17.3.13 Reconciling and closing periods 

<mark>You can reconcile the integrated modules with the general ledger on screen and/or using reports. You can close periods at any time, for all or individual modules. A period can either be closed as the last step, or as the first step, and then opened only for the user(s) involved in the reconciliations. Jeeves ERP includes dedicated programs for reconciliation work, including:</mark> 

- <mark>Preparations (for example, checking journals from other modules and general ledger vouchers and creating periodic vouchers)</mark> 

- <mark>Reconciliation of work in progress</mark> 

- <mark>Reconciliation of inventory (including calculation of inventory value per end of period)</mark> 

- <mark>Reconciliation of goods in transfer</mark> 

- <mark>Reconciliation of order-specific inventory</mark> 

- <mark>Reconciliation of dispatched, not yet invoiced deliveries</mark> 

- <mark>Reconciliation of received, not yet invoice matched deliveries</mark> 

- <mark>Reconciliation of accounts receivable ledger</mark> 

- <mark>Reconciliation of accounts payable ledger</mark> 

- <mark>Reconciliation of balanced projects</mark> 

- <mark>Reconciliation of fixed assets ledger</mark> 

- <mark>Reconciliation of interim account(s)</mark> 

- <mark>Reconciliation of VAT/tax accounts</mark> 

- <mark>Corrections</mark> 

- <mark>Closing work (including printing VAT/tax reports)</mark> 

- <mark>Closing the period</mark> 

#### 17.3.14 Liquidity forecasts 

<mark>There is a liquidity planning function for analysis of planned incoming and outgoing payments already due or that will become due within given time intervals.</mark> 

<mark>You can create liquidity plans for all customers and/or suppliers or for an individual customer or supplier. You choose whether the planning should also include open orders and purchase orders. You can see which orders, purchase orders and invoices are covered by the planning. You can include time and expenses from assignments/projects. You can also enter manual transactions of different types, either one-off transactions such as extraordinary payments, or recurring transactions such as salaries and company tax.</mark> 

<mark>You can calculate opening cash balance on accounts marked as Cash accounts. You can automatically calculate and include VAT amounts from customer orders, purchase orders, upcoming assignment invoices and VAT accounts in the liquidity forecast. Alternatively, you can enter VAT transactions manually in the program node for manual transactions.</mark> 

<mark>You can specify whether the forecast should be calculated in real credit time, i.e. based on the customer/supplier's average (actual) credit time (invoice date-payment date), instead of nominal credit time, i.e. based on the current payment terms on the invoice (invoice date-due date). It is even possible to create a retroactive forecast, i.e. analyze the liquidity for a specific date back in time.</mark> 

<mark>177</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP General ledger 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0178-01.png)


<mark>You can view the forecast in the Jeeves program form, print the forecast if desired, and you can display the forecast in the form of a matrix. A report definition has been prepared for the liquidity forecast in Jeeves ERP report generator and is provided as part of Jeeves ERP packaging.</mark> 

<mark>There is a function for copying the currently selected liquidity forecast to a new one. When you press the button, all the parameter settings and conditions are copied to a new forecast number. You can make any changes to the settings and conditions, then run the calculation by pressing the Create forecast button. You can then delete, adjust or add any individual transactions and re-run the calculation.</mark> 

### 17.4 Annual routines 

<mark>The annual routines consist of creating a new fiscal year, importing and modifying the chart of accounts and generating opening balances.</mark> 

#### 17.4.1 Opening balances 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP you can produce opening balances for a new fiscal year in three ways:</mark> 

- <mark>Automatically generate an opening balance based on the previous year's outcome.</mark> 

- <mark>Automatically create an opening balance voucher via Jeeves conversion routine. You can also translate accounts in this routine.</mark> 

- <mark>Enter opening balances manually.</mark> 

<mark>Manual entry is done in the normal voucher entry program but with period 0.</mark> 

<mark>Automatic generation creates an opening balance voucher based on the balance table for the previous year. You can generate opening balances for several companies at the same time. Opening balance can be created several times for a fiscal year. The voucher contains the difference between the previous year's outcome and the current year's previously transferred opening balance, i.e. if an opening balance is created several times, the voucher only contains the changes between the batches. The opening balance voucher is not entered directly in the General Ledger, but in the interim voucher table, where you can make changes before transferring it to the General Ledger.</mark> 

#### 17.4.2 Change of fiscal year 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP, you can create and work in an unlimited number of fiscal years. The year can have any number of periods and you can apply split fiscal years if required.</mark> 

<mark>You run a program that copies all definitions on the accounts and other account segments (cost centers, cost units etc.) to the new year. It also copies all tables containing rules for integration with the general ledger.</mark> 

<mark>You can generate fiscal years for several companies at the same time by setting a parameter in the system data for the companies and running this program from the template company to which they are linked.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes functionality for replication between fiscal years to make it easier to manage changes in the charts of accounts and posting rules between fiscal years. This applies to new records, changes and deletions. If you would like to avoid deletion in subsequent fiscal years, check the Do not replicate to FY field before you delete the record.</mark> 

#### 17.4.3 Import and export of chart of accounts 

<mark>Jeeves provides functionality for importing chart of accounts and SRU-codes from external sources.</mark> 

<mark>You can also export a chart of accounts to file in order to import it into another database.</mark> 

<mark>178</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP General ledger 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0179-01.png)


### 17.5 Report generator 

<mark>Jeeves ERP's report generator is primarily used to compile values from the accounting tables in the database, for example to produce balance sheets and income statements with different layouts and cash forecasts.</mark> 

<mark>The report generator can handle all tables in the database. The report generator aggregates data by row and column. The report generator is best used in income statements and balance reports, where data must be totaled and presented in matrix format. Other matrix reports, such as sales statistics that are aggregated per district and customer category or item group, can also be produced with the report generator.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP's in-house developed report generator works with all SQL databases as long as the database, tables and columns/terms are defined.</mark> 

<mark>Besides compiling data from the database, you can also add free texts, own calculations etc.</mark> 

#### 17.5.1 Sub reports 

<mark>Reports can be broken down in several levels, for example per cost center or to show certain cost units’ share of the values for overlying cost centers. Sub reports are defined separately, and can therefore be reused in several reports.</mark> 

#### 17.5.2 Report specifications 

<mark>You use this function when you want to print a specification of one, several or all rows included in a report or sub report. Specifications of account ranges, headings, totals, etc. are defined separately, and can therefore be re-used in several reports.</mark> 

#### 17.5.3 Formats on numeric values 

<mark>You can define formats for numeric values in reports. You set the desired formats on the columns in your report definition. Any deviating formats can be determined by setting other formats on the rows in the report. From a drop-down list you can choose between the default formats from the Date and currency formats per company program, the predefined formats for the report generator as well as own defined formats.</mark> 

#### 17.5.4 Report groups 

<mark>You can use report groups to increase efficiency in generating the company's reports that need to be produced at regular intervals. A report group consists of several report requests that are to be generated at the same time. For example, you can create report groups for weekly or monthly printouts.</mark> 

#### 17.5.5 Distribution lists 

<mark>Jeeves ERP's report generator includes a function for creating distribution lists, i.e. lists of recipients of the reports. If you use this function you can print all reports from a generated batch per recipient and, with up to six selection codes, extract exactly the sub reports the recipient wants, for example only sub reports on the recipient's own cost center.</mark> 

#### 17.5.6 Presentation of generated reports 

<mark>Besides being able to print the reports, the results can be stored in unique tables per report definition, which allows you to display them on screen and drill-down to the underlying transactions. The values are stored in their own columns that you can access using normal SQL commands. In this case, Jeeves ERP also automatically creates a program in which you can view and search among the results in the same way as in normal Jeeves programs.</mark>

Jeeves ERP General ledger 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0180-01.png)


### 17.6 Financial statements in SQL Server Reporting Services 

<mark>Jeeves ERP offers functionality to generate financial statements for a specified company or group, account segments and project, using SQL Server Reporting Services (SSRS). The reports can be based on entities such as companies, which are grouped a dedicated Jeeves program.</mark> 

<mark>It is possible to show Profit and loss and Balance sheet data respectively for twelve consecutive months (rolling 12-month) regardless of which base month is selected, from the current month and twelve months backwards in time, or starting earlier than the current month. For Profit and loss, the report contains information for years/periods as specified and an additional column with the accumulated value for the 12 periods.</mark> 

### 17.7 Budget 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP you can enter budgets on all levels in the business, cost and project accounts. The budget routine uses the chart of accounts in the general ledger for each year, which means that the account's budget rules, account segment rules etc. are also applied. With the use of allocation curves the budgeted amounts can be allocated over the year’s periods according to predefined rules.</mark> 

<mark>A budget can be created/entered in three ways:</mark> 

<mark>1) Enter budget records for the fiscal year with simultaneous allocation over the year's periods.</mark> 

<mark>2) Load/enter "preliminary" budget records for the fiscal year and then allocate over the year's periods and convert to "normal" budget.</mark> 

<mark>3) Enter budget details without using allocation curves.</mark> 

<mark>If you use automatic allocation you can choose between percentages per period, with automatic allocation of the remaining amount if required, or even allocation over the periods.</mark> 

#### 17.7.1 Budget revisions 

<mark>You can define budget revisions according to own requirements. You can use budget revisions to identify which revision budget transactions belong to, and to update the budget balance table for a certain revision, as well as to lock a budget after updating the balances.</mark> 

### 17.8 Import/Conversion 

<mark>Jeeves ERP's conversion routine is mainly used to load data from external systems, for example to load accounting data before implementing a new Jeeves installation. You can, however, use the routine for other purposes.</mark> 

<mark>Conversion is governed by tables and means that the external accounting material is changed to Jeeves record layout, after any account translations, according to the company’s posting rules.</mark> 

<mark>Conversion is made by first describing the layout of the source table (the external system file) and its terms, and then describing the processing, i.e. the separate steps in the conversion, any conditions for conversion to take place as well as a description of how the terms in the source table should create terms in the Jeeves ERP record.</mark> 

<mark>The conversion creates a voucher header that shows (among other things) the posting’s total balance and individual voucher rows. Compression can be made, which means transactions with the same posting are aggregated. The result of the conversion is stored in an interim table where the transactions can be accessed for correction. When any errors have been corrected you transfer the interim vouchers to general ledger.</mark> 

#### 17.8.1 Company consolidation 

<mark>You can use the conversion routine for group consolidation. The counterpart term can be used to eliminate transactions between companies within the group.</mark>

### 17.9 Other functions 

#### 17.9.1 Revaluation of the general ledger 

<mark>There is a function for revaluation of the general ledger at the current exchange rates and posting of exchange rate differences based on posting templates.</mark> 

<mark>You can run revaluation with or without reversal of the revaluation postings in the next period.</mark> 

<mark>All accounts that have been flagged for revaluation in the chart of accounts are included in the revaluation, with the exception of accounts receivable and accounts payable accounts, which are revaluated separately.</mark> 

### 17.10 Localizations/internationalization 

<mark>Most functions in Jeeves General ledger are common to all countries. There are some, however, that are specific to certain countries, as described below.</mark> 

#### 17.10.1 Import/export (Sweden only) 

<mark>There is a function for importing SIE files of type 4. You can either create a new company with a chart of accounts, posting templates etc. based on a template company in connection with the import, or you can import only vouchers to an existing company.</mark> 

<mark>You can either import vouchers directly into the general ledger or load them into the interim voucher table first and transfer them to the general ledger later. The latter makes it possible to search for and correct erroneous vouchers before they are transferred to the general ledger.</mark> 

#### 17.10.2 Tax declaration (Sweden only) 

<mark>There is a function to create and store Swedish tax declaration file in your system for upload to the Swedish tax agency. You can use the relevant button in the VAT/Tax reporting periods program to save the file in XML format and use it for electronic filing of the tax declaration. You can add any additional information in the file using the text editor.</mark> 

#### 17.10.3 Standardized account statement (SRU) 

_SRU coding is a localization for the Swedish market but can be used in any country for own grouping of accounts._ 

<mark>You can link the accounts in the chart of accounts to the account groups in the standardized account statement, either account by account or via a help table that works with intervals of accounts.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP provides a standard chart of accounts and SRU codes, in both English and Swedish along with functions for import and export. The chart of accounts and SRU codes can be imported in new installations and when creating new fiscal years.</mark> 

<mark>Each account can be linked to up to 3 different SRU codes. SRU code 2 and 3 can be used if an account occurs in several SRU groupings. If you have loaded the basic chart of accounts, there is a large number of standard records for the earnings statement for incorporated companies, financial institutions, etc., already filled in.</mark> 

#### 17.10.4 Account mapping 

<mark>You can capture account mapping details, for example: for mapping Norwegian account classes against Swedish ones if the Swedish chart of accounts is used by Norwegian companies running SAFT export. Example: For type 1, SAFT Norway, for Swedish accounts 26xx you enter the corresponding Norwegian account class 27xx. This will be set as StandardAccountID 27xx in the Norwegian SAFT file.</mark>

#### 17.10.5 Non-VAT transactions 

###### _Originally developed for Norway, but available to all users._ 

<mark>The general ledger can be set up so that voucher rows from non-VAT related transactions are kept free from VAT related information. This is a requirement from auditors in some countries, such as Norway.</mark> 

#### 17.10.6 Standard Audit File for Tax (SAF-T) – Norway 

<mark>All Norwegian VAT returns must be filed based on the SAF-T code format. The VAT return must be in XML format and is based on the Standard Audit File for Tax (SAF-T) code list. This is a legal requirement to be followed and used by all companies in Norway from 2022.</mark> 

<mark>It is possible to create a SAF-T file with anonymized accounting and base data for testing with the Norwegian Tax Administration (Skatteetaten). This anonymization is required, otherwise the test file will be rejected by Skatteetaten.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves has a program where you can define VAT/tax report lines that can be used to make a oneto-one identification of which data is reported and with which VAT SAF-T code in the output XML file. These report lines are similar to the list of VAT/tax rates but may have separate lines for input and output entries.</mark> 

<mark>If you have linked VAT/tax boxes for the respective Norway VAT report line and defined the VAT/tax declaration for the VAT boxes, you can filter records and view records on the Norwegian VAT report line. This is useful when reporting VAT for Norway to correct any historical VAT reporting submitted with errors.</mark> 

##### 17.10.6.1 Corrections in VAT reports (Norway) 

<mark>All Norwegian VAT returns must be filed based on the SAF-T code format. The VAT return must be in XML format and is based on the Standard Audit File for Tax (SAF-T) code list. This is a legal requirement to be followed and used by all companies in Norway from 2022.</mark> 

<mark>From 2022, it is not possible to submit an additional VAT return where only the details to be corrected or added are included when reporting VAT for Norway. To correct the historical VAT reporting, the taxpayer needs to do the following as required to replace the previously submitted return:</mark> 

<mark>- original reports must be canceled and re-sent in full, including corrections</mark> 

<mark>- VAT amount from adjustment or sale of fixed assets must be included in the VAT report. - when requested, a customer should be able to separate parts of the original report and send it to the authorities.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves includes support for these country-specific reporting rules.</mark> 

##### 17.10.6.2 <mark>Fictitious import VAT</mark> 

In the SAF-T file for Norway, fictitious import VAT is reported according to the Norwegian Tax authority based on setup in the VAT/tax program. 

##### 17.10.6.3 Norwegian income statements account codes 

<mark>You can define a Norwegian Income statements account code; for example RF-1167 for Income statement 2. The value you enter in this field is mapped to the Norwegian income statement account and used as the basis to display the account code  in the output SAF-T file.</mark> 

<mark>Currently the following codes are supported:</mark> 

- <mark>Standard for SAF-T Norway standard</mark> 

- <mark>RF-1167 for SAF-T Norway RF-1167 (Income Statement 2)</mark> 

<mark>The master files for SAF-T Norway contain checks to ensure that the accounts that appear in the master files are limited to accounts that are part of a transaction or that have an inbound balance and/or an outgoing balance. They include all customers with any invoice transaction during the</mark>

<mark>selected interval, or those customers that have opening/closing balance in the selected period in the master files that are submitted to Norwegian Tax Administration (Skatteetaten).</mark> 

##### 17.10.6.4 Anonymized data for Norwegian SAF-T files 

When creating the file for Norwegian Financial SAF-T with accounting data for testing with the Norwegian Tax Administration (Skatteetaten) via the Norwegian digital hub Altinn, the file must be anonymized. You can do this when defining the SAF-T data definition in the Standard audit file - tax program. Once you have done this, the file is anonymized when you export it; i.e., it will not contain any information that may identify the sender or any third parties, for instance, personal information like individual names and addresses and contact information or company names and information about companies, or account numbers, IBAN-numbers, etc. 

##### 17.10.6.5 Recovery of VAT/tax on bad debts and reversal of VAT/tax deductions 

<mark>Other localized functionality for Norway is the possibility to show invoices as bad debts in the company’s submission for recovery of VAT/tax payments made on the invoice from the tax authorities. Another is support for reversal of earlier VAT deductions on fixed assets.</mark> 

#### 17.10.7 Standard Audit File for Tax (SAF-T) – Denmark 

<mark>The Danish Accounting Act requires accounting systems to support the use of the SAF-T international standard file format. Jeeves ERP includes functionality to extract the SAF-T file according to specifications from the Danish Business Authority.</mark> 

#### 17.10.8 e-Invoicing and business response messages – Denmark 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes support for eInvoicing and for sending and receiving business response messages in Denmark through NemHandel. The eInvoice s are transferred and converted to the relevant format via Pagero.</mark> 

#### 17.10.9 VAT refunding 

_Originally developed for the UK, but available to all users._ 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes functionality for VAT refunding on customer invoices. See the chapters on Jeeves Sales and Jeeves Accounts receivable for details.</mark> 

#### 17.10.10 Journal groups 

_This function was originally a localization for the French market but is available as standard._ 

<mark>Integration sources can be grouped into journal groups. This group number and the associated description are stored on the voucher row and are intended for selection purposes. For example, you can enter each bank as a payment method for outgoing payments and the bank's Journal group number on the payment method. You can then select all voucher rows related to a certain bank within a voucher.</mark> 

#### 17.10.11 Journal entry 

<mark>The Journal entry program is a preparatory system to the ledgers and the general ledger. You use one and the same program to enter different types of transactions, general ledger transactions, customer/supplier invoices, incoming/outgoing payments, etc., and then transfer these to the general ledger by running a journal. When doing so, records are created in each relevant module, i.e. an invoice in the accounts receivable ledger, a supplier invoice and associated approval record in the accounts payable ledger, an incoming/outgoing payment journal, etc. This is a common way of handling financial transactions in certain countries, for example in Norway.</mark>

Jeeves ERP General ledger 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0184-01.png)


#### 17.10.12 Cash book processing 

<mark>There are two solutions for cash book processing:</mark> 

- <mark>The cash book processing makes it possible to run several cash books for incoming and outgoing cash payments. Different cash books may, for example, pertain to different cost centers or currencies.</mark> 

- <mark>The variation of cash book processing, originally developed for the Polish market, makes it possible to run several cash books for incoming and outgoing cash payments with increased functionality for definition, numeration, posting, and reconciliation of cash books.</mark> 

<mark>Cash book processing is an extension of the Journal entry function.</mark> 

#### 17.10.13 Import and matching of bank statements 

<mark>There is functionality for importing bank statements including customer and supplier payments and other bank transactions, matching the payments against open invoices and integrating the bank statement with the general ledger. The function is used in countries such as Germany and the Netherlands.</mark> 

<mark>There are two alternative solutions; one for companies using the Journal entry program and one for "normal" integration between the accounts receivable/payable ledgers and the general ledger.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP attempts to identify open customer/supplier invoices using the bank account number, invoice number, payment amount etc. You check the automatically matched rows and continue manually matching the remaining rows. You can also manually add rows for, for example, advance payments, interest or bank charge (if not imported), currency transactions and the like. If you enter an advance payment in this way, an advance invoice is automatically created from it.</mark> 

<mark>When all the rows on a bank statement have been matched and the amount reconciled, the bank statement header is assigned the status Can be flagged ready. Only then can it be integrated with the general ledger.</mark> 

<mark>Electronic bank statement files that comply with the CAMT.053 standard (countries who are a part of SEPA) can be imported, matched and integrated with general ledger.</mark> 

#### 17.10.14 FIFO valuation of currencies 

<mark>In some countries, such as Hungary and Poland, it is a legal requirement that acquisitions of foreign currencies be valued according to FIFO (First in first out). The FIFO value is then used in outgoing payments in currency, as well as related costs. This means that outgoing payments are made at the earliest exchange rate. If you have more than one bank account for a currency, the FIFO value is calculated separately for each account.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP creates a new FIFO balance or updates an existing record with the same accounting date and currency rate when integrating incoming payments with the general ledger. This applies to:</mark> 

- <mark>incoming payments from customers</mark> 

- <mark>manual incoming payments, for example, bank interest and</mark> 

- <mark>credits from suppliers</mark> 

<mark>During outgoing payment Jeeves ERP uses the earliest balance first and locks the relevant balance record(s). This applies to:</mark> 

- <mark>outgoing payments to suppliers</mark> 

- <mark>manual outgoing payments</mark> 

<mark>There is also a program for printing information about the available currency balances on the bank account(s).</mark>

#### 17.10.15 1099 reporting (USA only) 

<mark>In the United States, companies are required to send a 1099-MISC form to any external consultants and subcontractors who are paid more than a specific amount per year. Jeeves ERP includes functionality for setting up the 1099 reporting categories and thresholds, and for printing the report.</mark> 

#### 17.10.16 GDPdU export (Germany only) 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes a program for exporting financial data for auditing purposes in accordance with legal requirements in Germany. The data is exported in a format that is readable and processable in applications used by all financial authorities in Germany. The format is called GDPdU, which in English stands for "Principles of data access and auditing of digital documents".</mark> 

#### 17.10.17 Making tax digital (UK only) 

<mark>Making Tax Digital (MTD) is a key part of the United Kingdom's plans to make the tax system easier for individuals and businesses to streamline tax administration. MTD introduces digital recordkeeping for VAT-registered businesses in the UK. Customers of Her Majesty’s Revenue and Customs (HMRC) will use digital record-keeping software to interact directly with government systems via the MTD APIs.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves will receive permission to access and send data digitally to HMRC. Once an account is registered with login information for HMRC online services using the VAT/tax number of the business, VAT/tax reports can be sent from Jeeves to HMRC. Relevant information is logged and saved in Jeeves to allow you to keep track of data relating to the submission of the VAT report to HMRC.</mark> 

#### 17.10.18 Reports in e-SFS format (Poland only) 

<mark>Poland legally requires companies to prepare financial statements in electronic form with the specified logical structure and format (e-SFS). These reports are also available in .xml file format.</mark> 

#### 17.10.19 JPK_VAT reporting (Poland only) 

<mark>From 1 October 2020, Poland introduced the JPK_V7M file for taxpayers to submit monthly VAT returns and JPK_V7K file for taxpayers to submit quarterly VAT returns. This is a periodical filing that will replace the existing JPK_VAT files and the VAT return declaration. The JPK_VAT is an extensive form which includes classifications of transaction types and codes to identify certain type of goods and services. A new version of the structure of the JPK_VAT invoice has been published for tax books and accounting documents which is effective from 1 January 2022, Jeeves supports the updated format with suitable fields and format updates.</mark> 

<mark>The files will provide information on:</mark> 

- <mark>split payments</mark> 

- <mark>commodity groups depending on the type of item/service being sold - for both sales and purchase transactions</mark> 

- <mark>procedures and transactions types</mark>

## 18 Jeeves ERP Fixed assets 

<mark>The fixed assets ledger in Jeeves ERP consists of routines for entering and managing fixed assets, for depreciations and for integration with the general ledger.</mark> 

### 18.1 Fixed assets management 

#### 18.1.1 Entering fixed assets 

<mark>Fixed assets can be entered manually or automatically from supplier invoices or from manual vouchers. If you use automatic entry you can activate cost accumulation for existing fixed assets. For both methods you can apply automatic numbering of fixed assets based on fixed assets types.</mark> 

#### 18.1.2 Fixed asset types and groups 

<mark>You can handle all fixed asset types (buildings, machines, fixed assets). You can also divide them into any number of fixed asset groups that can be used when selling fixed assets, creating depreciations and postings etc.</mark> 

#### 18.1.3 Fixed asset areas and locations 

<mark>There are programs for defining areas and locations of fixed assets according to own requirements. These can represent buildings, departments, rooms, working areas or the like.</mark> 

#### 18.1.4 Insurance management 

<mark>There are fields that can be used as the basis for insurance management.</mark> 

### 18.2 Fixed assets transactions 

#### 18.2.1 Fixed asset transactions 

<mark>There is a large number of standard transaction types, which can be entered via a journal or directly:</mark> 

- <mark>Entry of new fixed asset</mark> 

- <mark>Change status of fixed asset</mark> 

- <mark>Division of fixed asset</mark> 

- <mark>Addition costs from supplier invoice</mark> 

- <mark>Discard of fixed asset</mark> 

- <mark>Sale of fixed asset</mark> 

- <mark>Movement of fixed assets (placement only)</mark> 

- <mark>Movement of fixed assets to other account and/or account segment(s)</mark> 

- <mark>Change in acquisition value</mark> 

- <mark>Change in residual value</mark> 

- <mark>Change in depreciated amount</mark> 

#### 18.2.2 Direct entry 

<mark>There is a simple transaction entry form in which you enter the fixed asset ID, choose a transaction type, enter the amount and any other information required for the transaction type in question.</mark> 

<mark>186</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Fixed assets 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0187-01.png)


#### 18.2.3 Entry journals 

<mark>There is also an optional, overlying form for transaction entry via a journal. This is a legal requirement in certain countries. The entry journal gives better control of fixed assets transactions. Each transaction must be printed out, also the journal must be approved and its status set to Ready before a transaction journal can be created and integrated with the general ledger.</mark> 

#### 18.2.4 Integration with general ledger 

<mark>Fixed assets transactions are posted automatically according to posting templates, and integrated with the general ledger, by running a report program.</mark> 

### 18.3 Depreciations 

<mark>Recorded depreciations can be made according to plan or according to an own depreciation method. They can be done from the beginning of the year, from the acquisition date or an activation date. Fixed assets are automatically depreciated if sold or discarded.</mark> 

<mark>Fixed assets depreciation can be scheduled as a job considering depreciation methods and accounting date. A journal is created for each asset method in the Fixed assets depreciation -> GL table.</mark> 

#### 18.3.1 Recorded depreciations 

<mark>Depreciation according to plan can be calculated and created based on the economic life of the asset (specified on the asset group). The fixed assets that do not have any calculated depreciation are:</mark> 

- <mark>rented/leased fixed assets,</mark> 

- <mark>assets with no residual value and</mark> 

- <mark>assets that are acquired after the date to which the depreciation refers.</mark> 

<mark>Depreciation is calculated from the period the asset was acquired and up to and including the period before it is sold/discarded. A fixed asset that is acquired at the end of one month will therefore be depreciated for this month but not for the month during which it was sold/discarded.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP also keeps track of when the Fixed asset was last depreciated, so the next calculated depreciation will cover all periods that have elapsed since then. If the fixed asset has not been depreciated yet, the year and period of the acquisition date is used to calculate the number of periods.</mark> 

#### 18.3.2 Depreciation periodicity 

<mark>For each fixed asset group you can set up the desired depreciation periodicity:</mark> 

- <mark>Annually</mark> 

- <mark>Biannually</mark> 

- <mark>Every four months</mark> 

- <mark>Quarterly</mark> 

- <mark>Monthly</mark> 

<mark>You can choose between degressive and linear depreciation, or accelerated depreciation (highest of degressive and linear).</mark> 

#### 18.3.3 Simulating depreciations 

<mark>You can simulate future depreciations, which gives you the possibility to be more efficient in the budgeting and forecasting process. In the current version, simulations only cover forecasted depreciations for fixed assets with a linear depreciation method, according to plan and monthly depreciations.</mark> 

<mark>187</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Fixed assets 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0188-01.png)


#### 18.3.4 Integration with general ledger 

<mark>Depreciations are posted automatically according to posting templates, and integrated with the general ledger, by running a report program.</mark> 

### 18.4 Ledger information 

#### 18.4.1 Reconciliation of fixed assets ledger 

<mark>There is a program for reconciliation of the fixed assets ledger balance against the general ledger per specified date. In the list displayed you will see the relevant fixed assets and at the bottom a number of totals, calculated values as of the specified date including fixed assets transactions that have not updated the general ledger, also the values as of the specified date that have updated the general ledger, as well as current values. The totals shown are:</mark> 

- <mark>acquisition amounts</mark> 

- <mark>depreciated amounts</mark> 

- <mark>residual value</mark> 

### 18.5 Localizations/internationalization 

<mark>Most functions in Jeeves Fixed assets are common to all countries. There are some, however, that are specific to certain countries, as described below.</mark> 

#### 18.5.1 Local legal requirements regarding depreciations 

<mark>There is a number of local legal requirements that are specific to certain countries but can be incorporated in Jeeves ERP by setting parameters. One example is in Germany where the current maximum depreciation percentage is limited to 25% per year by law, but an additional rule says that this amount is not allowed to exceed the linear depreciation times 2.5. A second example is "Zero" depreciations, used to retain records of fixed assets that are never really fully depreciated, for example Forest. A third example is depreciations ending at the end of the acquisition year. The monthly depreciation amount is calculated in proportion to the number of months remaining until the end of the acquisition year.</mark> 

#### 18.5.2 Tax depreciations (Sweden only) 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP, there are functions for calculating and performing tax depreciations and integrating them with the general ledger.</mark> 

<mark>Recorded depreciations are performed as planned and are integrated with the general ledger every month. At the turn of the year you can make a comparison between recorded depreciations and tax depreciations. Differences may arise between recorded depreciations and tax depreciations. If you wish to show the same value of fixed assets in your annual closing report as in your declaration, you can record over- and under-depreciations as appropriations.</mark> 

<mark>This can be performed according to one of two rules and you can choose the one that gives the lowest outgoing value of the fixed assets, and thereby the greatest possible depreciation:</mark> 

- <mark>Principle rule (also called 30 % rule) means an annual depreciation of maximum 30 % of the fixed assets' posted residual value.</mark> 

- <mark>Supplementary rule (also called 20 % rule) means an annual depreciation of 20 % of the fixed assets' total requisition value.</mark> 

<mark>The form is designed to comply with the tax authority's calculation basis for the principle rule on the left-hand side and the supplementary rule on the right-hand side.</mark> 

<mark>188</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Fixed assets 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0189-01.png)


#### 18.5.3 Half year straight line (linear) depreciations (US and Canada) 

<mark>You can apply half year, linear depreciations, a method that is a legal requirement in the US for property other than real estate. The half year convention means that, irrespective of which month of the year you begin using the property, you must treat it as if you began using it in the middle of the year. Only half of the full-year depreciation is allowed in the first year, with the remaining balance being deducted in the final year of the depreciation schedule, or the year that the property is sold.</mark> 

#### 18.5.4 Negative acquisition amount 

<mark>In some countries, negative fixed assets are used for accounting purpose. To accommodate this, you can enter fixed assets with a negative acquisition amount and save the record where required.</mark>

Jeeves ERP Multi-company administration 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0190-01.png)


## 19 Jeeves ERP Multi-company administration 

<mark>In Jeeves you can quickly and efficiently set up multiple companies in the same database or in different databases. You can process and analyze transactions and produce reports for multiple companies within your organization.</mark> 

### 19.1 Multiple companies in same database 

<mark>All applications can be made available to any number of companies in the same database. New companies can be set up at any time, even during production.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP provides a definition of tables to be copied to newly created companies. Almost all tables in the database are represented here, which means that additions are only necessary for own developed tables. All tables defined here are by default flagged for copying, including own developed tables, and for "normal" users this is only editable for own tables. System administrators can, however, edit all table definitions.</mark> 

<mark>Here there is also a checkbox for flagging tables that CAN be replicated when entering, changing and deleting data in the template company. Specify which of these SHALL be replicated in the Common tables/companies program.</mark> 

### 19.2 Company access 

<mark>There is a program for administration of access rights to companies in multi-company databases. There is also a program, View user access to companies, you can use for viewing access rights across all companies. By default, all users have access to all companies, but if you grant a user or group access rights to a specific company, the user/group is blocked from all other companies.</mark> 

### 19.3 Switching companies 

<mark>It is quick and easy to switch company, either via drop-down menu or a company code. You can have different background colors for different companies in the database. Background settings are especially useful for differentiating between companies in order to prevent users from doing things in the wrong company.</mark> 

### 19.4 Settings when switching companies 

<mark>Settings can be saved to a specific company, which means that program menus, forms, lists, etc. can have different designs when you switch company. You can also use the ReadSiteSettingsOnCompanyChange kernel parameter to display date, time, numeric and monetary values in different formats when you switch company.</mark> 

### 19.5 Multi-company searches 

<mark>There is an option on the search menu that you can use to search in several companies at the same time. Each selection of companies is saved in a list under this menu. You can re-use your selection of companies in several searches simply by checking the selection and conducting a new search.</mark> 

### 19.6 Template companies 

<mark>A template company is used to simplify creation of new companies. You can have one template company per type of company in your area of business, for example: Manufacturing, Distribution, Service, Sales etc. Data that is to be common for several companies is entered into the template company, for example: chart of accounts, cost centers, etc. When a new company is created the data is copied from the template company to the new company. You can then decide if new entries, changes and deletions are to be replicated from the template company to the other companies.</mark> 

<mark>190</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Multi-company administration 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0191-01.png)


<mark>All tables in the database can be copied, which is useful for quickly creating training or test companies with realistic transactions and history. There is also an option (per table) to automatically update production companies with changes made in the template company.</mark> 

### 19.7 Multi-company fiscal year routine 

<mark>You can set up simultaneous creation of new fiscal year and opening balances for all or several companies with the same template company.</mark> 

### 19.8 Defaults 

<mark>In the system data you can set up global and company specific defaults such as default inventory location, default salesman etc. Much of the data that is entered here will be given as proposals when working with Jeeves ERP but can be changed.</mark> 

### 19.9 Multi-company reports 

<mark>There is a program for definition of the reports that can be printed/run simultaneously for several companies. The information you enter here is global, so you only need to enter the reports in one of the companies. You can run reports across several companies, for instance run summary reports such as account balance reports, and get the sum on the selected accounts for all companies. You can also save these company selections.</mark> 

<mark>The user must have a right to work in the company in question. Also, a template must be set up for the report with the same name, but not necessarily the same appearance, in the relevant company. If you want the templates to have the same appearance, use the Copy report templates program to copy templates between the companies.</mark> 

### 19.10 Report templates 

<mark>You can set up company specific report templates or set up common report templates via a template company.</mark>
