Jeeves ERP Document management (EDM) 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0192-01.png)


## 20 Jeeves ERP Document management (EDM) 

<mark>Jeeves (electronic) document management (EDM) includes functionality for entry, structure and classification of documents, as well as for searching, version management, authorization control and logging. You can also create, display, print and send files with e-mail directly from a Jeeves form. Through integration with other Jeeves modules, you can link a document to, and thereby make it accessible from, the business object or transaction (for example: customer, order, invoice etc.) from which the document originates. You can select a linked document directly from a dropdown menu on Jeeves ERP’s menu bar.</mark> 

<mark>Every document is represented by a record in the database that points to the file that constitutes the document in question. The files are stored on a central file server. Links to related business objects are specified on the document record. For each record a document type is also specified. This functions as a logical grouping of documents with the same content or function. The document type also specifies the directory where the files are located and which file types the documents may consist of. The file type specifies which programs may be used to open or change files of the specified file type.</mark> 

<mark>A document can also be related to another document. In this way you can create document structures with several child documents related to a parent document. A document structure that refers to items can be converted into a BOM which can then be used in Jeeves Production module.</mark> 

### 20.1 Basic document management 

<mark>The basic functions/steps in Jeeves Document management are:</mark> 

- <mark>create a document record</mark> 

- <mark>create a file linked to the record or</mark> 

- <mark>retrieve an existing file and link to the record or</mark> 

- <mark>copy an existing document record with associated file to a new record with a new file</mark> 

<mark>There are buttons for these actions. The relevant buttons are activated depending on the step being performed. You can also enter files using drag-and-drop.</mark> 

<mark>You can associate programs, browsers and directories with document and/or file types. You can control which programs are used to create, edit and view documents, and for MS Word and Excel, which template is used for new files.</mark> 

<mark>Besides the automatically generated information, you can set document properties such as own document ID, description, author and links to business objects or transactions such as item or item group, company, contact, project, customer order, manufacturing order, campaign, quotation, etc. If you jumped to the document management program using the Document management button, the link(s) to the current business object or transaction will automatically be filled in.</mark> 

<mark>You can set up your own categories for categorization of your documents, for example to which organization or process they belong.</mark> 

### 20.2 Print to EDM 

<mark>You can define that certain reports should be printed directly to EDM. On selecting the report from Jeeves ERP, the report is generated in the desired format and automatically linked to the record. You can then view and print the report as and when needed. You can also print multiple records to PDM simultaneously.</mark> 

<mark>The CheckPrinttoEDM parameter allows you to enable/disable the Print to EDM functionality. You specify the directory for the document type to be printed to EDM in the rules for printout.</mark> 

<mark>The generated files are saved with the following naming convention: ‘Report Template Name _ Record Identifier _ DateTime Stamp’. For example: rfaktfu_10176_290915122051.pdf.</mark> 

<mark>192</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Document management (EDM) 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0193-01.png)


### 20.3 Document structures 

<mark>You can use document structures to:</mark> 

- <mark>organize multiple documents consisting of several records, for example drawings,</mark> 

- <mark>gather associated documents, for example, documents which originate from a certain project, and</mark> 

- <mark>build directory structures for easier navigation.</mark> 

<mark>A parent document can in turn constitute a child document in relation to another document. In this way you can build structures at several levels. Copying business object data from the parent document to the newly created child document is controlled via a parameter.</mark> 

<mark>There are embedded lists that show the structure relationships. The Consists of list displays all document structure records where the overlying record constitutes a parent document, while the Included in list shows all document structure records where the overlying record constitutes a child document. To create a more easily navigated interface, the Consists of list can be displayed as a graphical tree structure.</mark> 

<mark>You can even create template structures and base new document structures on them. New document records are created in the PDM table where the Document ID, Document Description, Short note and File name fields are copied from the template record. No business object links are set for the new document record.</mark> 

### 20.4 Version management 

<mark>Each new version of a document corresponds to a new file. Version management is performed through reference to the unique ID of both a previous document record and the original record. Which version is valid is indicated by a check in the Valid PDM checkbox. There is a program that only displays valid document records, i.e. current revisions and editions. A second program shows only previous revisions and editions. Otherwise, both programs contain the same functionality for creating and viewing documents and structures.</mark> 

<mark>A new version of a document corresponds to a cloned record where in principle only the unique ID, previous ID, version name and validity periods differ. Business links and other document data are the same because the new version fulfills the same function as the previous one.</mark> 

<mark>Version increment is performed in two steps, a new database record is created and a new version of the related file is created. Any file link to the newly created record must be entered using dragand-drop. The previous version does not automatically become invalid when a new version is created; you check the Valid PDM box manually. An alternative version increment method, with slightly different functionality, is check-out and check-in.</mark> 

<mark>To make it easier to keep track of version changes there are two alphanumeric terms, version and revision, each linked to an underlying index, which facilitates gradual, serial increments. The user sets up alphanumeric or numeric series, and determines when and how these should be used. A common method is that Revision increases for every individual change while Version is increased for more extensive changes. Validity periods are linked to both terms. Date and signature are automatically set on version and revision changes.</mark> 

<mark>In order to guarantee that documents are only changed by one person at a time they must be checked out. At check-out, a copy of a document (file) is created in a directory specified for the login signature. Checked out documents can only be edited by the check-out signature. On checking in, a new document record and file are entered with the document record's Unique ID, in the directory specified for the document type. Alternatively, there is a setting to receive a prompt at each check-in asking whether or not the previous file should be overwritten.</mark> 

<mark>A version change can also be relevant for a document structure, on a change of an underlying document or on the addition of an underlying document. You can set that the document structure is complete, which means that included document structure records cannot be changed, added to or deleted.</mark>

Jeeves ERP Document management (EDM) 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0194-01.png)


### 20.5 Access rights 

<mark>You must set up all users or resource groups who are authorized to use the Document management module. Users’ access rights can be controlled at two levels:</mark> 

- <mark>document record - the right to view records in the EDM programs. This can be controlled using Restrictive views or default search terms. Suitably, you control which document types various user groups have access to.</mark> 

- <mark>file - access rights to files. This is controlled at role level per document type. The following rights can be controlled:</mark> 

   - <mark>create file</mark> 

   - <mark>edit file</mark> 

   - <mark>delete file</mark> 

   - <mark>read file</mark> 

   - <mark>publish file</mark> 

   - <mark>approve file</mark> 

### 20.6 Approval 

<mark>Document approval is based on a file having to be approved by an authorized user before it is accessible to other users. A checked-in file whose document type is marked for approval will not become valid until it is approved. Consequently, it is not immediately viewable in the Document, valid program. It can, however, be viewed by authorized users in the Document approval program/node. There are checkboxes that indicate if the document record:</mark> 

- <mark>is valid</mark> 

- <mark>is under approval</mark> 

- <mark>requires approval</mark> 

- <mark>has a file linked</mark> 

<mark>When approving, if there are several different versions of the file under approval, the approved version will be set to valid and the others will be deleted.</mark> 

### 20.7 Logging 

<mark>You can log both document access and version management. You define what is to be logged in the EDM activity types program. The documents which are logged are controlled per document type. The following standard types are supplied.</mark> 

- <mark>Created</mark> 

- <mark>Checked out</mark> 

- <mark>Checked in</mark> 

- <mark>Opened</mark> 

- <mark>New version</mark> 

- <mark>Printed</mark> 

- <mark>By E-mail</mark> 

<mark>You can also set up your own types, but then you must also define your own logging method.</mark> 

<mark>You access the log via a node in the document management programs.</mark> 

### 20.8 File management 

<mark>Documents can be stored either centrally or locally (but not on local machines and disks). One of the main advantages with document management is a central storage site, which is why this is usually preferred. The file status specifies whether a file exists for the document record and if so, if it is local or central, or has been archived.</mark> 

<mark>For centrally stored files you can specify directory paths for document types either with mapped letters or as UNC paths. You can specify an absolute path to the server and directory under which</mark> 

> <mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

<mark>194</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

Jeeves ERP Document management (EDM) 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0195-01.png)


<mark>all directories that are defined for the different document types lie. In this way it is easy, for example, to move all documents to another directory or file server without needing to change the directories for the document types. Even if this parameter is set, you can specify directories with paths to mapped drives. Jeeves ERP checks first whether a document type directory refers to a mapped drive.</mark> 

<mark>For locally stored files, the easiest way to enter local file links is using the Get file button or dragand-drop. The file pointer is dropped in the Document file path field. After the field is validated, a file name and file extension are filled in automatically.</mark> 

<mark>There are also functions with which you can:</mark> 

- <mark>move files from local to central</mark> 

- <mark>delete central files</mark> 

- <mark>copy files to the desired directory</mark> 

- <mark>create and delete directories directly from Jeeves ERP</mark> 

<mark>All file transfer is done with Windows file management routines. Encrypted streaming is also supported as an alternative to file transfer. This allows a higher security level to be set and files become accessible via the Internet.</mark> 

<mark>You can zip and archive files recorded in the document management module, in order to increase efficiency in file management and backup if your company has large numbers of files and images.</mark> 

<mark>The following three functions can also handle files that have been zipped and archived:</mark> 

- <mark>Show file</mark> 

- <mark>Mail file</mark> 

- <mark>Print file</mark> 

### 20.9 MS Office integration 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes integration with Microsoft Word, Excel and Outlook. You can specify a template for document types linked to Microsoft Word or Microsoft Excel. When you create a new document using the Create file button, Word or Excel opens with an empty record as an instance of a specific format template.</mark> 

<mark>You can save Word documents and Excel workbooks directly to Jeeves EDM to a certain document type. You can also save and send mail using MS Outlook. You can either save an e-mail in Outlook's msg format or as a text file, with sender, date, addressees, subject and content, in a specified directory. Attached appendices can be saved to other directories in their existing format. Appendices can also be entered as document structure records linked to the mail document.</mark>

General functions and features in Jeeves ERP 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0196-01.png)


## 21 General functions and features in Jeeves ERP 

<mark>The functions described below are available and applicable throughout the whole system.</mark> 

### 21.1 VAT/tax management 

#### 21.1.1 VAT/tax rates and declarations 

<mark>You can have any number of VAT rates, controlled by customer, supplier, item, or entered manually. Jeeves ERP automatically calculates VAT amounts with or without consideration of early settlement discounts. VAT codes can also control postings. You can also set up sales taxes for USA and Canada.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP has a general solution for all countries' VAT declarations. There is also a number of country specific VAT declarations described among the localizations for the General ledger.</mark> 

### 21.2 Currency management 

#### 21.2.1 Currencies and rates 

<mark>You can have any number of currency codes and rates controlled by date, current rates which are used in invoicing, in ledgers and so on, and calculation rates for calculating cost prices and so on. This is mutual functionality for sales, purchases and service, in ledgers and the general ledger, for projects etc.</mark> 

#### 21.2.2 Switch currency 

<mark>It is possible to switch currency on-the-fly, i.e. show amounts in any currency according to current rate (amounts are always saved in base currency).</mark> 

### 21.3 Language management 

#### 21.3.1 Language management when printing external documents 

<mark>You can enter item descriptions, units, payment terms, delivery terms, delivery methods, currency clause texts, general texts etc. in different languages. Jeeves ERP automatically selects translations when printing external documents depending on the customer’s/supplier’s language.</mark> 

#### 21.3.2 Language management of data 

<mark>Jeeves ERP even includes unique functionality for translating standard/own drop-down list records and other data that needs to be translated such as account names, payment terms, activity descriptions, etc. This is used if, when switching language in the client, the language of drop-down lists and certain data is also to be changed.</mark> 

### 21.4 EU sustainability regulations 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes functionality to support companies in the EU to comply with sustainability regulations. You can set a markup in percent on TARIC codes for Carbon Border Adjustment Mechanism (CBAM) and the CBAM cost will be calculated per purchase order row. You can also set a CO2 equivalent value per unit or kilogram on items for the Corporate Sustainability Reporting Directive (CSRD). Total CO2 equivalent values are shown per sales order row and purchase order row.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP also includes support for the EU Deforestation Regulation (EUDR), with functionality for managing Due Diligence Statements and flagging relevant TARIC codes and trading areas.</mark> 

<mark>196</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

General functions and features in Jeeves ERP 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0197-01.png)


### 21.5 Time zone management 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes functionality for time zone management, which allows you to set up time zones for companies in the database, as well as for users, in a different time zone from the database server.</mark> 

<mark>The purpose is to set dates and times in local time where each transaction takes place, and not according to the database server's clock. For example, if the database server is located in Sweden and a user in the USA picks a customer order, the picking transactions shall have the local time according to the relevant time zone in the USA.</mark> 

### 21.6 Change log 

<mark>You can log new entries, changes and deletions. You can also control whether these events require a reason code and/or a password. For further information see the overview of the System administration functions.</mark> 

### 21.7 General import/export 

<mark>Jeeves ERP's import function can be used for importing files of various formats, for example import of cost prices from an external application, freight costs from carriers etc. First, you create a table in the database in question to hold the imported data. You enter an import definition which, among other things, specifies the path and name of the data file to be imported, and into which table in which database, as well as which column in the table, the data should be imported. You save the file to the folder in question and check its content/format. You import the file by running the Import report.</mark> 

<mark>Export is done in a similar manner. First, you enter an export definition which, among other things, specifies which table in which database data will be retrieved from, and where the work file should be saved, if you want to create one. If you are exporting in the SIE format, SRU codes must be entered or imported by installing Jeeves ERP base chart of accounts. Also, if other dimensions than 1, 2 and 6 - CC, CU and project - you must define these SIE dimensions. You are then ready to either export data to a work table and from there create a file, or you can create a file directly via the mail function.</mark> 

### 21.8 Import external data to Jeeves on cloud without VPN connection 

<mark>Jeeves ERP allows file handling to work without a VPN connection using the Definition and import external data->work table via dialog program.</mark> 

<mark>This can be files to be imported via the conversion routine, usually financial data for the General ledger, or it can be files for incoming and outgoing payment receipts etc. You can also enter own procedures that handle the imported data for further processing in the system.</mark> 

<mark>This program uses the Import file via dialog function to open a file dialog that enables you to navigate to the desired folder and select the import file specified in the In-/output file field to update the database.</mark> 

<mark>Once you have selected the file to be loaded into the table specified in the Value term table name field, the stored procedure specified in the Procedure, after field is run to process the data.</mark> 

<mark>The updated information can be viewed as follows:</mark> 

- <mark>in the Incoming payment file header program for BGMAX in text format and Camt.054</mark> 

- <mark>in the Bank statement header program for CAMT.053 in text and XML formats</mark> 

- <mark>in the cloud environment, imported supplier invoices can be viewed in the Review imported</mark> 

<mark>supplier invoices program by clicking the View pdf invoice button.</mark> 

<mark>197</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

General functions and features in Jeeves ERP 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0198-01.png)


### 21.9 Export files to local system from Jeeves on cloud without VPN connection 

<mark>When running Jeeves in cloud environments, a VPN connection is necessary to access and export files to your local system. However, for those who cannot use a VPN connection, Jeeves ERP allows file handling to work without a VPN connection using the Export Jeeves data -> file via dialog program.</mark> 

<mark>When you want to start the export, click the Export file via dialog button to open a file dialog that enables you to navigate to the desired folder and then enter the new .txt file name or select the existing .txt file to identify the file to be created/updated with the exported data. Once you have entered the new .txt file name or selected the existing .txt file, the data will be updated from the table specified in the Value term table name field based on the SQL condition mentioned in the DB term/expression 2, where, and Sorting/weighting by fields. The filename and path are updated in the In-/output file field once the export is completed. Note that you can browse and select a different file than the one specified in the In-/output file field and this is then saved as the default file and file path.</mark> 

### 21.10 Jeeves Message integrator (JMI) 

<mark>There is a general integrator called Jeeves Message integrator (JMI). The aim is to make the JMI solution as general as possible to allow integration with any type of external application where the integration is based on XML messages. Besides XML files, JMI can also handle other file types, such as flat text files and Excel files, by using plug-ins. At present, JMI is used in the integration with the Sales configurator and Astro WMS, and for general export and import via Microsoft Excel. The latter can be very useful if you need to process data in Excel and then import the processed data back into the Jeeves database.</mark> 

<mark>JMI is comprised of programs for:</mark> 

- <mark>setting up the integration installation</mark> 

- <mark>mapping incoming and outbound XML messages</mark> 

- <mark>monitoring and tracing transactions</mark> 

- <mark>logging errors and synchronizing data</mark> 

<mark>There are also special Jeeves programs for handling sales configurations in customer orders and for sending shipment plans to Astro.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP provides a standard XML mapping for import of price lists, based on the columns that are possible to update in the costing and pricing programs. You can adjust the mapping, for example if the columns names in the file from the supplier are different from Jeeves ERP's column names. You can also add any additional calculations to be done in conjunction with the import.</mark> 

<mark>Some of the advantages of JMI are as follow:</mark> 

- <mark>It provides one single preparatory system for all XML messages from all applications (most integrations are based on one table per message) and does not require interim storage.</mark> 

- <mark>Most of the setup is done from within Jeeves.</mark> 

- <mark>It enables parallel processing of messages where applicable, and can handle large volumes of transactions.</mark> 

- <mark>It offers extensive monitoring and exception management, with the possibility to correct and re-run transactions that do not comply with business or integrity rules.</mark> 

### 21.11 Excel integration 

<mark>Besides being able to print standard reports, dynamic reports and SSRS reports to various formats, including Excel, you can also export data directly from any Jeeves program to Excel. You simply select the required records and then select the required columns in the list before choosing the Excel export option.</mark>

General functions and features in Jeeves ERP 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0199-01.png)


### 21.12 Data replication, conversion and management rules 

<mark>Some data, for example in Inter company transactions (ICT), will be common to several companies/units. Common data can be entered and maintained centrally and replicated in the other companies/units. You can set up conditions for replication, for example only items that are sellable should be replicated.</mark> 

<mark>Some of the data may need to be converted between companies/units; for example, an item's acquisition method could be Manufacture to requirement at the delivering unit but Purchase to requirement at the selling unit.</mark> 

<mark>If data is entered and maintained centrally, you need to define rules for data management, such as which companies/units are allowed to change and delete data.</mark> 

<mark>There are also so-called modification points that can be used to define the data to be included in customer orders in delivering units and to be updated on changes from the selling unit, as well as for conversions and own calculations.</mark> 

### 21.13 Jeeves Graphical planning 

<mark>There is an optional tool for graphical resource planning integrated with the Production, Service and Time, projects, assignments modules.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves Graphical planning is used to graphically view capacities and levels of usage for various kinds of resources, and for detailed planning. Jeeves Graphical planning can be used to simulate various planning situations and, if you like, save changes to Jeeves. Re-planning is performed via drag-and-drop. In Jeeves Production you can plan setup, personnel/operation, subsequent activities, including overlapping operations, and control deviations from Jeeves ERP’s standard calendar. Jeeves Graphical planning also helps to identify bottle-necks. You can view activities per resource and also a graph showing resource load and backlog.</mark> 

### 21.14 Graphical planning system integration 

<mark>Jeeves ERP also offers an integration with a fully functional graphical planning tool that is developed and maintained by a chosen solutions provider. The new tool called EQ Plan facilitates the management of manufacturing orders in Jeeves ERP. Note that this integration is currently only implemented with the production module.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP enables you to define different planning levels that can be set for a resource group. Based on the selected planning level, the system will load the relevant manufacturing related information from Jeeves ERP to EQ Plan (via the integration endpoint). It also allows you to save and view information and settings for EQ Plan. The values, for example: color settings, are read by EQ Plan when loading data from Jeeves ERP.</mark> 

<mark>You can define resource groups and their properties for the graphical planning system. Once you have defined a resource group, you can link it to one or more manufacturing groups.</mark> 

<mark>A user with sufficient administrative rights can manage manufacturing operations using EQ Plan. When creating or modifying the plan for one or several resources at a specific point in time, this user can trigger a locking mechanism to make sure that other users running EQ plan do not interfere with the planning. When a resource has been locked, no other user will be able to alter the plan for that particular resource. Once a connection is successfully established between EQ Plan, the endpoint (JSB), and Jeeves ERP, EQ Plan will call the locking mechanism at regular intervals depending on how the management of the manufacturing plan is progressing.</mark> 

<mark>You can organize your data using versioning in EQ Plan. The version mentioned in the Graphical planning value (GpsValue) field will be applicable to all GPS services. You can also define resource types for planning, restrict changes to start and end dates for labor operations using lock behavior, and enable/disable the following options in EQ Plan: lock, merge, split, delete, and copy. In addition, you can split a labor operation into another operation as per the conditions specified or merge two operations that were previously split into a single operation.</mark>

General functions and features in Jeeves ERP 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0200-01.png)


### 21.15 Localizations/internationalization 

<mark>Most general functions in Jeeves ERP are common to all countries. There are some, however, that are specific to certain countries, as described below.</mark> 

#### 21.15.1 General data protection regulation (GDPR) (EU countries) 

<mark>Jeeves ERP includes functions for handling the General data protection regulation within EU countries.</mark> 

###### **Rename (anonymize) companies** 

<mark>For a company, if the Consent checkbox has been unchecked in the Companies program and if no customers' orders are in progress and no invoices exist for the past one year, as a GDPR controller, you can do the following:</mark> 

- <mark>rename the company where you no longer have consent to keep their data,</mark> 

- <mark>remove the contact persons associated with the company,</mark> 

- <mark>remove the Points of delivery connected with the company, and</mark> 

- <mark>remove the EU/VAT information pertaining to the company.</mark> 

###### **Remove contact persons** 

<mark>In the Contacts (kp) program if the Consent checkbox has been unchecked for a contact person, as a GDPR controller you can remove the contact person, including a web user. This can be done even if the contact person is associated with a number of companies.</mark> 

#### 21.15.2 Online credit check (Sweden only) 

<mark>Jeeves ERP has a built-in function that makes it quick and easy to run a credit check on a company in the company table. You can also join the agreement that Jeeves Information Systems AB has signed with the Swedish credit information agency (UC).</mark> 

#### 21.15.3 Postens eBrev (Nordic countries) 

<mark>Jeeves eBrev (which in English means e-Letter) is a function to define and create text files in accordance with the postal service's eBrev standard. The text files are sent to postal service for print out, sealing and distribution.</mark> 

<mark>The function is available for a number of reports that are included in the Rules for printout program. For each report, eBrev templates are added which are obtained when running reports based on the current language, country, customer category for the company to be processed.</mark> 

<mark>When reports are run, nothing is printed out. Instead, a record is created in an outbox for eBrev with reference to a certain eBrev template. By running a separate function, an eBrev file is created based on the records in the outbox, which are sent by FTP to the postal service.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves eBrev includes a program for definition of various eBrev templates and eBrev jobs, creating eBrev files and distribution of files using FTP.</mark>

## 22 Jeeves ERP e-Applications 

### 22.1 e-Approval of supplier invoices 

<mark>Jeeves e-Approval is a function for the electronic distribution and approval of supplier invoices. This is built on the combination of a standard process in Jeeves Workflow and a web application.</mark> 

#### 22.1.1 Approval chains in e-Approval of supplier invoices 

<mark>Jeeves e-approval is based on custom approval chains. An approval chain is a hierarchical and rolebased approval flow. By hierarchical we mean that the chain controls the order in which an approval will be performed. By role-based we mean that approval takes place at a resource group level independent of the individual. You can create new approval chains easily from an existing approval chain. You can create approval chains for normal invoicing and for collective invoicing.</mark> 

<mark>On the approval chain you can set up approval rules to control whether posting and manual allocation are allowed, whether it is allowed for postings to be changed, including others’ postings.</mark> 

#### 22.1.2 e-Approval work flow 

<mark>The e-Approval work flow consists primarily of the following steps:</mark> 

- <mark>Supplier invoices are scanned and stored in the form of PDF files and then interpreted.</mark> 

- <mark>The files are imported, or, alternatively, the invoices can be opened and invoice data entered manually. If Pagero AB's service for receiving eInvoice s is used, Pagero creates an eInvoice  file in Jeeves ERP's standard format and a corresponding invoice file in PDF format, and notifies the user that there are files to be downloaded.</mark> 

- <mark>Jeeves programs for review of supplier invoices allow the accounts payable administration to open an image of the scanned invoice, enter approval data, and then transfer the invoice to accounts payable. The invoice receives the status: Pending approval.</mark> 

- <mark>This creates one instance of the approval chain built according to the specified approval chain in Jeeves Workflow. An e-mail is sent to the approver stating that an invoice has arrived for approval.</mark> 

- <mark>The approver reviews the invoice in a special web interface for e-approval, and once the invoice is approved, a final approval record and a general ledger voucher that updates the balance are usually created. Alternatively, via a parameter, you can choose to let the accounts payable administration perform final review, approval, and balance update. If the web functionality for invoice matching is used the approver can match the invoice against one or more received purchase orders via the web before final approval.</mark> 

#### 22.1.3 e-Approval on the web 

<mark>The web interface of e-Approval helps approvers to work more efficiently. The enhancements include the ability to:</mark> 

- <mark>Match the invoice against one or more received purchase orders before final approval.</mark> 

- <mark>Approve an invoice from the invoice matching page</mark> 

- <mark>Filter purchase orders in the matching page</mark> 

- <mark>Choose to view one or all purchase orders</mark> 

- <mark>Add a posting in the list instead of a separate page</mark> 

- <mark>Use Jeeves ERPs shortcut function to set pre-defined entries when posting</mark> 

- <mark>Show arrival posting on demand</mark> 

- <mark>Choose to display VAT/tax codes VAT-mandatory accounts in the Posting table page</mark> 

- <mark>Enter the VAT/tax code when you add a custom row to an existing purchase order on the invoice</mark> 

- <mark>View information on escalated invoices easily</mark>

Jeeves ERP e-Applications

<mark>The Current approver column in the invoice list displays the signatures of the current approvers for the incoming invoices. The name associated with a signature is displayed when you place the cursor over the signature.</mark> 

<mark>The approver can view arrival postings in a pop-up window. This information cannot be modified as it is read-only.</mark> 

<mark>In the Supplier invoice program, there are two buttons to approve or reject an invoice that is distributed in an approval chain. These function buttons can be used in place of the equivalent buttons that exist in the web solution.</mark> 

#### 22.1.4 User interface 

<mark>When you log into Jeeves e-Approval you are presented with a list of invoices you must approve or check. Invoices with numbers displayed in red will become due within 5 days or are already due for payment. The date by which the invoice must be approved is also shown in the Escalation column. Escalation means that after this date, the invoice will be forwarded to a manager or other colleague. The original approver can, however, still handle the invoice as usual.</mark> 

<mark>The activities in the list are assigned a colored symbol in the left margin that shows their status, which also indicates the invoice's route to your approval list; if it came directly to you or if it was forwarded from another recipient for inquiry, etc.</mark> 

<mark>When you choose an invoice from the list, a graphic image of the scanned invoice is displayed along with invoice information interpreted during scanning, any notes that were made during the review and postings that were created according to the posting template. There are numerous options you can choose:</mark> 

- <mark>add notes to invoice</mark> 

- <mark>block payment of invoice</mark> 

- <mark>approve invoice</mark> 

- <mark>send invoice back to administration</mark> 

- <mark>send invoice to colleague for inquiry</mark> 

- <mark>send invoice to colleague for information</mark> 

- <mark>pause invoice</mark> 

- <mark>add approver</mark> 

- <mark>show approval chain</mark> 

- <mark>create new posting</mark> 

- <mark>copy posting</mark> 

- <mark>restart approval chain (used by Workflow administrators in order to force the invoice to another approver)</mark> 

- <mark>match invoice against received purchase order rows</mark> 

- <mark>show documents</mark> 

- <mark>add appendices</mark> 

- <mark>display log</mark> 

<mark>If you cancel a supplier invoice in the Jeeves client, Jeeves ERP checks if there is a connected workflow instance in Jeeves Workflow that has not already been canceled. If there is a workflow instance connected, it will also be canceled. This eliminates the need for manual cancelation in Jeeves Workflow.</mark> 

#### 22.1.5 Administration 

<mark>The e-application includes a few simple administrative functions for:</mark> 

- <mark>choosing one e-mail for each new invoice or one e-mail per day</mark> 

- <mark>changing passwords</mark> 

- <mark>entering absence periods and specify another colleague as a reserve</mark> 

- <mark>editing voucher templates</mark>

#### 22.1.6 Workflow to-do list 

<mark>There is also a program in the Jeeves client that shows a list of active activities from Jeeves Workflow. The activities are automatically added to your to-do list. The activities show when an invoice is currently in the process, for example that it has been distributed to an approver, waiting to be signed, and activities waiting to be executed by Administration.</mark> 

<mark>The workflow to-do list also displays advance invoices. Two approval records are created as follows and the advance invoice is shown:</mark> 

- <mark>An arrival approval record with no voucher</mark>

<mark>The Jeeves program also offers many options:</mark>

- <mark>Approve (close) activity</mark> 

- <mark>Reject (cancel) activity</mark> 

- <mark>Show entry (connected record in Jeeves ERP)</mark> 

- <mark>Skip activity (and progress to next activity)</mark> 

- <mark>Pause/resume activity</mark> 

- <mark>Delegate activity (to another resource group or user)</mark> 

- <mark>Delete activity</mark> 

- <mark>Restart process (used by Workflow administrators in order to force the invoice to another approver)</mark> 

#### 22.1.7 Invoice list filtering 

Invoice lists in e-Approval support predefined date ranges, reducing manual filtering during daily work. Improved comment visibility supports clearer collaboration among approvers and accounts payable staff when handling invoices. 

### 22.2 e-Sales 

#### 22.2.1 Technical platform 

<mark>e-Sales is compatible with the most recent version of the portal platform, Liferay. It includes a responsive mobile interface and access to applications and solutions of Liferay. Real-time data is loaded selectively from Jeeves database to provide improved performance and better user experience.</mark> 

#### 22.2.2 e-Sales B2B 

<mark>e-Sales B2B (Business to business) is the e-application for Sales that handles products variants (for example models, sizes, colors etc.), package items and batch items. It is for customer companies to place orders via your website. Refer to the chapter on</mark> _<mark>Jeeves Sales</mark>_ <mark>for more information.</mark> 

#### 22.2.3 User interface 

<mark>You (read “the customer” in B2B) select an item, either via the product tree or by searching for the item. The shopping cart displays items and their prices and quantities as you add them to it by clicking on Buy. Estimated delivery date is displayed on each row. You can update the quantity and the requested delivery date on rows in the shopping cart and type in any order row texts. In B2S you can enter discounts on rows, in which case you override any automatically set discount. You can also remove rows.</mark>

#### 22.2.4 Product designer 

<mark>Support for designing items to order using the features and functions of product configuration is extended to e-Sales.</mark> 

<mark>If an item is defined as a configurable item by checking the Configurable item field in the Items program, it can be used as a configuration model in the Product designer. The Product designer screen can be accessed as follows:</mark> 

- **<mark>From the products tab</mark>** <mark>: You can click the Design button associated with a configurable item in the List/Grid view or in the item details page to open the Product designer screen and specify requirements for the features connected to the model.</mark> 

- **<mark>From the shopping cart</mark>** <mark>: You can click on the gear icon, which shows the text Design item when you point to it, on the right of the item row for a configurable item to open the Product designer screen and specify requirements for the features connected to the model.</mark> 

<mark>The features connected to the model are displayed within named tabs or as cards within an accordion view that can be expanded or collapsed by clicking on the header.</mark> 

<mark>The tabs/cards are sorted according to the feature group’s sequence number. The rows on the tab/card are sorted according to the feature’s sequence number. Each tab or card represents a feature group and the rows contain features linked to the model and grouped within the feature groups.</mark> 

<mark>A number of visualization artifacts/aids are available in the Product designer for e-Sales including the accordion view, web display options, status icons, identification of mandatory fields, and range constraints displayed below numeric features.</mark>

##### 22.2.4.1 Validate configuration 

<mark>Once the configuration is ready for validation, you can validate if the configuration is complete, for example that it does not contain missing or conflicting answers.</mark> 

- <mark>If the design is complete; the configuration status is automatically changed to Activated, the Product designer screen is closed, and the item is moved to the shopping cart for further action.</mark> 

- <mark>If the design is incomplete, you will receive a message asking if the item can be added to the cart although the product design is not complete. If you click OK, the Product designer screen is closed and the item is moved to the shopping cart and displayed under Product designs in progress for further action. If you click Cancel, you can continue making changes to the item in the Product designer.</mark> 

##### 22.2.4.2 Undo configuration changes 

<mark>You can also undo any changes made by you, remove the order row, and close the Product designer screen. If any mandatory features are unanswered or any features answers are in conflict, an exclamation icon is displayed against the feature groups to which the features belong and an Answer required dialog box appears:</mark> 

- <mark>If you have not answered one or more required features, each feature is displayed one by one along with the feature group that it belongs to until you answer all the features.</mark> 

- <mark>If any answers are in conflict with answers provided to other features, the features in conflict are displayed along with the feature group that they belong to.</mark> 

#### 22.2.5 Customer claims 

<mark>You can create a customer claim against an item in the e-Sales order page. You must provide the quantity, relevant information, and the reason for making the claim before submitting. The claim number can be used to track the progress.</mark> 

<mark>You can also view the list of claims connected to the customer along with the claim number, item number, description, quantity, order number, row number, planned completion date, and status.</mark> 

#### 22.2.6 Impersonate function 

<mark>Using the impersonate function, you can enter orders on behalf of other users. By clicking on Impersonate, you can see all the subsidiaries' users. Select the user on whose behalf you intend to enter orders and you will be logged in as that user.</mark> 

### 22.3 e-Invoices 

<mark>You can receive electronic invoices including invoice images via a third party service. There are standard functions in Jeeves ERP for interpretation, review and transfer to the accounts payable ledger. Approval can either be done in the accounts payable ledger or via Jeeves ERP's e-Approval module. The advantages of electronic invoicing are many; it is quicker, less expensive, better for the environment, minimizes administration etc.</mark> 

<mark>You can display electronic supplier invoices in a PDF browser. There are buttons for viewing invoices in an embedded browser and a button for viewing invoices in an external browser. This function requires that Adobe Acrobat Reader is installed on the client computer.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP supports integrated electronic invoice exchange through Pagero. Outgoing invoices can be monitored throughout delivery, providing visibility into processing and delivery status. Incoming invoices can be retrieved and processed automatically, with support for reliable processing, error handling, retry management, and user notifications. If processing must be repeated, an invoice can be retrieved again from within Jeeves ERP.</mark>

## 23 System administration and own development 

<mark>Jeeves ERP has a wealth of functions that support system administrators, implementation consultants, developers and the like when setting up, customizing, running and monitoring the system. They can also take advantage of Microsoft SQL’s functionality for querying and manipulating the Jeeves database.</mark> 

### 23.1 System configuration 

<mark>Jeeves ERP can be set up in many different ways to satisfy different needs for accessibility, performance and security. The different configurations in the form of thin and rich clients allow access both within the own network and over the Internet via encrypted tunnels. Any number of server processes can be set up on one or more physical machines in order to manage increased load, and the built-in load balancing distributes the load evenly between processes. By default, all communication is encrypted and compressed but this can be turned off if desired.</mark> 

<mark>To enable running the Jeeves Client through Direct Access, Jeeves ERP supports IPV6 addresses in the communication between server and client for some configurations. This offers an option for smoother system access as no VPN tunnel is required to connect to the Jeeves Client from an outside network.</mark> 

#### 23.1.1 System/configuration settings 

<mark>There are a large number of parameters that can be set up by system administrators, or the equivalent, to control how Jeeves ERP should function in general. This includes parameters about how the database is set up, how information should be displayed (language, font, formats etc.), how the client should behave etc. The parameters can be set per company, group and/or user, depending on the parameter.</mark> 

### 23.2 Jeeves Cloud optimizations 

<mark>You can set up the Jeeves Cloud in AWS. You can also set up the system for domain controller independent user authentication and enable file sharing between the business server and the client.</mark> 

<mark>See also the sections on importing external data to Jeeves Cloud and exporting files to local systems from Jeeves Cloud without a VPN connection under the chapter about General functions.</mark> 

#### 23.2.1 Security identifier for user authentication 

<mark>The Security identifier (SID) enables you to set up authorization to Jeeves ERP for Windows users without a domain account. The SecurityMode parameter allows you to authorize Windows user authentication against a Security identifier (SID) stored in Jeeves ERP.</mark> 

<mark>You can provide the authenticated Windows users' details, including domain and temporary password. If the SecurityMode kernel parameter is set to Windows user authentication against SID stored in Jeeves ERP, and a user attempts to log into Jeeves ERP, a check is made to verify the following:</mark> 

- <mark>the Signature matches the signature in the Users table</mark> 

- <mark>the Windows user exists in the table</mark> 

- <mark>the SID exists for the user.</mark> 

#### 23.2.2 File sharing between business server and client 

<mark>The ClientCache folder enables automatic distribution of files, for instance reports and images used in the reports, from the server to the clients, implying direct file access to a central server folder is not necessary.</mark> 

<mark>206</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

System administration and own development 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0207-01.png)


<mark>Manually create a shared folder on the server that mirrors the ClientCache folder on the client. This folder should contain all files that need to be distributed (mainly report files and images used in Jeeves standard reports) to all connected clients. Once this is done and you start the Jeeves client, Jeeves ERP checks that the ClientCache folder exists. Jeeves ERP then initiates a file comparison (size and checksum) between the files stored in the specified folder on the server and those in the ClientCache folder in the client. It then generates a list of files which are changed on the server or missing on the client and sends the list to the server, which streams the files to the ClientCache folder on the client.</mark> 

#### 23.2.3 AWS S3 bucket access 

<mark>There are kernel parameters under the Cloud section to facilitate the copying of application files from the S3 bucket with the following configurations: Region, AccessKeyId, SecretAccessKey and PathForS3.</mark> 

<mark>There are application parameters to enable AWS S3 bucket access for EDM.</mark> 

### 23.3 Help routines/utilities 

#### 23.3.1 Utilities 

<mark>There are system administration functions for checking processing times, periodic and yearly routines, language management utilities etc.</mark> 

<mark>You can, for example, show the number of invoices per day, per hour, etc.</mark> 

- <mark>number of invoices per day</mark> 

- <mark>number of invoices per hour</mark> 

- <mark>processing time per invoice</mark> 

- <mark>details of the invoices that took longest</mark> 

<mark>There are functions to move customers’, suppliers’ and items’ turnover to the previous year.</mark> 

<mark>There are also language management utilities with which you can manage system and base data, such as drop-down list data, in different languages. You can even use this to translate data texts, for example item descriptions, including data in own tables. The language of the texts is switched when you switch language in the client. Note that this does not affect printouts.</mark> 

#### 23.3.2 Scheduler 

<mark>There is a possibility to schedule print and update jobs. This is described in the Output management section.</mark> 

### 23.4 Security functions 

<mark>With the SecurityMode kernel parameter you can specify which type of authorization Jeeves ERP shall use:</mark> 

- <mark>0 = Default SQL Login</mark> 

- <mark>1 = Windows user authentication against SQL Server and User (sy2), then SQL Login</mark> 

- <mark>2 = Windows user authentication against SQL Server and User (sy2)</mark> 

- <mark>3 = Windows user authentication against User (sy2), then SQL Login</mark> 

- <mark>4 = Windows user authentication against User (sy2)</mark> 

- <mark>5 = Windows user authentication against SID stored in Jeeves ERP</mark> 

- <mark>6 = Federated authentication using identity service</mark> 

<mark>207</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

System administration and own development 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0208-01.png)


#### 23.4.1 Users and user groups 

<mark>In Jeeves ERP, there are two ways in which you can create groups of users to which you can grant permissions and connect settings. There is an earlier, simpler solution, where the user groups are not stored in the site repository and a later, more advanced way of working with groups of users etc.</mark> 

<mark>There are pre-defined site groups for the various roles that normally exist in distribution and manufacturing companies etc. This means that you only need to define user logins and connect them to the relevant role groups, and you only need to create company specific groups if there are any. For installations based on pre-defined roles and settings there are certain restrictions regarding own groupings.</mark> 

#### 23.4.2 Company access 

<mark>There is a program for administration of access rights to companies in multi-company databases. By default, all users have access to all companies, but if you grant a user or group access rights to a company, the user/group is blocked from all other companies. Access rights can be set on the following levels:</mark> 

- <mark>User</mark> 

- <mark>Authorization group</mark> 

- <mark>User group in the site</mark> 

<mark>You can grant access rights either by creating new records in the embedded list and typing in the signature(s) or group id(s) that are to have access to the current company, or by using the program nodes and selecting signatures or groups from the left list and transferring them to the right list.</mark> 

#### 23.4.3 SQL authorization 

<mark>If required there is a possibility to use MS SQL Server’s authorization functions. There is a mechanism that allows Jeeves ERP to be logged in as the owner of the tables in the database, which means that the database administrator does not need to specify the rights for every user and table. The result is that every user initially has full rights to the database. It is then possible to deny certain rights to users.</mark> 

#### 23.4.4 Security manager 

<mark>There is also a built-in tool for security management that enables you to set up authorization on almost all Jeeves objects. The Security manager works with the Users, Site group, Company and Company group dimensions.</mark> 

<mark>One work method is to build a tree structure by importing a program menu file. From the programs in the object tree structure you can access all the related objects, such as fields and buttons etc., to which you can grant or deny users access. However, you can work without a menu if you have your own, structured method of adding Jeeves objects to the Security manager.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP's Security manager is based on the SQL standard called CRUDE, which stands for:</mark> 

<mark>C - Create (i.e. create record)</mark> 

- <mark>R - Read (for example, read record or field value)</mark> 

- <mark>U - Update (for example, update record or field value)</mark> 

- <mark>D - Delete (i.e. delete record)</mark> 

<mark>E - Execute (i.e. execute program, computed field, user function, node or report)</mark> 

<mark>These permissions are not based on permission levels, i.e. a user can have permission to delete but not to update. There are naturally certain relationships, for example you must have the permission to execute a program in order to have any of the other permissions in the program.</mark> 

<mark>208</mark> ( <mark>215)</mark> 

<mark>© Copyright Forterro Sweden AB. All Rights Reserved.</mark> 

System administration and own development 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0209-01.png)


<mark>Permissions are inherited by default from top level down through sublevels. If an object has no permission setting, i.e. if neither Grant nor Deny is checked, the Security manager reads the permissions at runtime are deduced from the overlying object.</mark> 

<mark>There are auditing functions in the Security manager in order to comply with SOX requirements. You can print audit reports, you can search in the permissions data that is created and you can make even deeper analyses where required.</mark> 

#### 23.4.5 Federated authentication 

<mark>With Jeeves cloud-based Identity service, you can log into Jeeves ERP using federated authentication. This offers possibilities for IT to streamline the user administration and apply additional security layers, like multi-factor authentication (MFA). This is available for the Desktop client, Classic client, and Mobile client (iOS and Android). It is based on Open-ID Compliant Services (OICS) and has been verified for Azure AD.</mark> 

### 23.5 Log functions 

#### 23.5.1 Change log 

<mark>You can log new entries, changes and deletions. Logging is done at field level and can include owndeveloped fields, even from own tables. You can also control whether these events require a reason code and/or a password. The purpose of the development is to make it possible for Jeeves ERP users to meet requirements from the American Food and Drug Administration (FDA) concerning logging of events in the database.</mark> 

#### 23.5.2 Log processes 

<mark>There are also functions for automatic logging of:</mark> 

- <mark>currently logged in users in Jeeves ERP,</mark> 

- <mark>currently active and canceled processes,</mark> 

- <mark>certain report runs (when and by whom as well as certain data),</mark> 

- <mark>certain multi row inserts and updates,</mark> 

- <mark>login history, etc.</mark> 

#### 23.5.3 Handling server and client log files 

<mark>The server log files can be automatically zipped and moved to the backup folder. Each zipped log file is named with its creation date. You can also truncate the log files, i.e. remove the oldest file, after a specified count is reached.</mark> 

<mark>The name of the Desktop client log, which is automatically created when you start the client, includes the process ID, signature as well as date and time, making the log file both small and easy to handle.</mark> 

### 23.6 Database administration 

<mark>There are built-in functions in Jeeves ERP for maintenance of the database, for extraction and copying of information to and from databases, etc. You can activate logging of SQL statements for trouble-shooting purposes. There is also an advanced log, in which you can see which macros and user functions are executed in the current program, execution times etc.</mark>

System administration and own development 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0210-01.png)


### 23.7 Developer menu 

<mark>There is a special menu that is normally only available to developers, system administrators and such users. The menu contains options to restart functions and reload windows when doing own development and settings without having to restart Jeeves ERP. There are also corresponding functions to reload when Jeeves' system parameters have been changed and after manual change of local settings (in the site repository) etc.</mark> 

<mark>From here you can also access more advanced options for database administration, such as Recreate indexes and Recompile window procedures.</mark> 

### 23.8 Own development 

<mark>Jeeves ERP provides extensive functionality for adding own modifications and development. We also provide functionality for identifying these customizations in customers' installations for the purpose of Cloud readiness and for facilitating upgrades. We recommend that the Import all changes from DB function in the Added/modified objects program is used as part of this process. The recommendation applies to all installations from Jeeves ERP 6.0 Update 7 and later.</mark> 

###### **Note:** 

- You are not allowed to modify standard Jeeves code in the database or files. 

- <mark>If you create a mobile routine you must store the source code in Jeeves' programs/tables for customization (their names start with the prefix "UsrDef" which stands for User defined). By doing so, you make information about your modifications, which is valuable to</mark> customers and partners, visible within Jeeves ERP. 

- <mark>In order to avoid conflicts with Jeeves standard and other apps, you must follow Jeeves Information Systems' name convention for names of all non-standard objects (tables,</mark> terms, connections, programs, subtags, macros etc.) within all own modifications. 

#### 23.8.1 Name convention 

<mark>Unique name q_A_B_C where:</mark> 

<mark>q_ = Is used to differentiate it from Jeeves standard. This prefix is already used, and it is well established within the Jeeves sphere.</mark> 

<mark>A = Your partner ID.</mark> 

<mark>B = Branch/vertical/application, a suitable name of your choice.</mark> 

<mark>C = Name of your own developed object, for example, an added term, table, macro, trigger, sub tag, database object, program etc.</mark> 

<mark>The ID can only consist of combinations of A-Z , 0-9 and _.</mark> 

<mark>Example: q_JIS_Service_itemclass</mark> 

<mark>Partners must also make sure that their IDs are unique within their own organization.</mark> 

<mark>You can read more about our name convention in the document about Jeeves ERP's apps standards and rules on our partner web.</mark> 

#### 23.8.2 Own adaptations in Jeeves 

<mark>If you use Jeeves ERP’s programs to hold own programs, windows, views, tables, connections, constraints, own database SQL objects (added, replaced or subtags), this can be used as a form of documentation of your adaptations. Most of the objects can also be a part of an application.</mark>

System administration and own development 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0211-01.png)


#### 23.8.3 Own program functionality 

- <mark>You can add your own tables, views, windows and programs to Jeeves ERP and also make fields from your own tables available in standard form windows.</mark> 

- <mark>Tables and views must be created in the database along with primary key, indexes and defaults.</mark> 

- <mark>Own programs can display information from Jeeves ERP's tables and views or own tables and views.</mark> 

- <mark>Own programs can consist of an unlimited number of levels with embedded list windows and/or underlying program nodes. They can also be used as embedded lists and nodes in Jeeves ERP's standard programs.</mark> 

- <mark>You can make own windows and check-off (left-right list) windows</mark> 

- <mark>You can make total windows in your own programs</mark> 

- <mark>You can choose which fields to display in the form and/or list, and how to display: dropdown lists, show fields as checkboxes</mark> 

- <mark>You can choose if your program allows insert, update and or delete</mark> 

- <mark>You can also add Jeeves ERP’s standard nodes to other standard nodes</mark> 

- <mark>You can create right-clicks in standard Jeeves programs as well as in own programs</mark> 

- <mark>You can create drop-down fields in own program based on standard or own data.</mark> 

- <mark>Drop-down lists based on own tables can be translated and displayed in the relevant language when you switch language in the client.</mark> 

#### 23.8.4 Managing own modifications in database logic 

- <mark>You can use subtags (subtags are tagged own code to be placed in existing location tags in our standard code) to modify standard database SQL objects such as procedures, triggers, views and database functions.</mark> 

- <mark>Note that the subtag must be placed in a location tag in the object. If location tag is missing we, Jeeves Information Systems, must add it in our standard code. Otherwise it will disappear after upgrading.</mark> 

- <mark>If subtags are entered in the intended program and an upgrade takes place, the subtags are re-installed in the database automatically. However, if you install fixes manually or by JvsSQLRun, you must remember to re-install subtags manually in this program. You can use the same program for own added or replaced standard procedures, triggers, views, database functions and table functions. In this program, there are nodes for history regarding whole objects and subtags.</mark> 

- <mark>The program with content can be used as a documentation of own adaptations of modified standard code (in subtags) as well as own or replaced triggers, procedures views etc.</mark> 

- <mark>You can add own language managed texts to be used by macros and own SQL code</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves Information Systems provide templates for stored procedures, views, functions and table functions.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves ERP also provides several applications for exporting/importing own development; from a simpler export/import program to the more comprehensive Apps builder. They run the same code, but the Apps builder is recommended since it automatically provides documentation of all included customizations.</mark> 

#### 23.8.5 .NET integration 

<mark>It is possible to integrate Jeeves ERP and Microsoft .NET to expand Jeeves ERP’s macro language with your own functionality written in any of .NET's programming languages, for example, integrating Jeeves ERP with your own Web services, adding your own forms in the macro, etc.</mark> 

<mark>You can use Jeeves .NET integration in two ways; you can call .NET from Jeeves ERP and call Jeeves ERP from .NET. When doing so, you have full access to all objects in the Jeeves database.</mark>

System administration and own development 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0212-01.png)


#### 23.8.6 Define own macros 

<mark>Users can add their own functionality in Jeeves ERP, such as own calculations and controls, by creating a macro using the Macro option on the</mark> **<mark>Tools</mark>** <mark>menu. The statements allowed in a userdefined macro are the same as in the built-in functions in Jeeves, User functions. These functions are defined in the Appl.ufu file. You can look at this file for tips when writing macros.</mark> 

###### **Note** 

Do not include complex SQL code in a macro. Move the code to a procedure and call the <mark>procedure from the macro instead. Basic rule: Business logic in the database and user</mark> interaction logic in macros. 

<mark>Some examples of different ways of using macros:</mark> 

- <mark>To do something that is done often. In this case you can create a button or shortcut command to activate the macro.</mark> 

- <mark>To let the macro fill in a field with a calculated value during entry. In this case you link the macro so that it is activated when the user has entered a value in a certain field.</mark> 

- <mark>To check what is entered in a field. You link the macro so that it is activated at validation of the entered value. In this way you can check the entered value using the macro and decide whether the value should be accepted.</mark> 

- <mark>To check whether a record can be entered</mark> 

- <mark>To start another Windows application</mark> 

- <mark>To display own calculated values in own fields (macro terms)</mark> 

- <mark>Print reports from Jeeves ERP</mark> 

- <mark>Execute a procedure</mark> 

- <mark>Execute database maintenance options</mark> 

<mark>Macros can be executed from:</mark> 

- <mark>the macro editor</mark> 

- <mark>buttons</mark> 

- <mark>short commands (accelerators)</mark> 

- <mark>macro events</mark> 

- <mark>other macros</mark> 

- <mark>own fields (macro-terms)</mark> 

<mark>There are several hundred functions that can be inserted into macros for handling programs, fields, forms, files, SQL expressions, mathematical functions, communication etc. All macro functions are described in Jeeves documentation.</mark> 

#### 23.8.7 Communication with external programs 

<mark>It is possible to exchange information and communicate with Jeeves ERP from other programs, such as MS Word, via an ActiveX/COM interface.</mark>

### 23.9 Jeeves API Platform and standard APIs 

<mark>Jeeves API Platform is a standardized integration service platform that can be used for both standard API development by Jeeves and for development of customized APIs by Jeeves consultants and partners. Jeeves standard APIs are delivered as packages in addition to the annual releases.</mark> 

- <mark>Jeeves ERP's Sales API is intended to make it easier for Jeeves customers to integrate with Jeeves ERP:s sales capabilities from other, external systems such as CRM and e-commerce systems, to support their sales processes.</mark> 

- <mark>Jeeves ERP's Finance APIs are intended to add efficiency and compliance in the financial processes. It is part of Jeeves’ strategy to create connectivity for a variety of external systems and sources.</mark> 

- <mark>Jeeves ERP:s Manufacturing APIs are intended to enable interaction with external systems and sources for resource planning and management. For example, an external system may trigger start and stop of manufacturing operations, report picked and scrapped materials, etc.</mark> 

- <mark>Jeeves ERP:s Inventory optimization APIs will open up the possibility to interact with multiple external Warehouse optimization systems. As in our earlier integrations, they will be used for sending information from Jeeves ERP, such as item information, forecasts, transactional demand, etc. They will also be used to create purchase requisitions and purchase orders in Jeeves ERP from external systems, as well as provide suggestions to adjust inventory parameters where needed. The initial version is only mapped against the third party software EazyStock, powered by Syncron.</mark> 

- <mark>Jeeves ERP:s generic Transport administration (TA) API enables integration with multiple TA service providers' systems, for handling standardized transport documentation from Jeeves ERP:s dispatch programs. For further details, see the chapter about the Sales module.</mark> 

<mark>One common component to the APIs is the API service customization program, which can be used to make adaptations to the standard interface where necessary to adapt to the external system. From this program you can publish/unpublish API endpoints by using the Valid to use checkbox. You can restrict API endpoints to a client or group of clients. By default, all endpoints are hidden unless a client or group is specified.</mark> 

<mark>You can customize any of the API stored procedures. Modification tags are also provided in each section to make it easy to customize. For further information, see the chapters on the API platform and APIs in Jeeves ERP documentation.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves API platform also includes a repository where Jeeves maintains the properties that are used in Jeeves standard APIs, and where developers/consultants should maintain the properties in their custom APIs. This way, we will achieve a single, uniform definition of each property in all API response and request bodies, so that the APIs can more easily be kept consistent.</mark> 

<mark>Once the properties have been defined, a request and response schema can be created by adding a record in the model definition with schema details and all related properties and sub schemas (arrays). Both on the model rows and the model, there are functions for automatically validating and generating the JSON schema, if desired.</mark> 

<mark>The repository also provides a tool for documenting the properties and their mappings with Jeeves tables and columns, which is also reflected in Swagger.</mark> 

<mark>Consumers of Jeeves APIs, both standard and custom APIs, who need up-to-date data or handle big data that are slow moving, can subscribe to changes/events in the Jeeves database. Subscription is based on entities defined on the dynamic API endpoint in Jeeves API platform. Through pagination, consumers can retrieve data sets in a structured manner. When an API endpoint returns a large amount of data, pagination allows the data to be divided into smaller portions.</mark> 

<mark>The API platform includes functionality for exporting API endpoint definitions to external SQL files in order to submit for scanning for security, performance etc.</mark>

#### 23.9.1 Jeeves Outbound API Service 

<mark>The Jeeves Outbound API Service enables Jeeves ERP business processes to connect securely to external web services and cloud-based services. External services can be called directly from business processes, using either synchronous calls for an immediate response or asynchronous calls for background processing.</mark> 

<mark>The service provides centralized, secure management of credentials and supports multiple authentication methods, including client credentials, JWT-based authentication, and API keys. Multiple external services can be configured for each Jeeves environment, enabling organizations to extend their business processes through controlled and standardized integrations.</mark> 

### 23.10 Jeeves Apps builder 

<mark>You can build your own applications in Jeeves ERP. (Apps is an abbreviation of the word</mark> _<mark>applications</mark>_ <mark>, and refers to a piece of software or a packaged solution.) Jeeves Apps builder offers many advantages; here are some examples:</mark> 

- <mark>Built-in import and export functions make it quick and easy to manage solutions.</mark> 

- <mark>Objects and solutions can be re-used.</mark> 

- <mark>The need for technical expertise and consulting can be reduced when introducing new functionality or customizations.</mark> 

- <mark>You can export your adaptations and import them into another database.</mark> 

<mark>A Jeeves application can consist of one or more of the following components:</mark> 

#### 23.10.1 Jeeves application 

- <mark>database objects (views, triggers, procedures, database functions, table functions own or replaced Jeeves’ standard)</mark> 

- <mark>sub tags (application’s unique modifications to Jeeves ERP’s standard procedures, triggers, views and database functions)</mark> 

- <mark>customers’ own programs, nodes, windows, tables, views, fields, connections</mark> 

- <mark>own added nodes to Jeeves standard</mark> 

- <mark>own added computed columns</mark> 

- <mark>script executed when importing apps</mark> 

#### 23.10.2 Data 

- <mark>template companies (all tables with a value in the Company code column)</mark> 

- <mark>database tables and columns:</mark> 

   - <mark>tables without the Company code column</mark> 

   - <mark>tables to be excluded from the template company</mark> 

   - <mark>tables in the template company filtered with a certain condition</mark> 

   - <mark>tables with or without data</mark> 

#### 23.10.3 Settings – all settings saved to site repository groups 

- <mark>site repository groups and group structures</mark> 

- <mark>settings</mark> 

- <mark>menus</mark> 

- <mark>macros</mark> 

- <mark>buttons etc.</mark> 

<mark>The applications are defined in a program in Jeeves ERP and thereafter exported with a separate executable program, Jeeves Apps builder. The program can either be started from within Jeeves ERP, or run stand-alone or by script.</mark>

System administration and own development 


![](images/Jeeves_ERP_2026.Q2.2_Product_overview.pdf-0215-01.png)


<mark>The export results in a zip file that can be imported in any Jeeves installation of the same version as the application from which the application was exported. The zip file has a logical folder structure to make it easy to identify the individual objects on which the application is built. This makes the maintenance of the application easier, enabling changes without making a new export.</mark> 

<mark>When you import an application, the contents of the application are installed in the import database (site and/or Jeeves database). Example: Database objects are compiled into the database. Tables are created with primary keys and defaults, with or without data depending on import type. Site group structures are created and all site data, such as macros, macro events, buttons, settings and menus etc. are installed and ready to use.</mark> 

<mark>Jeeves Apps Builder is used when installing Jeeves ERP to install all settings in our Manufacturing and Distribution packages, as well as data in our template companies.</mark> 

###### **Note** 

When working with apps and own modifications it is important to follow our name <mark>convention in order to avoid conflicts with Jeeves standard and other apps. This applies to names of all non-standard objects (tables, terms, connections, programs, subtags, macros,</mark> groups, apps etc.) within all own modifications. 

#### 23.10.4 Extended name convention 

<mark>Unique name q_A_B_C where:</mark> 

<mark>q_ = Is used to differentiate it from Jeeves standard. This prefix is already used and it is well established within the Jeeves sphere.</mark> 

<mark>A = Your partner ID.</mark> 

<mark>B = Branch/vertical/application, a suitable name of your choice.</mark> 

<mark>C = Name of your own developed object, for example: an added term, table, macro, trigger, sub tag, database object, program etc.</mark> 

<mark>The ID can only consist of combinations of A-Z , 0-9 and _.</mark> 

<mark>Example: q_JIS_Service_itemclass</mark> 

<mark>Partners must also make sure that their IDs are unique within their own organization.</mark> 

<mark>You can read more about our name convention in the document about Jeeves ERP's apps standards and rules on our partner web.</mark>
